# Science course — CURRICULUM (the path)

Science is one world course, organised by **levels, not grades**:
a learner climbs when they are ready, not when a school calendar
says so. The school grades the books come from appear only in
each lesson's sources (provenance), never as the course's
organisation. The path climbs by the size of the thing (see
TITLES.md): **First Steps → Location and Maps → The Human Body →
Materials → Light → Weather and Seasons → Soil and Water →
Plants → Animals → Natural Resources → Our Environment → Family,
School and Community → Work and Heritage → Keeping Healthy →
Science Skills**.

**The first page (what learners are told):** everything in this
course comes from government textbooks — the primary level from
Ethiopia's Environmental Science books — except the **Tip
boxes**: a tip in a box is from us, the people making LiqLearns.
Tips are ours; the lessons are the books'. See ABOUT.md for the
full book list and the acceptance reckoning.

## Where the build stands

2026-11-12.** All three bands built: A (G1–G3) 74 lessons ·
B (G4) +39 created · C (G5–G6) +37 created, with the middle
bands also extending the earlier lessons' depth. **150 lessons**
in total; 296 media filed (12000–12188, 12200–12222, 13200–13248,
13300–13332, 13400–13470). Grade 1 stands on teacher-guide
evidence (no student book held); the G5/G6 teacher guides in
hand are truncated scans and are labelled as such wherever they
are used. The books' own chapter Review sections were not built
as lessons (standing exclusion). Build logs:
`liqlearns-courses-work/ethiopia-2026-11-11/build-logs/band-es-*.md`.

**General Science (Ethiopia, Grades 7–8) — COMPLETE
2026-11-12.** All 14 bands built and registered: 154
new lessons (Grade 7: 85; Grade 8: 69), 1,966
questions, 244 media, plus 12 grown lessons deepened
in place. Five new titles were opened (Earth and
Space, Motion Force and Energy, Sound, Heat,
Electricity and Magnetism). Ten lessons stand on
teacher-guide evidence only, at acceptance 0, each
labelled: the Grade 7 laboratory report, the seven
kinds of reaction around the home, the atmosphere and
measuring-the-too-big-and-the-too-small lessons,
resource depletion, environmental degradation, the
two Grade 8 measurement lessons, and the two Grade 8
solar-system comparison lessons.

## Unit 1 — First Steps

1. **Care for Your Book** (`first-steps/care-for-your-book`) — how to care for your school book so it stays clean, whole and readable all year.

## Unit 2 — Location and Maps

1. **Absolute Location — A Place's Exact Position** (`location-and-maps/absolute-location-a-places-exact-position`) — absolute location (ፍፁማዊ መገኛ) — a place's exact position, stated in latitude then longitude, in degrees and minutes.
2. **Directions — Right, Left and the Four Directions** (`location-and-maps/directions-right-left-and-the-four-directions`) — the directions — right and left, front and back on your own body, and east, west, north and south found from the sun.
3. **East Africa — Its Countries and Capitals** (`location-and-maps/east-africa-its-countries-and-capitals`) — the region of East Africa as one place: located relatively and absolutely, grouped into its nineteen countries, with every capital named.
4. **Ethiopia's Famous Places by Direction** (`location-and-maps/ethiopias-famous-places-by-direction`) — placing Ethiopia's famous places by their direction from Addis Ababa — the heritage sites and the natural sites alike.
5. **GPS and Digital Maps** (`location-and-maps/gps-and-digital-maps`) — how GPS and the digital map tools — Google Maps and Google Earth — find places, measure and show the Earth.
6. **Kebele and Woreda — Reading a Sketch Map** (`location-and-maps/kebele-and-woreda-reading-a-sketch-map`) — how villages group into a kebele and kebeles into a woreda, and how to read — and draw — a woreda sketch map.
7. **Kebele, Woreda and Zone** (`location-and-maps/kebele-woreda-and-zone`) — the next step up: a zone is a group of neighbouring woredas — and why knowing a place's location matters.
8. **Latitude Lines and the Equator** (`location-and-maps/latitude-lines-and-the-equator`) — latitude lines (የኬክሮስ ዲግሪ) — the imaginary lines drawn east–west around the earth, and the equator, the 0° line they are measured from.
9. **Longitude Lines and the Greenwich Meridian** (`location-and-maps/longitude-lines-and-the-greenwich-meridian`) — longitude lines (የኬንትሮስ ዲግሪ) — the imaginary lines drawn pole to pole, measured from the 0° Greenwich meridian.
10. **Map Scale, Key and Symbols** (`location-and-maps/map-scale-key-and-symbols`) — reading a map through the information printed around it — its scale, its north arrow, its key and colours, its symbols, and its year.
11. **My Neighbours and My First Sketch Map** (`location-and-maps/my-neighbours-and-my-first-sketch-map`) — naming the neighbours around your home, saying which side each lives on, and drawing your first sketch map (teacher-guide evidence only).
12. **The National Grid and Grid References** (`location-and-maps/national-grid-and-grid-references`) — reading positions on the National Grid — eastings, northings, and 4-figure and 6-figure grid references.
13. **Neighbours — and Measuring a Bearing** (`location-and-maps/neighbours-and-measuring-a-bearing`) — why neighbours matter to each other — trade, shared resources, security and kinship — how neighbours are located by distance and direction (teacher…
14. **Our Woreda's Neighbours** (`location-and-maps/our-woredas-neighbours`) — the names and positions of the woredas around our own — neighbours at woreda scale.
15. **Relative Location — Where a Place Lies** (`location-and-maps/relative-location-where-a-place-lies`) — relative location (አንጻራዊ መገኛ) — describing where a place lies by the things around it and by direction, from a market stall to a whole zone.
16. **The Amhara Region — Where It Sits and Its Zones** (`location-and-maps/the-amhara-region-where-it-sits-and-its-zones`) — the Amhara Region fixed every way this course has learned — by its neighbours, by its coordinates, and zone by zone with capitals — plus its famous…
17. **What Location Means** (`location-and-maps/what-location-means`) — what location (መገኛ) means — every thing and every place is somewhere, and we say where by pointing to what is near it.
18. **Where My Home Is** (`location-and-maps/where-my-home-is`) — saying where your own home is — which side of the school it lies on, and how it stands compared with the woreda.

## Unit 3 — The Human Body

1. **Adolescence and its changes** (`the-human-body/adolescence-and-its-changes`) — adolescence (ወጣትነት) is the fast change from child to adult — about 14–16 for boys and 12–14 for girls — and its changes split into the primary char…
2. **Experiment 2.1: saliva and the starch test** (`the-human-body/experiment-saliva-and-the-starch-test`) — saliva does real chemical work on food — shown by the book's two-glass experiment — and iodine tests a food for starch
3. **Menstruation and menstrual hygiene** (`the-human-body/menstruation-and-menstrual-hygiene`) — menstruation is a healthy sign that a girl has matured — not a disease and not a defect — blood flows 4–6 days about every 28 days, and it is manag…
4. **Parts of the body and the five senses** (`the-human-body/parts-of-the-body-and-the-five-senses`) — the learner can name the outside parts of the body, name the five sense organs, and say what each part and each sense does
5. **Teeth: two sets, four jobs** (`the-human-body/teeth-two-sets-and-four-jobs`) — a person gets two sets of teeth in a life — the milk set of 20 and the permanent set of 32 — and the four kinds of tooth each do a different job
6. **The brain** (`the-human-body/the-brain`) — the brain is the body's control centre — it has three parts and sits protected inside the skull
7. **The digestive system** (`the-human-body/the-digestive-system`) — food travels through one long tube — mouth, pharynx, oesophagus, stomach, small intestine, large intestine, rectum, anus — and each part does its o…
8. **The heart** (`the-human-body/the-heart`) — the heart is a muscle that pumps blood around the body by squeezing (contracting) and relaxing — and you can feel it work
9. **The human reproductive system** (`the-human-body/the-human-reproductive-system`) — the male testes make sperm and the female ovaries make eggs; when a sperm meets an egg in the uterine tube that meeting is fertilisation, and when …
10. **The kidneys** (`the-human-body/the-kidneys`) — the kidneys — two bean-shaped organs — keep the body's water and salt in balance, remove waste, and help the body make red blood cells
11. **The liver** (`the-human-body/the-liver`) — the liver is the largest internal organ, sitting on the right side under the ribs — it makes bile, filters the blood and makes the proteins that cl…
12. **The lungs and breathing** (`the-human-body/the-lungs-and-breathing`) — the lungs are a spongy pair inside the chest that take in oxygen from the air and give out carbon dioxide
13. **The microscope — seeing the unseen** (`the-human-body/the-microscope-seeing-the-unseen`) — a microscope makes tiny things big enough to see — pond life, parasites in clinic samples, and cells themselves were all unseen before it (Grade 7 General Science)
14. **The microscope — simple and compound, and total magnification** (`the-human-body/simple-and-compound-microscopes`) — a simple microscope has one lens and magnifies about 10–20×; a compound microscope multiplies eyepiece by objective — total magnification is their product (Grade 7 General Science)
15. **The microscope — parts and what each does** (`the-human-body/parts-of-the-compound-microscope`) — every part of the compound microscope has a job — the viewing parts magnify, the stage holds the specimen, and the mirror, condenser and diaphragm manage the light (Grade 7 General Science)
16. **The microscope — using it well, and making your own** (`the-human-body/using-a-microscope-and-making-your-own`) — carry a microscope by arm and base together, mount a specimen under a cover slip in a drop of water, focus low power first — and you can build a working simple microscope from a bottle and a drop of water (Grade 7 General Science)
17. **Cells — the discovery of the cell** (`the-human-body/the-discovery-of-the-cell`) — cells are the building blocks of all living things — Hooke saw dead cork cell walls in 1665 and coined the word 'cell', and Leeuwenhoek saw living single-celled animalcules in pond water in 1674 (Grade 7 General Science)
18. **Cells — inside a cell: organelles and their jobs** (`the-human-body/inside-a-cell-organelles-and-their-jobs`) — every cell has a membrane, cytoplasm, DNA and ribosomes; inside, each organelle has a job — nucleus, mitochondria, and in plants the wall and chloroplasts (Grade 7 General Science)
19. **Cells — shape, size and function** (`the-human-body/cell-shape-size-and-function`) — cells stay small — on average 10–20 µm — so materials can cross them easily, and each cell's shape fits its job: disc, spindle or long and branched (Grade 7 General Science)
20. **Cells — from one cell to a whole organism** (`the-human-body/from-one-cell-to-a-whole-organism`) — some organisms live as one cell doing everything, and multicellular organisms divide the labour across five levels: cell, tissue, organ, organ system, organism (Grade 7 General Science)
21. **Cells — respiration: releasing energy from food** (`the-human-body/respiration-releasing-energy-from-food`) — cells release energy from glucose with oxygen in the mitochondria, making ATP; without enough oxygen they ferment instead — making alcohol or lactic acid, with far less energy (Grade 7 General Science)
22. **Cells — photosynthesis and its partnership with respiration** (`the-human-body/photosynthesis-and-its-partnership-with-respiration`) — in chloroplasts, chlorophyll traps light to turn carbon dioxide and water into glucose and oxygen; photosynthesis and respiration interlock — each one's products are the other's raw materials (Grade 7 General Science)
23. **The human body — systems working together** (`the-human-body/the-human-body-systems-working-together`) — a body system is a group of organs working together on one job — parts alone do nothing, organised systems do the work — and the body runs seven systems (Grade 8 General Science)
24. **Skin — the integumentary system** (`the-human-body/skin-the-integumentary-system`) — the skin is a three-layer organ — epidermis, dermis, subcutaneous fat — with hair and nails built of keratin; it does five jobs from protection to temperature control (Grade 8 General Science)
25. **Muscles — how movement happens** (`the-human-body/muscles-how-movement-happens`) — muscles move the body by pulling in pairs — skeletal, cardiac and smooth muscle; a flexor contracts while its extensor partner relaxes (Grade 8 General Science)
26. **The skeleton — frame, joints and fractures** (`the-human-body/the-skeleton-frame-joints-and-fractures`) — the skeleton is one frame in two parts — axial (centre) and appendicular (limbs) — joined by joints with ligaments and cartilage; bones break in named ways and mend (Grade 8 General Science)
27. **Reproduction, puberty and staying safe** (`the-human-body/reproduction-puberty-and-staying-safe`) — puberty brings primary and secondary characteristics (girls about 11–13, boys about 14–16 as printed); the male and female organs do the work of reproduction, and staying safe is part of the teaching (Grade 8 General Science)

28. **What psychology is** (`the-human-body/what-psychology-is`) — what is psychology? — the study of the mind and of behaviour, opening the Step-up chapter that steps up from the body to the person (Australia, Grade 9 — *Pearson Science 9*, 11.1, printed p. 432)
29. **The social self** (`the-human-body/the-social-self`) — the self as it exists among other people, formed, shown and changed in our relations with others — the closing section of the Step-up chapter (Australia, Grade 9 — *Pearson Science 9*, 11.3, printed p. 451)
30. **Human growth and development stages** (`the-human-body/human-growth-and-development-stages`) — the stages of human development — infant, young child, teenager, adult — and the physical changes and abilities at each stage — curriculum evidence only, acceptance 0 (Bahamas, Grade 1 — *Primary Science Curriculum Guidelines — Grades 1-2*, printed p. 66)

31. **Reproductive health and hygiene** (`the-human-body/reproductive-health-and-hygiene`) — keeping the reproductive system healthy by daily hygiene, cleanliness and staying safe — closing the Grade 6 book's run on the reproductive organs, puberty and preventing pregnancy (Cambodia, Grade 6 — *វិទ្យាសាស្ត្រ ថ្នាក់ទី៦ (Science, Grade 6)*, Lesson 9, printed p. 52; built for the Cambodia placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/cambodia-placements.md)

32. **Measuring growth — height charts and averages** (`the-human-body/measuring-growth-height-charts-and-averages`) — measuring the heights of ten pupils, finding the average height, plotting a bar chart of height against age, and estimating P9 heights from a P1–P8 chart (Seychelles, Grade 8 (P8) — *Seychelles Integrated Science — Food and Growth*, Unit 7, Topic 1, printed booklet pp. 1–3; built for the Seychelles placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/seychelles-placements.md)
33. **Looking after your heart and circulation** (`the-human-body/looking-after-your-heart-and-circulation`) — keeping the circulation healthy — the diseases that strike it (anaemia, narrowed arteries, raised blood pressure) and the guard against them: varied good food, exercise that stirs the heart and circulation, cleaning wounds with water and soap so germs stay out, and seeing the doctor for bleeding or nosebleeds (Sudan, Grade 6 — *العلوم (Science), Grade 6*, Unit 1, Lesson 1, printed p. 5)
34. **Emotions — and how we feel** (`the-human-body/emotions-and-how-we-feel`) — my emotions — the feelings a person has and can name: joy, sadness, anger and fear; emotions show in the face and body, and no feeling stays at its strongest for ever (Angola, Grade 3 — *Estudo do Meio, Manual da 3.ª Classe*, printed p. 24; built for the Angola placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/angola-placements.md)
35. **Early pregnancy — and its risks** (`the-human-body/early-pregnancy-and-its-risks`) — early pregnancy (a gravidez precoce): pregnancy while the mother's own body is still growing, and its risks for the young mother, the baby and her schooling (Angola, Grade 6 — *Ciências da Natureza, Manual da 6.ª Classe*, printed p. 100; built for the Angola placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/angola-placements.md)

36. **Tissues — plant and animal** (`the-human-body/tissues-plant-and-animal`) — a tissue defined and classified: a group of like cells joined to do one job, sorted into plant tissues and animal tissues, with nervous and reticular connective tissue named among the animal tissues (Dominican Republic, Grade 6 — *Ciencias de la Naturaleza 6*, printed pp. 28, 32; built for the Dominican Republic placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/dominican-republic-placements.md)

37. **The sense of smell** (`the-human-body/the-sense-of-smell`) — how the sense of smell helps us know things we cannot see, and keeping the nose safe (Kuwait, Grade 1, Term 1 — *Science, Grade 1, Term 1 (Student Book)*, Section 2 printed p. 43; built for the Kuwait placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/kuwait-placements.md)

38. **The sense of touch** (`the-human-body/the-sense-of-touch`) — how we discover things by touch — hot and cold, rough and smooth, hard and soft — and what harms the hand (Kuwait, Grade 1, Term 1 — *Science, Grade 1, Term 1 (Student Book)*, Section 2 printed p. 47; built for the Kuwait placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/kuwait-placements.md)

39. **The sense of taste** (`the-human-body/the-sense-of-taste`) — how the tongue knows sweet, sour, salty and bitter, and how taste warns that food has spoiled (Kuwait, Grade 1, Term 1 — *Science, Grade 1, Term 1 (Student Book)*, Section 2 printed p. 52; built for the Kuwait placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/kuwait-placements.md)

40. **Cell division** (`the-human-body/cell-division`) — how one cell copies its hereditary material and divides into two — growth, repair and the doorway to reproduction (the Biology mitosis lesson is its secondary-depth twin) (Kuwait, Grade 9, Term 1 — *Science, Grade 9, Term 1 (Student Book)*, printed p. 30; built for the Kuwait placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/kuwait-placements.md)

41. **Stimulus and response — how the body reacts** (`the-human-body/stimulus-and-response-how-the-body-reacts`) — a stimulus is a change the body can sense, a response is the body's reaction to it — sense, then answer, one event in two halves (China, Grade 5 (Hebei) — *科学五年级上册 (Science, Grade 5, first volume) — Hebei edition*, printed p. 15; built for the China placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/china-placements.md)

42. **Homeostasis — keeping the body in balance** (`the-human-body/homeostasis-keeping-the-body-in-balance`) — the body's inside conditions — temperature, water, the things dissolved in its blood — held steady while the outside changes, by the sense–answer–return pattern (Eritrea, Grade 8 — *Science for Grade 8 Textbook*, printed p. 34; built for the Eritrea placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/eritrea-placements.md)
43. **Measuring blood pressure** (`the-human-body/measuring-blood-pressure`) — Blood pressure measurement — measuring the pressure of the blood as part of the circulatory system — as Rwanda's Primary 6 SET book closes its circulation unit with it (Rwanda, Primary 6 — *Science and Elementary Technology for Rwanda Schools, Pupil's Book Primary 6*, printed p. 232; built for the Rwanda placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/rwanda-placements.md)
44. **Problems of the tongue and mouth** (`the-human-body/problems-of-the-tongue-and-mouth`) — Tongue disorders and problems — what can go wrong with the tongue — as Rwanda's Primary 4 SET book teaches inside its sensory organs unit, beside the tongue's functions and hygiene (Rwanda, Primary 4 — *Science and Elementary Technology for Rwandan Schools, Pupil's Book Primary 4*, printed p. 230; built for the Rwanda placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/rwanda-placements.md)
45. **Love or infatuation — telling the difference** (`the-human-body/love-or-infatuation-telling-the-difference`) — Love or infatuation — telling the difference between them — as Rwanda's Primary 5 SET book teaches it at the close of its reproductive system unit (Rwanda, Primary 5 — *Science and Elementary Technology for Rwandan Schools, Pupil's Book 5*, printed p. 179; built for the Rwanda placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/rwanda-placements.md)
46. **Inside the body — internal organs** (`the-human-body/inside-the-body-internal-organs`) — inside the body — the internal organs, the parts you cannot see from outside, as Zimbabwe's Grade 3 guide opens its health topic with them (Zimbabwe, Grade 3 — *PlusOne Science and Technology* teacher's guides (Grade 3 printed p. 1), guide evidence only, acceptance 0; built for the Zimbabwe placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/zimbabwe-placements.md)
47. **Hormones and the endocrine system** (`the-human-body/hormones-and-the-endocrine-system`) — the endocrine system and the important hormones of the human body — released from glands on demand, with unwanted reactions when more or less than the needed amount is released — as Bangladesh's Class 9 books teach it (Bangladesh, Grade 9 — *Science Investigative Study Class 9*, printed p. 163; Grade 9 — *Science Exercise Book Class 9*, printed p. 75; built for the Bangladesh placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/bangladesh-placements.md)
48. **The immune system — how the body defends itself** (`the-human-body/the-immune-system-how-the-body-defends-itself`) — the immune system of the human body and its general process — and how antibiotics prevent bacterial infections — as Bangladesh's Class 9 books teach it (Bangladesh, Grade 9 — *Science Investigative Study Class 9*, printed p. 178; Grade 9 — *Science Exercise Book Class 9*, printed p. 80; built for the Bangladesh placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/bangladesh-placements.md)

49. **Breastfeeding — the baby's first food** (`the-human-body/breastfeeding-the-babys-first-food`) — the mother's milk first: the colostrum of the first three days as a complete food carrying natural protection against germs; natural feeding against the bottle on cost, cleanliness, the child's health and the mother's closeness (Yemen, Grade 9 — *Science, Grade 9, Part 2*, printed p. 130; built for the Yemen placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/yemen-placements.md)
50. **Biological and social traits of boys and girls** (`the-human-body/biological-and-social-traits-of-boys-and-girls`) — sorting the characteristics of males and females into biological traits (fixed from before birth — reproductive organs, pregnancy, menstruation, beard) and social traits (changeable — hairstyle, clothes, jobs, tastes) (Vietnam, Grade 5 — *Khoa học 5*, printed p. 85; built for the Vietnam placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/vietnam-placements.md)

51. **Fertilisation, pregnancy and birth** (`the-human-body/fertilisation-pregnancy-and-birth`) — one sperm enters the ovum and forms the zygote, the new individual's first cell; the amniotic sac protects the baby; morula, blastula, gastrula drawn stage by stage, the chorion's villi entering the endometrium to form the placenta, passing nutrients, oxygen and antibodies from mother to child; birth: sac breaks, contractions, expulsion — and double fertilisation in flowering plants as the parallel the same books teach (Nicaragua, Grade 6 — *Ciencias Naturales, 6to grado*, printed pp. 55–59; Grade 9 — *Ciencias Naturales, Noveno grado*, printed pp. 142–144; Grade 9 Módulo — *Módulo Autoformativo de Ciencias Naturales, 9no grado*, printed p. 25; built for the Nicaragua placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/nicaragua-placements.md)

52. **The eye and ear — inside and out** (`the-human-body/the-eye-and-ear-inside-and-out`) — the eye outside and inside — the retina with its light-sensitive cells sending the message to the brain — and the ear outside and inside — ear drum, ossicles and cochlea — each compared with other animals' eyes and ears (South Sudan, Primary 5 — *Primary Science, Pupil's Book 5*, 2.2 External structure of the eye, SS P5 SB, printed p. 25; built for the South Sudan placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/south-sudan-placements.md)

## Unit 4 — Materials

1. **Chemical change: burning and gas-making** (`materials/chemical-change-burning-and-gas-making`) — a chemical change makes a new body — proved by the book's Experiment 2 (paper burned to ash) and Experiment 3 (lemon and yeast filling a balloon wi…
2. **Density: why things sink or float** (`materials/density-why-things-sink-or-float`) — density is the amount of matter packed into a body's volume — and it decides whether a thing sinks or floats: the iron ball sinks, the sponge floats
3. **Describing things: matter and its properties** (`materials/describing-things`) — everything around us is matter, and we tell things apart by their properties: colour, shape, size, taste, smell, soft or rough, heavy or light, swe…
4. **Electricity and magnets** (`materials/electricity-and-magnets`) — electric energy comes from dry cells and generators converting other energies; current needs a charge difference and a closed circuit; magnets pull…
5. **Elements, compounds and mixtures** (`materials/elements-compounds-and-mixtures`) — matter is anything that takes up space and has mass, built of atoms; pure matter splits into elements (one kind of atom) and compounds (elements ch…
6. **Force, friction and gravity** (`materials/force-friction-and-gravity`) — a force is a push or pull — contact or non-contact; force changes shape, direction and speed; friction fights motion between touching surfaces (sta…
7. **Heat and how it travels** (`materials/heat-and-how-it-travels`) — heat is energy flowing from a hotter body to a colder one — a temperature difference is required — and it travels three ways: conduction (handed al…
8. **How materials behave: stretchy, breakable, shapable** (`materials/how-materials-behave`) — when we pull, drop and beat materials, they behave in kinds: some stretch and spring back, some break, some can be drawn into wire or beaten into s…
9. **Mixtures and how to separate them** (`materials/mixtures-and-how-to-separate-them`) — mixtures are heterogeneous (ልይ ዘር — the components show) or homogeneous (ዋህድ ዘር — hidden, like a solution of solute in solvent), and because a mixt…
10. **Motion, distance and speed** (`materials/motion-distance-and-speed`) — motion is change of place relative to a reference point; motions sort by path into straight-line, circular, oscillating and rotational; distance is…
11. **Oxides, acids, bases and salts** (`materials/oxides-acids-bases-and-salts`) — oxides — an element joined with oxygen — come in four characters (basic, acidic, amphoteric, neutral); basic oxides with water make bases, acidic o…
12. **Properties that identify a thing** (`materials/properties-that-identify-a-thing`) — every material has properties — colour, length, hardness, shape and more — and the properties are how we tell one material from another
13. **Simple machines** (`materials/simple-machines`) — simple machines multiply force, multiply speed/distance, or change force's direction — but never multiply work; the six kinds are the lever (three …
14. **Solids, liquids and gases** (`materials/solids-liquids-and-gases`) — matter exists in three states: solid keeps shape and volume; liquid keeps volume but takes the container's shape; gas has neither and spreads to fi…
15. **Sound and vibration** (`materials/sound-and-vibration`) — sound is energy made when bodies vibrate; it travels only through matter, never through a vacuum; Experiment 5 finds what vibrates in six Ethiopian…
16. **Symbols — writing the elements** (`materials/symbols-writing-the-elements`) — an element's symbol is a one- or two-letter shorthand, first letter capital, second small — most from English names, some from Latin (Fe, Cu, Au, K, Na), which is why Ca/Cu and He/Hg must not be confused (Grade 7 General Science)
17. **Formulas — reading what a compound is made of** (`materials/formulas-reading-what-a-compound-is-made-of`) — a formula lists the element types and, by subscript, the atom counts; element molecules may be monoatomic, diatomic or polyatomic (Grade 7 General Science)
18. **Formulas — valence, the combining power** (`materials/formulas-valence-the-combining-power`) — valence is how many partners an atom combines with, read off compounds; some elements vary — Fe is 2 in FeCl₂ but 3 in FeCl₃ (Grade 7 General Science)
19. **Formulas — writing binary compounds (criss-cross)** (`materials/formulas-writing-binary-compounds-criss-cross`) — symbols side by side, valences on top, simplify, cross them down as subscripts — calcium nitride becomes Ca₃N₂ (Grade 7 General Science)
20. **Formulas — naming binary compounds** (`materials/formulas-naming-binary-compounds`) — metal first, non-metal renamed with -ide; variable-valence metals carry a Roman numeral; non-metal pairs use Greek prefixes (Grade 7 General Science)
21. **Formulas — polyatomic ions and their compounds** (`materials/formulas-polyatomic-ions-and-their-compounds`) — charged atom groups with memorised formulas criss-cross the same way, brackets where a group repeats — drilled by the book's 100-card ion game (Grade 7 General Science)
22. **Formulas — the two meanings of a formula** (`materials/formulas-the-two-meanings-of-a-formula`) — qualitatively a formula names which elements are present; quantitatively its coefficients and subscripts count molecules and atoms (Grade 7 General Science)
23. **Reactions — reactants become products** (`materials/reactions-reactants-become-products`) — a reaction converts reactants into products — word equation first, then symbols; magnesium ribbon burns to a new ash (Grade 7 General Science)
24. **Reactions — five signs to look for** (`materials/reactions-five-signs-to-look-for`) — colour change, a solid forming, gas forming, texture change, energy change — and any one sign alone does not prove a reaction (Grade 7 General Science)
25. **Reactions — conservation of mass** (`materials/reactions-conservation-of-mass`) — mass is neither created nor destroyed in a reaction; atom kinds and counts are unchanged (Grade 7 General Science)
26. **Equations — balancing by inspection** (`materials/equations-balancing-by-inspection`) — equal atom counts each side, subscripts never changed; inspection balancing stepped through to 4K + O₂ → 2K₂O (Grade 7 General Science)
27. **Equations — balancing by LCM, and modelling with circles** (`materials/equations-balancing-by-lcm-and-modelling-with-circles`) — the LCM method totals valence numbers and divides back into coefficients; coloured-circle models build the balance physically (Grade 7 General Science)
28. **Reactions — chemistry in everyday life** (`materials/reactions-chemistry-in-everyday-life`) — burning, cooking, smelting, brewing — reactions as the basis of energy and culture; the guide's full tella-making process filed as teacher evidence (Grade 7 General Science)
29. **Reactions — seven kinds of reaction around the home** (`materials/reactions-seven-kinds-of-reaction-around-the-home`) — combustion, double displacement, electrochemistry, digestion, acid–base, soap action, cooking changes — teacher-guide evidence only, acceptance 0 (Grade 7 General Science)
30. **Matter — the particle theory** (`materials/matter-the-particle-theory`) — matter occupies space and has mass, and everything is made of tiny particles that attract each other, have spaces between them, always move, and move faster when heated; Democritus filed as teacher evidence (Grade 7 General Science)
31. **Matter — diffusion** (`materials/matter-diffusion`) — particles spread from where they are crowded to where they are spread out — fast in gases, slower in liquids, essentially not at all in solids (Grade 7 General Science)
32. **Changes — useful and harmful changes in daily life** (`materials/changes-useful-and-harmful`) — the changes of daily life sort into useful and harmful — and most useful ones are worked on purpose, at home, every day (Grade 7 General Science)
33. **Separating mixtures — magnet and evaporation** (`materials/separating-magnet-and-evaporation`) — two separations worked by hand: a magnet lifts iron out of a mixture, and heating drives water off a solution leaving the solid behind (Grade 7 General Science)
34. **Separating mixtures — settling, pouring off and filtering** (`materials/separating-settling-pouring-and-filtering`) — muddy water is cleaned in stages: let the solid settle, pour the clear layer off, then filter what remains through paper (Grade 7 General Science)
35. **Separating mixtures — the separatory funnel** (`materials/separating-the-separatory-funnel`) — two liquids that refuse to mix are parted in a stoppered funnel: settle into layers, then drain the lower layer through the stopcock and stop exactly at the boundary (Grade 7 General Science)
36. **Separating mixtures — distillation** (`materials/separating-distillation`) — a liquid mixture is parted by boiling point: the lower-boiling part vaporises first, condenses, and is collected as distillate — the way of the laboratory still and of the local araki still alike (Grade 7 General Science)
37. **Separating mixtures — local methods** (`materials/separating-local-methods`) — the community's own separation kit — sieving, winnowing, decanting coffee in the jebena, clarifying tella and tej, and the local still — is the laboratory's methods in home dress (Grade 7 General Science)
38. **Early ideas of the atom — Democritus against Aristotle** (`materials/early-ideas-of-the-atom-democritus-against-aristotle`) — matter was pictured two rival ways in ancient Greece — Democritus: built of indivisible atoms with empty space between; Aristotle and Plato: endlessly divisible — and Dalton's atomic theory later settled the argument on evidence (Grade 8 General Science)
39. **Inside the atom — protons, neutrons, electrons** (`materials/inside-the-atom-protons-neutrons-electrons`) — an atom holds protons and neutrons in a nucleus with electrons around it; Z counts the protons, A counts protons plus neutrons, and the amu is 1/12 of a carbon-12 atom (Grade 8 General Science)
40. **Isotopes — and why atomic masses are not whole numbers** (`materials/isotopes-and-why-atomic-masses-are-not-whole-numbers`) — atoms of the same element can carry different numbers of neutrons — isotopes — and an element's printed atomic mass is the weighted average of its isotopes, which is why it is not a whole number (Grade 8 General Science)
41. **Molecules — atoms joined** (`materials/molecules-atoms-joined`) — atoms join into molecules — monoatomic, diatomic or polyatomic — and the molecule is the smallest particle of a substance that still behaves as that substance (Grade 8 General Science)
42. **Organic and inorganic compounds** (`materials/organic-and-inorganic-compounds`) — carbon is the dividing line: organic compounds are carbon compounds — with named exceptions (carbon oxides, carbonates and a few others counted inorganic); inorganic compounds cover everything else (Grade 8 General Science)
43. **Hydrocarbons — alkanes, alkenes, alkynes** (`materials/hydrocarbons-alkanes-alkenes-alkynes`) — compounds of carbon and hydrogen only fall into three homologous series — alkanes CₙH₂ₙ₊₂ (single bonds), alkenes CₙH₂ₙ (one double bond), alkynes CₙH₂ₙ₋₂ (one triple bond) (Grade 8 General Science)
44. **Oxides — basic, acidic and in-between** (`materials/oxides-basic-acidic-and-in-between`) — oxides split into basic oxides (metal oxides that form bases), acidic oxides (non-metal oxides that form acids), amphoteric oxides that act both ways, and neutral oxides that do neither (Grade 8 General Science)
45. **Acids and bases — definitions and naming** (`materials/acids-and-bases-definitions-and-naming`) — acids release H⁺ in water and bases release OH⁻ — soluble bases are alkalis — and an acid's name is built from its anion: -ide → hydro-…-ic, -ate → -ic, -ite → -ous (Grade 8 General Science)
46. **Indicators and pH — testing with colour** (`materials/indicators-and-ph-testing-with-colour`) — indicators change colour in acid and in base, and the pH scale 0–14 fixes the strength: below 7 acid, 7 neutral, above 7 base — with local plant indicators as the eco-friendly test (Grade 8 General Science)
47. **Working with acids and bases — properties, uses, precautions** (`materials/working-with-acids-and-bases-properties-uses-precautions`) — acids react with active metals to release hydrogen, taste sour, conduct and serve in batteries, food and industry; bases feel soapy — and the safety rules are content, not a footnote (Grade 8 General Science)
48. **Neutralisation — cancelling acid with base** (`materials/neutralisation-cancelling-acid-with-base`) — acid + base → salt + water: neutralisation, put to work treating wastewater, farming acid soils back toward the pH a crop needs, and at home (Grade 8 General Science)
49. **Salts — preparation, names and uses** (`materials/salts-preparation-names-and-uses`) — salts are made three ways (acid + base, acid + metal, acid + carbonate), named from their acid, and used everywhere from table salt to fertiliser (Grade 8 General Science)
50. **Materials and their properties — hard, strong, flexible** (`materials/materials-and-their-properties-hard-strong-flexible`) — the three mechanical properties tested one at a time: hardness by the scratch contest (the harder scratches the softer), strength by loading to failure, flexibility by bending — with elastic and brittle as its neighbours — and materials chosen by matching the property to the job — teacher-guide evidence only, acceptance 0 (Gabon, Grade 4 — Super Efficace, Guide pédagogique, 4e année)
51. **Rust and corrosion — how air and water attack iron** (`materials/rust-and-corrosion-how-air-and-water-attack-iron`) — rust is iron transformed, not dirt on it: the three-nail experiment proves corrosion needs air (oxygen) AND water together, salt speeds it, and every protection — paint, oil, galvanising, tin, stainless steel, dry storage — is that proof applied — teacher-guide evidence only, acceptance 0 (Gabon, Grade 4 — Super Efficace, Guide pédagogique, 4e année)
52. **Materials around us — metal, wood and what things are made of** (`materials/materials-around-us-metal-wood-and-what-things-are-made-of`) — every object is made of materials, named by their family marks (metal cold, shiny, ringing, some of it magnetic; wood warm with grain; glass transparent and sharp-breaking), and most objects are assemblies — part by part, each material chosen for its part's job — teacher-guide evidence only, acceptance 0 (Gabon, Grade 5 — Super Efficace, Guide pédagogique, 5e année)
53. **Air is there — air takes up space and can be pushed** (`materials/air-is-there-air-takes-up-space-and-can-be-pushed`) — air is real matter: the cup pushed straight into water keeps its paper dry because trapped air occupies the cup, and pushed air pushes back — balloons, tyres, pumps and wind at work — teacher-guide evidence only, acceptance 0 (Gabon, Grade 3 — Super Efficace, Guide pédagogique, 3e année)
54. **Air can be squeezed — compressing air and its spring back** (`materials/air-can-be-squeezed-compressing-air-and-its-spring-back`) — sealed air compresses into a smaller space and springs back to its first volume — air is elastic — while water refuses to compress at all; the spring runs pumps, tyres, cushions, sprayers and air brakes — teacher-guide evidence only, acceptance 0 (Gabon, Grade 5 — Super Efficace, Guide pédagogique, 5e année)
55. **The candle trick — air and burning** (`materials/the-candle-trick-air-and-burning`) — a flame put under a glass goes out: burning needs air, and when the air trapped under the glass is used up the flame stops — the candle trick itself, watched and explained (Finland — *Luova LUMA -hankkeen aktiviteettipankki*, activity 2, p. 5)
56. **Colours dancing in milk — surface tension** (`materials/colours-dancing-in-milk-surface-tension`) — drops of colour rest on milk's surface film, and a touch of soap breaks the film and chases the fat, so the colours swirl and dance (Finland — *Luova LUMA -hankkeen aktiviteettipankki*, activity 8, p. 14)
57. **Heat paints colours — bilberry indicator** (`materials/heat-paints-colours-bilberry-indicator`) — bilberry juice changes colour in warm water and in cold water, so heat itself is read from a colour change — Finland's own way, using a local berry (Finland — *Luova LUMA -hankkeen aktiviteettipankki*, activity 11, p. 18)
58. **Sand flowing — and what changes it** (`materials/sand-flowing-and-what-changes-it`) — dry sand pours grain over grain like a slow liquid, and what affects its flowing is found by changing one thing at a time and comparing pours (Finland — *Luova LUMA -hankkeen aktiviteettipankki*, activity 14, p. 22)
59. **Making natural dyes from plants** (`materials/making-natural-dyes-from-plants`) — finding which plant parts make colour for materials, and how those parts dissolve in water — the dye-making investigation inside the mixtures unit — teacher-guide evidence only, acceptance 0 (Timor-Leste, Grade 3 — *Ciências Naturais, Grade 3 Teacher Guide: Unit Lesson Plans, Period 2*, printed p. 513)
60. **Dissolving — when solids disappear into liquids** (`materials/dissolving-when-solids-disappear-into-liquids`) — water and other liquids as solvents: a solid disappears into the liquid and is still there, and the dissolving races test what changes the speed — fine sugar against cubes, stirring against still, warm water against cold (Serbia, Grade 3 — *Природа и друштво 3*, printed p. 112)
61. **Rocks — and what they are** (`materials/rocks-and-what-they-are`) — what rocks are: the hard, natural solid material the Earth itself is made of — not alive, not made by people — met in the cliff, the river stone and the pebble — teacher-guide evidence only, acceptance 0 (Albania, Grade 2 — *Libër mësuesi — Dituri natyre 2*, printed p. 43)
62. **Evaporation — and what speeds it up** (`materials/evaporation-and-what-speeds-it-up`) — evaporation as a liquid turning into vapour from its surface without boiling, and its speed factors: warmth, moving air and a wider surface speed it up; cold, still air and a small surface slow it down — teacher-guide evidence only, acceptance 0 (Albania, Grade 5 — *Libër mësuesi — Dituri natyre 5*, printed p. 38)
63. **Colloids and the Tyndall effect** (`materials/colloids-and-the-tyndall-effect`) — a colloid is a mixture whose tiny particles are spread through another substance and never settle out; the Tyndall effect — a light beam's path made visible as the colloid's particles scatter it — is the test that tells a colloid from a true solution (Costa Rica, Grade 7 — *Ciencias, Sétimo, Código 80002, Antologías CONED*, printed p. 92)

64. **Building new objects from a small set of pieces** (`materials/building-new-objects-from-a-small-set-of-pieces`) — modelling how an object made of a small set of pieces can be disassembled and reshaped into a new object with a different function (United States, Grade 2 — *2nd Grade — for Utah SEEd Standards, Revised 2025*, 2.3.3, printed p. 98)
65. **Water expands when it freezes** (`materials/water-expands-when-it-freezes`) — water breaks the usual rule: as it freezes it expands, taking more room as ice than it did as water — its dilatation — so ice floats and a full closed bottle can crack (Cuba, Grade 5 — *Ciencias Naturales, Quinto grado*, printed p. 100)
66. **Water droplets — adhesion and cohesion** (`materials/water-droplets-adhesion-and-cohesion`) — why a water drop is round on one surface and flat on another: cohesion, water holding to itself, against adhesion, water holding to the surface — drops on foil and on a coin, drops joining, the detergent test, and glass plates stuck together by a water film (Samoa, Year 10 — *Science — Year 10, Book 2 (MESC Samoa)*, printed pp. 51–52)

67. **Physical changes of matter** (`materials/physical-changes-of-matter`) — changes of state that can be reversed — melting, freezing, evaporating, condensing — where the substance stays itself (Cambodia, Grade 6 — *វិទ្យាសាស្ត្រ ថ្នាក់ទី៦ (Science, Grade 6)*, Lesson 12, printed p. 74; built for the Cambodia placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/cambodia-placements.md)

68. **Gases in the air — oxygen and carbon dioxide** (`materials/gases-in-the-air-oxygen-and-carbon-dioxide`) — preparing oxygen by heating potassium permanganate; burning carbon makes carbon dioxide, which turns limewater milky; charcoal made by burning wood in a limited supply of air (Seychelles, Grade 7 (P7) — *Seychelles Integrated Science — Air and Weather*, Unit 2, Topic 5, printed booklet pp. 14–16; built for the Seychelles placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/seychelles-placements.md)

69. **Growing crystals** (`materials/growing-crystals`) — growing crystals of magnesium sulphate or copper sulphate from solution; crystals have regular shapes with straight edges (Seychelles, Grade 7 (P7) — *Seychelles Integrated Science — Water*, Unit 3, Project, printed booklet p. 12; built for the Seychelles placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/seychelles-placements.md)
71. **Textiles — what clothes are made of** (`materials/textiles-what-clothes-are-made-of`) — textile materials (os materiais têxteis): what cloth is made from, and the different kinds of clothing sorted by their fabrics (Angola, Grade 1 — *Estudo do Meio, Manual da 1.ª Classe*, printed p. 53, and Grade 2 — *Estudo do Meio, Manual da 2.ª Classe*, printed p. 55; built for the Angola placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/angola-placements.md)
72. **Bodies and substances — what things are** (`materials/bodies-and-substances-what-things-are`) — the idea of a body and a substance (noção de corpo e de substância): a body is anything that takes up space, and a substance is what a body is made of — the opening idea of Angola's Grade 5 book (Angola, Grade 5 — *Ciências da Natureza, Manual da 5.ª Classe*, printed p. 8; built for the Angola placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/angola-placements.md)
73. **What air is made of** (`materials/what-air-is-made-of`) — the components of air (os componentes do ar): air as a mixture of gases, nitrogen the largest part, and why the gases that compose the air matter (Angola, Grade 5 — *Ciências da Natureza, Manual da 5.ª Classe*, printed pp. 18–19; built for the Angola placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/angola-placements.md)
74. **The properties of water** (`materials/the-properties-of-water`) — the physical properties of water (as propriedades físicas da água): colourless, without taste or smell of its own, flowing and taking the shape of its vessel while keeping its amount (Angola, Grade 5 — *Ciências da Natureza, Manual da 5.ª Classe*, printed p. 50; built for the Angola placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/angola-placements.md)

70. **Compound machines** (`materials/compound-machines`) — two or more simple machines joined together so they work as one — scissors (levers + wedges) and the bicycle (wheel and axle + levers + chain drive) (Afghanistan, Grade 4 — *Science, Health and Environment, Grade 4*, Lesson 26, printed p. 53; built for the Afghanistan placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/afghanistan-placements.md)

75. **Degradable and non-degradable things** (`materials/degradable-and-non-degradable-things`) — which things break down and return to the soil, and which stay for years without breaking down — the burying test tells them apart (Bhutan, Class IV — *Science, Class IV*, §1.5, printed p. 13; built for the Bhutan placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/bhutan-placements.md)

76. **Soluble and insoluble substances** (`materials/soluble-and-insoluble-substances`) — which substances dissolve in water and which never dissolve however long you stir — and that dissolving is a physical change, the substance still there and winnable back (Bhutan, Class IV — *Science, Class IV*, §3.2, printed p. 36, and Class V — *Science, Class V*, §2.3, printed p. 29; built for the Bhutan placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/bhutan-placements.md)

77. **Solutions — solute and solvent** (`materials/solutions-solute-and-solvent`) — a solution taken apart into its two named parts — the solute that dissolves and the solvent it dissolves in (Bhutan, Class VII — *Science, Class VII*, Ch. 7, printed p. 136; built for the Bhutan placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/bhutan-placements.md)

78. **Fluids and superfluids** (`materials/fluids-and-superfluids`) — a fluid is a material that flows, liquid or gas, and a superfluid is a fluid that flows without friction at all (Dominican Republic, Grade 6 — *Ciencias de la Naturaleza 6*, printed p. 112; built for the Dominican Republic placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/dominican-republic-placements.md)

79. **What matter is** (`materials/what-matter-is`) — everything around us is matter — it has mass and takes up space, even the air; light, heat, sound and shadows are not matter (Kuwait, Grade 1, Term 2 — *Science, Grade 1, Term 2 (Student Book)*, printed p. 19; built for the Kuwait placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/kuwait-placements.md)

80. **Mass — how much matter is in a thing** (`materials/mass-how-much-matter-is-in-a-thing`) — mass is the amount of matter in a body, measured in grams and kilograms with a balance — not size, and not weight (Kuwait, Grade 2, Term 2 — *Science, Grade 2, Term 2 (Student Book)*, printed p. 18; built for the Kuwait placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/kuwait-placements.md)

81. **Traditional Chinese joinery — mortise and tenon** (`materials/traditional-chinese-joinery-mortise-and-tenon`) — a wooden tongue (tenon) fitting a wooden hole (mortise), joining by fit alone with no nails — the joinery of old Chinese buildings and bridges, China's own craft way (China, Grade 3 — *科学三年级上册 (Science, Grade 3, first volume)*, printed p. 58; built for the China placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/china-placements.md)

82. **Strong shapes — triangles, arches and columns** (`materials/strong-shapes-triangles-arches-and-columns`) — a triangle holds its shape while a square frame racks; a wide base stands steadier; a column carries weight down; an arch carries its load along its curve into its feet (China, Grade 3 — *科学三年级上册 (Science, Grade 3, first volume)*, printed p. 55; built for the China placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/china-placements.md)

83. **Design and build a model building** (`materials/design-and-build-a-model-building`) — designing and building a model building (a strong bridge, a tall steady tower), showing it, testing it and improving it — closing with Lu Ban, the master carpenter who invented the saw and the plane (China, Grade 3 — *科学三年级上册 (Science, Grade 3, first volume)*, printed pp. 59, 61; built for the China placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/china-placements.md)

84. **Inventing a labour-saving device** (`materials/inventing-a-labour-saving-device`) — designing and making a device that does a job with less effort, joining the simple machines, then testing it and improving it (China, Grade 5 — *科学五年级下册 (Science, Grade 5, second volume)*, printed pp. 57, 60; built for the China placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/china-placements.md)

85. **Air has mass** (`materials/air-has-mass`) — air proved to have mass by weighing: the ball balanced heavier once inflated, soybeans added against it, more air reading as more mass on the scale — with Galileo's sealed bottle as the book's enrichment (China, Grade 3 — *科学三年级下册 (Science, Grade 3, second volume)*, printed pp. 30, 31; built for the China placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/china-placements.md)

86. **Springs and elastic force** (`materials/springs-and-elastic-force`) — the spring in a ballpoint pen, stretched and pressed, always pulling back to its first shape; hung weights lengthen it step for step — elasticity, the property the spring force meter is built on (China, Grade 3 — *科学三年级下册 (Science, Grade 3, second volume)*, printed p. 37; built for the China placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/china-placements.md)

87. **Design and build a powered toy car** (`materials/design-and-build-a-powered-toy-car`) — studying propeller, motor and sail toy cars, choosing a power strong enough to run at least 1 metre, building the car, then testing its distance and time and improving weak power (a thicker rubber band, a bigger balloon) (China, Grade 3 — *科学三年级下册 (Science, Grade 3, second volume)*, printed pp. 57, 59; built for the China placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/china-placements.md)

88. **Nanotechnology and its uses** (`materials/nanotechnology-and-its-uses`) — nanotechnology introduced by its applications — working with matter at the nanometre scale, where materials behave in new ways — teacher-guide evidence only, acceptance 0 (Sri Lanka, Grade 9 — *Science, Grade 9, Teachers' Guide (Implemented from 2018)*, competency 4.1; built for the Sri Lanka placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/sri-lanka-placements.md)
89. **Caring for and storing materials safely** (`materials/caring-for-and-storing-materials-safely`) — Keeping and maintaining materials and tools properly — at home, at school and in the field — and storing them safely, as Rwanda's SET books teach in every year (Rwanda, Primary 2, 4, 5 and 6 SET books — keeping and maintaining home and school materials, agricultural tools and made objects, and storing tools properly; built for the Rwanda placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/rwanda-placements.md)
90. **Dangerous gases in the air** (`materials/dangerous-gases-in-the-air`) — The dangerous components of air — carbon dioxide, water vapour and carbon monoxide named as dangers — as Rwanda's Primary 4 SET book teaches inside its air unit (Rwanda, Primary 4 — *Science and Elementary Technology for Rwandan Schools, Pupil's Book Primary 4*, printed p. 147; built for the Rwanda placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/rwanda-placements.md)
91. **Designing and modelling machines** (`materials/designing-and-modelling-machines`) — designing and modelling machines — machine designs and models, as Zimbabwe's Grade 6 guide teaches the unit (Zimbabwe, Grade 6 — *PlusOne Science and Technology* teacher's guides (Grade 6 printed p. 53), guide evidence only, acceptance 0; built for the Zimbabwe placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/zimbabwe-placements.md)
92. **Non-Newtonian fluids — neither solid nor liquid** (`materials/non-newtonian-fluids-neither-solid-nor-liquid`) — a soil/clay dough that is neither solid nor liquid, tested against the properties of both (Indonesia, Grade 3 — *Ilmu Pengetahuan Alam dan Sosial untuk SD/MI Kelas III (Edisi Revisi)*, Bab 8, printed p. 176; built for the Indonesia placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/indonesia-placements.md)
93. **Uses of air and its gases** (`materials/uses-of-air-and-its-gases`) — air and wind at work — turbines, drying, sailboats, tyres — and air's own gases at work one by one: oxygen in cylinders, nitrogen for fertilizer and food packets, carbon dioxide for fizzy drinks and fire extinguishers, as Bangladesh's Class 5 book teaches them (Bangladesh, Grade 5 — *Elementary Science Class 5*, printed p. 26; built for the Bangladesh placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/bangladesh-placements.md)
94. **The chemistry of cleanliness — soap and detergent** (`materials/the-chemistry-of-cleanliness-soap-and-detergent`) — the chemistry of cleanliness and hygiene — cleaning with soap and detergent — as Bangladesh's Class 8 book teaches it inside its everyday-chemistry chapters (Bangladesh, Grade 8 — *Science Investigative Study Class 8*, printed p. 168; built for the Bangladesh placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/bangladesh-placements.md)
95. **Three states of wax in a burning candle** (`materials/three-states-of-wax-in-a-burning-candle`) — a burning candle shows all three states of wax at once — solid below, liquid at the wick, gas at the flame — Bangladesh's Class 9 book's own demonstration (Bangladesh, Grade 9 — *Science Investigative Study Class 9*, printed p. 73; built for the Bangladesh placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/bangladesh-placements.md)
96. **Soap and detergents on water** (`materials/soap-and-detergents-on-water`) — the effect of soap and detergents on different water types, in Fiji's island context (Fiji, Year 9 — *Basic Science, Science Book 1 — Lower Secondary*, printed pp. 41–42; built for the Fiji placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/fiji-placements.md)

97. **Using water — state changes in daily life** (`materials/using-water-state-changes-in-daily-life`) — how I benefit from the changes of water's states: the state changes of the water a child meets every day, read through what each change does for us (Yemen, Grade 3 — *Science, Grade 3, Part 1*, printed p. 84; built for the Yemen placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/yemen-placements.md)
98. **Surface tension — the skin on liquids** (`materials/surface-tension-the-skin-on-liquids`) — the stretched skin on a liquid's surface: a needle and a razor blade floated though denser than water, pond insects walking on the film, soap films on a wire ring, and the pull measured in newtons per metre (Yemen, Grade 9 — *Science, Grade 9, Part 2*, printed p. 53; built for the Yemen placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/yemen-placements.md)
99. **Capillarity — water climbing narrow tubes** (`materials/capillarity-water-climbing-narrow-tubes`) — water rises in narrow tubes against gravity, highest in the narrowest tube; the same climb carries soil water up through root hairs to the whole plant (Yemen, Grade 9 — *Science, Grade 9, Part 2*, printed p. 62; built for the Yemen placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/yemen-placements.md)
100. **Viscosity — why some liquids pour slowly** (`materials/viscosity-why-some-liquids-pour-slowly`) — a steel ball sinks slowly through car oil, faster through cooking oil, fastest through water; the inner friction between a liquid's layers, and why engines are oiled to cut wear (Yemen, Grade 9 — *Science, Grade 9, Part 2*, printed p. 68; built for the Yemen placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/yemen-placements.md)

101. **Physical changes — shape, state and appearance** (`materials/physical-changes-shape-state-and-appearance`) — Ch. (Saudi Arabia, Grade 3, Part 2 — *Science, Grade 3, Part 2*, printed pp. 93–94; built for the Saudi Arabia placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/saudi-arabia-placements.md)
102. **Solutions and concentration — how much is dissolved** (`materials/solutions-and-concentration-how-much-is-dissolved`) — concentration: the property that describes the amount of material dissolved in the solvent — the more sugar dissolved, the higher the solution's concentration (two pictured glasses, little sugar and much sugar) (Saudi Arabia, Grade 3, Part 2 — *Science, Grade 3, Part 2*, printed p. 97; built for the Saudi Arabia placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/saudi-arabia-placements.md)
103. **Gears — toothed discs that pass motion on** (`materials/gears-toothed-discs-that-pass-motion-on`) — the gears (التروس): toothed discs used to transfer motion from one disc to another, and they are found in different sizes (Saudi Arabia, Grade 3, Part 2 — *Science, Grade 3, Part 2*, printed p. 135; built for the Saudi Arabia placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/saudi-arabia-placements.md)
104. **Changing state — melting, freezing, evaporating, condensing** (`materials/changing-state-melting-freezing-evaporating-condensing`) — the four changes of state named and diagrammed — melting, freezing, evaporating, condensing — with fog, sea salt and rain as applications, and wax, sanitiser alcohol and hospital oxygen as daily-life cases (Vietnam, Grade 5 — *Khoa học 5*, printed p. 19; Vietnam, Grade 4 — *Khoa học 4*, printed p. 9; Vietnam, Grade 4 — *Khoa học 4*, printed p. 12; built for the Vietnam placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/vietnam-placements.md)

105. **Components of air and their uses** (`materials/components-of-air-and-their-uses`) — air's recipe by percentage — nitrogen 78, oxygen 21, carbon dioxide 0.03, inert gases 0.97 — oxygen as the active part that supports burning, the limewater test for carbon dioxide, and the uses of each gas (South Sudan, Primary 6 — *Primary Science, Pupil's Book 6*, 3.2 Components of air and their properties: the percentage composition (nitrogen 78, oxygen 21, carbon dioxide 0.03, inert gases 0.97), oxygen as the active part that supports burning (candle under a gas jar), the limewater test for carbon dioxide in breathed-out air, and the uses of each gas, SS P6 SB, printed p. 55; built for the South Sudan placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/south-sudan-placements.md)

106. **How gases change with temperature and pressure** (`materials/how-gases-change-with-temperature-and-pressure`) — the relationship between temperature, pressure and volume of air: heated air expands, increasing pressure reduces volume, and wind as air moving from high to low pressure (South Sudan, Primary 8 — *South Sudan Primary Science, Pupil's Book 8*, 4.4 Relationship between temperatures, pressure and volume of air, SS P8 SB, printed p. 81; built for the South Sudan placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/south-sudan-placements.md)

107. **What a vacuum is** (`materials/what-a-vacuum-is`) — a vacuum as a space with no matter in it — no life can be there — used to explain the vacuum flask, where no conduction or convection can happen without matter (South Sudan, Primary 8 — *South Sudan Primary Science, Pupil's Book 8*, 4.5 Vacuum, SS P8 SB, printed p. 86; built for the South Sudan placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/south-sudan-placements.md)

## Unit 5 — Light

1. **Light and dark: what light is** (`light/light-and-dark`) — light and dark are a pair: where there is no light, there is darkness — ጨለማ
2. **Light travels in straight lines** (`light/light-travels-in-straight-lines`) — light travels only in straight lines — proved by the book's Experiment 4: a candle's flame is seen through a straight tube but not through a bent one
3. **Reflection, refraction and splitting light** (`light/reflection-refraction-and-splitting-light`) — on a smooth surface light reflects under two laws (the rays and the normal in one plane; angle of incidence = angle of reflection, Experiment 7); c…
4. **Sources of light: lamps people make and light from nature** (`light/sources-of-light`) — light comes from two families of sources: natural — sun, moon and stars; and human-made — kuraz lamp, bulb, candle, torch, fanos — and the learner …
5. **Sunlight keeps everything alive, and what light is used for** (`light/sunlight-keeps-everything-alive`) — the sun is our main light source: plants make their food with sunlight, animals eat plants, people eat both — without sunlight nothing lives; sunli…
6. **Things light passes through** (`light/things-light-passes-through`) — some materials let light through — glass, water, air — and we test them the book's way, with a torch
7. **Things that block light, and shadows** (`light/things-that-block-light-and-shadows`) — wall, wooden table and book block light; blocked light is why houses are dark at night and why you cast a shadow in the sun; leafy trees give shade
8. **Light — how seeing works** (`light/light-how-seeing-works`) — two rival theories of seeing are judged on evidence — rays leaving the eyes to fetch the object, against light reflected from the object into the eye; the reflected-light account wins (Grade 8 General Science)
9. **Light — sources, and the straight-line rule** (`light/light-sources-and-the-straight-line-rule`) — bodies split into luminous (making their own light) and non-luminous (seen only by reflected light — the Moon among them), and light travels in straight lines (Grade 8 General Science)
10. **Materials and light — transparent, translucent, opaque** (`light/materials-and-light-transparent-translucent-opaque`) — a semi-guided investigation — predict first, then devise your own test — sorts everyday materials into the three classes the science names (Grade 8 General Science)
11. **Reflection — laws, mirror images and the periscope** (`light/reflection-laws-mirror-images-and-the-periscope`) — reflection splits into regular (polished surfaces) and diffuse (rough surfaces) under the same two laws; a plane mirror's virtual image has four properties, and two mirrors build a periscope (Grade 8 General Science)
12. **Refraction — why the spoon looks broken** (`light/refraction-why-the-spoon-looks-broken`) — when light crosses obliquely into a different transparent medium its direction changes at the boundary — bending towards the normal going in, away coming out (Grade 8 General Science)
13. **Dispersion — splitting and remixing white light** (`light/dispersion-splitting-and-remixing-white-light`) — white light is a mixture of seven colours — violet, indigo, blue, green, yellow, orange, red — split by a prism because each colour bends differently, and remixed by Newton's disc (Grade 8 General Science)
14. **Diffraction gratings — grooved surfaces splitting light by wavelength** (`light/diffraction-gratings-grooved-surfaces-splitting-light-by-wavelength`) — a surface of fine, even grooves — a CD is one — returns a copy of the wave from every groove, and each wavelength reinforces at its own angle, fanning white light into a spectrum ordered purely by wavelength (Finland, Grade 7 pupil practical — Oppilastyö: Valon aaltoluonne)

15. **Waves and how they carry energy** (`light/waves-and-how-they-carry-energy`) — a wave is a travelling disturbance, and what travels in it is energy, not the material — Australia's step back: waves named after meeting heat, sound and light (Australia, Grade 9 — *Pearson Science 9*, 5.1, printed p. 179)
16. **Low- and high-frequency radiation around us** (`light/low-and-high-frequency-radiation-around-us`) — radiation sorted by frequency alone into its two families: low-frequency radiation, the long gentle waves of signals and warmth, and high-frequency radiation, the short waves that carry more energy (Australia, Grade 9 — *Pearson Science 9*, 5.3–5.4, printed p. 198)

17. **Waves — amplitude and wavelength** (`light/waves-amplitude-and-wavelength`) — modelling the regular patterns of waves described by amplitude and wavelength — water and rope models (United States, Grade 4 — *4th Grade Science for Utah SEEd Standards, Revised 2025*, 4.3.1, printed p. 110)
18. **Using wave patterns to send information** (`light/using-wave-patterns-to-send-information`) — designing a solution to an information-transfer problem using wave patterns — light signals and codes (United States, Grade 4 — *4th Grade Science for Utah SEEd Standards, Revised 2025*, 4.3.3, printed p. 126)
19. **Colour mixing — primary and secondary colours** (`light/colour-mixing-primary-and-secondary-colours`) — mixing primary-colour plasticine gives secondary colours; white lightens, black darkens; a mixed colour cannot be unmixed — the experiment called "The colour inventor" — teacher-guide evidence only, acceptance 0 (Cabo Verde, Grade 1 — *Atividades Científicas, 1.º ano* (teacher/activity guide), Experiment 4, printed p. 24)
20. **Seeing colours — why things look the colour they do** (`light/seeing-colours-why-things-look-the-colour-they-do`) — the spinning colour disc gathers the seven colours back into white; see-through coloured bodies look the colour of the light they let pass; solid bodies look the colour they reflect — a yellow ball reflects yellow, white bodies reflect all colours, black bodies take all in and reflect none; and the red ball through red, green and blue glass in turn (Sudan, Grade 6 — *العلوم (Science), Grade 6*, Unit 7, Lesson 4, printed pp. 70–72)

21. **Vision defects and correcting them** (`light/vision-defects-and-correcting-them`) — short sight and long sight — where the eye's image misses the retina — and the concave and convex lenses that put it back (Kuwait, Grade 9, Term 1 — *Science, Grade 9, Term 1 (Student Book)*, printed p. 169; built for the Kuwait placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/kuwait-placements.md)

22. **The pinhole camera** (`light/the-pinhole-camera`) — a dark box, a tiny hole and a screen: light travelling straight through the hole paints the scene upside down on the screen, no lens at all (Eritrea, Grade 6 — *Science for Grade Six (Science 6 Textbook)*, printed p. 68; built for the Eritrea placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/eritrea-placements.md)
23. **Sources that give both light and heat** (`light/sources-that-give-both-light-and-heat`) — The relationship between sources of light and sources of heat — that some sources give both — as Rwanda's Primary 2 SET book closes its light and heat unit with it (Rwanda, Primary 2 — *Science and Elementary Technology, Primary Two, Pupil's Book*, printed p. 91; built for the Rwanda placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/rwanda-placements.md)

24. **How bright light is — lux and distance** (`light/how-bright-light-is-lux-and-distance`) — the brightness of light measured in lux with a luxmeter; brightness falls as you move away from the source; candle, lamp and LED compared by reading distance; light in greenhouses and in film work (North Macedonia, Grade 5 — *Природни науки за 5 одделение (Natural Sciences for Grade 5)*, 5.10, printed pp. 142–143; built for the North Macedonia placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/north-macedonia-placements.md)

25. **Cameras and optical instruments** (`light/cameras-and-optical-instruments`) — the camera and optical instruments, closing Yemen's Grade 8 refraction unit after lenses and how the eye sees (Yemen, Grade 8 — *Science, Grade 8, Part 2*, printed p. 57; built for the Yemen placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/yemen-placements.md)

26. **The electromagnetic spectrum beyond visible light** (`light/the-electromagnetic-spectrum-beyond-visible-light`) — Ch. ## Unit 6 — Weather and Seasons

1. **Day and night, rainbow and flood** (`weather-and-seasons/day-and-night-rainbow-and-flood`) — a natural event happens in nature outside human control; day and night are a natural cycle — 24 hours, 12 of sunlight and 12 without — and rainbow …
2. **Measuring temperature, rain and wind** (`weather-and-seasons/measuring-temperature-rain-and-wind`) — climate's elements — temperature, rainfall amount, wind and air pressure — are measured, and two instruments have names to learn: the wind vane (ዊን…
3. **Reading climate tables and averaging** (`weather-and-seasons/reading-climate-tables`) — a climate table holds month-by-month temperature and rainfall for real stations — Gondar, Bahir Dar and Kombolcha — and reading it means finding we…
4. **Seasons and climate zones of our country** (`weather-and-seasons/seasons-and-climate-zones-of-our-country`) — Ethiopia's seasons — bega the dry season, kiremt the rainy, farming season — and its five climate zones by altitude: berha, qolla, weyna dega, dega…
5. **Today's weather** (`weather-and-seasons/todays-weather`) — weather is the air's condition now — sunny, rainy, cloudy, cold, hot, windy — changing hour by hour and morning to evening, and we observe and repo…
6. **Weather and climate** (`weather-and-seasons/weather-and-climate`) — weather changes hour by hour; climate is the air's long pattern — the Grade 3 book says judging it needs at least 30 years of records — and its ele…
7. **What controls a place's climate** (`weather-and-seasons/what-controls-a-places-climate`) — three things control a place's climate — height above sea level, distance from the equator, and distance from seas and oceans: near the equator is …
8. **El Niño — when the Pacific warms** (`weather-and-seasons/el-nino-when-the-pacific-warms`) — every 3–7 years the trade winds weaken, the upwelling stops and the eastern Pacific warms — named "the Christmas child" by South American fishermen because it is noticed near Christmas — teacher/article evidence only, acceptance 0 (Iceland, Grade 11 record — *El Niño* (Í dagsins önn article sheet), printed p. 1)
9. **El Niño and La Niña — ENOS** (`weather-and-seasons/el-nino-and-la-nina-enos`) — ENOS, the El Niño–Southern Oscillation, taught whole: the warm phase (El Niño) and the cold phase (La Niña) as the two ends of one Pacific swing, each with its effects (Costa Rica, Grade 7 — *Ciencias, Sétimo, Código 80002, Antologías CONED*, printed p. 117)

10. **Preparing for a weather forecast** (`weather-and-seasons/preparing-for-a-weather-forecast`) — using a forecast to decide how to prepare — clothing, items to carry, and readiness for severe weather such as floods, storms and extreme heat (United States, Kindergarten — *Kindergarten — for Utah SEEd Standards, Revised 2025*, K.1.2, printed p. 23)
11. **Designing solutions to weather hazards** (`weather-and-seasons/designing-solutions-to-weather-hazards`) — designing, testing and optimising a solution that reduces the effects of a weather-related hazard — flood barriers and similar (United States, Grade 3 — *3rd Grade Science for Utah SEEd Standards, Revised 2025*, 3.1.3, printed p. 28)
12. **Air pressure and what it does** (`weather-and-seasons/air-pressure-and-what-it-does`) — the air presses in all directions — atmospheric pressure, its consequences, and the experiments that prove it exists (Armenia, Grade 6 — *Բնություն 6 (Nature 6)*, §§27–28, printed pp. 98, 101)
13. **Winds and their kinds** (`weather-and-seasons/winds-and-their-kinds`) — wind is air on the move, and winds come in kinds, told apart by direction, steadiness and strength (Armenia, Grade 6 — *Բնություն 6 (Nature 6)*, §29, printed p. 103)
14. **How winds form** (`weather-and-seasons/how-winds-form`) — winds form where the Sun heats neighbouring places unequally: warm air rises, cooler air moves in underneath, and those movements join into the circulation of the winds (Cuba, Grade 5 — *Ciencias Naturales, Quinto grado*, printed pp. 69, 73)


15. **Climate and daily life in Cambodia** (`weather-and-seasons/climate-and-daily-life-in-cambodia`) — how Cambodia's climate affects people's activities — work follows the water and the heat — taught straight after the book's seasons-and-climate-in-Cambodia lesson, its seasons learned from its own weather; Cambodia's own way (Cambodia, Grade 7 — *វិទ្យាសាស្ត្រ ថ្នាក់ទី៧ (Science, Grade 7)*, Earth Ch. 2, Lesson 4, printed p. 280; built for the Cambodia placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/cambodia-placements.md)

21. **Why a weather forecast matters** (`weather-and-seasons/why-a-weather-forecast-matters`) — why we need to know the coming weather: choosing heavy clothes in winter, the farmer's dates for sowing and harvesting, and the pilot who needs the forecast to fly his plane in safety (Saudi Arabia, Grade 3, Part 2 — *Science, Grade 3, Part 2*, printed p. 18; built for the Saudi Arabia placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/saudi-arabia-placements.md)

25. **Snowstorms and tornadoes** (`weather-and-seasons/snowstorms-and-tornadoes`) — the snowstorm: snow with low temperature and strong winds covers the plants, cars and roads (a snowstorm that struck the Al-Lawz mountains in the Tabuk region); the funnel tornado (tornado): rotating winds forming a long… (Saudi Arabia, Grade 3, Part 2 — *Science, Grade 3, Part 2*, printed p. 26; built for the Saudi Arabia placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/saudi-arabia-placements.md)

28. **Clouds — kinds by shape and height** (`weather-and-seasons/clouds-kinds-by-shape-and-height`) — clouds form when rising air cools to the dew point and vapour condenses on tiny particles; they are classified by shape and height — low clouds (cumulus, which can thicken into heavy rain and thunder; layered stratus; and fog,… (Saudi Arabia, First Intermediate, Part 2 — *Science, First Intermediate, Part 2*, printed p. 24; built for the Saudi Arabia placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/saudi-arabia-placements.md)
