## Unit 7 — Soil and Water

1. **Drought: what it is and how we hold it back** (`soil-and-water/drought-what-it-is-and-how-we-hold-it-back`) — drought is when rain fails for a long time and water sources dry up — caused chiefly by an irregular climate, and also following deforestation — an…
2. **Ethiopia's rivers and lakes** (`soil-and-water/ethiopias-rivers-and-lakes`) — Ethiopia — Africa's water tower — drains three ways: the western system (Baro, Abbay, Tekeze → the Nile), the south-eastern (Wabi Shebele, Genale →…
3. **Famine, and how it differs from drought** (`soil-and-water/famine-and-how-it-differs-from-drought`) — famine can follow drought — food and water for animals and people run short — but drought alone does not mean famine, because stored food reserves …
4. **Loam, clay and sand — the squeeze test** (`soil-and-water/loam-clay-and-sand-the-squeeze-test`) — the three main soils are told apart by the squeeze test: fertile loam (ለም አፈር) holds then half-crumbles into a rounded lump; clay (ሸክላ) squeezes sm…
5. **Soil fertility, erosion, and why soil matters** (`soil-and-water/soil-fertility-erosion-and-why-soil-matters`) — fertility is decided by parent rock, chemical content, land shape, climate, remains and small living things — and fine smooth soil is carried away …
6. **The great lakes and the Nile** (`soil-and-water/the-great-lakes-and-the-nile`) — most of Africa's lakes lie in East Africa — Victoria (66,400 km², third in the world by area, shallow at 92 m), Malawi and Tanganyika (deepest at 1…
7. **Water problems: sharing, pollution and the lake weed** (`soil-and-water/water-problems-sharing-pollution-and-the-lake-weed`) — East Africa's water faces three printed problems: shared rivers with no agreement (Ethiopia contributes 86% of the Nile yet downstream countries ob…
8. **Water: why every living thing needs it** (`soil-and-water/water-why-every-living-thing-needs-it`) — water serves everything alive and everything people build: drinking, cooking and cleanliness; digesting food and carrying waste out of the body; gr…
9. **What soil is and what is in it** (`soil-and-water/what-soil-is-and-what-is-in-it`) — soil is the layer of the ground from the surface down to where plant roots reach, and it holds minerals, tiny living things, plant and animal remai…
10. **Soil — types, formation and loss** (`soil-and-water/soil-types-formation-and-loss`) — soil is told into loam, clay and sand by particle size and feel, and holds water very differently (clay most, loam in between, sand almost none) — and soil is lost by erosion (Grade 8 General Science)
11. **Saving the soil — conservation methods** (`soil-and-water/saving-the-soil-conservation-methods`) — soil is saved by a farmer's toolkit — afforestation, leaving crop residue as cover, terracing, check dams, shelter belts and contour ploughing (Grade 8 General Science)
12. **Water — why it is precious and how to save it** (`soil-and-water/water-why-it-is-precious-and-how-to-save-it`) — water covers 71% of the Earth's surface yet over 96% of it is saline, and the human body is about 60% water — so the fresh water living things need is precious and must be saved (Grade 8 General Science)
13. **Treating water to make it safe — filtering and settling** (`soil-and-water/treating-water-to-make-it-safe-filtering-and-settling`) — dirty water is treated in a chain: settling lets the heavy dirt sink, filtering through cloth, sand and gravel traps the fine dirt that is left, and treated water is kept covered and drawn with a clean cup — with the honest limit that boiling is the surest final step — teacher-guide evidence only, acceptance 0 (Gabon, Grade 3 — Super Efficace, Guide pédagogique, 3e année)
14. **River basins — watersheds** (`soil-and-water/river-basins-watersheds`) — hydrographic basins: what a basin is and its characteristics — the land a river system drains — the distribution of Mexico's basins, and basins in economic development (Mexico, Grade 7 — *Ética, naturaleza y sociedades, Primer grado de secundaria*, printed pp. 87–92)
15. **Rivers and lakes — where water gathers and flows** (`soil-and-water/rivers-and-lakes-where-water-gathers-and-flows`) — surface waters: the parts of a river — source, course, bed, mouth and banks — lowland and mountain rivers, and lakes and ponds (Serbia, Grade 3 — *Природа и друштво 3*, printed p. 6)
16. **Frost heave and þúfur mounds** (`soil-and-water/frost-heave-and-thufur-mounds`) — þúfur, the heath's little mounds, are frost-made: moist soil put in a freezer expands and lifts as it freezes, and that same lifting, winter after winter, raises the mounds — teacher-guide evidence only, acceptance 0 (Iceland, Grade 5 — *Líf á landi — Kennsluleiðbeiningar*, printed p. 41)
17. **Community water supply and rural aqueducts** (`soil-and-water/community-water-supply-and-rural-aqueducts`) — rural aqueducts that supply community drinking water, administered in Costa Rica by the community's own water association, an ASADA, under official rules — the country's own way (Costa Rica, Grade 7 — *Ciencias, Sétimo, Código 80002, Antologías CONED*, printed p. 113)
18. **Swamps and glaciers** (`soil-and-water/swamps-and-glaciers`) — the land's two slow water stores — the swamp, water standing in the land, and the glacier, water held frozen as ice — and the significance of each (Armenia, Grade 6 — *Բնություն 6 (Nature 6)*, §22, printed p. 77)
19. **Groundwater and springs** (`soil-and-water/groundwater-and-springs`) — rain soaks in and collects as groundwater, the land's hidden store, which comes out as springs and rises under pressure as artesian water (Armenia, Grade 6 — *Բնություն 6 (Nature 6)*, §23, printed p. 82)
20. **The saltiness and temperature of the sea** (`soil-and-water/the-saltiness-and-temperature-of-the-sea`) — salinity: the ocean as 96.5% water and 3.5% dissolved solids, in parts per thousand, changed by rainfall and evaporation; and ocean temperature: 26–28°C around Samoa, sunlight to about 60 metres, mixing and evaporation (Samoa, Year 11 — *Science — Year 11, Book 1 (MESC Samoa)*, printed p. 114, with the lagoon graph printed p. 94)
21. **Desalination — making seawater drinkable** (`soil-and-water/desalination-making-seawater-drinkable`) — getting fresh water from seawater: separating the water from its dissolved salt so it can be drunk — set as the interdisciplinary project Water by the Sea (Egypt, Primary 5 — *Primary 5 Science, Science Term 2 (Discovery Education Science Techbook, Egypt edition)*, printed p. 39)
22. **Modern irrigation — saving water in farming** (`soil-and-water/modern-irrigation-saving-water-in-farming`) — designing a new modern irrigation system, judged by how much of its water reaches the plants' roots instead of being lost to sun, wind and runoff (Egypt, Primary 6 — *Primary 6 Science, Science Term 2 (Discovery Education Science Techbook, Egypt edition)*, interdisciplinary project, printed p. 80)

23. **Cambodia's rivers, lakes and waterways** (`soil-and-water/cambodias-rivers-lakes-and-waterways`) — the country's waterway system — its rivers and lakes read as one joined whole — country-specific content (Cambodia, Grade 7 — *វិទ្យាសាស្ត្រ ថ្នាក់ទី៧ (Science, Grade 7)*, Earth Ch. 3, Lesson 1, printed p. 286; built for the Cambodia placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/cambodia-placements.md)
24. **The route of water to the tap** (`soil-and-water/the-route-of-water-to-the-tap`) — following water's route through pipes, storage tanks and pumps to the tap, with domestic and industrial consumption told apart in a building drawing (Venezuela, Grade 2 — *Comunidad con Ciencia (Colección Bicentenario)*, printed pp. 45–47; built for the Venezuela placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/venezuela-placements.md)
25. **Water management — dams and distribution** (`soil-and-water/water-management-dams-and-distribution`) — reservoirs (embalses) behind named Venezuelan dams, controlled discharge, and distribution networks carrying water stored high down to the people (Venezuela, Grade 2 — *Comunidad con Ciencia (Colección Bicentenario)*, printed pp. 54–62; built for the Venezuela placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/venezuela-placements.md)

26. **Hard and soft water** (`soil-and-water/hard-and-soft-water`) — the lather test that tells hard water from soft, how hardness is removed, and hard water's advantages and disadvantages weighed on both sides (Bhutan, Class VI — *Science, Class VI*, §§2.3–2.5, printed pp. 24, 28, 31; built for the Bhutan placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/bhutan-placements.md)

27. **Water as a coolant** (`soil-and-water/water-as-a-coolant`) — water carrying heat away — the coolant circulated through engines, and the body's own water cooling in blood and sweat (Sri Lanka, Grade 7 — *Science Part – I, Grade 7*, printed p. 58; built for the Sri Lanka placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/sri-lanka-placements.md)

28. **Landslides and earth slips** (`soil-and-water/landslides-and-earth-slips`) — a mass of soil and rock breaking loose and sliding downhill — its causes in soaked, cleared and cut slopes, and staying safe from it (Sri Lanka, Grade 8 — *Science Part – II, Grade 8*, printed p. 135; built for the Sri Lanka placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/sri-lanka-placements.md)
29. **Storing water safely** (`soil-and-water/storing-water-safely`) — Water storage — storing water so it stays safe to use — as Rwanda's Primary 5 SET book closes its water unit with it (Rwanda, Primary 5 — *Science and Elementary Technology for Rwandan Schools, Pupil's Book 5*, printed p. 104; built for the Rwanda placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/rwanda-placements.md)
30. **Sources of water around us** (`soil-and-water/sources-of-water-around-us`) — sources of water — where the water around us comes from, as Zimbabwe's guides teach the topic from ECD level up (Zimbabwe, ECD A and Grade 3 — *PlusOne Science and Technology* teacher's guides (ECD A printed p. 27; Grade 3 printed p. 39), guide evidence only, acceptance 0; built for the Zimbabwe placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/zimbabwe-placements.md)

31. **Compost and worm farming** (`soil-and-water/compost-and-worm-farming`) — organic fertiliser (abono orgánico) feeds the soil in ecological agriculture: a compost pile built layer by layer with an air column; vermiculture (lombricultura) — worm beds of 1 × 1.5 m and 60 cm high, and worm humus produced on dry, well-drained ground near organic waste, a blackish acidic substance that improves soil structure and supplies nutrients (Nicaragua, Grade 9 — *Ciencias Naturales, Noveno grado*, printed pp. 90–93; Grade 9 Módulo — *Módulo Autoformativo de Ciencias Naturales, 9no grado*, printed pp. 66–67; built for the Nicaragua placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/nicaragua-placements.md)

## Unit 8 — Plants

2. **Forests and what they give** (`plants/forests-and-what-they-give`) — plants are living things that make their own food, stay in one place, grow and reproduce — and forests give building, paper, furniture, shelter for…
3. **Kinds of plants and their parts** (`plants/kinds-of-plants-and-their-parts`) — plants come in kinds — big trees, medium shrubs, small plants — and every plant has its parts: root, stem or trunk, leaf, flower (and fruit); our c…
4. **Plants give us things and food** (`plants/plants-give-us-things-and-food`) — houses, clothes, chairs, tables and paper come from plants; plants feed people — cabbage, orange, maize — and animals, and bees and butterflies fee…
5. **Plants that heal** (`plants/plants-that-heal`) — the local medicinal plants the Grade 3-level list names — garlic, tena adam, dama kese, haregresa, feto, grawa, moringa (shiferaw), kebericho, tosi…
6. **What a plant needs to grow** (`plants/what-a-plant-needs-to-grow`) — a plant needs soil, water, air and sunlight — proved by the book's four-tin experiment: bean seeds sown the same day in an empty tin, soil only, wa…
7. **Plants — seed plants: gymnosperms and angiosperms** (`plants/seed-plants-gymnosperms-and-angiosperms`) — seed plants split into gymnosperms with naked seeds, often in cones, and angiosperms whose seeds are enclosed in fruit (Grade 7 General Science)
8. **Plants — seedless plants: mosses, liverworts and ferns** (`plants/seedless-plants-mosses-and-ferns`) — bryophytes such as mosses are small moist-place plants reproducing by spores, while ferns have true roots, stems, leaves and vascular tissue — and still no seeds or flowers (Grade 7 General Science)
9. **How plants sense and respond** (`plants/how-plants-sense-and-respond`) — plants perceive their surroundings in their own way: environmental changes show in the plant, and Guatemala's guide frames it as perception of the cycles of time — the plant's life timed by the turning of day and season — teacher-guide evidence only, acceptance 0 (Guatemala, Grade 7 — *Ciencias Naturales I, Guía de orientaciones metodológicas para docentes*, printed p. 42)
10. **Living things live where their needs are met** (`plants/living-things-live-where-their-needs-are-met`) — a living thing lives where its needs are met: forest plants in the forest, garden plants in the garden — taught Sweden's way as a sorting task, "Var ska växterna vara?" (Where should the plants be?): cut-out plants stuck one by one into a two-page garden-and-forest scene, each in the place it belongs (Sweden, Grade 1 — *Växter omkring oss A — kopieringsunderlag*, printed pp. 90–91; PARTIAL copy pages only, as filed)
11. **Meadows and pastures — homes of grasses and pollinators** (`plants/meadows-and-pastures-homes-of-grasses-and-pollinators`) — hay meadows and pastures, the meadow's medicinal plants, and the butterflies and bees that pollinate the flowers (Serbia, Grade 3 — *Природа и друштво 3*, printed p. 60)
12. **Flowers — and how plants reproduce** (`plants/flowers-and-how-plants-reproduce`) — the flower as the plant's seed-making part: petals, stamens making pollen, the pistil holding the future seeds; pollination as pollen's journey, then seeds form — teacher-guide evidence only, acceptance 0 (Albania, Grade 5 — *Libër mësuesi — Dituri natyre 5*, printed p. 13)
13. **Fruits and seeds** (`plants/fruits-and-seeds`) — the fruit as the covering a flowering plant builds around its seeds, guarding them while they ripen; the seed inside as the next plant packed, with food for its first growth — teacher-guide evidence only, acceptance 0 (Albania, Grade 5 — *Libër mësuesi — Dituri natyre 5*, printed p. 15)
14. **Seed dispersal — how seeds travel** (`plants/seed-dispersal-how-seeds-travel`) — seeds travelling away from their parent by shape: wings and parachutes on the wind, hooks on fur, fruit inside animals, floating on water — proved with paper-flyer seed models made and dropped by the pupils — teacher-guide evidence only, acceptance 0 (Albania, Grade 5 — *Libër mësuesi — Dituri natyre 5*, printed p. 17)
15. **Life on lava fields — mosses and ferns first** (`plants/life-on-lava-fields-mosses-and-ferns`) — new lava is colonised first by mosses (hraungambri, the woolly fringe-moss) and ferns; counting the spore capsules on one fern frond as the counting activity — teacher-guide evidence only, acceptance 0 (Iceland, Grade 5 — *Líf á landi — Kennsluleiðbeiningar*, printed pp. 14–18)
16. **Identifying trees by their leaves** (`plants/identifying-trees-by-their-leaves`) — naming tree species on a forest trip with the tree-key handbook (Ég greini tré) — teacher-guide evidence only, acceptance 0 (Iceland, Grade 5 — *Líf á landi — Kennsluleiðbeiningar*, printed p. 21)
17. **Plant defences — thorns and poisons** (`plants/plant-defences-thorns-and-poisons`) — plants grown with thorns are unpopular with grazers; few such wild species in Iceland — teacher-guide evidence only, acceptance 0 (Iceland, Grade 5 — *Líf á landi — Kennsluleiðbeiningar*, printed p. 32)
18. **Wetland plants — sedges and horsetails** (`plants/wetland-plants-sedges-and-horsetails`) — telling sedges and horsetails apart from other plant kinds, including the wintergreen horsetail relative vetrarkvíðastör with its long seed spikes — teacher-guide evidence only, acceptance 0 (Iceland, Grade 5 — *Líf á landi — Kennsluleiðbeiningar*, printed p. 38)
19. **Reproducing plants — seeds and other ways** (`plants/reproducing-plants-seeds-and-other-ways`) — the different ways to grow a new plant: from a seed, and without starting from a seed — from a bulb or a cutting — teacher-guide evidence only, acceptance 0 (Djibouti, Grade 3 — *Guide du maître — Sciences Expérimentales, 3ème année*, Module 3, printed pp. 29, 40; PARTIAL — damaged file, as filed)
20. **Life stages of a plant** (`plants/life-stages-of-a-plant`) — the great stages of a plant's life, compared with the stages of a human life — teacher-guide evidence only, acceptance 0 (Djibouti, Grade 3 — *Guide du maître — Sciences Expérimentales, 3ème année*, printed p. 42; PARTIAL — damaged file, as filed)
21. **Plant adaptations — to where they live** (`plants/plant-adaptations-to-where-they-live`) — plant adaptations for desert, rain forest and swampy conditions, and how adaptations assist survival — curriculum evidence only, acceptance 0 (Barbados, Classes 2–3 — *Primary Science Syllabus, Class One–Four*, printed p. 11)
22. **Life cycle of a flowering plant** (`plants/life-cycle-of-a-flowering-plant`) — an annual plant's circle on the bean: the seed's embryo, coat and stored food; germination needing warmth, moisture and air; growth, flowering and new seeds (Bulgaria, Grade 4 — *Човекът и природата за 4. клас*, Lesson 39, printed p. 78)
23. **Seed germination and growth patterns** (`plants/seed-germination-and-growth-patterns`) — germination by experiment (root first, then shoot), the functions of the plant parts found by testing them, growth patterns read off ruler measurements, and the two-seed race: predicting which seed germinates first and comparing growth rates — curriculum evidence only, acceptance 0 (Bahamas, Grade 3 — *Primary Science Curriculum Guidelines — Grades 3-4*, printed p. 76; Grade 4 return, printed p. 112)
24. **Indigenous plants of The Bahamas** (`plants/indigenous-plants-of-the-bahamas`) — identifying and describing the indigenous flowering plants of The Bahamas — plants that belong to the islands by nature, named and described from flower, leaf, stem, fruit and place — the country's own flora as content — curriculum evidence only, acceptance 0 (Bahamas, Grade 4 — *Primary Science Curriculum Guidelines — Grades 3-4*, printed p. 112)

25. **Sorting fruits and vegetables** (`plants/sorting-fruits-and-vegetables`) — classifying vegetables and fruits: grouping by kind and saying the rule — a kindergarten activity-bank lesson — guide evidence only, acceptance 0 (Cambodia, Kindergarten — *សៀវភៅសិក្សាគោលសម្រាប់មត្តេយ្យសិក្សា — វិទ្យាសាស្ត្រ (Core Book for Kindergarten Education: Science)*, activity 2.7, printed p. 110; built for the Cambodia placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/cambodia-placements.md)

26. **Root kinds — tap, fibrous and storage** (`plants/root-kinds-tap-fibrous-and-storage`) — tap roots and fibrous roots, paired with net-veined and parallel-veined leaves in dicots and monocots; roots hold and support the plant (Seychelles, Grade 7 (P7) — *Seychelles Integrated Science — Soil and Living Things*, Unit 4, Topics 3–4, printed booklet pp. 5–8; built for the Seychelles placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/seychelles-placements.md)
30. **Wild plants and sown plants** (`plants/wild-plants-and-sown-plants`) — plantas espontâneas e semeadas: plants that grow by themselves, their seeds arriving by wind, water or animals, against plants sown and tended by people (Angola, Grade 2 — *Estudo do Meio, Manual da 2.ª Classe*, printed p. 59; built for the Angola placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/angola-placements.md)

27. **Pollination — by insects and by hand** (`plants/pollination-by-insects-and-by-hand`) — pollination is pollen reaching the stigma; bees and moths carry it on hooked grains caught in their fur; pumpkin and passion fruit flowers pollinated by hand with a brush (Seychelles, Grade 8 (P8) — *Seychelles Integrated Science — Living Things Reproduce*, Unit 6, Topic 3, printed booklet pp. 4–5; built for the Seychelles placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/seychelles-placements.md)

28. **Stomata, transpiration and how leaves lose water** (`plants/stomata-transpiration-and-how-leaves-lose-water`) — stomata are tiny holes in leaves through which plants take in air and transpire water; preparing a slide of stomata; timing how long an unwatered plant takes to wilt (Seychelles, Grade 8 (P8) — *Seychelles Integrated Science — Food and Growth*, Unit 7, Topic 10, printed booklet pp. 26–28; built for the Seychelles placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/seychelles-placements.md)
29. **Pollination and fertilisation — how seeds and fruits form** (`plants/pollination-and-fertilisation-how-seeds-and-fruits-form`) — pollination — pollen carried to the stigma by wind, insects, water and humans, in the same flower or across flowers; fertilisation — the pollen grain joining the ovule inside the ovary; then the ovary grows into the fruit and the fertilised ovules inside it become the seeds (Sudan, Grade 6 — *العلوم (Science), Grade 6*, Unit 2, Lesson 2, printed pp. 13–16)

31. **The vascular system in plants** (`plants/the-vascular-system-in-plants`) — the plant's pipes: xylem carrying water up from the roots, phloem carrying food from the leaves to every part (Kuwait, Grade 7, Term 2 — *Science, Grade 7, Term 2 (Student Book)*, printed p. 31; built for the Kuwait placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/kuwait-placements.md)

32. **How water moves through a plant** (`plants/how-water-moves-through-a-plant`) — water's journey through a plant: in through the roots, up along the stem, out through the leaves into the air — the plant a through-road from soil to sky, proved with coloured water (China, Grade 3 — *科学三年级下册 (Science, Grade 3, second volume)*, printed p. 20; built for the China placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/china-placements.md)

33. **The plant factory — growing plants indoors** (`plants/the-plant-factory-growing-plants-indoors`) — plants grown indoors with every need supplied by machines — light from lamps, water and food measured in — and the learner's own plant factory designed and built (China, Grade 6 — *科学六年级上册 (Science, Grade 6, first volume)*, printed p. 62; built for the China placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/china-placements.md)

34. **Transport in plants** (`plants/transport-in-plants`) — the plant's two journeys — water up from the roots, food out from the leaves — taught as one transport system (Sri Lanka, Grade 8 — *Science Part – II, Grade 8*, printed p. 51; built for the Sri Lanka placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/sri-lanka-placements.md)

35. **Plant growth substances** (`plants/plant-growth-substances`) — the plant's own hormones — chemicals made in one part that steer growth in another: germination, flowering, ripening and leaf fall timed by their effects (Sri Lanka, Grade 9 — *Science Part – I, Grade 9*, printed p. 83; built for the Sri Lanka placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/sri-lanka-placements.md)

36. **Artificial plant growth substances in farming** (`plants/artificial-plant-growth-substances-in-farming`) — growth substances made by people and applied to crops — rooting cuttings, setting and ripening fruit, timing growth to the farmer's calendar (Sri Lanka, Grade 9 — *Science Part – I, Grade 9*, printed p. 86; built for the Sri Lanka placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/sri-lanka-placements.md)
37. **Classifying plants by stems and flowers** (`plants/classifying-plants-by-stems-and-flowers`) — grouping plants by stem type — tree, shrub and herb compared — and by whether they have flowers or not, as Bangladesh's Class 3 book teaches it in its opening plants chapter (Bangladesh, Grade 3 — *Elementary Science Class 3*, printed p. 5; built for the Bangladesh placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/bangladesh-placements.md)
38. **Seasonal fruits and vegetables** (`plants/seasonal-fruits-and-vegetables`) — Bangladesh's seasonal fruits and vegetables sorted by season — the country's own way of teaching food sources, as its Class 3 book teaches it (Bangladesh, Grade 3 — *Elementary Science Class 3*, printed p. 25; built for the Bangladesh placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/bangladesh-placements.md)

39. **How plants make new plants — flowers, pollen, seeds and life cycles** (`plants/how-plants-make-new-plants-flowers-pollen-seeds-and-life-cycles`) — Unit 1, Ch. (Saudi Arabia, Grade 2, Part 1 — *Science, Grade 2, Part 1*, printed pp. 38–44; built for the Saudi Arabia placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/saudi-arabia-placements.md)

40. **The flower and the fruit** (`plants/the-flower-and-the-fruit`) — the flower's four whorls: calyx of sepals (gamosepalous when joined), stamens free or fused (monadelphous, diadelphous…), flower types by ovary position (epigynous, perigynous, hypogynous) and inflorescences (spike, raceme, umbel, panicle); the fruit as the transformed ripe ovary: dry indehiscent fruits (achene, samara, caryopsis of wheat, rice, maize), dehiscent (capsule, legume, silique), aggregate fruits (strawberry) and multiple fruits; the seed as the angiosperms' main dispersal organ (embryo, cotyledons, testa) (Nicaragua, Grade 9 — *Ciencias Naturales, Noveno grado*, printed pp. 36–39 and 42–45; built for the Nicaragua placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/nicaragua-placements.md)

41. **Plant survival — special parts and dispersal** (`plants/plant-survival-special-parts-and-dispersal`) — thorns, stinging hairs, poison and thick bark against enemies, waxy and needle leaves against climate, and seeds dispersed by water, animals, explosion and wind (Malaysia, Grade 5 — *Sains Tahun 5*, printed p. 94; built for the Malaysia placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/malaysia-placements.md)

42. **How plants change with the seasons** (`plants/how-plants-change-with-the-seasons`) — how plants change through the seasons, taught in two parts: watching plants through the turning year, then putting the changes in order — teacher-guide evidence only, acceptance 0 (Morocco, Grade 1 — *Guide Moufid en Éveil Scientifique 1 — CP*, printed pp. 51–52; built for the Morocco placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/morocco-placements.md)

43. **Comparing plants — here and in other places** (`plants/comparing-plants-here-and-in-other-places`) — plants of the local area compared with plants from other parts of the world — their differences and similarities, with the weather conditions of the place as the reason plants grow differently in different areas (South Sudan, Primary 1 — *Primary Science 1, Pupil's Book*, 2.2 Differences and similarities in plants from local and other parts of the world: plants grow in different areas due to the weather conditions of the place, SS P1 SB, printed p. 33; built for the South Sudan placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/south-sudan-placements.md)

44. **Parts of a seed** (`plants/parts-of-a-seed`) — the structure of the seed: bean and maize seed parts — testa, micropyle, scar, embryo, plumule, radicle, cotyledon, endosperm — and monocotyledons against dicotyledons (South Sudan, Primary 4 — *Primary Science, Pupil's Book 4*, 3.1 Structure of the seed: bean and maize seed parts (testa, micropyle, scar, embryo, plumule, radicle, cotyledon, endosperm) and monocotyledons against dicotyledons, SS P4 SB, printed p. 38; built for the South Sudan placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/south-sudan-placements.md)

## Unit 9 — Animals

1. **Grouping animals: by young, by movement, by food and by body** (`animals/grouping-animals`) — animals sort into groups: egg-layers against milk-givers; by movement (flying, crawling, walking) and home (land, water, both); by food (meat-eater…
2. **Looking after our animals** (`animals/looking-after-our-animals`) — cared-for animals get enough food and water, a clean shelter, medical care when sick, and handling that does not hurt them — and cared-for animals …
3. **Tame animals and wild animals** (`animals/tame-animals-and-wild-animals`) — domestic animals live under people's care; wild animals live in forest, grassland, caves and water — and domestic animals give people great service…
4. **What animals need, and animals at work for people** (`animals/what-animals-need-and-animals-at-work`) — animals need food, shelter and water; they repay care with food (meat, milk, eggs, yoghurt, cheese, butter), transport (horse, donkey, mule, camel)…
5. **Living things — what makes something alive** (`animals/living-things-what-makes-something-alive`) — living things share a set of life characteristics together — sensitivity, reproduction, growth, respiration, nutrition, excretion, adaptation, homeostasis and metabolism — while non-living things do not (Grade 7 General Science)
6. **Classification — sorting the living world** (`animals/classification-sorting-the-living-world`) — classification groups living things by similarity and difference so life can be studied in order, newcomers identified and relationships revealed (Grade 7 General Science)
7. **Classification — scientific names and the seven ranks** (`animals/scientific-names-and-the-seven-ranks`) — every organism has one scientific name of two parts — genus and species — and sits in seven ranks from Kingdom down to Species (Grade 7 General Science)
8. **Kingdoms — the five kingdoms of living things** (`animals/the-five-kingdoms-of-living-things`) — living things are divided into five kingdoms — Monera, Protista, Fungi, Plantae and Animalia — separated by cell structure, nutrition, body organisation and reproduction (Grade 7 General Science)
9. **Animals — what makes an animal** (`animals/animals-what-makes-an-animal`) — animals are multicellular consumers with muscle and nervous tissue whose cells have no walls and no chloroplasts; they divide into invertebrates without a backbone and vertebrates with one (Grade 7 General Science)
10. **Animals — fish** (`animals/animals-fish`) — fish are cold-blooded aquatic animals that breathe through gills, move and balance with fins, and are covered in scales (Grade 7 General Science)
11. **Animals — amphibians and the frog** (`animals/animals-amphibians-and-the-frog`) — amphibians are ectothermic animals with smooth, moist, scaleless skin; their young breathe with gills in water and their adults breathe with lungs on land (Grade 7 General Science)
12. **Animals — reptiles** (`animals/animals-reptiles`) — reptiles are cold-blooded land animals with dry, rough, scaly skin that breathe with lungs — turtles and tortoises, crocodiles and alligators, lizards and snakes (Grade 7 General Science)
13. **Animals — birds** (`animals/animals-birds`) — birds are warm-blooded animals with a beak, two wings, two legs and feathers — feathers belong to birds alone — and beaks shaped to their food (Grade 7 General Science)
14. **Animals — mammals** (`animals/animals-mammals`) — mammals bear live young and feed them on milk, have hair, breathe with lungs and are warm-blooded — even whales and dolphins, which must surface to breathe air (Grade 7 General Science)
15. **Animals — invertebrates: insects and earthworms** (`animals/invertebrates-insects-and-earthworms`) — more than 95% of animals have no backbone; insects are known by six legs and three body parts, and the earthworm enriches the soil with its castings (Grade 7 General Science)
16. **Protists — protozoa and algae under the microscope** (`animals/protists-protozoa-and-algae`) — protists are mostly unicellular eukaryotes that are neither plant, fungus nor animal; protozoa move by pseudopodia, flagella or cilia, and plant-like algae photosynthesise without root, stem or leaf (Grade 7 General Science)
17. **Monera — bacteria: helpful, harmful, everywhere** (`animals/monera-bacteria-helpful-harmful-everywhere`) — Monera are prokaryotes with no nuclear membrane; bacteria sour milk and poison food yet also curdle milk usefully, and blue-green algae are photosynthetic bacteria, not true algae (Grade 7 General Science)
18. **Fungi — moulds, mushrooms and yeasts** (`animals/fungi-moulds-mushrooms-and-yeasts`) — fungi are eukaryotic, chitin-walled organisms that cannot make food — they digest dead matter and absorb it, parasitise living hosts, or live symbiotically; yeast (ersho) raises dough and ferments tella and tej (Grade 7 General Science)
19. **Animals — Who am I? vertebrate review game** (`animals/who-am-i-vertebrate-review-game`) — the five vertebrate groups can be told apart from clue cards — milk and fur, feathers and hard-shelled eggs, leathery scales, jelly eggs with gills-then-lungs, and gills with fins and scales (Grade 7 General Science)
20. **Animal word–picture reading support** (`animals/animal-word-picture-reading-support`) — Sweden's "Lässtöd" (reading support): a picture–word table naming 24 animals, picture and name together, so pupils who cannot yet read can still name and work with the animals — the picture carries the animal until the word can be read (Sweden, Grade 1 — *Djur omkring oss B — kopieringsunderlag*, printed p. 97; PARTIAL copy pages only, as filed)
21. **Life cycles of plants and animals** (`animals/life-cycles-of-plants-and-animals`) — the stages a living thing passes through from beginning to adult, in Sweden's set of ten small creeping things: the four-stage road egg, larva, pupa, adult (butterfly, bumblebee, fly, mosquito, ladybird, wasp, ant; the grasshopper's stages carried exactly as printed — egg, pupa, larva, grasshopper) and the simpler roads with no pupa (earthworm: jelly, cocoon, worm; spider: egg, cocoon, spider) (Sweden, Grade 1 — *Samtalsbilder, Tio små kryp*, PDF pp. 1–20)
22. **Snails and slugs in the grassland** (`animals/snails-and-slugs-in-the-grassland`) — grassland snails: how the brekkubobbi may have reached Iceland (with birds or people), and the measure-a-snail's-speed activity — teacher-guide evidence only, acceptance 0 (Iceland, Grade 5 — *Líf á landi — Kennsluleiðbeiningar*, printed pp. 29–30)
23. **Animal teeth — and what they eat** (`animals/animal-teeth-and-what-they-eat`) — do all animals have the same teeth? Teeth and diet: an animal's teeth fit what it eats — teacher-guide evidence only, acceptance 0 (Djibouti, Grade 3 — *Guide du maître — Sciences Expérimentales, 3ème année*, printed p. 27; PARTIAL — damaged file, as filed)
24. **Camouflage — how animals hide** (`animals/camouflage-how-animals-hide`) — camouflage defined, taught through insect examples, and why it matters for survival — curriculum evidence only, acceptance 0 (Barbados, Class 3 — *Primary Science Syllabus, Class One–Four*, printed p. 14)
25. **Inheritance — genes and traits** (`animals/inheritance-genes-and-traits`) — inherited traits against acquired traits, genes as DNA instructions showing through proteins, Mendel's tall × short pea crosses (all tall first, short returning 1 in 4 — dominant and recessive), sex determination by the father's X or Y, and evolution as inherited variation kept by selection over generations (India, Class X — *Science, Textbook for Class X* (NCERT), Ch. 9 Heredity and Evolution, printed p. 142)
26. **Herbivores, carnivores and omnivores** (`animals/herbivores-carnivores-and-omnivores`) — animals grouped by food: plant-eaters, animal-eaters, and eaters of both, taught inside the food-chain lesson (Bulgaria, Grade 3 — *Човекът и природата за 3. клас*, Lesson 15, printed p. 36)
27. **Adaptations for life in water and on land** (`animals/adaptations-for-life-in-water-and-on-land`) — water fittings (fins, shells, tentacles, webbed feet; the dolphin is not a fish) and land fittings for movement, feeding, winter and defence (Bulgaria, Grade 3 — *Човекът и природата за 3. клас*, Lessons 18–19, printed pp. 42, 44)
28. **How animals breathe — lungs, gills, skin and surface** (`animals/how-animals-breathe-lungs-gills-skin-and-surface`) — breathing as a life process: oxygen taken in to release energy from food, by lungs, gills, skin or the whole surface; plants breathe too (Bulgaria, Grade 4 — *Човекът и природата за 4. клас*, Lesson 36, printed p. 72)
29. **Camouflage and warning colours** (`animals/camouflage-and-warning-colours`) — protective colouring both ways: the mantis hidden in green and the squirrel's seasonal coat against the wasp's warning stripes and the salamander's signal spots (Bulgaria, Grade 4 — *Човекът и природата за 4. клас*, Lesson 52, printed p. 103)
30. **Evolution and natural selection** (`animals/evolution-and-natural-selection`) — the diversity and evolution of living things, taught off a timeline: life appearing on Earth 3.8 billion years ago, living things changing generation after generation, and natural selection keeping the differences that fit — teacher-guide evidence only, acceptance 0 (Gabon, Grade 5 — *Super Efficace Sciences expérimentales et technologie, 5e année — Guide pédagogique*, printed p. 48)

31. **Young are like, but not exactly like, their parents** (`animals/young-are-like-but-not-exactly-like-their-parents`) — young plants and animals resemble their parents but not exactly — the carrots orange and cone-shaped yet different in size and taste — and patterns of inherited traits read from data (United States, Grades 1 and 3 — *Utah SEEd Standards* books, 1.2.3 printed p. 83; 3.2.2 printed p. 53)
32. **How parents help their young survive** (`animals/how-parents-help-their-young-survive`) — offspring signals (crying, chirping) and parent responses (feeding, comforting, protecting) — survival behaviour in two halves (United States, Grade 1 — *1st Grade — for Utah SEEd Standards, Revised 2025*, 1.2.4, printed p. 93)
33. **How the environment can change traits** (`animals/how-the-environment-can-change-traits`) — the environment can affect the traits of living things — the explanation built from evidence, not guessed (United States, Grade 3 — *3rd Grade Science for Utah SEEd Standards, Revised 2025*, 3.2.3, printed p. 62)
39. **Inside an animal — body systems of a bird** (`animals/inside-an-animal-body-systems-of-a-bird`) — inside the hen: the skeleton, and the digestive, respiratory, circulatory and reproductive systems — the close inside study that follows the outside study of the bird (Angola, Grade 6 — *Ciências da Natureza, Manual da 6.ª Classe*, printed p. 55; built for the Angola placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/angola-placements.md)
34. **Copying nature to solve problems — biomimicry** (`animals/copying-nature-to-solve-problems-biomimicry`) — designing a solution to a human problem by mimicking how plants and animals use their external parts to survive and grow (United States, Grade 2 — *2nd Grade — for Utah SEEd Standards, Revised 2025*, 2.2.4, printed p. 68)
35. **Making food and drink with micro-organisms — fermentation** (`animals/making-food-and-drink-with-micro-organisms-fermentation`) — yoghurt making in full: lactic-acid bacteria turn lactose into lactic acid; heating, pasteurising, homogenising, inoculating with the starter, incubating at about 43°C, coagulation — and yeast in beer making (with the Vailima factory visit) and bread making (Samoa, Year 11 — *Science — Year 11, Book 1 (MESC Samoa)*, printed pp. 69–73)

36. **How animals reproduce** (`animals/how-animals-reproduce`) — reproduction: new animals of the parents' kind — many animals lay eggs and the young hatch, while mammals are born live — taught straight after the reproduction of plants (Cambodia, Grade 5 — *វិទ្យាសាស្ត្រ ថ្នាក់ទី៥ (Science, Grade 5)*, Ch. 1, Lesson 3, printed p. 22; built for the Cambodia placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/cambodia-placements.md)
37. **Evolution — evidence and fossils** (`animals/evolution-evidence-and-fossils`) — biological evolution: species change over time, known from evolutionary evidence, with fossils as a window on the past, and genetic variability tested by the experiment with beans (Ecuador, Grade 9 — *EC G9 Ciencias Naturales*, printed pp. 41–46; built for the Ecuador placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/ecuador-placements.md)
38. **How animals move — swim, walk, fly** (`animals/how-animals-move-swim-walk-fly`) — how each animal moves in its place — swimming, walking and flying as the ways animals move, taught animal by animal (Sudan, Grade 4 — *العلوم الطبيعية ٤ (Natural Sciences 4)*, Unit 3, Lesson 1, printed p. 26)

40. **Animal body coverings — fur, feathers and scales** (`animals/animal-body-coverings-fur-feathers-and-scales`) — what covers animals' bodies — fur, feathers, scales and shells — and what each covering does (Kuwait, Grade 1, Term 1 — *Science, Grade 1, Term 1 (Student Book)*, printed p. 46; built for the Kuwait placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/kuwait-placements.md)

41. **How living things respond to their environment** (`animals/how-living-things-respond-to-their-environment`) — living things respond to light, heat, water, gravity and touch — plants by growing, animals by moving — because responding is a sign of life (Kuwait, Grade 4, Term 1 — *Science, Grade 4, Term 1 (Student Book)*, printed p. 31; built for the Kuwait placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/kuwait-placements.md)

42. **Viruses** (`animals/viruses`) — what viruses are — hereditary material in a protein coat, not cells — and how they differ from living things, multiplying only inside a living cell (Kuwait, Grade 5, Term 1 — *Science, Grade 5, Term 1 (Student Book)*, printed p. 14; Kuwait, Grade 7, Term 1 — *Science, Grade 7, Term 1 (Student Book)*, printed p. 42; built for the Kuwait placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/kuwait-placements.md)

43. **Reproduction in living organisms** (`animals/reproduction-in-living-organisms`) — how every living thing makes more of its own kind — parents and offspring — so life continues (Kuwait, Grade 6, Term 1 — *Science, Grade 6, Term 1 (Student Book)*, printed p. 154; built for the Kuwait placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/kuwait-placements.md)

44. **Types of reproduction — asexual and sexual** (`animals/types-of-reproduction-asexual-and-sexual`) — one parent making copies against two parents whose cells join, making offspring that differ — the test is how many parents (Kuwait, Grade 6, Term 1 — *Science, Grade 6, Term 1 (Student Book)*, printed p. 159; built for the Kuwait placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/kuwait-placements.md)

45. **The life cycle of a silkworm** (`animals/the-life-cycle-of-a-silkworm`) — the whole life of a silkworm: eggs hatching, the larva growing through its moults, the pupa in its silk cocoon, and the moth laying the eggs that begin again — China's own sericulture way (China, Grade 4 — *科学四年级下册 (Science, Grade 4, second volume)*, printed pp. 15, 20, 24; built for the China placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/china-placements.md)

46. **Animal behaviour — instinct and learned** (`animals/animal-behaviour-instinct-and-learned`) — behaviour and its two kinds: innate behaviour (fighting, seeking shelter, feeding, reproduction, social life, caring for the young, exploring, escaping enemies) against learned behaviour picked up through training (a parrot riding a bicycle, trained dolphins, a cat that learns to open a door) (Syria, Grade 8 — *Biology and Earth (علم الأحياء والأرض), Eighth Basic Grade*, Unit 4, printed pp. 218–221; built for the Syria placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/syria-placements.md)

47. **Evolution and the origin of life** (`animals/evolution-and-the-origin-of-life`) — the evolutionary process as one arc — the origin of the Earth, then the origin of life, then the evolution of living organisms as a continuing process (Sri Lanka, Grade 9 — *Science Part – I, Grade 9*, printed pp. 98–102; built for the Sri Lanka placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/sri-lanka-placements.md)
48. **Animals and their young** (`animals/animals-and-their-young`) — animals and their young ones — matching each animal to its young, as Zimbabwe's ECD A guide teaches them (Zimbabwe, ECD A — *PlusOne Science and Technology* teacher's guides (ECD A printed p. 21), guide evidence only, acceptance 0; built for the Zimbabwe placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/zimbabwe-placements.md)

49. **Animal senses — how animals sense the world** (`animals/animal-senses-how-animals-sense-the-world`) — senses in some animals and the stimuli they answer: the mole's touch (six times more touch receptors than a human hand), the crocodile's touch, the dog's picking-out hearing, the owl's hearing (North Macedonia, Grade 4 — *Природне науке за четврти разред (Natural Sciences for Grade 4)* (Serbian edition), printed pp. 59–60; built for the North Macedonia placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/north-macedonia-placements.md)
50. **Animal behaviour and communication** (`animals/animal-behaviour-and-communication`) — how animals behave and communicate: signals, calls, raising fins, puffing up, and the sense of smell, used for protection and for attracting — teacher-guide evidence only, acceptance 0 (Fiji, Year 4 — *Elementary Science Year 4 — Teacher Resource Material*, printed pp. 10–11; built for the Fiji placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/fiji-placements.md)
51. **Group names for animals** (`animals/group-names-for-animals`) — the names for groups of animals: a herd of cows, a school of fish, a flock of birds, a brood of hens, a litter of cats and dogs, a colony of ants, a hive of bees, an army of caterpillars — teacher-guide evidence only, acceptance 0 (Fiji, Year 2 — *Elementary Science Year 2 — FALD: Learning to Know, Notes and Activities*, printed pp. 26–28; built for the Fiji placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/fiji-placements.md)

52. **Hereditary diseases and marrying relatives** (`animals/hereditary-diseases-and-marrying-relatives`) — when relatives marry: hidden carriers from shared ancestors (Mediterranean anaemia), a medical check before marriage as the safeguard, and the diseases one by one — hereditary dwarfism, colour blindness on the X chromosome, Down's pattern, albinism (Yemen, Grade 9 — *Science, Grade 9, Part 2*, printed pp. 142, 147; built for the Yemen placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/yemen-placements.md)
53. **Mutations — sudden changes in the inherited material** (`animals/mutations-sudden-changes-in-the-inherited-material`) — a sudden change in the inherited material, told through a child born with an extra chromosome 13 (Patau's pattern): a chromosome pair failing to part in division makes 47 chromosomes instead of 46; the agents that can trigger such changes — ultraviolet and X-rays, chemicals and mustard gas (Yemen, Grade 9 — *Science, Grade 9, Part 2*, printed p. 144; built for the Yemen placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/yemen-placements.md)

54. **Animal body symmetry and axes** (`animals/animal-body-symmetry-and-axes`) — invertebrate body symmetry types named: spherical, radial and bilateral planes of symmetry (Nicaragua, Grade 7 Módulo — *Módulo Autoformativo de Ciencias Naturales, Séptimo grado*, printed pp. 65–66; built for the Nicaragua placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/nicaragua-placements.md)

55. **Asexual reproduction — budding, fission and spores** (`animals/asexual-reproduction-budding-fission-spores`) — cell reproduction without gametes: bipartition/binary fission in bacteria, yeasts, unicellular algae and protozoa, budding (gemación) and sporulation; and plant reproduction two ways — vegetative/asexual (no union of cells, offspring identical, keeping well-adapted individuals) against sexual reproduction (Nicaragua, Grade 8 Módulo — *Módulo Autoformativo de Ciencias Naturales, 8vo grado*, printed pp. 5–6; Grade 4 — *Ciencias Naturales, 4to grado*, printed pp. 47–49; built for the Nicaragua placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/nicaragua-placements.md)

56. **Animal survival — special parts and behaviours** (`animals/animal-survival-special-parts-and-behaviours`) — spines, poison, camouflage and shed tails against enemies; migration, hibernation and blubber against weather; eggs guarded and young defended (Malaysia, Grade 5 — *Sains Tahun 5*, printed p. 50; built for the Malaysia placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/malaysia-placements.md)

57. **Useful microorganisms in medicine, farming and industry** (`animals/useful-microorganisms-in-medicine-farming-and-industry`) — yeast and Lactobacillus in food and drink, E. coli making insulin, Penicillium making penicillin, nitrifying bacteria in farming, and eco-enzyme cleaner (Malaysia, Grade 11 (Form 5) — *Sains Tingkatan 5*, printed p. 28; built for the Malaysia placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/malaysia-placements.md)

58. **How microorganisms grow — conditions and colonies** (`animals/how-microorganisms-grow-conditions-and-colonies`) — the conditions of growth found by experiment — Bacillus grows fastest at pH 7 (Malaysia, Grade 11 (Form 5) — *Sains Tingkatan 5*, printed p. 26; built for the Malaysia placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/malaysia-placements.md)

59. **How animals keep themselves clean** (`animals/how-animals-keep-themselves-clean`) — how animals clean themselves: dogs rolling, licking and nibbling their fur, and chickens giving themselves a dust bath and shaking it off — done to prevent germs and pests (South Sudan, Primary 2 — *Primary Science 2, Pupil's Book*, 1.4 How animals clean themselves: dogs roll and bite their fur; chickens give themselves a dust bath and shake it off, SS P2 SB, printed p. 26; built for the South Sudan placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/south-sudan-placements.md)

60. **How feeding shows in teeth, beaks and mouthparts** (`animals/how-feeding-shows-in-teeth-beaks-and-mouthparts`) — reading an animal's feeding group off its mouth: herbivore and carnivore teeth, birds' beaks — grain, filter, flesh, nectar and fruit feeders — and insects' mouthparts: chewing, piercing-sucking, sponging (South Sudan, Primary 4 — *Primary Science, Pupil's Book 4*, 2.1 the book's own way: reading an animal's feeding group off its teeth (grazers and browsers, ruminants, canines and claws), birds' beaks (grain eaters, filter feeders, flesh eaters, nectar and fruit feeders) and insects' mouthparts (chewing, piercing-sucking, sponging), SS P4 SB, printed p. 11; built for the South Sudan placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/south-sudan-placements.md)

## Unit 10 — Natural Resources

1. **Caring so they last: looking after natural resources** (`natural-resources/caring-so-they-last`) — the harms of loss — soil washed away, fertility and yields falling, wild animals fleeing and vanishing, climate disturbed — and the care that answe…
2. **Energy sources around us** (`natural-resources/energy-sources-around-us`) — energy is the ability to do a work; Ethiopia's power comes from water, sun, wind, fuel and biogas — hydro dams (GERD, Gilgel Gibe I and II, Tana Be…
3. **How a rich place becomes bare** (`natural-resources/how-a-rich-place-becomes-bare`) — a printed memoir: a place once rich in native plants, wild animals and birdsong ('the monkeys' house', 1970 E.C.) found forty years later a barren,…
4. **Minerals from the ground** (`natural-resources/minerals-from-the-ground`) — minerals are things dug from the ground or found on it — stone, gravel, sand, salt, iron, and in Grade 3 also coal, fuel/oil and gold; they are non…
5. **Ones that come back and ones that finish** (`natural-resources/ones-that-come-back-and-ones-that-finish`) — resources sort into two families: renewable ones come back after use and are not exhausted (water, air, plants), and non-renewable ones are finishe…
6. **Plants and wild animals as resources** (`natural-resources/plants-and-wild-animals-as-resources`) — plants keep the climate steady and serve food, industry and more; wild animals, not tamed and living wild, earn the country income through tourism …
7. **Water, soil and air around us** (`natural-resources/water-soil-and-air-around-us`) — soil, water and air are natural resources: soil is the fertile top layer where roots reach, in black, red and brown; water comes from river, well, …
8. **What counts as a natural resource** (`natural-resources/what-counts-as-a-natural-resource`) — a natural resource is everything found in nature, on or under the Earth, that serves people directly or indirectly — soil, air, water, plants, anim…

9. **Oil — from the ground to our home** (`natural-resources/oil-from-the-ground-to-our-home`) — oil's journey whole: searched for underground, drilled and brought up, then transported to the home that uses it — the book's feature name for oil is "black gold" (Greece, Grade 6 — *«Φυσικά» ΣΤ΄ Δημοτικού — Ερευνώ και Ανακαλύπτω*, Energy ch. 3, printed p. 18)
10. **Refining crude oil — what a refinery does** (`natural-resources/refining-crude-oil`) — crude oil is the raw oil as it comes from the ground; a refinery processes it, and usable products come out — followed through the book's feature, a barrel with a history (Greece, Grade 6 — *«Φυσικά» ΣΤ΄ Δημοτικού*, Energy ch. 4, printed p. 20)
11. **Oil as a raw material — more than fuel** (`natural-resources/oil-as-a-raw-material`) — oil's second life: not burned but built into products beyond fuels, with detergents as the book's feature example (Greece, Grade 6 — *«Φυσικά» ΣΤ΄ Δημοτικού*, Energy ch. 6, printed p. 24)
12. **Natural gas — a precious gas** (`natural-resources/natural-gas-a-precious-gas`) — natural gas searched for underground and pumped up — the book calls it a precious gas, and gives its finding its own chapter before its use as an energy source (Greece, Grade 6 — *«Φυσικά» ΣΤ΄ Δημοτικού*, Energy ch. 9, printed p. 30)
13. **Bioclimatic design of buildings** (`natural-resources/bioclimatic-design-of-buildings`) — houses designed around the Sun and water — the energy unit's closing feature spread, where technology meets the Sun and the water (Greece, Grade 6 — *«Φυσικά» ΣΤ΄ Δημοτικού*, Energy unit feature spread, printed p. 39)

14. **Storing energy — fuels, batteries and food** (`natural-resources/storing-energy-fuels-batteries-and-food`) — stored chemical energy in Seychelles: petrol, gas, kerosene, charcoal, batteries and food (Seychelles, Grade 9 (P9) — *Seychelles Integrated Science — Conserving Our Energy*, Unit 11, Topic 4, printed booklet pp. 10–11; built for the Seychelles placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/seychelles-placements.md)
15. **Fuels we use** (`natural-resources/fuels-we-use`) — fuels we use — the types of fuel and why they matter, as Zimbabwe's Grade 1, 2, 3 and 4 guides teach the unit (Zimbabwe, Grade 1, Grade 3, Grade 4, Grade 2 — *PlusOne Science and Technology* teacher's guides (Grade 1 printed p. 68; Grade 3 printed p. 71; Grade 4 printed p. 69; Grade 2 printed p. 84), guide evidence only, acceptance 0; built for the Zimbabwe placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/zimbabwe-placements.md)
16. **Solar energy — from the sun** (`natural-resources/solar-energy-from-the-sun`) — solar energy — the energy that comes from the sun, as Zimbabwe's Grade 5 guide teaches it as a unit of its own (Zimbabwe, Grade 5 — *PlusOne Science and Technology* teacher's guides (Grade 5 printed p. 63), guide evidence only, acceptance 0; built for the Zimbabwe placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/zimbabwe-placements.md)
17. **Natural resources of Bangladesh** (`natural-resources/natural-resources-of-bangladesh`) — the mineral resources of Bangladesh — ore minerals and energy minerals — as the closing chapter of Bangladesh's Class 8 Investigative Study teaches the home country's own resources (Bangladesh, Grade 8 — *Science Investigative Study Class 8*, printed p. 186; built for the Bangladesh placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/bangladesh-placements.md)

18. **Checking a product's whole life cycle** (`natural-resources/checking-a-products-whole-life-cycle`) — science, technology and society: environment-friendly tools and the product life-cycle analysis — six stages (getting raw materials, manufacture, transport to shops and homes, use and reuse, recycling, and final disposal by… (Saudi Arabia, First Intermediate, Part 2 — *Science, First Intermediate, Part 2*, printed p. 208; built for the Saudi Arabia placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/saudi-arabia-placements.md)
19. **The Hoàng Cầm smokeless stove** (`natural-resources/the-hoang-cam-smokeless-stove`) — the field kitchen of the Hòa Bình campaign (1951–1952) whose smoke is scattered so enemy planes cannot spot it — Vietnam's own fuel-saving heritage (Vietnam, Grade 5 — *Khoa học 5*, printed p. 41; built for the Vietnam placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/vietnam-placements.md)

20. **Ocean resources and overfishing** (`natural-resources/ocean-resources-and-overfishing`) — Malaysia's seafood and aquaculture animals, and what overfishing would do to the sea's produce (Malaysia, Grade 11 (Form 5) — *Sains Tambahan Tingkatan 5*, printed p. 185; built for the Malaysia placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/malaysia-placements.md)

## Unit 11 — Our Environment

1. **Caring for our environment: soil, water and air** (`our-environment/caring-for-our-environment-soil-water-and-air`) — care, resource by resource: stopping soil erosion (eroded land against protected land), keeping water sources unpolluted, keeping the air clean — w…
2. **Caring for our surroundings, and reusing what we throw away** (`our-environment/caring-for-our-surroundings-and-reusing`) — our resources are being badly damaged, so we protect them: planting and tending trees, disposing of waste properly, preventing forest fire, cleanli…
3. **Dirty water and what it brings** (`our-environment/dirty-water-and-what-it-brings`) — liquid waste — urine, wash-water and effluent from homes and factories — brings bad smell, respiratory illness (colds, asthma), breeding places for…
4. **Dry rubbish in its place** (`our-environment/dry-rubbish-in-its-place`) — dry waste — peelings (onion, potato, banana, orange), paper and plastic — goes into a waste container and then to a proper disposal place, or the h…
5. **Pollution and its four kinds** (`our-environment/pollution-and-its-four-kinds`) — Ethiopia's main environmental problems are listed together — soil erosion, land damage, deforestation, water shortage, biodiversity loss and pollut…
6. **Resources around us, and why they matter** (`our-environment/resources-around-us-and-why-they-matter`) — natural resources are soil, water, plants, air and wild animals; man-made resources are monuments, bridges, houses of worship, government buildings…
7. **Resources people make — and looking after them** (`our-environment/resources-people-make`) — things people create for use — dam, mosque, church, school, hospital — are human-made resources; as shared property they must be used and kept prop…
8. **Waste, flies, germs and trachoma** (`our-environment/waste-flies-germs-and-trachoma`) — waste dumped anywhere breeds flies, and flies carry germs from waste to people's bodies — one named result is trachoma, an eye disease that untreat…
9. **What waste is and its three kinds** (`our-environment/what-waste-is-and-its-three-kinds`) — waste is what is left over after things are used — leftover food and products, human and animal waste — and it comes in three states: solid waste, …
10. **Cycles — the carbon cycle** (`our-environment/cycles-the-carbon-cycle`) — carbon travels in a loop — released into the air by respiration and burning, taken in by plants for photosynthesis, passed to animals that eat the plants, returned by death and decomposition, and stored away in fossil fuels until burning releases it again (Grade 7 General Science)
11. **Cycles — the water cycle** (`our-environment/cycles-the-water-cycle`) — water travels in an endless loop — evaporation and transpiration rise, condensation gathers the vapour into clouds, precipitation returns it as rain, and flow carries it back to the water bodies (Grade 7 General Science)
12. **Energy resources — resource depletion and its drivers** (`our-environment/energy-resources-resource-depletion-and-its-drivers`) — resource depletion is using a resource faster than it can be replenished until it is exhausted — driven by seven named drivers — teacher-guide evidence only, acceptance 0 (Grade 7 General Science)
13. **Energy resources — environmental degradation and its effects** (`our-environment/energy-resources-environmental-degradation-and-its-effects`) — environmental degradation is the environment's deterioration, chiefly through resource depletion — and its effects fall in five families: human health, biodiversity and more — teacher-guide evidence only, acceptance 0 (Grade 7 General Science)
14. **Ecosystems — populations, communities and their surroundings** (`our-environment/ecosystems-populations-communities-and-their-surroundings`) — living things are organised in widening levels — a habitat is where a kind lives, a population is all of one kind in a place, a community is all the kinds together (Grade 8 General Science)
15. **How living things affect each other** (`our-environment/how-living-things-affect-each-other`) — living things that meet affect each other in six named ways — predation, competition, parasitism, mutualism, commensalism and amensalism (Grade 8 General Science)
16. **Food chains, food webs and energy pyramids** (`our-environment/food-chains-food-webs-and-energy-pyramids`) — living things are linked by eating: producers make food, consumers eat, and decomposers return the remains — energy moves through the chain in a pyramid (Grade 8 General Science)
17. **Cycles of matter — carbon, nitrogen, water** (`our-environment/cycles-of-matter-carbon-nitrogen-water`) — carbon, oxygen, nitrogen and water cycle round and round between the living and non-living parts of the ecosystem, while energy flows one way through (Grade 8 General Science)
18. **Air pollution and acid rain** (`our-environment/air-pollution-and-acid-rain`) — air pollution loads the air with harmful matter — including particulates small enough to lodge in lungs — and two pollutant gases, sulphur and nitrogen oxides, make acid rain (Grade 8 General Science)
19. **Global warming — the greenhouse effect** (`our-environment/global-warming-the-greenhouse-effect`) — a parked car in the sun shows the greenhouse effect: its windows let light in and hold the heat; greenhouse gases — carbon dioxide, methane and more — do the same to the Earth (Grade 8 General Science)
20. **Forests — what they give and what cutting them costs** (`our-environment/forests-what-they-give-and-what-cutting-them-costs`) — forests give timber, fuel wood, fodder, fibre, fruit, herbal drugs and industrial raw materials, and they shelter wildlife, build and hold soil, and shape climate (Grade 8 General Science)
21. **Biodiversity — keeping the variety of life** (`our-environment/biodiversity-keeping-the-variety-of-life`) — biodiversity is the variety of life, counted in species; its value is direct (medicine and agriculture) and indirect (ecosystems that work), and it is kept by protecting habitats (Grade 8 General Science)
22. **Local knowledge, local conservation** (`our-environment/local-knowledge-local-conservation`) — the unit closes by turning to the people who have conserved a place longest: pupils interview local people on how they conserve water, forest and soil (Grade 8 General Science)
23. **Air pollution — what dirties the air** (`our-environment/air-pollution-what-dirties-the-air`) — the three families of air dirt — smoke from burning (the biggest), fumes from engines, dust from bare dry ground — what dirty air does to lungs, eyes and plants, and cleaning at the source: never burn rubbish, manage cooking smoke, plant trees — teacher-guide evidence only, acceptance 0 (Gabon, Grade 3 — Super Efficace, Guide pédagogique, 3e année)
24. **Climate change impacts and adaptation in the Caribbean** (`our-environment/climate-change-impacts-and-adaptation-in-the-caribbean`) — how climate change affects island life — rising seas and flooding at the coast, heavier rain and stronger storms, hotter and drier spells straining food and fresh water — and how learners can minimise its impact: adapting (preparing for storms, floods and dry spells, protecting the coast) and minimising (burning less fossil fuel, protecting trees, wasting less) — curriculum-guide evidence only, acceptance 0 (Jamaica, Grade 7 — National Standards Curriculum Guide: Science, Grades 7–9 — Integrated Science)
25. **Mangroves — protecting coast and sea** (`our-environment/mangroves-protecting-coast-and-sea`) — the mangrove tree part by part — roots in salt water and mud, trunk, branches and leaves — and what it gives: Palauans' food and market dependence on the mangroves, and home ground for the saltwater crocodile, the mangrove crab and the night heron — teacher's-edition evidence only, acceptance 0 (Palau, Grade 5 — *Ridge to Reef Education — Teacher's Edition*, Ch. 3, printed pp. 24–28)
26. **Seagrass beds as nursery habitat** (`our-environment/seagrass-beds-as-nursery-habitat`) — seagrass is a flowering plant of the sea: its leaves photosynthesise, so the beds stay in sunlit shallow water; most Palau seagrasses spread by their roots; and Enhalus acoroides releases its pollen onto the sea surface at full and new moon — teacher's-edition evidence only, acceptance 0 (Palau, Grade 5 — *Ridge to Reef Education — Teacher's Edition*, Ch. 4, printed pp. 32–36)
27. **Coral polyps — the animals that build reefs** (`our-environment/coral-polyps-the-animals-that-build-reefs`) — the polyp is a tiny animal that extends tentacles to catch microscopic floating animals; polyps joined together build the reef, and reefs come in kinds — the Helen Reef atoll among them — teacher's-edition evidence only, acceptance 0 (Palau, Grade 5 — *Ridge to Reef Education — Teacher's Edition*, Ch. 5, printed pp. 40–42)
28. **Fish spawning aggregations** (`our-environment/fish-spawning-aggregations`) — how many reef fish reproduce: they gather in one place to release eggs and sperm into the water together, so the eggs and sperm meet — the gathering is the way — teacher's-edition evidence only, acceptance 0 (Palau, Grade 5 — *Ridge to Reef Education — Teacher's Edition*, printed p. 45)
29. **Ocean literacy — how the ocean and people connect** (`our-environment/ocean-literacy-how-the-ocean-and-people-connect`) — the seven essential principles of ocean literacy: one big ocean; the ocean shapes the Earth; the ocean drives weather and climate; the ocean made the Earth habitable; the ocean's great diversity of life; ocean–human interconnection; and the ocean largely unexplored — teacher-guide evidence only, acceptance 0 (Canada, Grades 5–9 — *Science First Peoples: Teacher Resource Guide, Grades 5 to 9*, Unit 8 Ocean Connections, printed p. 171)
30. **Habitats — where a living thing lives** (`our-environment/habitats-and-animal-adaptations`) — what a habitat is: the place where a living thing lives, taught inside Learning Experience 2, "Our surroundings, a space for life" — the surroundings as the space in which a living thing's life happens — teacher-guide evidence only, acceptance 0 (Guatemala, Grade 7 — *Ciencias Naturales I, Guía de orientaciones metodológicas para docentes*, printed p. 38)
31. **Endangered species of Guatemala** (`our-environment/endangered-species-of-guatemala`) — what it means for a species to be in danger of extinction — the kind itself at risk of ending for ever — taught as Guatemala's own content: the species in danger of extinction in Guatemala, set as a complementary activity — teacher-guide evidence only, acceptance 0 (Guatemala, Grade 7 — *Ciencias Naturales I, Guía de orientaciones metodológicas para docentes*, printed p. 66)
32. **Biomes of the world** (`our-environment/biomes-of-the-world`) — a biome is the type of vegetation of a region, so the world's regions sort by their plant cover, taught inside Learning Experience 3, "Our environment, a harmonious space" — teacher-guide evidence only, acceptance 0 (Guatemala, Grade 9 — *Ciencias Naturales III, Guía de orientaciones metodológicas para docentes*, printed p. 54)
33. **Tabu — closed seasons protecting a place** (`our-environment/tabu-closed-seasons-protecting-a-place`) — declaring a place tabu (closed) so its plants and animals recover: nobody takes from the place while the tabu stands, plants grow back and animals breed and grow, and the tabu is lifted when the place is strong again — Vanuatu's traditional conservation practice taught as science — teacher-guide evidence only, acceptance 0 (Vanuatu, Grade 3 — *SAENS — Tija Gaed, Yia 3*, Term 2, Wik 4, Lesen 12: Putum tabu long envaeromen, printed p. 78)
34. **Plankton — the ocean's tiniest life** (`our-environment/plankton-the-oceans-tiniest-life`) — life began in the sea, and its tiniest life still anchors it: phytoplankton — tiny plants seen only with microscopes — drift on the currents, produce over half the world's oxygen by photosynthesis, and anchor the ocean food webs (Nauru, Years 5–6 — *Primary Reader 2 — Living Sustainably with the Pacific Ocean (Years 5–6, Science)*, USP ESD Reader 2, printed p. 2)
35. **Pond and lake life** (`our-environment/pond-and-lake-life`) — the standing-water community, from reeds at the edge and water lilies on the surface to frogs, fish and water birds (Serbia, Grade 3 — *Природа и друштво 3*, printed p. 62)
36. **River life — from source to mouth** (`our-environment/river-life-from-source-to-mouth`) — one river community changing down its course: trout in the fast cold upper course, catfish and carp in the slow lower course (Serbia, Grade 3 — *Природа и друштво 3*, printed p. 64)
37. **Iceland's habitats and their living worlds** (`our-environment/iceland-habitats-and-their-living-worlds`) — a young volcanic island's living world organised by habitat (lava, forest, soil, grassland, wetland, heath, gravel plain, highland, human-made land, shore), each habitat known by its characteristic organism — teacher-guide evidence only, acceptance 0 (Iceland, Grade 5 — *Líf á landi — Kennsluleiðbeiningar*, printed pp. 3–13)
38. **Decomposers — rotting and recycling in nature** (`our-environment/decomposers-rotting-and-recycling-in-nature`) — rotting and digestion in the forest floor: fungi and many-legged soil animals (millipedes) break dead matter down — teacher-guide evidence only, acceptance 0 (Iceland, Grade 5 — *Líf á landi — Kennsluleiðbeiningar*, printed p. 22)
39. **Wetlands — and why they must not be drained** (`our-environment/wetlands-and-why-they-must-not-be-drained`) — draining wetlands badly shrank wader habitat (the water rail is nearly extinct in Iceland, the red-necked phalarope rare); restoring wetlands, and peat bogs binding carbon dioxide — teacher-guide evidence only, acceptance 0 (Iceland, Grade 5 — *Líf á landi — Kennsluleiðbeiningar*, printed pp. 39–40)
40. **Invasive species — lupine and mink in Iceland** (`our-environment/invasive-species-lupine-and-mink-in-iceland`) — brought-in living things as a risk: the lupine (a relative of white clover, used to reclaim land) spreading, and the mink as an invasive predator at the top of its food web — teacher-guide evidence only, acceptance 0 (Iceland, Grade 5 — *Líf á landi — Kennsluleiðbeiningar*, printed pp. 47–48)
41. **Adaptation — how living things fit where they live** (`our-environment/adaptation-how-living-things-fit-where-they-live`) — why the same animals are not found everywhere; how animals adapt when their environment's conditions change; how plants adapt to the conditions of their environment — teacher-guide evidence only, acceptance 0 (Djibouti, Grade 4 — *Guide de l'enseignant — Sciences Expérimentales, 4ème année*, Leçons 11, 12, 15, printed pp. 43, 46, 55)
42. **Taro pits and patches** (`our-environment/taro-pits-and-patches`) — pits dug down to wet ground — on low islands down to the freshwater lens — where giant swamp taro grows, and the patch shelters shrimp, crabs, fish and young dragonflies and mosquitoes (Micronesia, Grades 3–5 — *Our Low Island Home*, printed p. 4; Grades 6–8 — *Pacific Low Island Environments*, printed p. 7)
43. **Beaches — made of coral and shell** (`our-environment/beaches-made-of-coral-and-shell`) — low-island sand and rock are broken coral and shells; dark volcanic sand against white coral-and-shell sand on high islands; tiny sand animals feed crabs and seabirds, and salt-tolerant shore plants hold the lagoon-facing shore (Micronesia, Grades 3–5 — *Our Low Island Home*, printed p. 6; Grades 6–8 — *Pacific High Island Environments*, printed p. 16; *Pacific Low Island Environments*, printed p. 8)
44. **Cloud forest** (`our-environment/cloud-forest`) — the mountaintop forest standing in cloud: thin soil, small trees, and mosses and ferns covering everything (Micronesia, Grades 6–8 — *Pacific High Island Environments*, printed p. 7)
45. **Estuaries — where river meets sea** (`our-environment/estuaries-where-river-meets-sea`) — fresh and salt water mix; the saltiness changes with the tide and with distance, and the mixing makes a rich food supply (Micronesia, Grades 6–8 — *Pacific High Island Environments*, printed p. 13)
46. **Patch reefs** (`our-environment/patch-reefs`) — solid reef islands on the lagoon floor, growing toward the sunlight, giving shallow-water animals their hiding places (Micronesia, Grades 6–8 — *Pacific Low Island Environments*, printed p. 13)
47. **Flood and landslide emergency plans** (`our-environment/flood-and-landslide-emergency-plans`) — making a community emergency plan for floods and landslides before the emergency comes — Costa Rica's own preparedness task (Costa Rica, Grade 7 — *Ciencias, Sétimo, Código 80002, Antologías CONED*, printed p. 124)
48. **Coral reefs — a Caribbean habitat** (`our-environment/coral-reefs-a-caribbean-habitat`) — the coral reef as habitat and ecosystem: reef organisms, its importance to the Caribbean, and how people destroy reefs — curriculum evidence only, acceptance 0 (Barbados, Class 4 — *Primary Science Syllabus, Class One–Four*, printed p. 17)
49. **Natural and human-made communities** (`our-environment/natural-and-human-made-communities`) — communities that grow by themselves (forest, sea, river, swamp) against communities people make and keep (park, fishpond, field, garden) (Bulgaria, Grade 4 — *Човекът и природата за 4. клас*, Lesson 48, printed p. 95)
50. **Communities in fresh and salt water** (`our-environment/communities-in-fresh-and-salt-water`) — fresh waters (water lilies, carp, catfish, pike, herons) against salt waters (algae near the sunlit surface, plankton) (Bulgaria, Grade 4 — *Човекът и природата за 4. клас*, Lesson 49, printed p. 96)
51. **Communities of plains and mountains** (`our-environment/communities-of-plains-and-mountains`) — the plains (fertile soil, meadows, small mammals, camouflaged partridge) against the mountains (broadleaf forest, conifers, alpine shrubs, edelweiss, wild goats, eagles) (Bulgaria, Grade 4 — *Човекът и природата за 4. клас*, Lesson 51, printed p. 100)
52. **In defence of our nature — a country project** (`our-environment/in-defence-of-our-nature-a-country-project`) — the project: research a protected territory near home — its species, its rare inhabitants, and why it matters (Bulgaria, Grade 4 — *Човекът и природата за 4. клас*, Lesson 56, printed p. 109)
53. **Mangroves — four types, and why they matter** (`our-environment/mangroves-four-types-and-why-they-matter`) — the four types of mangroves (red, black, white, buttonwood), the characteristics of each, the organisms found in mangrove ecosystems, and the importance of mangroves to the islands — curriculum evidence only, acceptance 0 (Bahamas, Grade 4 — *Primary Science Curriculum Guidelines — Grades 3-4*, printed p. 115)

54. **Living underground — the soil as a home** (`our-environment/living-underground-the-soil-as-a-home`) — the underground living environment: the soil itself as a home for living things, taught as a living environment of its own, between the land-and-air and water environments in the living-environments module (Moldova, Grade 3 — *Științe: Manual pentru clasa a III-a*, Modulul 2, Lecția 4, printed p. 43)

55. **Natural hazards — monitoring, prediction and protection** (`our-environment/natural-hazards-monitoring-prediction-and-protection`) — using data to monitor and predict natural hazards and to protect communities — tsunami warning systems and similar (United States, Grade 8 — *8th Grade — for Utah SEEd Standards, Revised 2025*, 8.4.5, printed p. 187)
56. **Natural zones of the Earth** (`our-environment/natural-zones-of-the-earth`) — the Earth's living cover changes in ordered belts — natural zones, each with its own climate, soil, plants and animals (Armenia, Grade 6 — *Բնություն 6 (Nature 6)*, §40, printed p. 135)
57. **Ocean currents and upwelling** (`our-environment/ocean-currents-and-upwelling`) — the ocean environment around Samoa: the southeast trade winds driving currents, warm surface water over cold deep water with the thermocline between, and upwelling bringing deep water up (Samoa, Year 10 — *Science — Year 10, Book 3 (MESC Samoa)*, printed pp. 27–31)
58. **Coral reefs — what they give and why they are being lost** (`our-environment/coral-reefs-what-they-give-and-why-they-are-being-lost`) — what oceans, lagoons and reefs give the Samoan way of life, and the Global Coral Reef Monitoring Network 2000 report: 27% of the world's reefs effectively lost, the 1998 bleaching, sediment and nutrient pollution, over-exploitation, mining and reclamation — and Pacific reefs in reasonably good health under traditional management and marine protected areas, carried as a summary of the report (Samoa, Year 11 — *Science — Year 11, Book 1 (MESC Samoa)*, printed pp. 93–94, 98)
59. **Patterns in a community — zonation, layers and succession** (`our-environment/patterns-in-a-community-zonation-layers-and-succession`) — zonation (the bands around the Aleipata blowholes and up a volcanic island from coastal marsh to cloud forest), stratification (the forest's layers, each with its own light, wind and shelter) and succession (plants colonising the Saleaula lava fields until the first plants die out), investigated by rope transects and profile diagrams (Samoa, Year 11 — *Science — Year 11, Book 1 (MESC Samoa)*, printed pp. 99–105)
60. **Green cities — designing for a changing climate** (`our-environment/green-cities-designing-for-a-changing-climate`) — the unit project: building environmentally friendly cities, designed as one system answering heat, water, soil and living things together (Egypt, Primary 6 — *Primary 6 Science, Science Term 2 (Discovery Education Science Techbook, Egypt edition)*, printed p. 78)
61. **Dissolved gases in water and aquatic life** (`our-environment/dissolved-gases-in-water-and-aquatic-life`) — atmospheric gases dissolve in water, and that dissolved store is a condition of every aquatic habitat (Egypt, Secondary 10 — *Integrated Science, First Secondary Grade, First Semester*, Topic 1-7, printed p. 43)
62. **Water pressure and depth adaptation** (`our-environment/water-pressure-and-depth-adaptation`) — water pressure grows with depth, and an organism's body is fitted to the pressure of the depth it lives at (Egypt, Secondary 10 — *Integrated Science, First Secondary Grade, First Semester*, Topic 1-9, printed p. 56)
63. **Nanotechnology and the environment** (`our-environment/nanotechnology-and-the-environment`) — nanomaterials in energy efficiency and drug delivery, weighed with their environmental dimensions for the biosphere's sustainability (Egypt, Secondary 10 — *Integrated Science, First Secondary Grade, Second Semester*, Topic 3-5, printed p. 45)
