64. **The flooded forest and its problems** (`our-environment/the-flooded-forest-and-its-problems`) — Cambodia's flooded forest, living with the yearly flood on its waterway system, and the forest's environmental problems (Cambodia, Grade 7 — *វិទ្យាសាស្ត្រ ថ្នាក់ទី៧ (Science, Grade 7)*, Earth Ch. 3, Lesson 2, printed p. 294; built for the Cambodia placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/cambodia-placements.md)
65. **How people change the environment** (`our-environment/how-people-change-the-environment`) — human actions and their effect on the environment — the general lesson that opens its chapter, before environmental pollution in detail (Cambodia, Grade 9 — *វិទ្យាសាស្ត្រ ថ្នាក់ទី៩ (Science, Grade 9)*, Biology Ch. 5, Lesson 1, printed p. 244; built for the Cambodia placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/cambodia-placements.md)

66. **Exploring our local environment** (`our-environment/exploring-our-local-environment`) — studying a puddle habitat (temperature over forty minutes, surface area by chicken-wire grid) and the full habitat investigation: describe, measure, name three plants and two animals, draw a food chain (Seychelles, Grade 9 (P9) — *Seychelles Integrated Science — Conserving Our Environment*, Unit 13, Topic 2 + Project 1, printed pp. 5, 13; built for the Seychelles placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/seychelles-placements.md)
67. **Natural disaster prevention and preparedness** (`our-environment/natural-disaster-prevention-and-preparedness`) — the volcanic eruption process in detail, the prevention measures to take before eruptions and natural disasters, and an album of Ecuador's volcanoes (Ecuador, Grade 5 — *EC G5 Ciencias Naturales*, printed pp. 116–121; built for the Ecuador placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/ecuador-placements.md)
68. **Ecuador's natural regions and their life** (`our-environment/ecuador-natural-regions-and-their-life`) — Ecuador's natural regions located, with the flora and fauna most representative of each region (Ecuador, Grade 5 — *EC G5 Ciencias Naturales*, printed p. 112; built for the Ecuador placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/ecuador-placements.md)
69. **Natural disasters — and how people face them** (`our-environment/natural-disasters-and-how-people-face-them`) — disasters (الـكـوارث) as a world problem, placed in the Man-and-the-environment chapter among the problems every country shares, and how people face them together (Sudan, Grade 12 — *الجغرافيا والدراسات البيئية (Geography and Environmental Studies)*, Chapter 3, printed p. 183)
70. **Socionatural events — threats and risks** (`our-environment/socionatural-events-threats-and-risks`) — floods, slides and earthquakes read through threat, vulnerability and risk, with prevention in the community (Venezuela, Grade 6 — *Con Ciencia para el Porvenir (Colección Bicentenario)*, printed pp. 55–70; built for the Venezuela placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/venezuela-placements.md)
71. **Food security and trophic chains in Venezuela** (`our-environment/food-security-and-trophic-chains-in-venezuela`) — food security depending on trophic chains and on who controls production and distribution — Venezuela's own integrated way (Venezuela, Grade 7 — *Alimentando con Ciencias (Colección Bicentenario)*, printed pp. 61–67; built for the Venezuela placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/venezuela-placements.md)
72. **Measuring biodiversity — the Simpson index** (`our-environment/measuring-biodiversity-simpson-index`) — measuring a locality's biodiversity with an index, nearer 1 = more diverse (worked example: region A 0.78 vs region B 0.54) (Venezuela, Grade 7 — *Alimentando con Ciencias (Colección Bicentenario)*, printed pp. 77–81; built for the Venezuela placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/venezuela-placements.md)
73. **Our footprints in nature** (`our-environment/our-footprints-in-nature`) — the human impact each life leaves on nature, measured and then reduced (Venezuela, Grade 8 — *El Porvenir de la Vida (Colección Bicentenario)*, printed pp. 138–153; built for the Venezuela placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/venezuela-placements.md)

74. **Desertification — and fighting it** (`our-environment/desertification-and-fighting-it`) — desertification — and fighting it — how land turns to desert, and the green belt planted to hold the sand back (Mauritania, Grade 4 — *Sciences Naturelles, 4e année fondamentale*, printed p. 54, and Grade 6 — *Sciences Naturelles, 6e année fondamentale*, printed pp. 63–64; built for the Mauritania placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/mauritania-placements.md)
75. **Protected areas and wetlands** (`our-environment/protected-areas-and-wetlands`) — protected areas and wetlands — places set apart under protection guarding whole habitats, and wetlands as ecosystems in their own right (Mauritania, Grade 8 — *Sciences de la Vie et de la Terre, 2ème AS*, Unit V, printed pp. 110–122; built for the Mauritania placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/mauritania-placements.md)
76. **Primary and secondary productivity** (`our-environment/primary-and-secondary-productivity`) — primary and secondary productivity — the producers' making as the ecosystem's income, and the consumers' smaller second-hand making built from it (Mauritania, Grade 10 — *Sciences Naturelles, 4ème AS*, printed p. 113; built for the Mauritania placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/mauritania-placements.md)

77. **Coastal ecosystems — dunes, seagrass and coral banks** (`our-environment/coastal-ecosystems-dunes-seagrass-and-coral-banks`) — the coast walked shore to sea: dunes and their coastal vegetation, seagrass meadows — the ocean's prairies — and the Banco de la Plata, paradise of corals and humpback whales (Dominican Republic, Grade 5 — *Ciencias de la Naturaleza 5*, printed pp. 164–166; built for the Dominican Republic placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/dominican-republic-placements.md)

78. **Kinds of natural habitats** (`our-environment/kinds-of-natural-habitats`) — the kinds of natural habitats — land homes (forest, grassland, desert) and water homes (sea, river, lake, pond) — and what each home gives its living things (Kuwait, Grade 2, Term 1 — *Science, Grade 2, Term 1 (Student Book)*, Section 2 printed p. 16; built for the Kuwait placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/kuwait-placements.md)

79. **How ecosystems change** (`our-environment/how-ecosystems-change`) — ecosystems change slowly and suddenly, by nature and by people, and their living things fit in, move, or die out (Kuwait, Grade 4, Term 1 — *Science, Grade 4, Term 1 (Student Book)*, Section 2 printed p. 12; built for the Kuwait placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/kuwait-placements.md)

80. **Extinct and endangered living things** (`our-environment/extinct-and-endangered-living-things`) — kinds gone for ever against kinds still living but few and falling — and how endangered kinds are saved (Kuwait, Grade 4, Term 1 — *Science, Grade 4, Term 1 (Student Book)*, Section 2 printed p. 20; built for the Kuwait placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/kuwait-placements.md)

81. **Flood danger and protection** (`our-environment/flood-danger-and-protection`) — the dangers of flowing water (washing away houses, flooding roads), the impacts of floods and landslides, preventing floods and landslides where we live, and keeping safe when flooding happens (Laos, Grade 2 — *Science and Environment, Grade 2*, Ch. 25, printed pp. 103–104, and Grade 3 — *Science and Environment, Grade 3*, Ch. 1, printed pp. 5–8; built for the Laos placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/laos-placements.md)
82. **How land is used** (`our-environment/how-land-is-used`) — how land is used — residential, agricultural, commercial and natural land, as Zimbabwe's guides teach land use from ECD level up (Zimbabwe, ECD B, Grade 1, Grade 3, Grade 4, Grade 5, Grade 6, Grade 2 — *PlusOne Science and Technology* teacher's guides (ECD B printed p. 26; ECD B printed p. 27; Grade 1 printed p. 48; Grade 3 printed p. 45; Grade 4 printed p. 45; Grade 5 printed p. 44; Grade 6 printed p. 46; Grade 2 printed p. 54), guide evidence only, acceptance 0; built for the Zimbabwe placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/zimbabwe-placements.md)

83. **The ozone layer and its holes** (`our-environment/the-ozone-layer-and-its-holes`) — the ozone covering shields Earth's surface from harmful ultraviolet rays; freon (CFC) gases thin it over the North and South Poles (noticed in 1980); where it thins, skin diseases grow commoner and plants and animals die; people now make far fewer of these gases and the covering is slowly recovering (North Macedonia, Grade 6 — *Природни науки 6 (Natural Sciences 6)*, printed p. 189; built for the North Macedonia placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/north-macedonia-placements.md)
84. **Habitats where plants grow** (`our-environment/habitats-where-plants-grow`) — plant habitats — sunny places, shady moist places, water, both land and water, saline soil, and plants growing on other trees — as Bangladesh's Class 4 book sorts them (Bangladesh, Grade 4 — *Elementary Science Class 4*, printed p. 13; built for the Bangladesh placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/bangladesh-placements.md)
85. **Habitats where animals live** (`our-environment/habitats-where-animals-live`) — animal habitats — burrows and soil, bushes, trees and nests, water, and animals living both on land and in water — as Bangladesh's Class 4 book sorts them (Bangladesh, Grade 4 — *Elementary Science Class 4*, printed p. 15; built for the Bangladesh placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/bangladesh-placements.md)
86. **Climate change — mitigation and adaptation** (`our-environment/climate-change-mitigation-and-adaptation`) — the two strategies against climate change — mitigation (less fossil fuel, more solar and wind, planting trees, saving energy) and adaptation (shelters, coastal afforestation, salt-tolerant crops, stronger buildings) — with Bangladesh's own hazard-vulnerability map and disaster calendar (Bangladesh, Grade 5 — *Elementary Science Class 5*, printed p. 93; built for the Bangladesh placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/bangladesh-placements.md)
87. **Bangladesh's changing environment and biodiversity** (`our-environment/bangladeshs-changing-environment-and-biodiversity`) — the changing environment of Bangladesh and its impact on biodiversity — the country's own case study, closing Bangladesh's Class 6 Investigative Study (Bangladesh, Grade 6 — *Science Investigative Study Class 6*, printed p. 149; built for the Bangladesh placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/bangladesh-placements.md)
88. **Living things tell their own story** (`our-environment/living-things-tell-their-own-story`) — the living world retold as a drama whose characters are not human — 'Life All Over the World' in Bangladesh's Class 6 Exercise Book (Bangladesh, Grade 6 — *Science Exercise Book Class 6*, printed p. 131; built for the Bangladesh placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/bangladesh-placements.md)
89. **Biodiversity of Bangladesh** (`our-environment/biodiversity-of-bangladesh`) — the biodiversity of Bangladesh itself, surveyed as the home case — and the natural diversity of its different parts — in Bangladesh's Class 7 book (Bangladesh, Grade 7 — *Science Investigative Study Class 7*, printed p. 10; Grade 7 — *Science Investigative Study Class 7*, printed p. 93; built for the Bangladesh placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/bangladesh-placements.md)
90. **Introduced species and the environment** (`our-environment/introduced-species-and-the-environment`) — introduced (non-native) species in Fiji and how they changed the environment, and the difference between endemic and introduced (Fiji, Year 6 — *Year 6 Elementary Science*, printed pp. 34–41; built for the Fiji placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/fiji-placements.md)
91. **Ocean zones and the intertidal zone** (`our-environment/ocean-zones-and-the-intertidal-zone`) — the ocean's zones by depth, sunlight and temperature, and the intertidal zone where the ocean meets the shore, Fiji's way (Fiji, Year 10 — *Year 10 Basic Science — Chapter 1: Living Things and the Environment*, printed pp. 38–43; built for the Fiji placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/fiji-placements.md)

92. **Desert and polar habitats — hot dry and cold dry** (`our-environment/desert-and-polar-habitats-hot-dry-and-cold-dry`) — Unit 2, Ch. (Saudi Arabia, Grade 2, Part 1 — *Science, Grade 2, Part 1*, printed pp. 96–101; built for the Saudi Arabia placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/saudi-arabia-placements.md)
93. **Protecting animals from severe weather** (`our-environment/protecting-animals-from-severe-weather`) — science, technology and society: how do zoos prepare for marine hurricanes? (Saudi Arabia, First Intermediate, Part 2 — *Science, First Intermediate, Part 2*, printed p. 36; built for the Saudi Arabia placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/saudi-arabia-placements.md)
94. **Balanced ecosystems and what upsets them** (`our-environment/balanced-ecosystems-and-what-upsets-them`) — the balanced ecosystem: when all an ecosystem's factors work together it stays in balance, though it is always changing — a pond drying in drought drives out the frogs and insects that cannot move on, while land living things… (Saudi Arabia, First Intermediate, Part 2 — *Science, First Intermediate, Part 2*, printed p. 166; built for the Saudi Arabia placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/saudi-arabia-placements.md)
95. **Studying populations — marking and recapture** (`our-environment/studying-populations-marking-and-recapture`) — how populations are studied: to follow the monarch butterfly's migration, students mark each butterfly caught, release it, and count how many of the recaptured ones carry marks — the mark–release–recapture way of estimating a… (Saudi Arabia, First Intermediate, Part 2 — *Science, First Intermediate, Part 2*, printed p. 169; built for the Saudi Arabia placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/saudi-arabia-placements.md)
96. **Limiting factors — what stops a population growing** (`our-environment/limiting-factors-what-stops-a-population-growing`) — limiting factors: food, space and water cap a population's growth — in the pond, a dry season shrinks the water, mosquitoes multiply as frogs and their predators thin out, and more rain restores the balance (Saudi Arabia, First Intermediate, Part 2 — *Science, First Intermediate, Part 2*, printed p. 169; built for the Saudi Arabia placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/saudi-arabia-placements.md)
97. **Predation and competition — animals eating and rivalling** (`our-environment/predation-and-competition-animals-eating-and-rivalling`) — relations between living things in the community: predation, the commonest of them (the ladybird eating plant aphids, the sparrow hunting insects), and competition, when two kinds seek the same food, water or space (Saudi Arabia, First Intermediate, Part 2 — *Science, First Intermediate, Part 2*, printed p. 170; built for the Saudi Arabia placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/saudi-arabia-placements.md)
98. **Living together — mutualism, commensalism and parasitism** (`our-environment/living-together-mutualism-commensalism-and-parasitism`) — symbiosis in its three kinds: mutualism, where both gain (the bird that picks insects off the zebra's skin); commensalism, where one gains and the other is unaffected (birds nesting in trees); parasitism, where one gains at… (Saudi Arabia, First Intermediate, Part 2 — *Science, First Intermediate, Part 2*, printed p. 170; built for the Saudi Arabia placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/saudi-arabia-placements.md)
99. **Habitat and niche — where an animal lives and what it does** (`our-environment/habitat-and-niche-where-an-animal-lives-and-what-it-does`) — habitat and ecological niche: the niche is the living thing's role (the pond snail that eats algae, keeps the water clean and releases oxygen), the habitat is the place it lives (the catfish at the pond's bottom; the Arabian… (Saudi Arabia, First Intermediate, Part 2 — *Science, First Intermediate, Part 2*, printed p. 171; built for the Saudi Arabia placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/saudi-arabia-placements.md)

4. **Food — a balanced diet** (`keeping-healthy/food-a-balanced-diet`) — all nutrient groups in the right mix; the bread-and-tea case; the family meal-plan table (both Grade 4 regional guides as labelled evidence).
5. **Food — how food gets spoiled** (`keeping-healthy/food-how-food-gets-spoiled`) — the causes of fouling, the diseases that follow, and "Our friend and the doctor" (plot summary).

7. **Food — the six nutrients** (`keeping-healthy/food-the-six-nutrients`) — the six nutrients, their jobs and foods; water is about 67% of the body.
8. **Food — what food is** (`keeping-healthy/food-what-food-is`) — food is everything we eat and drink that carries useful things for the body and does no harm; yesterday's three meals recalled.
9. **Food — where our food comes from** (`keeping-healthy/food-where-our-food-comes-from`) — food is anything eaten or drunk that suits health; about 33 foods sorted into the four source groups.
10. **Healthy food and spoiled food** (`keeping-healthy/healthy-food-and-spoiled-food`) — telling healthy food from unhealthy and spoiled food — fresh, clean and not overloaded with sugar, salt and fat, against spoiled, unhygienic food
11. **HIV and AIDS: how it spreads and how it does not** (`keeping-healthy/hiv-and-aids`) — AIDS is caused by the HIV virus; it spreads through blood and sometimes from mother to child — and it does NOT spread by touching, handshakes, shar…
12. **Keeping our bodies clean** (`keeping-healthy/keeping-our-bodies-clean`) — keeping the whole body clean as a daily habit — washing, teeth, nails, hair and clothes — and knowing why it matters
13. **Keeping our home clean** (`keeping-healthy/keeping-our-home-clean`) — keeping home and toilet clean with household cleaning materials (teacher-guide evidence only, counted nowhere).
14. **Moving our bodies and sleep on time** (`keeping-healthy/moving-our-bodies-and-sleep-on-time`) — exercise and sport keep people active, strong and healthy; enough sleep, on time, keeps learning and work strong

16. **Signs, lights and crossing rules** (`keeping-healthy/signs-lights-and-crossing-rules`) — the traffic signs and lights and what each colour commands, with the crossing rules gathered in one table
17. **Traffic accidents and the rules of the road** (`keeping-healthy/traffic-accidents-and-the-rules-of-the-road`) — how children are hurt on roads, and the road rules that prevent it: keep left on the pedestrian way, cross only at the zebra, looking both ways
18. **Using medicines properly** (`keeping-healthy/using-medicines-properly`) — medicines help only when used properly — the Oromia Grade 4 guide's subtopic: the dose measured and directed by a health worker is the measure of p…
19. **What disease is, and how it spreads** (`keeping-healthy/what-disease-is-and-how-it-spreads`) — what disease is, how sickness travels — germs, flies and mosquitoes, dirt — and how cleanliness stops it
20. **Washing hands stops the spread** (`keeping-healthy/washing-hands-stops-the-spread`) — microbes reach the body on dirty hands and washing hands breaks that route; cleanliness (aseo) and good food together prevent disease, explained from the drawings as Peru's Grade 1 ficha closes (Peru, Grade 1 — *Cuadernillo de fichas — Ciencia y Tecnología, Primaria 1*, Ficha 4, *Las enfermedades y su cuidado*, printed p. 27)

21. **Staying safe online** (`keeping-healthy/staying-safe-online`) — my online safety (Çevrim İçi Güvenliğim): not sharing personal information, strong passwords kept secret, chatting only with people you know, and what counts as violating someone's personal space online (Turkey, Grade 2 — *Hayat Bilgisi 2. Sınıf, 1. Kitap (Life Studies, Grade 2, Book 1)*, printed p. 54)
22. **Health and wellbeing — what it means** (`keeping-healthy/health-and-wellbeing-what-it-means`) — Oraanga e Pitoenua: health and wellbeing — what it means, how opinions about it differ, and asking the community about it (Cook Islands, Grade 8 — *Ko Au e Toku Aorangi: Kai No te Oraanga Meitaki — Me, Myself, My Environment: Nutrition*, printed p. 10)
23. **My first 1000 days** (`keeping-healthy/my-first-1000-days`) — Toku Tai Tauatinianga Ra Mua: how the first 1000 days shape later health, told through Professor Barker's discovery — the Hertfordshire birth records, the surprise finding, and the questions scientists still work on (Cook Islands, Grade 8 — *Ko Au e Toku Aorangi: Kai No te Oraanga Meitaki — Me, Myself, My Environment: Nutrition*, printed p. 60)

24. **Environmental diseases and where they come from** (`keeping-healthy/environmental-diseases-and-where-they-come-from`) — diseases whose cause lies in the surroundings themselves — the air, the water, the substances and conditions people live among — sorted from infectious diseases by where they come from (Australia, Grade 9 — *Pearson Science 9*, 8.3, printed p. 328)
25. **Water safety and harbour safety equipment** (`keeping-healthy/water-safety-and-harbour-safety-equipment`) — getting to know safety equipment on piers and docks — Iceland's harbour-side safety way — teacher-guide evidence only, acceptance 0 (Iceland, Grade 3 — *Halló heimur 3*, Ch.5 aims, printed p. 1)
26. **Fireworks safety** (`keeping-healthy/fireworks-safety`) — learning how to handle fireworks — teacher-guide evidence only, acceptance 0 (Iceland, Grade 3 — *Halló heimur 3*, Ch.5 aims, printed p. 1)
27. **Diarrhoea and worm infestations** (`keeping-healthy/diarrhoea-and-worm-infestations`) — diarrhoea diseases: causes (contaminated water and food), using ORS/SSS at home with amounts by age — and worm infestations: threadworm and other worms, how they enter, signs (Uganda, Primary Four — *Home-Study Learning, Primary Four, Science*, printed pp. 23–27)
28. **Immunisation and immunity** (`keeping-healthy/immunisation-and-immunity`) — immunity and immunisation: how vaccines make the body resistant and cut child deaths; childhood immunisable diseases (measles — virus, sores, rash; vaccine by injection in the left upper arm — and the immunisation table); cholera prevention (boiled water, covering food); the child health card (Uganda, Primary Five — *Home-Study Learning, Primary Five, Science*, printed pp. 1–8)
29. **First aid at home** (`keeping-healthy/first-aid-at-home`) — the first-aid gestures: the first help given in the first minutes after a hurt — stop, look, and call for help first, then comfort, clean water for small cuts and burns, pressure on bleeding, and rest — teacher-guide evidence only, acceptance 0 (Gabon, Grade 5 — *Super Efficace Sciences expérimentales et technologie, 5e année — Guide pédagogique*, printed p. 28)
30. **Blood and blood donation** (`keeping-healthy/blood-and-blood-donation`) — blood is the body's carrier, carrying substances around the body, and donating part of it is an act that keeps other people alive — the importance of blood donations, Cuba's own way (Cuba, Grade 6 — *Ciencias Naturales, Sexto grado*, printed p. 142)
31. **Public health care in our country** (`keeping-healthy/public-health-care-in-our-country`) — in our country the people's health is cared for: health care as a shared public task for the whole people, closing the Grade 6 human-body unit (Cuba, Grade 6 — *Ciencias Naturales, Sexto grado*, printed p. 162)


32. **Dengue and malaria — mosquito diseases** (`keeping-healthy/dengue-and-malaria-mosquito-diseases`) — two diseases carried by mosquitoes, taught together and stopped by breaking the mosquito's road — clearing the standing water it breeds in and protecting people from bites (Cambodia, Grade 4 — *វិទ្យាសាស្ត្រ ថ្នាក់ទី៤ (Science, Grade 4)*, Ch. 2, Lesson 3, printed p. 54; built for the Cambodia placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/cambodia-placements.md)
33. **Drugs and their effects** (`keeping-healthy/drugs-and-their-effects`) — what drugs are, then their effects on the body and on life — a two-lesson chapter closing the Grade 8 Biology health run after food and diet (Cambodia, Grade 8 — *វិទ្យាសាស្ត្រ ថ្នាក់ទី៨ (Science, Grade 8)*, Biology Ch. 6, Lessons 1–2, printed pp. 238, 242; built for the Cambodia placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/cambodia-placements.md)
34. **Diseases that do not spread** (`keeping-healthy/diseases-that-do-not-spread`) — non-infectious diseases, taught straight after pathogens and infectious diseases so the difference between the two kinds is learned whole (Cambodia, Grade 9 — *វិទ្យាសាស្ត្រ ថ្នាក់ទី៩ (Science, Grade 9)*, Biology Ch. 4, Lesson 3, printed p. 236; built for the Cambodia placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/cambodia-placements.md)

35. **Testing foods for sugar, fat and protein** (`keeping-healthy/testing-foods-for-sugar-fat-and-protein`) — testing foods for sugar with Benedict's reagent, for fat or oil by the grease-spot method, and for protein with Millon's reagent (Seychelles, Grade 8 (P8) — *Seychelles Integrated Science — Food and Growth*, Unit 7, Topic 6, printed booklet pp. 15–18; built for the Seychelles placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/seychelles-placements.md)
44. **Keeping food safe — preserving food** (`keeping-healthy/keeping-food-safe-preserving-food`) — preserving food (a conservação dos alimentos): drying, salting, smoking, keeping cold and covered storage holding back spoiling, taught in Grade 2 and deepened in Grade 5 (Angola, Grade 2 — *Estudo do Meio, Manual da 2.ª Classe*, printed p. 52, and Grade 5 — *Ciências da Natureza, Manual da 5.ª Classe*, printed p. 91; built for the Angola placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/angola-placements.md)
45. **Sanitation — clean water and waste** (`keeping-healthy/sanitation-clean-water-and-waste`) — basic sanitation (saneamento básico): clean water drawn, carried and stored against dirt and germs, and waste — rubbish, dirty water, the latrine — taken safely away (Angola, Grade 4 — *Estudo do Meio, Manual da 4.ª Classe*, printed p. 65; built for the Angola placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/angola-placements.md)
46. **Sexually transmitted infections** (`keeping-healthy/sexually-transmitted-infections`) — sexually transmitted infections (as infecções sexualmente transmissíveis) taught whole: the notion, symptoms, prevention and treatment, just before the book's HIV/AIDS lesson (Angola, Grade 6 — *Ciências da Natureza, Manual da 6.ª Classe*, printed p. 104; built for the Angola placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/angola-placements.md)

36. **Keeping our senses safe** (`keeping-healthy/keeping-our-senses-safe`) — looking after your eyes and the other senses you learn through: light, rest, glare and early help when sight changes (Seychelles, Grade 9 (P9) — *Seychelles Integrated Science — Conserving Our Health*, Unit 12, Topic 'Looking after your eyes', PDF scan p. 68; built for the Seychelles placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/seychelles-placements.md)

37. **Parasites — inside and outside the body** (`keeping-healthy/parasites-inside-and-outside-the-body`) — a parasite lives on or in a host and feeds off it; internal parasites inside the body (in places like the gut, taking the body's food) and external parasites outside it (on skin, hair or clothes, biting and sometimes carrying disease) (Afghanistan, Grade 5 — *Science, Health and Environment, Grade 5*, Lessons 50–52, printed pp. 107, 109, 111; built for the Afghanistan placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/afghanistan-placements.md)
38. **Water hidden in food and products** (`keeping-healthy/water-hidden-in-food-and-products`) — 'water even in the soup': reading food labels to find water as an ingredient in products (Venezuela, Grade 1 — *Ciencias para Explorar, Crear e Inventar (Colección Bicentenario)*, printed p. 45; built for the Venezuela placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/venezuela-placements.md)

39. **Harmful eating habits** (`keeping-healthy/harmful-eating-habits`) — harmful eating habits — the eating patterns that work against the body, taught as their own lesson straight after the healthy eating habits (Mauritania, Grade 3 — *Sciences Naturelles, 3e année fondamentale*, printed p. 17; built for the Mauritania placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/mauritania-placements.md)

43. **Butane gas and its safe use** (`keeping-healthy/butane-gas-and-its-safe-use`) — butane gas and its safe use — butane colourless and easily liquefied, used in cookers and lighters, and gas preferred over charcoal to protect the environment (Mauritania, Grade 5 — *Sciences Naturelles, 5e année fondamentale*, printed p. 93; built for the Mauritania placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/mauritania-placements.md)

47. **Keeping the respiratory system healthy** (`keeping-healthy/keeping-the-respiratory-system-healthy`) — clean air and exercise for the breathing system, against its enemies: smoking, dust, fumes and chest infections (Kuwait, Grade 8, Term 1 — *Science, Grade 8, Term 1 (Student Book)*, printed p. 74; built for the Kuwait placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/kuwait-placements.md)

48. **Keeping the urinary system healthy** (`keeping-healthy/keeping-the-urinary-system-healthy`) — water through the day, not holding urine, and cleanliness — against infection and stones in the kidneys, bladder and passages (Kuwait, Grade 8, Term 2 — *Science, Grade 8, Term 2 (Student Book)*, printed p. 70; built for the Kuwait placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/kuwait-placements.md)

(Bangladesh, Grade 8 — *Science Exercise Book Class 8*, printed p. 149; built for the Bangladesh placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/bangladesh-placements.md)

75. **Viruses and the diseases they cause — influenza and COVID-19** (`keeping-healthy/viruses-and-the-diseases-they-cause-influenza-and-covid-19`) — viruses: they behave sometimes like living things and sometimes like non-living things, attack the living body and cause it disease — the novel coronavirus (COVID-19), colds and measles among them; influenza is a viral… (Saudi Arabia, Grade 4, Part 1 — *Science, Grade 4, Part 1*, printed p. 144; built for the Saudi Arabia placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/saudi-arabia-placements.md)
76. **Bacteria — friends and enemies: TB and cholera** (`keeping-healthy/bacteria-friends-and-enemies-tb-and-cholera`) — bacteria: found in the soil, the water of rivers and seas, the air and food, and inside the human body and on the skin — single-celled microscopic living things; among them useful bacteria (those in the digestive system, and… (Saudi Arabia, Grade 4, Part 1 — *Science, Grade 4, Part 1*, printed p. 144; built for the Saudi Arabia placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/saudi-arabia-placements.md)
77. **Knowing you are ill — symptoms and what to do** (`keeping-healthy/knowing-you-are-ill-symptoms-and-what-to-do`) — how do I know I am ill, and what must I do? (Saudi Arabia, Grade 4, Part 1 — *Science, Grade 4, Part 1*, printed p. 146; built for the Saudi Arabia placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/saudi-arabia-placements.md)
78. **Immunity and white blood cells — the body's defence** (`keeping-healthy/immunity-and-white-blood-cells-the-bodys-defence`) — how do we protect ourselves from disease? (Saudi Arabia, Grade 4, Part 1 — *Science, Grade 4, Part 1*, printed p. 154; built for the Saudi Arabia placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/saudi-arabia-placements.md)
79. **Vaccination — building artificial immunity** (`keeping-healthy/vaccination-building-artificial-immunity`) — vaccination: a way of forming artificial immunity by taking a dose of the vaccine, by injection or by mouth, to protect the body from diseases (pictured: the COVID-19 vaccine; the Vision 2030 Quality of Life programme box) (Saudi Arabia, Grade 4, Part 1 — *Science, Grade 4, Part 1*, printed p. 156; built for the Saudi Arabia placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/saudi-arabia-placements.md)
80. **Fast food and its harms** (`keeping-healthy/fast-food-and-its-harms`) — science reading: "Fast Food and Its Harms" — fast meals spread worldwide because they are quick to prepare, easy to reach and cheap, and are served in large amounts, especially to children; but they hold high levels of fats,… (Saudi Arabia, Grade 4, Part 1 — *Science, Grade 4, Part 1*, printed p. 180; built for the Saudi Arabia placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/saudi-arabia-placements.md)
81. **The nutrition pyramid and balanced meals** (`keeping-healthy/the-nutrition-pyramid-and-balanced-meals`) — eating a balanced, healthy diet judged against Vietnam's own Nutrition Pyramid for children aged 6–11 (National Institute of Nutrition, 2021) — daily amounts from cereals at the base to salt under 4 g at the top, 1,300–1,500 ml of water and 60 minutes of activity (Vietnam, Grade 4 — *Khoa học 4*, printed p. 88; Vietnam, Grade 4 — *Khoa học 4*, printed p. 90; Vietnam, Grade 4 — *Khoa học 4*, printed p. 91; built for the Vietnam placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/vietnam-placements.md)

82. **Diseases of too much and too little food** (`keeping-healthy/diseases-of-too-much-and-too-little-food`) — overweight and obesity (too much food, too little movement) set against stunting and iron-deficiency anaemia (too little food) — signs, causes and prevention, with Vietnam's own health facts (Vietnam, Grade 4 — *Khoa học 4*, printed p. 93; Vietnam, Grade 4 — *Khoa học 4*, printed p. 95; Vietnam, Grade 4 — *Khoa học 4*, printed p. 96; built for the Vietnam placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/vietnam-placements.md)

83. **Genetically modified foods** (`keeping-healthy/genetically-modified-foods`) — transgenic foods: genes added so plants resist pests, herbicides and drought; the common ones (maize, soya, cotton, potato, rice, tomato); Greenpeace health-risk claims and the environmental impact of unnatural cross-breeding (Nicaragua, Grade 6 — *Ciencias Naturales, 6to grado*, printed pp. 69–74; built for the Nicaragua placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/nicaragua-placements.md)

84. **Recommended Dietary Allowance and BMI** (`keeping-healthy/recommended-dietary-allowance-and-bmi`) — the Recommended Dietary Allowance table, and BMI calculated from mass and height, read against heart attack, high blood pressure, stroke and diabetes (Malaysia, Grade 10 (Form 4) — *Sains Rumah Tangga Tingkatan 4*, printed p. 184; built for the Malaysia placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/malaysia-placements.md)

85. **Fire extinguishers — types and how to use one** (`keeping-healthy/fire-extinguishers-types-and-how-to-use-one`) — extinguisher types matched to fire classes, the glass-fibre fire blanket, the PASS sequence, and the school extinguisher audit (Malaysia, Grade 10 (Form 4) — *Sains Tingkatan 4*, printed p. 10; built for the Malaysia placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/malaysia-placements.md)

86. **Ergonomics and safe lifting at work** (`keeping-healthy/ergonomics-and-safe-lifting-at-work`) — ergonomics — work fitted to the worker — and safe lifting: judging the load, posture, and load limits by body height (Malaysia, Grade 11 (Form 5) — *Sains Rumah Tangga Tingkatan 5*, printed p. 18; built for the Malaysia placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/malaysia-placements.md)

87. **Food contamination — types, causes and food poisoning** (`keeping-healthy/food-contamination-types-causes-and-food-poisoning`) — physical, chemical and biological contamination and cross-contamination, and the poisoning bacteria tabulated by symptoms, onset and food (Malaysia, Grade 11 (Form 5) — *Sains Rumah Tangga Tingkatan 5*, printed p. 59; built for the Malaysia placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/malaysia-placements.md)

88. **Food production technology — growing more food** (`keeping-healthy/food-production-technology-growing-more-food`) — quality breeds, farmer education, modern technology, biotechnology, drones and soil management raising the nation's food (Malaysia, Grade 11 (Form 5) — *Sains Tingkatan 5*, printed p. 66; built for the Malaysia placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/malaysia-placements.md)

89. **Free radicals and antioxidants** (`keeping-healthy/free-radicals-and-antioxidants`) — how free radicals form and harm the body, and antioxidants neutralising them — proved on browning apple pieces (Malaysia, Grade 10 (Form 4) — *Sains Tingkatan 4*, printed p. 212; built for the Malaysia placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/malaysia-placements.md)

90. **Traditional, modern and complementary medicine** (`keeping-healthy/traditional-modern-and-complementary-medicine`) — traditional, modern and complementary medicine compared, modern medicines classified, and health products chosen by their active ingredients (Malaysia, Grade 10 (Form 4) — *Sains Tingkatan 4*, printed p. 204; built for the Malaysia placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/malaysia-placements.md)

91. **Cooking methods — moist heat, dry heat and microwave** (`keeping-healthy/cooking-methods-moist-heat-dry-heat-and-microwave`) — blanching, poaching, boiling, simmering, stewing, steaming, double-boiling, braising and pot roasting; baking, grilling and the four fryings; and microwaves — 50 mm deep, never metal or foil (Malaysia, Grade 11 (Form 5) — *Sains Rumah Tangga Tingkatan 5*, printed p. 110; built for the Malaysia placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/malaysia-placements.md)

92. **Cleaning tools and what each one does** (`keeping-healthy/cleaning-tools-and-what-each-one-does`) — the cleaning tools and what each one does: the body's own items — toothbrush, soap, towel, comb, handkerchief, personal items never to be shared — and the surroundings' tools: broom, mop, rake, slasher, wheelbarrow and the rest, matched to the place each cleans (South Sudan, Primary 2 — *Primary Science 2, Pupil's Book*, 1.1 the cleaning items themselves: toothbrush and toothpaste, soap, towel, comb and handkerchief, and which are personal items never to be shared, SS P2 SB, printed p. 1; built for the South Sudan placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/south-sudan-placements.md)

93. **Stress, depression and mental health** (`keeping-healthy/stress-depression-and-mental-health`) — stress and depression taught as a named health topic in the Primary 7 health unit — the health of the mind named beside the health of the body (South Sudan, Primary 7 — *South Sudan Primary Science, Pupil's Book 7*, 2.5 Stress and depression, SS P7 SB, printed p. 60; built for the South Sudan placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/south-sudan-placements.md)

94. **Home nursing — caring for the sick at home** (`keeping-healthy/home-nursing-caring-for-the-sick-at-home`) — home nursing taught as its own topic: caring for a sick person at home, as a skill of the home, in the Primary 7 health unit (South Sudan, Primary 7 — *South Sudan Primary Science, Pupil's Book 7*, 2.6 Home nursing, SS P7 SB, printed p. 63; built for the South Sudan placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/south-sudan-placements.md)

## Unit 15 — Science Skills

1. **Debating change: new foods or old ways?** (`science-skills/debating-change-new-foods-or-old-ways`) — the book prints a debate — 'The New One and the Old One' — over whether food culture must change: new productive crops against slow-changing habits…
2. **Experiment 1: melting ice and boiling water** (`science-skills/experiment-1-melting-ice-and-boiling-water`) — Experiment 1 proves a change of state: ice on a big spoon over a candle flame melts at 0 °C and water boils at 100 °C at sea level (thermometer tak…
3. **Living and non-living things** (`science-skills/living-and-non-living-things`) — living things have the characteristics of life; we sort the things around us into living and non-living — goat, tree, child against stone, river, c…
4. **Making a wind vane and rain gauge** (`science-skills/making-a-wind-vane-and-rain-gauge`) — the project: build a wind vane and a rain gauge with your teacher, record the wind's direction daily at break time, and report weekly to the class
5. **Measuring: making and using a balance** (`science-skills/making-and-using-a-balance`) — a working balance can be built from two shoe-polish tins, string and wood — and weighing a water-filled bottle against an air-filled one makes mass…
6. **Measuring: traditional and modern measures** (`science-skills/measuring-traditional-and-modern-measures`) — people measured long before rulers existed — with arm-measures, footsteps, shadows and the balance — and modern instruments replaced them because a…
7. **Measuring: temperature, thermometers and the Celsius scale** (`science-skills/temperature-thermometers-and-the-celsius-scale`) — temperature is the measure of a body's hotness or coldness and it decides the body's state; it is measured with a thermometer; the world unit is th…
8. **Measuring: units of mass, length and time** (`science-skills/units-of-mass-length-and-time`) — the standard units join by fixed relations — 1 kg = 1000 g; 1 m = 100 cm = 1000 mm; 1 km = 1000 m; 1 day = 24 hours, 1 hour = 60 minutes, 1 minute …
9. **Measuring: volume and displacement** (`science-skills/volume-and-displacement`) — volume (ይዘት/ምድገት) — how much space a body takes — is measured by displacement: lower the body into water and read how far the level rises in a grad…
10. **What science is — evidence, inference and tentative knowledge** (`science-skills/what-science-is`) — science is knowledge built on evidence: we observe, we infer, we hold what we know as tentative — ready to change when better evidence comes (Grade 7 General Science)
11. **The branches of science** (`science-skills/the-branches-of-science`) — science splits into branches by what it studies: life, physical and earth science — and above them, natural science studies nature while social science studies people (Grade 7 General Science)
12. **Two kinds of knowledge — conventional science and indigenous knowledge** (`science-skills/two-kinds-of-knowledge`) — conventional knowledge — tentative, evidence-tested, written down — and indigenous knowledge — built by experience over generations and woven into a culture (Grade 7 General Science)
13. **Science and technology — how they differ and feed each other** (`science-skills/science-and-technology`) — science explores and discovers; technology applies knowledge and invents — each feeds the other, and technology can help or harm (Grade 7 General Science)
14. **Doing science honestly — ethics and Ethiopian scientists** (`science-skills/doing-science-honestly`) — science has an ethics — honesty in reporting, care in analysis, credit where due — and Ethiopia's own scientists show that ethics at work (Grade 7 General Science)
15. **Measurement — scalars, vectors and prefixes** (`science-skills/scalars-vectors-and-prefixes`) — some quantities need only a size (scalars), others need size and direction (vectors) — and SI prefixes shrink or stretch units by powers of ten, from femto to peta (Grade 7 General Science)
16. **Measuring time — from shadows to clocks** (`science-skills/measuring-time-from-shadows-to-clocks`) — time was first read from the sun — shadows, the sun's position, birdsong at dawn — and clocks made it exact; building a sundial joins the two (Grade 7 General Science)
17. **Measurement — accuracy and precision** (`science-skills/accuracy-and-precision`) — accuracy is closeness to the true value; precision is closeness of repeated readings to each other — and readings can be precise yet inaccurate (Grade 7 General Science)
18. **Laboratory safety — the ten rules** (`science-skills/laboratory-safety-the-ten-rules`) — laboratories hold hazardous, poisonous, flammable and explosive materials, and most accidents come from carelessness, impatience and unauthorised experiments — the ten rules are the guard (Grade 7 General Science)
19. **Laboratory safety — hazard signs and protective equipment** (`science-skills/hazard-signs-and-protective-equipment`) — ten hazard signs warn by sight — corrosive, flammable, toxic and the rest — and protective equipment reduces harm without removing the hazard (Grade 7 General Science)
20. **Laboratory equipment — names, uses, and making your own** (`science-skills/laboratory-equipment-names-uses-and-making-your-own`) — thirteen common tools, each with its job — and when the school has no kit, the same tools are made from local materials (Grade 7 General Science)
21. **Writing a laboratory report** (`science-skills/writing-a-laboratory-report`) — an experiment is written up in seven parts — Title, Aim, Theory, Materials, Data/Observation, Result and Discussion, Conclusion — teacher-guide evidence only, acceptance 0 (Grade 7 General Science)
22. **The scientific method — observe, hypothesise, test, conclude** (`science-skills/the-scientific-method-observe-hypothesise-test-conclude`) — an investigation runs in a working sequence — observe, ask, hypothesise, test, analyse and conclude — and its results can harden into a law or support a theory (Grade 8 General Science)
23. **Semi-guided investigations — your question, your method** (`science-skills/semi-guided-investigations-your-question-your-method`) — in a semi-guided investigation the question is given but the method is yours — and the mark of real inquiry is revising a hypothesis when the evidence refuses it (Grade 8 General Science)
24. **Fair tests — variables and control groups** (`science-skills/fair-tests-variables-and-control-groups`) — a test is fair only when one thing changes, one thing is measured, and everything else is held still — with a control group that gets no treatment as the comparison baseline (Grade 8 General Science)
25. **Measurement — quantities, units, prefixes, accuracy and precision (Grade 8)** (`science-skills/measurement-quantities-units-prefixes-and-accuracy-grade-8`) — measurement rests on seven fundamental quantities, prefixes stretch and shrink their units by tens — and every set of readings must pass two different judgements: accuracy against the true value, precision against itself — teacher-guide evidence only, acceptance 0 (Grade 8 General Science)
26. **Measuring time the old way — sundial, sand-glass, water clock** (`science-skills/measuring-time-the-old-way-sundial-sand-glass-water-clock`) — before clocks, time was read from the sun, from falling sand and from flowing water — each old clock works, and each depends on something that shifts, which is exactly its reliability problem — teacher-guide evidence only, acceptance 0 (Grade 8 General Science)
27. **Skills of practical study — observe, predict, hypothesise, record, conclude** (`science-skills/skills-of-practical-study-observe-predict-hypothesise-record-conclude`) — practical science as a taught skill sequence — observe, predict, frame a testable hypothesis, design a fair test, record honestly in prepared tables with repeats, analyse, and conclude only what the evidence supports (Kuwait, Grade 11 — *Physics, Grade 11, Term 1 — applications workbook*, front matter, printed p. 8)
28. **Reading and drawing data graphs** (`science-skills/reading-and-drawing-data-graphs`) — Processing Data and Present Your Findings: sorting data, looking for patterns, and presenting results as graphs and a poster that tells the story (Cook Islands, Grade 8 — *Ko Au e Toku Aorangi: Kai No te Oraanga Meitaki — Me, Myself, My Environment: Nutrition*, printed p. 22)
29. **NCSE assessment — how final marks are built** (`science-skills/ncse-assessment-how-final-marks-are-built`) — Trinidad and Tobago's own assessment design: Form 1 coursework counts 15%, Form 2 coursework 30%, Form 3 coursework 15%, plus examinations toward NCSE certification — curriculum-guide evidence only, acceptance 0 (Trinidad and Tobago, Forms 1–3 — *Secondary School Curriculum — Integrated Science, Forms One–Three*, Computing Final Marks table, printed p. 31)
30. **Science words — glossary of action verbs** (`science-skills/science-words-glossary-of-action-verbs`) — the closing glossary that defines the curriculum's action verbs (annotate, classify, compare, define, demonstrate, describe, observe) so learners know what each instruction word asks — curriculum-guide evidence only, acceptance 0 (Trinidad and Tobago, Forms 1–3 — *Secondary School Curriculum — Integrated Science, Forms One–Three*, closing glossary, printed p. 109)
31. **Indigenous technologies — living technologies** (`science-skills/indigenous-technologies-living-technologies`) — fibre technology, food preparation and preservation, textiles and woodwork as living technologies — a living science of land, resources and culture, still practised and handed down — teacher-guide evidence only, acceptance 0 (Canada, Grades 10–12 — *Secondary Science First Peoples: Teacher Resource Guide*, Unit 10 Living Technologies, printed p. 251)
32. **Science and industry — technology in daily life** (`science-skills/science-and-industry-technology-in-daily-life`) — technological products and their uses, and industry as science organised to make things and keep them — foods and how they are preserved among its indicators — curriculum evidence only, acceptance 0 (Ghana, Basic 2 — *Science Curriculum for Primary Schools (Basic 1–3)*, printed p. 53; Basic 5 — *Science Curriculum for Primary Schools (Basic 4–6)*, printed p. 31; Basic 7 — *Science Curriculum for Junior High Schools*, printed p. 47)
33. **Drawing biological specimens** (`science-skills/drawing-biological-specimens`) — how to draw a specimen: a drawing is not a diagram — true proportion, magnification stated, and ruled label lines — curriculum evidence only, acceptance 0 (Barbados, Appendix B — *Primary Science Syllabus, Class One–Four*, printed p. 119)
34. **Fieldwork — observing a community in nature** (`science-skills/fieldwork-observing-a-community-in-nature`) — the school excursion as a prepared study: choose a community, frame questions, carry notebook and magnifier, record the living things and the traces of human activity (Bulgaria, Grade 4 — *Човекът и природата за 4. клас*, Lesson 54, printed p. 106)

35. **Dealing with learners' misconceptions in science** (`science-skills/dealing-with-learners-misconceptions-in-science`) — pupils arrive with early and mistaken conceptions; the teacher surfaces the wrong first idea and works on it instead of just correcting it — teacher-guide evidence only, acceptance 0 (Greece, Grade 5 — *«Φυσικά» Ε΄ Δημοτικού teacher guide*, Part I, printed p. 29)

36. **Explorers and inventors — how people discover and create** (`science-skills/explorers-and-inventors-how-people-discover-and-create`) — Man — explorer and inventor: expeditions, explorers, inventors and scientists, from the named explorers to Edison and Liv Arnesen, and modern technologies (Moldova, Grade 4 — *Științe: Manual pentru clasa a IV-a*, Unit 1, Lesson 4, printed p. 19)

37. **Engineering design process** (`science-skills/engineering-design-process`) — the engineering design process — define a problem by its criteria and constraints, generate ideas, sketch and model, then test and modify each version until it is optimised (United States, Kindergarten and Grades 1–5 and 7 — *Utah SEEd Standards* books, printed pp. 10, 65 (KG); 134 (G1); 35 (G2); 85 (G3); 98 (G4); 44 (G5); 67 (G7))
38. **Everyday technology — design and first aid** (`science-skills/everyday-technology-design-and-first-aid`) — technology in everyday life as one design-and-make strand: paper folding/cutting/pasting, paper bags and cards, clay models of solids, hammer/wheels/gears from clay, first-aid box contents, digital thermometer and blood-pressure checks, phones/tablets as tools (Pakistan (Punjab), Grade 4 — *General Science 4*, Ch. 10, printed p. 112); designing a solar oven (Grade 6 — *General Science 6*, printed p. 126); drip and sprinkler irrigation compared, with the build-a-sprinkler activity (Grade 7 — *General Science 7*, §11.1, printed p. 131); making bioplastic from milk and vinegar and assembling a concave-mirror solar cooker (Grade 8 — *General Science 8*, printed p. 137)
39. **Order and chaos — entropy and self-organisation** (`science-skills/order-and-chaos-entropy-and-self-organisation`) — reversible against irreversible processes; entropy, the measure of disorder; and self-organisation — order building itself in open systems with energy flowing through (Armenia, Grade 10 — *Բնագիտություն 10 (Natural Science 10)*, Section 7, printed pp. 134, 141)
40. **Modelling in science** (`science-skills/modelling-in-science`) — a model keeps the likeness of the real thing in the features that matter, and modelling is one of science's methods of cognition (Armenia, Grade 10 — *Բնագիտություն 10 (Natural Science 10)*, §1.6, printed p. 24)
41. **Engineering design — bridges and loads** (`science-skills/engineering-design-bridges-and-loads`) — the interdisciplinary project: design a bridge to get to the other side, then build it and test it under load, step by step (Egypt, Primary 4 — *Primary 4 Science, Science Term 1 (Discovery Education Science Techbook, Egypt edition)*, printed p. 56)
42. **Robots and artificial intelligence — an introduction** (`science-skills/robots-and-artificial-intelligence-introduction`) — a project story: children design a homework robot, then debate what computers and AI can and cannot do (Egypt, Primary 6 — *Primary 6 Science, Science Term 1 (Discovery Education Science Techbook, Egypt edition)*, printed p. 116)


43. **Explaining and arguing in science** (`science-skills/explaining-and-arguing-in-science`) — scientific explanation skills: agree/disagree circles, discussion formats, odd-one-out, thought experiments — teacher-guide evidence only, acceptance 0 (Cambodia — *Student-Centred Science Teaching, Part 1*, Ch. 2, printed pp. 34–56; built for the Cambodia placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/cambodia-placements.md)
44. **Learning with analogies and models** (`science-skills/learning-with-analogies-and-models`) — teaching science with analogies and models: Focus-Action-Reflection, Synectics, role play, and learner-made analogies and models — teacher-guide evidence only, acceptance 0 (Cambodia — *Student-Centred Science Teaching, Part 3*, Ch. 5, printed pp. 3–20; built for the Cambodia placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/cambodia-placements.md)
45. **Science learning games** (`science-skills/science-learning-games`) — learning games for science: memory game, Jeopardy, last-winner, loop and domino games — a loop that will not close names the missing link — teacher-guide evidence only, acceptance 0 (Cambodia — *Student-Centred Science Teaching, Part 3*, Ch. 6, printed pp. 67–81; built for the Cambodia placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/cambodia-placements.md)

46. **Applied measurement problems in the school garden** (`science-skills/applied-measurement-problems-in-the-school-garden`) — solving measurement problems in the school agriculture project: watering-can volume, weighing a fruit without picking it, watering seed beds (Seychelles, Grade 7 (P7) — *Seychelles Integrated Science — Techniques and Measurements*, Unit 1, Project 1, printed booklet p. 8; built for the Seychelles placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/seychelles-placements.md)

47. **Using the gas burner safely** (`science-skills/using-the-gas-burner-safely`) — lighting a Bunsen burner step by step, opening the air hole, making a flame about 5 cm high, heating a test tube with a holder and never pointing it at anyone (Seychelles, Grade 7 (P7) — *Seychelles Integrated Science — Techniques and Measurements*, Unit 1, Topic 8, printed booklet p. 11; built for the Seychelles placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/seychelles-placements.md)

48. **Using a simple identification key** (`science-skills/using-a-simple-identification-key`) — using a simple three-stage key to identify an organism, and building a key from yes/no questions (Seychelles, Grade 7 (P7) — *Seychelles Integrated Science — Different Living Things*, Unit 5, Summary + Project, printed booklet pp. 9–11; built for the Seychelles placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/seychelles-placements.md)
49. **Scientific theories and laws — how explanations differ from descriptions** (`science-skills/scientific-theories-and-laws-how-explanations-differ-from-descriptions`) — a scientific theory is a tried explanation of a pattern noticed again and again in the natural world, not a mere guess, and open to change; scientific laws set against theories — explanation against description — in the unit's own assessment (Sudan, Grade 8 — *العلوم الطبيعية (Natural Sciences), Grade 8*, Unit 1, printed p. 10)
50. **Systems and subsystems in science** (`science-skills/systems-and-subsystems-in-science`) — a system as a set of structures, cycles and processes bound together and acting on each other (the human body, the planets round the Sun, the energy carried in a lightning bolt); the parts of a system interact, and systems are built of smaller systems — you are part of your school; the body is a system inside a larger one (Sudan, Grade 8 — *العلوم الطبيعية (Natural Sciences), Grade 8*, Unit 1, printed pp. 11–12)
51. **Scientific models — what they are and how they change** (`science-skills/scientific-models-what-they-are-and-how-they-change`) — what a scientific model is — a representation of a thing or event that helps us understand the world, in three kinds (physical, computer, mental); making models more accurate with more information (the sketch artist and the witness); using models to communicate ideas (maps, drawings, recipes, the globe), to test predictions (the closed box, wind tunnels) and to save time, money and lives (crash-test dummies, NASA's astronaut-training aircraft); and the limits of models — they change when new information arrives, as the solar-system model changed from Earth-centred to Sun-centred (Sudan, Grade 9 — *العلوم الطبيعية (Natural Sciences), Grade 9*, Unit 1, printed pp. 2–7)
52. **Venezuelan scientists — love of nature** (`science-skills/venezuelan-scientists-love-of-nature`) — Francisco Tamayo, the Venezuelan botanist who explored and classified the country's vegetation and sought medicines from plants (Venezuela, Grade 1 — *Ciencias para Explorar, Crear e Inventar (Colección Bicentenario)*, printed pp. 106–108; built for the Venezuela placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/venezuela-placements.md)
53. **Venezuelan popular inventors** (`science-skills/venezuelan-popular-inventors`) — the popular inventor of the Grade 2 book's complementary readings, and community technology (Venezuela, Grade 2 — *Comunidad con Ciencia (Colección Bicentenario)*, printed pp. 147–156; built for the Venezuela placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/venezuela-placements.md)

54. **DNA as evidence — forensic identification** (`science-skills/dna-as-evidence-forensic-identification`) — DNA's pattern differs from person to person, so a trace's pattern can be matched to identify whose it is; the book's closing project reading, in which a DNA match identifies a murderer, carried as a summary only (Dominican Republic, Grade 6 — *Ciencias de la Naturaleza 6*, printed p. 158; built for the Dominican Republic placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/dominican-republic-placements.md)

55. **Standards and quality marks** (`science-skills/standards-and-quality-marks`) — criteria and standards a product must meet, and the marks that certify it — Sri Lanka's own SLS mark and the international ISO marks — teacher-guide evidence only, acceptance 0 (Sri Lanka, Grade 9 — *Science, Grade 9, Teacher's Instructional Manual (Implemented in 2010)*, competency 4.7; built for the Sri Lanka placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/sri-lanka-placements.md)
56. **Everyday technology around us** (`science-skills/everyday-technology-around-us`) — everyday technology around us — the technology in daily life and what it is used for, as Zimbabwe's ECD and Grade 2 guides teach it (Zimbabwe, ECD A and ECD B and Grade 2 — *PlusOne Science and Technology* teacher's guides (ECD A printed p. 45; ECD B printed p. 45; Grade 2 printed p. 111), guide evidence only, acceptance 0; built for the Zimbabwe placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/zimbabwe-placements.md)
57. **Science and technology careers** (`science-skills/science-and-technology-careers`) — science and technology careers — the jobs science and technology open, as Zimbabwe's guides teach career opportunities at every level (Zimbabwe, ECD B, Grade 1, Grade 3, Grade 4, Grade 5, Grade 6, Grade 2 — *PlusOne Science and Technology* teacher's guides (ECD B printed p. 34; Grade 1 printed p. 62; Grade 3 printed p. 65; Grade 4 printed p. 64; Grade 5 printed p. 60; Grade 6 printed p. 67; Grade 2 printed p. 75), guide evidence only, acceptance 0; built for the Zimbabwe placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/zimbabwe-placements.md)
58. **Research and innovation** (`science-skills/research-and-innovation`) — research and innovation — finding out by research and making the new by innovation, as Zimbabwe's Grade 5 guide teaches the unit (Zimbabwe, Grade 5 — *PlusOne Science and Technology* teacher's guides (Grade 5 printed p. 85), guide evidence only, acceptance 0; built for the Zimbabwe placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/zimbabwe-placements.md)
59. **Dimensions of quantities** (`science-skills/dimensions-of-quantities`) — the dimension of a quantity worked out from its unit; derived quantities carry dimensions built from the base ones (Indonesia, Grade 10 — *Ilmu Pengetahuan Alam untuk SMA Kelas X*, Bab 1, printed p. 7; built for the Indonesia placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/indonesia-placements.md)
60. **Information and communication technology — the basics** (`science-skills/information-and-communication-technology-basics`) — information and communication technology (ICT) — the tools to create, collect, analyse and exchange information — split into communication technology and recording technology, as Bangladesh's Classes 3, 4 and 5 teach it (Bangladesh, Grade 3 — *Elementary Science Class 3*, printed p. 128; Grade 4 — *Elementary Science Class 4*, printed p. 88; Grade 5 — *Elementary Science Class 5*, printed p. 68; built for the Bangladesh placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/bangladesh-placements.md)
61. **Instructions and code — how machines work** (`science-skills/instructions-and-code-how-machines-work`) — humans and computers both follow instructions, and a machine's instructions are its code or commands — built, in Class 4, by joining blocks in logical order (Bangladesh, Grade 3 — *Elementary Science Class 3*, printed p. 132; Grade 4 — *Elementary Science Class 4*, printed p. 93; built for the Bangladesh placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/bangladesh-placements.md)
62. **How communication technology grew** (`science-skills/how-communication-technology-grew`) — the long road of communication — smoke signals and drums to the computer and internet — told together with the long road of recording, from cave paintings to the memory card, as Bangladesh's Class 4 book tells it (Bangladesh, Grade 4 — *Elementary Science Class 4*, printed p. 90; built for the Bangladesh placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/bangladesh-placements.md)
63. **Collecting, storing and sharing information** (`science-skills/collecting-storing-and-sharing-information`) — using information wisely — decide what you need, find sources, collect and record it, organise and share it clearly — and, in Class 5, collecting through the internet with keywords and storing and exchanging what you find (Bangladesh, Grade 4 — *Elementary Science Class 4*, printed p. 91; Grade 5 — *Elementary Science Class 5*, printed p. 70; built for the Bangladesh placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/bangladesh-placements.md)
64. **Scratch — first steps** (`science-skills/scratch-first-steps`) — Scratch — sprites and backdrops, the editor's code area, script area and stage, and a first program that moves a sprite when the green flag is clicked — taken further in Class 5 with the greater-number finder (Bangladesh, Grade 4 — *Elementary Science Class 4*, printed p. 94; Grade 5 — *Elementary Science Class 5*, printed p. 72; built for the Bangladesh placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/bangladesh-placements.md)
65. **Harms of technology** (`science-skills/harms-of-technology`) — the negative side of technology — coal power and farm chemicals polluting air, soil and water, weapon technology, and TV and computer addiction stealing exercise, play and free thinking — as Bangladesh's Class 5 book names it (Bangladesh, Grade 5 — *Elementary Science Class 5*, printed p. 66; built for the Bangladesh placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/bangladesh-placements.md)
66. **Exercise-book sessions — learning by doing** (`science-skills/exercise-book-session-learning-by-doing`) — the Exercise Book's own form: every unit is a set of sessions done, written and checked, not read — Bangladesh's own way of teaching science at this level (Bangladesh, Grade 6 — *Science Exercise Book Class 6*, printed p. 1; built for the Bangladesh placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/bangladesh-placements.md)

68. **Interpreting data — finding what a table tells us** (`science-skills/interpreting-data-finding-what-a-table-tells-us`) — skills focus "Interpreting Data": data gathered in an experiment is easier to read in a table or a drawing; the worked example is a table of the average air temperatures of Riyadh city for every month of the year (Saudi Arabia, Grade 3, Part 2 — *Science, Grade 3, Part 2*, printed p. 20; built for the Saudi Arabia placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/saudi-arabia-placements.md)
69. **Measuring length, volume and mass** (`science-skills/measuring-length-volume-and-mass`) — "How is matter measured?": length with the ruler and the metre tape (the metre is 100 centimetres); the volume of liquids with graduated vessels in litres (the litre is 1000 millilitres); and the volume of a solid by the… (Saudi Arabia, Grade 3, Part 2 — *Science, Grade 3, Part 2*, printed p. 70; built for the Saudi Arabia placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/saudi-arabia-placements.md)
70. **Descriptive writing — describing a material** (`science-skills/descriptive-writing-describing-a-material`) — scientific writing, "I describe matter": describe a slice of pizza to someone who has never seen it, through measurable observations; good descriptive writing uses words that tell the thing's appearance, sounds and smell,… (Saudi Arabia, Grade 3, Part 2 — *Science, Grade 3, Part 2*, printed pp. 84–85; built for the Saudi Arabia placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/saudi-arabia-placements.md)

340. **Cloning and plant tissue culture** (`biology/cloning-and-plant-tissue-culture`) — cloning copies one donor's heredity without fertilisation; Morocco teaches it by comparing two techniques — one based on an egg, the other on a specialised somatic cell from the mammary glands — and by comparing cloning with natural reproduction to deduce the principle (Morocco, Grade 8 — *Guide Almoufid SVT*, teacher's guide — evidence only, printed pp. 85–87)
341. **Brazilian biomes and the Mata Atlântica** (`biology/brazilian-biomes-and-the-mata-atlantica`) — Brazil's own biomes taught through the Mata Atlântica, the Atlantic Forest of Brazil's coast, whose disappearance Brazil's Paraná public textbook gives a whole chapter framed as a cry for help — "socorro!!! Cadê você???" (Help!!! Where are you???) (Brazil, Grade 10 — *Biologia — Livro Didático Público, Ensino Médio*, student textbook, Ch. 12, printed p. 193)
342. **Masquerade and mimicry — the hoverfly that imitates a bee** (`biology/masquerade-and-mimicry`) — a harmless animal survives by imitating a defended one; Costa Rica's Grade 11 anthology opens its whole ecology course with the case of Eristalis tenax, the hoverfly that imitates a bee — "Un caso muy especial de mimetismo" (a very special case of mimicry) (Costa Rica, Grade 11 — *Biología Undécimo — Antologías CONED*, student anthology, Unit 1 Lectura Reflexiva, printed p. 14)
343. **Coevolution — arms races and partnerships** (`biology/coevolution-arms-races-and-partnerships`) — species that live in close relationship shape each other's evolution, running as an arms race where the relationship harms and as a partnership where both sides come to fit each other; Costa Rica teaches Coevolución inside its relationships unit, between the symbiotic relationships and the parasitology strand (Costa Rica, Grade 11 — *Biología Undécimo — Antologías CONED*, student anthology, Unit 1, printed p. 28)
344. **Thermodynamics and entropy in ecosystems** (`biology/thermodynamics-and-entropy-in-ecosystems`) — ecosystems taught through the laws of thermodynamics and entropy outright: energy is transformed, never created or destroyed, and at every transfer some useful energy is lost as heat while entropy rises — so energy flows one way through the trophic levels while materials cycle (Costa Rica, Grade 11 — *Biología Undécimo — Antologías CONED*, student anthology, Unit 2, printed pp. 66–67)
345. **Mental health disorders** (`biology/mental-health-disorders`) — the human psyche develops, and its disorders are taught by name in Azerbaijan's Grade 11 biology: anxiety disorders (Тревожные расстройства), depression (Депрессии) and psychoses (Психозы), inside the book's human part between embryonic development and healthy family relations (Azerbaijan, Grade 11 — *Биология 11 (Biology 11)*, Russian-sector student textbook, Part VI §§38–41, printed pp. 144–155)
346. **Cancer and tumours** (`biology/cancer-and-tumours`) — cell division out of control: uncontrolled division of cells piles up into tumours (Опухоли), and the book closes its controlled-and-uncontrolled-division part with a section titled simply "Cancer" (Рак) (Azerbaijan, Grade 11 — *Биология 11 (Biology 11)*, Russian-sector student textbook, Part VII §§46–47, printed pp. 175–178)
347. **Eating disorders** (`biology/eating-disorders`) — eating disorders damage health: Ecuador's book names anorexia and bulimia and teaches them inside "Salud integral" (integral health), in a cause→effect table with drugs, alcohol, sedentarism and pollution, and notes plainly that Ecuador lacks updated statistics on anorexia and bulimia (Ecuador, Grade 11 — *Biología — 1ro BGU*, student textbook, Sección 6, Tema 2, printed pp. 80–81)
348. **Classification by form vs by genes** (`biology/classification-by-form-vs-by-genes`) — living things can be classified by their form — morphological classification (التصنيف الشكلي), grouping by outward shape and structure — or by their genes — genetic classification (التصنيف الجيني), grouping by the genetic material they carry; Palestine's Grade 10 book sets the two side by side and compares them as the closing section of its modern-classification chapter (Palestine, Grade 10 — *العلوم الحياتية (Life Sciences)*, student textbook, Unit 4 Ch. 1 §4, printed p. 101)
349. **Plant immunity** (`biology/plant-immunity`) — plants defend themselves against pathogens in two ways — structural immunity (cutinised epidermis, hairs/thorns, thickened cell walls; induced cork formation, tyloses blocking xylem, gum deposition in Acacia nilotica, hypersensitive death of injured tissue) and biochemical immunity (pathogen receptors, phenols and glycosides, non-protein amino acids like canavanine, antimicrobial proteins and detoxifying enzymes, post-infection strengthening) — and Egypt's book teaches immunity in plants first, before immunity in man (Egypt, Grade 12 — *BIOLOGY*, student textbook, Ch. 4, printed pp. 85–88)
350. **Gender identity and sexual orientation** (`biology/gender-identity-and-sexual-orientation`) — France's manual teaches identity and orientation inside its brain, pleasure and sexuality chapter ("Identités et orientations"): what gender is, gender stereotypes — with the matrilineal Khasi society as the counter-example — attraction, and the evolution of LGBT rights in France (France, Grade 10 — *SVT 2de GT — Manuel Libre de Sciences de la vie et de la Terre*, student manual, Thème 3 / "Cerveau, plaisir et sexualité" / "Identités et orientations" — repository path as cited in the placements, in place of a printed page)
351. **Endocrine disruptors** (`biology/endocrine-disruptors`) — substances that interfere with the hormone system, taught by France inside its hormones and human procreation chapter: bisphenol A — its effects, and its ban in certain uses — and DDT, presented in the chapter's documents as an endocrine-system lure (France, Grade 10 — *SVT 2de GT — Manuel Libre de Sciences de la vie et de la Terre*, student manual, Thème 3 / "Hormones et procréation humaine" / "Perturbateurs endocriniens" — repository path as cited in the placements, in place of a printed page)
352. **Modifying the microbiome — probiotics and faecal transplant** (`biology/modifying-the-microbiome-probiotics-and-faecal-transplant`) — the human microbiome deliberately modified as a treatment: faecal transplantation as a treatment, and probiotics — live micro-organisms given on purpose, their effects studied on weight and glycaemia (France, Grade 10 — *SVT 2de GT — Manuel Libre de Sciences de la vie et de la Terre*, student manual, Thème 3 / "Microbiote humain et santé" — repository path as cited in the placements, in place of a printed page)
353. **Support in plants and animals** (`biology/support-in-plants-and-animals`) — living things need support: a plant holds itself up towards the light and an animal's supporting structures hold its body up and give its muscles something to pull against; Sudan gives support a whole closing unit of its Grade 10 book, after the movement unit, covering support in plants and support in animals (Sudan, Grade 10 — *علم الأحياء – الصف الأول الثانوي (Biology — First Secondary Year)*, student textbook, Unit Five, printed pp. 137–149)
354. **Polygenic inheritance** (`biology/polygenic-inheritance`) — some traits are controlled not by one gene but by many genes together — polygenes, multiple-gene inheritance — each contributing a small part, so the trait grades through a population instead of splitting into neat kinds (Sudan, Grade 12 — *علم الأحياء – الصف الثالث الثانوي (Biology — Third Secondary Year)*, student textbook, Unit Two §5, printed p. 170)
355. **Telling plants from animals** (`biology/telling-plants-from-animals`) — plants and animals are told apart by how they live: plants make their own food with chlorophyll and stay rooted in one place, while animals eat other living things and move about (Uganda, Senior One — *Biology Textbook — Senior One (NCDC Prototype)*, student textbook, Ch 1, printed p. 8 — the named closing topic of the book's introduction chapter)
356. **Modified leaves** (`biology/modified-leaves`) — a leaf's ordinary flat green shape is only its starting shape: leaves are modified for other jobs — tendrils that climb (watermelon, passion fruit), traps that catch insects (Venus flytrap, pitcher plant), scales that store food (onion), spines that defend (cactus), swollen leaves that store water (bryophyllum, aloe vera, sisal) (Uganda, Senior One — *Biology Textbook — Senior One (NCDC Prototype)*, student textbook, Ch 5, Activity 5.6, printed pp. 81–82)
357. **Assessing an activity of integration** (`biology/assessing-an-activity-of-integration`) — an integrated real-life task is scored on a four-part evaluation grid: Accuracy, Coherence, Relevance, Excellence (Uganda, Senior One — *Biology Teacher's Guide — Senior One (NCDC Prototype)*, teacher's guide, closing pages — built on teacher-guide evidence only, acceptance 0)
358. **Soil — properties and fertility** (`biology/soil-properties-and-fertility`) — soil is a mixture whose parts settle into measurable layers — shake soil with water in a bottle, let it settle, measure each layer's height — and soil has chemical properties as well as physical ones (Uganda, Senior Two — *Biology Home-Study Learning — Senior Two* (NCDC, 2020), student home-study booklet, Topic: Soil, Lesson 1, PDF pp. 7–10)
359. **Traditional healing and medicinal plants** (`biology/traditional-healing-and-medicinal-plants`) — health as balance between natural, social and supernatural elements; the taulasea, the local healer, and fofo massage and manipulation; the named Samoan medicinal plants — 'ava'avaaitu (Macropiper puberulum) for "ghost sickness" and its sosolo bark infusion for mouth infections and coughs, ginger, Matalafi (Psychotria insularum) the most-used plant, and Moso'i (Cananga odorata) for constipation, stomachache and postpartum sickness — taught in the same unit as antiseptics, antibiotics and vaccination (Samoa, Year 12 — *Biology — Year 12* (MESC Samoa, 2004), student textbook, Unit 1, printed pp. 47–57)
360. **Transpiration and the transpiration stream** (`biology/transpiration-and-the-transpiration-stream`) — water vapour lost from the plant pulls the transpiration stream up from roots to leaves; transpiration taught by its three roles — support, transport and cooling — with the factors affecting its rate investigated (Samoa, Year 13 — *Biology — Year 13 Learning Guide* (MESC Samoa, 2004), student learning guide, Unit 4, printed p. 35)
361. **Fish adaptations to life in water** (`biology/fish-adaptations-to-life-in-water`) — a fish's body shows how it is adapted to water: streamlined shape, fins for movement and balance, gills for taking oxygen from water, a swim bladder for depth, and the lateral line for sensing water movement — the driving question of Finland's perch-dissection materials, posed before the dissection and answered after it (Finland — *Kalan rakenne* / *Kalan preparointi* AOE open teaching materials, pupil worksheets and teacher guides, grades 3–6 and 7–9 as stated — evidence only, acceptance 0, printed pp. 2, 2, 2–4 and 5 across the four fish materials)
362. **Sleep and the body** (`biology/sleep-and-the-body`) — sleep comes in stages and can be measured: total sleep, deep sleep, REM sleep and awakenings read from a wearable, and sleep drives next-day coping and concentration (Finland, grades 8–9 as stated — *Hyvinvointia mittaamalla* (Wellbeing by Measuring), student work-card pack — open teaching material, evidence only, acceptance 0, Card 4, printed p. 17)
363. **Finland's Move! fitness monitoring** (`biology/finlands-move-fitness-monitoring`) — Move! is Finland's national system for monitoring school pupils' physical functional capacity: endurance, strength and mobility test batteries read against national reference values (oph.fi/move) (Finland, grades 8–9 as stated — *Hyvinvointia mittaamalla*, student work-card pack — open teaching material, evidence only, acceptance 0, Card 14, printed p. 50)
364. **Ecosystem services — what nature does for us** (`biology/ecosystem-services-what-nature-does-for-us`) — nature's work for people, in three groups: provisioning (food, timber), regulating/supporting (pollination, water purification, nutrient cycling, carbon binding) and cultural (recreation); more diverse nature adapts better to climate change, disease and pests (Finland — *Luonnon monimuotoisuuden tutkiminen maastossa* (Exploring biodiversity in the field), teacher field-work pack — open teaching material, evidence only, acceptance 0, printed pp. 3–4)
