365. **Invasive alien species and how they spread** (`biology/invasive-alien-species-and-how-they-spread`) — species that crossed impassable barriers with human help and now spread and harm native nature — Finland's cases: mink (fur-farm escapee devastating archipelago birds), Spanish slug (arrived 1994, omnivorous mass species), garden lupin (outcompetes native plants), giant hogweeds (sap + sunlight causes burn-like skin damage); control by removal work parties, starting at first sighting (Finland — *Luonnon monimuotoisuuden tutkiminen maastossa*, teacher field-work pack — open teaching material, evidence only, acceptance 0, printed pp. 4, 15–16)
366. **Mineral nutrition of green plants** (`biology/mineral-nutrition-of-green-plants`) — a green plant does not live on light, air and water alone: it must take in mineral elements, and only some are essential — leave one out and the plant shows a deficiency symptom; hydroponics, growing plants in a measured mineral solution with no soil, is the test that proves the essential list, and nitrogen fixation turns air's nitrogen into a form plants can use (India, Class XI — *Biology — Textbook for Class XI* (NCERT, 2020-21 edition), student textbook, Ch. 12, printed p. 194)

367. **The biogenetic law and developmental patterns** (`biology/the-biogenetic-law-and-developmental-patterns`) — in its individual development (ontogeny), an organism briefly repeats the stages its group passed through in its evolutionary history (phylogeny) — the biogenetic law, stated as a law at the close of the development chapter, after the embryonic and postembryonic periods (Uzbekistan, Grade 9 — *Биология — Основы цитологии и генетики (Biology: Fundamentals of Cytology and Genetics)*, student textbook, Part IV Ch. 10 §42, printed p. 101)

368. **Food security and climate hazards** (`biology/food-security-and-climate-hazards`) — the world population's food supply taught as a biology topic, closing the Grade 9 nutrition sequence, with drought and flood hazards (ган гачиг, үер усны аюул) named as the dangers that cut it (Mongolia, Grade 9 — *Биологи IX (Biology, Grade 9)*, student textbook, Ch. 2 §2.2.4, printed p. 37)

369. **Cytoskeleton, cilia and flagella** (`biology/cytoskeleton-cilia-and-flagella`) — the cytoskeleton inside the cell holds its shape and moves things within it, while cilia beat and flagella whip to move the cell itself — taught as the named lesson “cells move” (Italy, Grade 9 — *La nuova biologia.blu PLUS — Le cellule e i viventi* (Sadava et al., Zanichelli), student textbook, A4.7, printed p. A80)

370. **The biology of cancer** (`biology/the-biology-of-cancer`) — cancer studied as a subject of biology in itself, given a full closing chapter — C12 — after all eleven system chapters of the human-body volume, a closing chapter most school books omit (Italy, Grade 11 — *La nuova biologia.blu PLUS — Il corpo umano* (Sadava et al., Zanichelli), student textbook, C12, PDF p. 267)

371. **Endosymbiosis: the origin of mitochondria and chloroplasts** (`biology/endosymbiosis-origin-of-mitochondria-and-chloroplasts`) — mitochondria and chloroplasts began as free-living bacteria living inside another cell — the endosymbiosis hypothesis (Lynn Margulis, 1967), taught as an “ancient pact” at the close of the cell unit (Italy, Grade 9 — *Campbell — Biologia: concetti e collegamenti PLUS, Primo biennio* (Linx/Pearson Italia), student textbook, Unità 2 closing, printed p. 62)

372. **Programmed cell death and immortal cells** (`biology/programmed-cell-death-and-immortal-cells`) — cells are mortal and much of their death is programmed — apoptosis, the cell dismantling itself on schedule — while immortal cells escape that programme, taught in the “Training for the future” close of the cell-division unit (Italy, Grade 9 — *Campbell — Biologia: concetti e collegamenti PLUS, Primo biennio* (Linx/Pearson Italia), student textbook, Unità 4 closing, printed p. 120)

373. **The genetic basis of cancer** (`biology/the-genetic-basis-of-cancer`) — cancer depends on mutations in the genes that control cell division, with many gene alterations contributing through defective proteins that interfere with signal transduction — the closing run of the molecular-biology-of-the-gene unit (Italy, Grade 11 — *Campbell — Biologia: concetti e collegamenti, Secondo biennio* (Linx/Pearson Italia), student textbook, Unità 1 closing, printed pp. 48–50)

374. **Regulation of gene expression** (`biology/regulation-of-gene-expression`) — which genes a cell expresses, when, and how much is controlled — regulation given a whole unit of its own, straight after the molecular biology of the gene (Italy, Grade 11 — *Campbell — Biologia: concetti e collegamenti, Secondo biennio* (Linx/Pearson Italia), student textbook, Unità 2, PDF p. 46)

375. **Cells as colonies and filaments — Volvox and Spirogyra** (`biology/cells-as-colonies-and-filaments-volvox-and-spirogyra`) — between one cell and a tissue stand the colony — Volvox, a hollow coenobium of up to 20,000 cells, reproducing asexually and sexually — and the filament — Spirogyra, cells joined end to end, each with a spiral chloroplast, multiplying by fragmentation (Nigeria, Grade 10 — *Senior Secondary Biology 1* (Learn Africa Plc, Third edition 2013), student textbook)

376. **Chemosynthesis — food-making without light** (`biology/chemosynthesis-nitrifying-and-sulphur-bacteria`) — autotrophs that build food with chemical energy instead of light: Nitrosomonas oxidising ammonium compounds and Nitrobacter oxidising nitrites — the nitrifying steps of the nitrogen cycle — and the sulphur bacteria oxidising sulphur compounds (Nigeria, Grade 10 — *Senior Secondary Biology 1* (Learn Africa Plc, Third edition 2013), student textbook)

377. **Growth curves — sigmoid and intermittent growth** (`biology/growth-curves-sigmoid-and-intermittent`) — growth measured and graphed: the smooth S-shaped sigmoid curve of most organisms against the stepped, intermittent curve of insects, which grow only at each moult; the fastest-growing regions of root and stem found by marking experiments, and the environmental factors growth needs (Nigeria, Grade 10 — *Senior Secondary Biology 1* (Learn Africa Plc, Third edition 2013), student textbook)

378. **Plant growth hormones** (`biology/plant-growth-hormones`) — the five hormone families — auxins, cytokinins, gibberellins, abscisic acid and ethylene — with auxin and apical dominance explained through hedge-cutting: remove the tip and the side buds are released (Nigeria, Grade 10 — *Senior Secondary Biology 1* (Learn Africa Plc, Third edition 2013), student textbook)

379. **Cell reactions — tactic and nastic movements** (`biology/cell-reactions-tactic-and-nastic-movements`) — cells and organisms answer their surroundings by moving: tactic movements of a whole cell or organism towards or away from a stimulus, and the nastic responses whose direction does not depend on the stimulus's direction (Nigeria, Grade 10 — *Senior Secondary Biology 1* (Learn Africa Plc, Third edition 2013), student textbook)

380. **Parasitic nutrition and parasite adaptations** (`biology/parasitic-nutrition-and-parasite-adaptations`) — parasitic nutrition built on West African parasites — Fasciola in sheep liver, Taenia solium and T. saginata, Wuchereria bancrofti (elephantiasis), the guinea-worm Dracunculus medinensis, plant nematodes of tomato and banana roots — and the adaptations listed one by one: attachment hooks and suckers, anti-enzymes, millions of eggs, intermediate hosts, host specificity (Nigeria, Grade 10 — *Senior Secondary Biology 1* (Learn Africa Plc, Third edition 2013), student textbook)

381. **Filter feeding and deposit feeding** (`biology/filter-feeding-and-deposit-feeding`) — feeding without hunting: filter feeding through the gill rakers of herrings and sardines and the baleen of the blue whale, and deposit feeding through soil and sediment, digesting the organic matter out of it (Nigeria, Grade 10 — *Senior Secondary Biology 1* (Learn Africa Plc, Third edition 2013), student textbook)

382. **Measuring abiotic factors in the field** (`biology/measuring-abiotic-factors-in-the-field`) — each abiotic factor with its instrument — temperature, relative humidity by wet-and-dry-bulb hygrometer, wind vane and anemometer, light meter, aneroid barometer for elevation — worked with Nigerian data: the Jos mean monthly temperature table (Federal Meteorological Service, Lagos), Nigerian day length shortest in December and longest in July, and short-day flowering of dry-season okro (Nigeria, Grade 10 — *Senior Secondary Biology 1* (Learn Africa Plc, Third edition 2013), student textbook)

383. **Nigeria's vegetation belts — the local biomes** (`biology/nigerias-vegetation-belts-local-biomes`) — Nigeria's own vegetation belts as local biomes: rain forest, the Southern and Northern Guinea savannahs (shea butter and locust bean trees, Isoberlinia doka), and the Sahel in Borno State near Lake Chad, worked with Maiduguri rainfall figures (Nigeria, Grade 10 — *Senior Secondary Biology 1* (Learn Africa Plc, Third edition 2013), student textbook)

384. **Agricultural classification of crops** (`biology/agricultural-classification-of-crops`) — plants classified twice over — botanically, and agriculturally by product, useful part and economic importance into twelve groups (root, vegetable, cereal, legume, fibre, oil, latex, beverage, fruit, spice, forage, cash crops) with Nigerian examples throughout: cassava, yam, guinea corn, benniseed, kola, oil palm (Nigeria, Grade 10 — *Senior Secondary Biology 1* (Learn Africa Plc, Third edition 2013), student textbook)

385. **Effects of agricultural activities on ecosystems** (`biology/effects-of-agricultural-activities-on-ecosystems`) — what clearing, the farming systems and bush burning do to the ecosystem — taught crop by crop and practice by practice (Nigeria, Grade 10 — *Senior Secondary Biology 1* (Learn Africa Plc, Third edition 2013), student textbook)

386. **Improving crop yield** (`biology/improving-crop-yield`) — five ways: soil conservation (terracing, mulching, rotation, no bush burning), fertilisers and manure, cultural practices, plant breeding for improved varieties (disease-resistant cassava, budded oranges), and putting more land under cultivation (Nigeria, Grade 10 — *Senior Secondary Biology 1* (Learn Africa Plc, Third edition 2013), student textbook)

387. **Food preservation and storage** (`biology/food-preservation-and-storage`) — the causes of harvest wastage (incomplete and late harvesting, weather, pests, poor storage — yams in barns) and the preservation methods with their principles: heating, freezing, and salting and sugaring by osmosis (Nigeria, Grade 10 — *Senior Secondary Biology 1* (Learn Africa Plc, Third edition 2013), student textbook)

388. **Tsetse fly, trypanosomiasis and vector control** (`biology/tsetse-fly-trypanosomiasis-and-vector-control`) — control of disease through the vector: the mosquito programme, then the tsetse fly's full life history and the control built on it — clearing shade resting places, spraying vehicles and resting sites, breeding nagana-resistant cattle, prompt treatment of sleeping sickness; antelopes as harmless reservoirs (Nigeria, Grade 10 — *Senior Secondary Biology 1* (Learn Africa Plc, Third edition 2013), student textbook)

389. **Public health — refuse, sewage and food safety** (`biology/public-health-refuse-sewage-and-food-safety`) — community health as a shared duty: refuse disposal (dustbin, incinerator, burial), sewage disposal (pit latrines, bucket toilets, flush toilet and septic tank with sludge turned to fertiliser), protection of water and food, and Ministry of Health inspection of abattoirs and eating places (Nigeria, Grade 10 — *Senior Secondary Biology 1* (Learn Africa Plc, Third edition 2013), student textbook)

390. **Estuaries, mangroves and the Lagos lagoon** (`biology/estuaries-mangroves-and-the-lagos-lagoon`) — estuary types (the open mouths of the Ogun, Osun and Osse rivers, the Niger delta, the Lagos lagoon system), salinity varying with tide and season, and the observed zonation on red-mangrove stilt roots — Mercierella where salinity stays high, the oyster Gryphaea where it is lower, the barnacle Balanus where it falls lowest; red mangrove (Rhizophora racemosa) adaptations (Nigeria, Grade 10 — *Senior Secondary Biology 1* (Learn Africa Plc, Third edition 2013), student textbook)

391. **The marsh habitat** (`biology/the-marsh-habitat`) — the marsh as a terrestrial-aquatic border habitat — waterlogged ground between open water and dry land — and its plant and animal adaptations (Nigeria, Grade 10 — *Senior Secondary Biology 1* (Learn Africa Plc, Third edition 2013), student textbook)

392. **Tropical rainforest — structure and adaptations** (`biology/tropical-rainforest-structure-and-adaptations`) — the rain forest studied practically: storeys, drip tips, buttress roots, cauliflory, climbers and shade plants, arboreal animal adaptations, and forest food chains built from the species found (Nigeria, Grade 10 — *Senior Secondary Biology 1* (Learn Africa Plc, Third edition 2013), student textbook)

393. **Savannah grasslands and derived savannah** (`biology/savannah-grasslands-and-derived-savannah`) — grassland characteristics and world names (prairies, steppes, pampas, veldt), Nigeria's savannah types — and derived savannah, the grassland humans maintain by farming and bush burning inside the rain-forest climate, which reverts to forest when the pressure stops (Nigeria, Grade 10 — *Senior Secondary Biology 1* (Learn Africa Plc, Third edition 2013), student textbook)

394. **Arid lands and desert adaptations** (`biology/arid-lands-and-desert-adaptations`) — the arid lands and how their plants and animals are fitted to them — water kept, water loss cut, scarcity endured (Nigeria, Grade 10 — *Senior Secondary Biology 1* (Learn Africa Plc, Third edition 2013), student textbook)

395. **Reproduction in earthworm and garden snail** (`biology/reproduction-in-earthworm-and-garden-snail`) — two hermaphrodites that still cross-fertilise: earthworms exchanging sperm at the clitellum with fertilisation inside the slipped-off cocoon; snails exchanging sperm and laying egg batches in the soil (Nigeria, Grade 10 — *Senior Secondary Biology 1* (Learn Africa Plc, Third edition 2013), student textbook)

396. **Plant and animal tissues** (`biology/plant-and-animal-tissues`) — a tissue is a group of similar cells doing one job together: Yemen's Grade 11 book teaches the animal tissues — epithelial (printed p. 25), connective (p. 31), muscular (p. 39), nervous (p. 42) — then the plant tissues, permanent (p. 45) and compound permanent, the vascular tissues (p. 49), and its activities book keys six microscope practicals to the same ground (Yemen, Grade 11 — *علم الأحياء وعلم الأرض — للصف الثاني الثانوي* (Biology and Earth Science, Second Secondary), student textbook, Unit 2, printed pp. 25–49; *الأنشطة والتجارب العملية* (Activities and Practical Experiments), student activities/practical book, Activities 3–8, printed pp. 12–27)

397. **Noise and radiation pollution** (`biology/noise-and-radiation-pollution`) — pollution the eye cannot see in air or water: noise pollution (التلوث الضوضائي, printed p. 173) and radiation pollution (التلوث الإشعاعي, printed p. 176) taught as named pollution kinds of their own, beside food pollutants (p. 178) and soil pollution (p. 180) (Yemen, Grade 12 — *علم الأحياء وعلم الأرض — للصف الثالث الثانوي* (Biology and Earth Science, Third Secondary), student textbook, Unit 7, printed pp. 173–180)
398. **Cell signalling and communication** (`biology/cell-signalling-and-communication`) — cells talk: a signalling molecule released by one cell binds the matching receptor on a target cell, the signal is propagated inside through a relay chain that amplifies it, and the cell responds — genes switched, enzymes started; single-celled organisms signal too, as quorum sensing shows (United States, Grade 12 — *Biology for AP® Courses* (OpenStax, Rice University), student textbook, Ch. 9, PDF pp. 371–395; and Grade 13 record — *Biology 2e* (OpenStax, Rice University, 2nd edition), student textbook, Ch. 9, PDF p. 257)
399. **Biological waste and lab accident management** (`biology/biological-waste-and-lab-accident-management`) — biological waste is sorted and disposed of by set methods — what may and may not go down the sink — and a lab accident is met by a set order of steps (Malaysia, Grade 10 — *Biology Form 4 (DLP)*, student textbook, Ch. 1, printed p. 6; parallel Bahasa Melayu edition *Biologi Tingkatan 4* at the same printed page)
400. **Membrane transport in daily life — food preservation** (`biology/membrane-transport-in-daily-life-food-preservation`) — the membrane chapter applied: osmosis at work in daily life, salt and sugar drawing water out of food and out of the microbes that would spoil it (Malaysia, Grade 10 — *Biology Form 4 (DLP)*, student textbook, Ch. 3, printed p. 65; parallel Bahasa Melayu edition *Biologi Tingkatan 4* at the same printed page)
401. **Blood clotting mechanism** (`biology/blood-clotting-mechanism`) — a cut vessel sealed by a mechanism: platelets and plasma proteins build a fibrin net that traps blood cells (Malaysia, Grade 10 — *Biology Form 4 (DLP)*, student textbook, Ch. 10, printed p. 183; parallel Bahasa Melayu edition *Biologi Tingkatan 4* at the same printed page)
402. **Formation of twins** (`biology/formation-of-twins`) — identical twins from one fertilised egg that splits, fraternal twins from two eggs fertilised separately — examined by comparison (Malaysia, Grade 10 — *Biology Form 4 (DLP)*, student textbook, Ch. 15, printed p. 300; parallel Bahasa Melayu edition *Biologi Tingkatan 4* at the same printed page)
403. **Meristems and plant growth curves** (`biology/meristems-and-plant-growth-curves`) — growth from the meristems — dividing tissues at root and shoot tips — and growth measured as curves: the sigmoid S-curve and the stepped curve (Malaysia, Grade 11 — *Biology Form 5 (DLP)*, student textbook, Ch. 1, printed p. 6; parallel Bahasa Melayu edition *Biologi Tingkatan 5* at the same printed page)
404. **Compensation point** (`biology/compensation-point`) — the light intensity at which photosynthesis exactly balances respiration — no net gain, no net loss (Malaysia, Grade 11 — *Biology Form 5 (DLP)*, student textbook, Ch. 2, printed p. 52; parallel Bahasa Melayu edition *Biologi Tingkatan 5* at the same printed page)
405. **Diversity in plant nutrition — carnivorous, epiphytes and parasites** (`biology/diversity-in-plant-nutrition-carnivorous-epiphytes-parasites`) — three non-standard ways of feeding: carnivorous plants trapping insects for minerals, epiphytes perched for support while making their own food, parasites stealing from a host (Malaysia, Grade 11 — *Biology Form 5 (DLP)*, student textbook, Ch. 3, printed p. 68; parallel Bahasa Melayu edition *Biologi Tingkatan 5* at the same printed page)
406. **Pollen and embryo sac development** (`biology/pollen-and-embryo-sac-development`) — the gamete-carrying structures built before pollination: pollen grains in the anther, the embryo sac with its egg cell inside the ovule — both through meiosis (Malaysia, Grade 11 — *Biology Form 5 (DLP)*, student textbook, Ch. 6, printed p. 116; parallel Bahasa Melayu edition *Biologi Tingkatan 5* at the same printed page)
407. **Plant adaptations to habitats** (`biology/plant-adaptations-to-habitats`) — plants fitted to the water of their habitat: xerophytes in dry places, hydrophytes in water, halophytes in salty ground, epiphytes perched above it (Malaysia, Grade 11 — *Biology Form 5 (DLP)*, student textbook, Ch. 7, printed p. 134; parallel Bahasa Melayu edition *Biologi Tingkatan 5* at the same printed page)
408. **Green technology** (`biology/green-technology`) — technology designed to protect the environment, taught by name as a practice of environmental sustainability (Malaysia, Grade 11 — *Biology Form 5 (DLP)*, student textbook, Ch. 10, printed p. 218; parallel Bahasa Melayu edition *Biologi Tingkatan 5* at the same printed page)
409. **Variation — continuous and discontinuous** (`biology/variation-continuous-and-discontinuous`) — variation sorted by kind: continuous variation grading smoothly (height, mass) against discontinuous variation in clear separate groups (blood group), and the genetic and environmental factors behind both (Malaysia, Grade 11 — *Biology Form 5 (DLP)*, student textbook, Ch. 12, printed p. 256; parallel Bahasa Melayu edition *Biologi Tingkatan 5* at the same printed page)
410. **Affective relationships and attachment** (`biology/affective-relationships-and-attachment`) — relationships have a biology: attachment — the bond a person builds with the people close to them — and the right to a life free of violence in relationships, taught as a named unit, "Biología de las relaciones afectivas", inside the biology book itself (Bolivia, Grade 8 — *Texto de Aprendizaje — Biología-Geografía — Segundo Año de Secundaria*, student textbook, printed pp. 156–158)
411. **Lung volumes and capacities** (`biology/lung-volumes-and-capacities`) — not all the air in the lungs moves with every breath: tidal volume, the inspiratory and expiratory reserves, the residual volume that never leaves, and the capacities built by adding volumes — vital capacity above all (Bhutan, Grade 9 — *BIOLOGY — CLASS NINE*, student textbook, Ch. 5 d, printed pp. 99–100)
412. **Limiting factors and Blackman's law** (`biology/limiting-factors-and-blackmans-law`) — photosynthesis runs at the speed of its scarcest need: Blackman's law of limiting factors, read off the rising-then-flattening rate graph, with the utilisation of photosynthesis products (Bhutan, Grade 10 — *BIOLOGY — CLASS 10*, student textbook, Ch. 2.1, printed pp. 37–38)
413. **The pressure-flow hypothesis** (`biology/the-pressure-flow-hypothesis`) — phloem sap is pushed from source to sink by a pressure difference: loading and osmosis raise the pressure at the source, unloading drops it at the sink (Bhutan, Grade 10 — *BIOLOGY — CLASS 10*, student textbook, Ch. 2.2, printed p. 41)
414. **Hormones in medicine and anabolic steroids** (`biology/hormones-in-medicine-and-anabolic-steroids`) — hormones as medicines — replacement, insulin for diabetes the standing example — and anabolic steroids, the synthetic male-hormone copies misused in sport, taught as one topic with its costs (Bhutan, Grade 10 — *BIOLOGY — CLASS 10*, student textbook, Ch. 4.2, printed pp. 104–106)
415. **The Hardy–Weinberg law** (`biology/the-hardy-weinberg-law`) — in a population that is not evolving, allele and genotype frequencies stay constant — p² + 2pq + q² = 1 — the balance against which selection, mutation, migration and drift are detected (Bhutan, Grade 10 — *BIOLOGY — CLASS 10*, student textbook, Ch. 6.4, printed pp. 166–167)
416. **Chromosome structure and karyotype** (`biology/chromosome-structure-and-karyotype`) — a cell's long DNA is packaged with protein into chromosomes — DNA wound around histone proteins into nucleosomes, the string folded into chromatin that is loose (euchromatin) or dense (heterochromatin) — each chromosome's shape is named by where its centromere sits, and a karyotype is the individual's whole set of chromosomes photographed, cut out and arranged in order (Indonesia, Grade 12 — *Biologi untuk SMA/MA Kelas XII*, student textbook, Ch. 2A, printed p. 70; the Grade 12 teacher guide adds Drosophila melanogaster's giant polytene chromosomes, Gambar 2.1, printed p. 70 — teacher-guide evidence)
417. **Gene interactions — epistasis and polymery** (`biology/gene-interactions-epistasis-and-polymery`) — genes at different loci do not always act alone — they interact: two genes together can produce a form neither makes by itself (the chicken's comb shape from the R and P genes), one gene can mask another gene's effect (epistasis — the masking gene is epistatic, the masked one hypostatic), and several genes can add their small effects together on one trait (polymery) (Indonesia, Grade 12 — *Biologi untuk SMA/MA Kelas XII*, student textbook, Ch. 2D, printed p. 104)
418. **Double fertilisation in flowering plants** (`biology/double-fertilisation-in-flowering-plants`) — in a flowering plant, fertilisation is double: of the two male gametes a pollen grain delivers into the embryo sac, one joins the egg cell to form the zygote that grows into the embryo, and the second joins the polar nuclei to form the endosperm — the food tissue that will feed the embryo inside the seed (Indonesia, Grade 11 — *Buku Panduan Guru Biologi untuk SMA/MA Kelas XI*, teacher's guide, Ch. 3, printed p. 108 — built on teacher-guide evidence only, acceptance 0)
419. **PCR — polymerase chain reaction** (`biology/pcr-polymerase-chain-reaction`) — PCR copies one chosen piece of DNA over and over in a tube by cycling three temperature steps — denaturation at about 96°C, where the double strand separates; annealing at 55–65°C, where primers bind; and extension at 72°C, where Taq polymerase builds the new strands — so the number of copies doubles with every cycle (Indonesia, Grade 12 — *Buku Panduan Guru Biologi untuk SMA/MA Kelas XII*, teacher's guide, Bab 2 answer key, printed p. 67 — built on teacher-guide evidence only, acceptance 0)
420. **Quranic instructions and the study of life** (`biology/quranic-instructions-and-the-study-of-life`) — Pakistan (Punjab)'s Grade 9 book gives the study of life a named religious framing of its own: its section “Quranic Instructions to Reveal the Study of Life” teaches that the Holy Quran instructs the study of life — the book's own answer to the question of why biology should be studied at all (Pakistan (Punjab), Grade 9 — *Biology Class 9 SNC* (Punjab Board (PCTB)), student textbook, Ch 1.4, printed p. 8)
421. **Stem cells** (`biology/stem-cells`) — cells that are not yet specialised: they can divide to make more cells like themselves, and they can turn into specialised cells of different kinds — the body's source of new specialised cells (Pakistan (Punjab), Grade 9 — *Biology Class 9 SNC* (Punjab Board (PCTB)), student textbook, Ch 3.5, printed p. 53)
422. **Excretion and osmosis in plants** (`biology/excretion-and-osmosis-in-plants`) — plants have no kidneys and no single excretory system: excess water leaves by transpiration and guttation, other wastes are stored away in leaves, bark and resins that are shed, and the water balance is kept by osmotic adjustments — changing the solute content of cells so water moves the way the plant needs (Pakistan (Punjab), Grade 9 — *Biology Class 9 SNC* (Punjab Board (PCTB)), student textbook, Ch 9.7–9.8, printed pp. 140–142)
423. **Biostatistics — mean, median and mode** (`biology/biostatistics-mean-median-mode`) — the statistics a biologist uses on living data: the mean (values added and divided by how many there are), the median (the middle value in order) and the mode (the value that occurs most often), with the set shown in a bar chart (Pakistan (Punjab), Grade 9 — *Biology Class 9 SNC* (Punjab Board (PCTB)), student textbook, Ch 11, printed pp. 163–167)
424. **Single-cell protein** (`biology/single-cell-protein`) — protein harvested from single-celled microorganisms — algae, yeasts, other fungi and bacteria grown in bulk on cheap feedstocks — dried and used as a protein-rich food or feed: protein grown in a tank instead of a field (Pakistan (Punjab), Grade 10 — *BIOLOGY 10* (Punjab Curriculum and Textbook Board, Lahore), student textbook, Ch 17.4, printed p. 147)
425. **Pharmacology — medicinal and addictive drugs** (`biology/pharmacology-medicinal-and-addictive-drugs`) — the study of drugs: medicinal drugs that treat disease — antibiotics that kill bacteria, and vaccines that train the body's defence before disease comes — set against addictive drugs, the drugs a body comes to depend on (Pakistan (Punjab), Grade 10 — *BIOLOGY 10* (Punjab Curriculum and Textbook Board, Lahore), student textbook, Ch 18, printed pp. 150–155)
426. **Growth and development** (`biology/growth-and-development`) — growth is an irreversible increase in size — more cells, bigger cells, more dry mass — while development is the whole change by which a living thing matures: cells differentiate, organs form and take up their work, and the organism becomes able to live and reproduce as an adult (Pakistan (Punjab), Grade 12 — *Biology Class 12 SNC* (Punjab Board (PCTB)), student textbook, Ch 19, PDF p. 159)
427. **Specialised cells and cell specialisation** (`biology/specialised-cells-and-cell-specialisation`) — a cell whose structure is fitted to one particular job: cheek cells told from ciliated cells by their structure, mucus and cilia sweeping the trachea, and division of labour as the gain (South Sudan, Secondary 1 — *Biology Student's Book 1* (MoGEI, © 2018), student textbook, §2.6, printed pp. 48–53)

428. **Stomata — opening by guard-cell turgor** (`biology/stomata-opening-by-guard-cell-turgor`) — a stoma opens when its two guard cells gain water by osmosis, become turgid and bulge outwards, letting carbon dioxide and oxygen move, and closes when they lose water and go flaccid (South Sudan, Secondary 1 — *Biology Student's Book 1* (MoGEI, © 2018), student textbook, §3.5, printed p. 79)

429. **The bryophyte life cycle — the moss** (`biology/bryophyte-life-cycle-moss`) — the green moss plant is the gametophyte, bearing antheridia and archegonia; the sporophyte — seta and capsule — grows out of it and stays dependent on it, and fertilisation depends on water (South Sudan, Secondary 3 — *Biology, Secondary 3 — Student's Book* (MoGEI, © 2018), student textbook, §1.6, printed p. 19)
430. **Afghanistan's seed plants** (`biology/afghanistans-seed-plants`) — Afghanistan's Grade 7 book closes its botany with the country's own seed plants — the conifers of the eastern forests, the wheat and barley of the fields, and the almond, pistachio, pomegranate, grape and mulberry of the orchards: seed plants in cones and seed plants in fruits, and what each group gives the country (Afghanistan, Grade 7 — *بیولوژی صنف هفتم (Biology, Grade 7 — Dari)*, student textbook, Ch. 6, printed p. 55)

431. **Algae — plant-like protists** (`biology/algae-plant-like-protists`) — the plant-like protists: chlorophyll-bearing, photosynthetic, with no true roots, stems or leaves — from single cells and filaments to the great seaweeds, the base of food chains in water (Afghanistan, Grade 8 — *بیولوژی صنف هشتم (Biology, Grade 8 — Dari)*, student textbook, Ch. 2, printed p. 19)

432. **Lichens — a symbiosis** (`biology/lichens-a-symbiosis`) — a lichen is two living things in one body — a fungus giving shelter, water and minerals, an alga making food — in crust, leafy and tufted forms, pioneers on bare rock and indicators of clean air (Afghanistan, Grade 8 — *بیولوژی صنف هشتم (Biology, Grade 8 — Dari)*, student textbook, Ch. 2, printed p. 22)

433. **Afghanistan's birds and mammals** (`biology/afghanistans-birds-and-mammals`) — the country's own two warm-blooded vertebrate groups surveyed at home: birds — feathers, wings, beaks, shelled eggs — from mountain raptors to farmyard birds, and mammals — hair and milk — from the markhor and snow leopard to the farm's sheep, goats and horses (Afghanistan, Grade 8 — *بیولوژی صنف هشتم (Biology, Grade 8 — Dari)*, student textbook, Ch. 4, printed p. 66)

434. **Economic importance of fish, birds and mammals** (`biology/economic-importance-of-fish-birds-and-mammals`) — what the three groups earn a household: fish as food and income, birds as eggs, meat and free pest control, mammals as milk, meat, wool, leather and labour — and why use must leave breeding stock and habitat intact (Afghanistan, Grade 8 — *بیولوژی صنف هشتم (Biology, Grade 8 — Dari)*, student textbook, Ch. 4, printed p. 66)

435. **Digestive system disorders and hygiene** (`biology/digestive-system-disorders-and-hygiene`) — the gut's own disorders — diarrhoea, dysentery, ulcers, worms — most of them carried in by dirty water, hands and food, and the hygiene chain of hands, water, food and eating habits that keeps them out (Afghanistan, Grade 9 — *بیولوژی صنف نهم (Biology, Grade 9 — Dari)*, student textbook, Ch. 1, printed p. 9)

436. **Common respiratory diseases** (`biology/common-respiratory-diseases`) — the air path's common diseases pinned to the part each strikes — colds and influenza, bronchitis, pneumonia, tuberculosis and asthma — how they spread through the air and how air, hygiene and vaccination prevent them (Afghanistan, Grade 9 — *بیولوژی صنف نهم (Biology, Grade 9 — Dari)*, student textbook, Ch. 2, printed p. 18)

437. **Blood and its components** (`biology/blood-and-its-components`) — blood as a liquid tissue: plasma the carrier, red cells carrying oxygen on haemoglobin, white cells defending, platelets clotting — four components, four jobs (Afghanistan, Grade 9 — *بیولوژی صنف نهم (Biology, Grade 9 — Dari)*, student textbook, Ch. 3, printed p. 22)

438. **Kidney failure and new treatments** (`biology/kidney-failure-and-new-treatments`) — when the kidneys can no longer clean the blood — its causes in diabetes, blood pressure, infection and stones, its warning signs, and the new treatments: dialysis cleaning the blood outside the body, and transplant replacing the organ's work with one donated kidney (Afghanistan, Grade 9 — *بیولوژی صنف نهم (Biology, Grade 9 — Dari)*, student textbook, Ch. 4, printed p. 38)

439. **Comparing invertebrate body systems** (`biology/comparing-invertebrate-body-systems`) — the comparative table across the invertebrate phyla: digestion from intracellular to a through tube, breathing from skin to tracheae, open against closed circulation, nerve net to brain and cord, flame cells to Malpighian tubules (Afghanistan, Grade 11 — *بیولوژی صنف یازدهم (Biology, Grade 11 — Dari)*, student textbook, Part 3 Ch. 8, printed p. 97)

440. **Comparing vertebrate body systems** (`biology/comparing-vertebrate-body-systems`) — one body plan tuned five ways: hearts of two, three and four chambers, single against double circulation, gills to air-sac lungs, ectotherm against endotherm, water eggs to milk-fed young (Afghanistan, Grade 11 — *بیولوژی صنف یازدهم (Biology, Grade 11 — Dari)*, student textbook, Part 4 Ch. 10, printed p. 135)

441. **Interactions between populations** (`biology/interactions-between-populations`) — competition, predation, parasitism, mutualism and commensalism — every interaction read by the signs each population carries, and the linked rise and fall of predator and prey (Afghanistan, Grade 11 — *بیولوژی صنف یازدهم (Biology, Grade 11 — Dari)*, student textbook, Part 5 Ch. 11, printed p. 148)

442. **World biomes** (`biology/world-biomes`) — the Earth's great life regions sorted by climate — tundra and taiga, deciduous forest and grassland, desert, savanna and tropical rainforest — rainfall and temperature drawing the edges, plants and animals fitted to each (Afghanistan, Grade 11 — *بیولوژی صنف یازدهم (Biology, Grade 11 — Dari)*, student textbook, Part 5 Ch. 12, printed p. 155)

443. **Fish dissection — a practical** (`biology/fish-dissection-practical`) — a guided dissection studies the fish from the outside in: first the external structure fitted to life in water, then — opened carefully, step by step — the internal organs in their places (Armenia, Grade 7 — *Կենսաբանություն 7 (Biology 7)*, student textbook, §21 laboratory work, printed p. 78)

444. **Eye and ear care — correction and prevention** (`biology/eye-and-ear-care-correction-and-prevention`) — short-sight and long-sight are corrected with lenses fitted to the fault, and sight and hearing are protected before damage is done (Armenia, Grade 8 — *Կենսաբանություն (Մարդ) 8 (Biology: The Human, Grade 8)*, student textbook, §14, printed p. 49)

445. **Posture and musculoskeletal hygiene** (`biology/posture-and-musculoskeletal-hygiene`) — correct posture for a growing body, preventing spinal curvature and flat feet, and exercise hygiene (Armenia, Grade 8 — *Կենսաբանություն (Մարդ) 8 (Biology: The Human, Grade 8)*, student textbook, §22, printed p. 81)

446. **The seashore community** (`biology/the-seashore-community`) — a seashore community studied as a whole: the living things of a shore live in zones between the tides, each kind living where it can stand the covering and uncovering of the sea (Fiji, Grade 11 — *Biology for All — Year 11*, student textbook, Ch. 6.2.2, printed p. 86)

447. **First aid basics** (`biology/first-aid-basics`) — first aid is the first help given to a person who is injured or suddenly taken ill, before medical help takes over: keep the person and yourself safe, keep the airway open and breathing going, stop bleeding, keep the person still and warm, and send for help (Fiji, Grade 11 — *Biology for All — Year 11*, student textbook, Ch. 8.5, printed p. 118)

448. **Saltwater intrusion and coastal aquifers** (`biology/saltwater-intrusion-and-coastal-aquifers`) — beneath a coast, fresh groundwater sits on denser salt water; when the sea level rises, the salt water pushes further inland and up into the fresh water — saltwater intrusion — and Fiji's coastal wells and aquifers are where it strikes first (Fiji, Grade 12 — *Biology for All — Year 12*, student textbook, Sub-strand 3.2, printed p. 193)

449. **Cyanobacteria — the blue-green algae** (`biology/cyanobacteria-blue-green-algae`) — cyanobacteria are bacteria that photosynthesise: single cells and filaments of Kingdom Monera that make their own food from sunlight like plants do, which is why they were long mistaken for algae (Fiji, Grade 13 — *Biology for All — Year 13 Biology*, student textbook, §3.2.2, printed p. 143)

450. **Body cavities — acoelomate, pseudocoelomate, coelomate** (`biology/body-cavities-acoelomate-pseudocoelomate-coelomate`) — animals are sorted by the cavity inside the body wall: acoelomates have no cavity, pseudocoelomates have a cavity not lined by mesoderm, and coelomates have a true coelom, a cavity fully lined by mesoderm, in which the organs lie and move (Fiji, Grade 13 — *Biology for All — Year 13 Biology*, student textbook, §3.2.6, printed pp. 195–201)
451. **Animal adaptations to environment** (`biology/animal-adaptations-to-environment`) — an animal is fitted to its home in form, structure and ways of life: Iraq's Grade 10 book gives adaptation its longest chapter (Ch. 6, printed pp. 77–120) — adaptation in general, then to life in water, then to life on land, closing with a head-to-head comparison of a bony fish and the pigeon (Iraq, Grade 10 — *علم الأحياء — للصف الرابع العلمي (Biology, Fourth Scientific)*, student textbook, Ch. 6 §1-6, printed p. 77)
452. **Civil defence and emergency safety** (`biology/civil-defence-and-emergency-safety`) — civil defence (الدفاع المدني), the organised protection of the public in emergencies, taught as biology-course content: the closing chapter of Iraq's Grade 7 book, in the safety unit it shares with first aid (Iraq, Grade 7 — *علم الأحياء — للصف الأول المتوسط (Biology, First Intermediate)*, student textbook, Unit 4 Ch. 9, printed p. 87)
453. **Locomotion across animals** (`biology/locomotion-across-animals`) — how animals move, traced comparatively from the amoeba's pseudopodia and Euglena's flagellum, through the earthworm's hydrostatic movement and the grasshopper's leg muscles, to vertebrate swimming, crawling and the flapping flight of strong birds such as the duck (Iraq, Grade 11 — *علم الأحياء — للصف الخامس العلمي (Biology, Fifth Scientific)*, student textbook, Ch. 4, printed p. 112)
454. **Hermaphroditism in animals** (`biology/hermaphroditism-in-animals`) — hermaphroditic reproduction (التكاثر الخنثي): one individual carrying both male and female reproductive organs — the closing section of the reproduction chapter in Iraq's Grade 12 capstone, straight after parthenogenesis (Iraq, Grade 12 — *علم الأحياء — للصف السادس العلمي (Biology, Sixth Scientific)*, student textbook, Ch. 3 §11-3, printed p. 137)
455. **Embryonic development in mammals** (`biology/embryonic-development-in-mammals`) — after fertilisation the zygote divides and the embryo settles in the uterus, fed across the placenta until birth (North Macedonia, Grade 8 — *БИОЛОГИЈА 8*, Theme 2, printed p. 138)
456. **Preventing sexual abuse** (`biology/preventing-sexual-abuse`) — the body is one's own: recognising unacceptable behaviour, refusing and getting away, and telling a trusted adult — abuse is always the offender's fault (North Macedonia, Grade 8 — *БИОЛОГИЈА 8*, Theme 3.12, printed p. 192)
457. **Adaptations to the environment** (`biology/adaptations-to-the-environment`) — a feature that fits an organism to its environment — abiotic factors, aquatic adaptations, and the different fits of different environments (North Macedonia, Grade 8 — *БИОЛОГИЈА 8*, Theme 4, printed pp. 202–219)
458. **Haploid and diploid** (`biology/haploid-and-diploid`) — two sets (2n) in body cells, one set (n) in gametes, restored at fertilisation — the chromosome number constant across generations (North Macedonia, Grade 10 — *БИОЛОГИЈА I*, Theme 2.7, printed p. 66)
459. **Cytokinesis — dividing the cytoplasm** (`biology/cytokinesis`) — the cytoplasm divides after the nucleus — by a pinching furrow in animal cells, by a growing cell plate in plant cells (North Macedonia, Grade 10 — *БИОЛОГИЈА I*, Theme 2.11, printed p. 81)
460. **Rudimentary organs and atavisms** (`biology/rudimentary-organs-and-atavisms`) — reduced, jobless structures inherited from ancestors, and ancestral features reappearing in individuals — both read as evidence for evolution (North Macedonia, Grade 10 — *БИОЛОГИЈА I*, Theme 4.6, printed p. 165)
461. **The ecological niche** (`biology/the-ecological-niche`) — the organism's whole way of life in its community — place, food, enemies, hours — distinct from its habitat, with competition when two kinds need the same one (North Macedonia, Grade 10 — *БИОЛОГИЈА I*, Theme 5.11, printed p. 224)
462. **Collecting and processing biological data** (`biology/collecting-and-processing-biological-data`) — planned collecting, honest recording in tables, processing by sorting, averages and graphs, and presentation others can check (North Macedonia, Grade 11 — *БИОЛОГИЈА II*, Themes 1.4–1.5, printed pp. 22–25)
463. **Plant tissues — meristem and permanent tissues** (`biology/plant-tissues-meristem-and-permanent`) — plant tissues sorted by division — meristem (творно) at the growing fronts, permanent tissues (трајни) as its finished, specialised product (North Macedonia, Grade 11 — *БИОЛОГИЈА II*, Themes 3.1–3.2, printed pp. 180–184)
464. **Ground and mechanical plant tissues** (`biology/ground-and-mechanical-plant-tissues`) — parenchyma the living ground tissue that fills, feeds and stores; mechanical tissue — collenchyma and sclerenchyma — the skeleton that holds the plant up (North Macedonia, Grade 11 — *БИОЛОГИЈА II*, Themes 3.3–3.4, printed pp. 190–194)
465. **Synthetic biology and CRISPR** (`biology/synthetic-biology-and-crispr`) — biology as engineering — designing and building living systems — and CRISPR, the bacterial system turned into a find-cut-repair gene-editing tool (North Macedonia, Grade 11 — *БИОЛОГИЈА II*, Theme 4.2, printed p. 247)
466. **Alternation of generations** (`biology/alternation-of-generations`) — sporophyte (2n, making spores by meiosis) and gametophyte (n, making gametes) taking turns — gametophyte-dominant in mosses, sporophyte-dominant in ferns (North Macedonia, Grade 13 elective — *БИОЛОГИЈА за IV година*, Ch. 3.I.2, printed pp. 104–110)
467. **Foundations of embryology** (`biology/foundations-of-embryology`) — development from the fertilised egg as its own science — cleavage, gastrulation, and the three germ layers from which every organ traces (North Macedonia, Grade 13 elective — *БИОЛОГИЈА за IV година*, Ch. 3.III, printed pp. 145–151)
468. **How exocrine glands are controlled** (`biology/how-exocrine-glands-are-controlled`) — the ducted glands — salivary, sweat, digestive — switched by fast nervous reflexes and timed hormonal signals, taught as the closing topic of the regulation year (North Macedonia, Grade 13 elective — *БИОЛОГИЈА за IV година*, Ch. 4.11, printed pp. 232–239)

469. **Algae — thallus plants** (`biology/algae-thallus-plants`) — algae are thallus plants: their whole body is a thallus, one simple plant body not divided into root, stem and leaves — the simplest body plan in the plant kingdom, set against the cormus plants built of tissues and true organs (Bulgaria, Grade 7 — *Биология и здравно образование 7. клас*, student textbook, Topic 15, printed p. 33)
470. **Modified plant organs** (`biology/modified-plant-organs`) — a plant organ can be modified, changed in form for a special job: tendrils to climb and hold, thorns to defend, storage organs to keep food and water — taught as a discussion/project topic (Bulgaria, Grade 7 — *Биология и здравно образование 7. клас*, student textbook, Topic 19, printed p. 42)
471. **Cloning — debate and technique** (`biology/cloning-debate-and-technique`) — cloning makes new living things that are genetic copies of a single parent, taught together with the debate about what it should and should not be used for, closing the biotechnology section (Bulgaria, Grade 9 — *Биология и здравно образование 9. клас*, student textbook, Topic 32, printed p. 116)
472. **Individual development — embryonic and post-embryonic** (`biology/individual-development-embryonic-and-postembryonic`) — every organism's individual development runs in order from the fertilised egg: the embryonic period to birth or hatching, then the post-embryonic period through growth and change to the adult (Bulgaria, Grade 10 — *Биология и здравно образование 10. клас*, student textbook, Topics 22–24, printed pp. 65–67)
473. **Modelling the cell membrane** (`biology/modelling-the-cell-membrane`) — a cell membrane can be modelled with simple materials — a glass dish of water, vegetable oil and fine sawdust — and each material in the model stands for one part of the real membrane's structure (Kuwait — built for the Kuwait placements, expansion-biology-2026-11-13/kuwait-placements.md)
474. **Surface area to volume ratio and cell size** (`biology/surface-area-to-volume-ratio-and-cell-size`) — as a cell grows, its volume grows faster than its surface area, so the surface-area-to-volume ratio falls — which is why cells stay microscopic and why dividing beats growing for exchanging materials (Kuwait — built for the Kuwait placements, expansion-biology-2026-11-13/kuwait-placements.md)
475. **Signs of a chemical reaction** (`biology/signs-of-a-chemical-reaction`) — a chemical reaction shows itself by observable signs — a colour change, a taste change, fizzing or a new substance forming — and naming the evidence is how you know a reaction, not just a mixture, has happened (Kuwait — built for the Kuwait placements, expansion-biology-2026-11-13/kuwait-placements.md)
476. **The fluid mosaic model of the cell membrane** (`biology/the-fluid-mosaic-model-of-the-cell-membrane`) — the cell membrane is a fluid mosaic: a double layer of phospholipids that can flow, with proteins scattered through it like tiles in a mosaic — and each kind of membrane protein does its own job (Kuwait — built for the Kuwait placements, expansion-biology-2026-11-13/kuwait-placements.md)
477. **Cell wall and extracellular matrix** (`biology/cell-wall-and-extracellular-matrix`) — outside the cell membrane there can be further structure: plants carry a cellulose cell wall, and animal cells sit in an extracellular matrix — the lesson covers the structures that lie outside the cell itself (Kuwait — built for the Kuwait placements, expansion-biology-2026-11-13/kuwait-placements.md)
478. **Cell cycle checkpoints and control** (`biology/cell-cycle-checkpoints-and-control`) — the cell cycle is controlled at checkpoints: at the G1/S checkpoint and the M checkpoint the cell verifies that conditions are right — growth factors present, DNA doubling completed, chromosomes aligned — and halts the cycle if a check fails (Kuwait — built for the Kuwait placements, expansion-biology-2026-11-13/kuwait-placements.md)
479. **Bulk transport — endocytosis and exocytosis** (`biology/bulk-transport-endocytosis-and-exocytosis`) — large particles cross the membrane in bulk, wrapped in membrane: endocytosis brings them in, and exocytosis sends them out — Golgi packs the material into secretory vesicles that fuse with the plasma membrane (Kuwait — built for the Kuwait placements, expansion-biology-2026-11-13/kuwait-placements.md)
480. **Cell junctions — tight, gap and desmosomes** (`biology/cell-junctions-tight-gap-and-desmosomes`) — neighbouring cells are joined by long-lived junctions built from protein fibres — tight junctions, gap junctions and desmosomes — each kind joining cells in its own way (Kuwait — built for the Kuwait placements, expansion-biology-2026-11-13/kuwait-placements.md)
481. **Animal tissues — epithelial, connective, muscle and nervous** (`biology/animal-tissues-epithelial-connective-muscle-and-nervous`) — an animal tissue is cells plus the intercellular matrix and fibres between them, and animal tissues fall into four kinds — epithelial, connective, muscle and nervous — each built for its job (Kuwait — built for the Kuwait placements, expansion-biology-2026-11-13/kuwait-placements.md)
482. **Bone structure and composition — ossein and minerals** (`biology/bone-structure-and-composition-ossein-and-minerals`) — bone is a composite: a protein part (ossein) and a mineral part — and the Grade 10 practical finds out what each part gives the bone by weighing and strength-testing matched bones before and after treatment (Kuwait — built for the Kuwait placements, expansion-biology-2026-11-13/kuwait-placements.md)
483. **Bone tissue histology — osteocytes and cartilage** (`biology/bone-tissue-histology-osteocytes-and-cartilage`) — bone tissue is organised: a periosteum wraps it, compact bone is built of Haversian systems and canals, spongy bone fills the ends, and red and yellow marrow sit inside — read on longitudinal and cross sections (Kuwait — built for the Kuwait placements, expansion-biology-2026-11-13/kuwait-placements.md)
484. **Osteoporosis and bone health** (`biology/osteoporosis-and-bone-health`) — in osteoporosis bones lose mass and mineral salts such as calcium, so fracture risk rises and the spine can curve — bone health is the health of the bone's material, not only its shape (Kuwait — built for the Kuwait placements, expansion-biology-2026-11-13/kuwait-placements.md)
485. **First aid for locomotor injuries — fractures, sprains, dislocations** (`biology/first-aid-for-locomotor-injuries-fractures-sprains-dislocations`) — locomotor injuries — muscle strains and tears among them — happen when a muscle or joint is stretched or loaded beyond its capacity; this lesson covers the injuries of the support-and-movement system and their first care (Kuwait — built for the Kuwait placements, expansion-biology-2026-11-13/kuwait-placements.md)
486. **Body symmetry and cephalisation** (`biology/body-symmetry-and-cephalisation`) — animal body plans are read by their symmetry — radial in simple animals like sea anemones, bilateral in most others — and cephalisation concentrates sense organs and nerve cells at the front end (Kuwait — built for the Kuwait placements, expansion-biology-2026-11-13/kuwait-placements.md)
487. **Flatworms — planaria and tapeworms** (`biology/flatworms-planaria-and-tapeworms`) — flatworms are among the first animals with three embryonic germ layers — ectoderm, endoderm and mesoderm — and the group runs from free-living planaria, whose eye spots are light-sensing cell clusters, to parasitic flukes and tapeworms with life cycles through their hosts (Kuwait — built for the Kuwait placements, expansion-biology-2026-11-13/kuwait-placements.md)
488. **Parasitic roundworms — Ascaris, pinworm, Trichina** (`biology/parasitic-roundworms-ascaris-pinworm-trichina`) — the roundworms are the phylum best known for species parasitic on their hosts, though free-living roundworms also hunt as predators with grasping mouthparts (Kuwait — built for the Kuwait placements, expansion-biology-2026-11-13/kuwait-placements.md)
489. **Birds — structure and adaptations for flight** (`biology/birds-structure-adaptations-for-flight`) — birds are built for flight: beaks matched to diet, no teeth, a highly efficient digestive system, air sacs driving air through the lungs in one path, nitrogen waste converted to uric acid, and long bones strong yet light with struts and air cavities (Kuwait — built for the Kuwait placements, expansion-biology-2026-11-13/kuwait-placements.md)
490. **Secondary growth — vascular cambium and wood formation** (`biology/secondary-growth-vascular-cambium-and-wood-formation`) — woody stems thicken by secondary growth: lateral meristems — the vascular cambium ring between xylem and phloem — lay down new tissue year by year, and that growth is the formation of wood (Kuwait — built for the Kuwait placements, expansion-biology-2026-11-13/kuwait-placements.md)
491. **Artificial propagation — cuttings, grafting and tissue culture** (`biology/artificial-propagation-cuttings-grafting-and-tissue-culture`) — farmers propagate plants artificially — from cuttings and by grafting — and the laboratory adds protoplast culture; flowering plants themselves add apomixis, seed without fertilisation (Kuwait — built for the Kuwait placements, expansion-biology-2026-11-13/kuwait-placements.md)
492. **Differential staining of plant sections — toluidine blue** (`biology/differential-staining-of-plant-sections-toluidine-blue`) — toluidine blue stains different plant tissues in different colours, so a thin hand-cut section of a plant stem under the microscope shows its tissues told apart by colour — differential staining (Kuwait — built for the Kuwait placements, expansion-biology-2026-11-13/kuwait-placements.md)
493. **Exploring inherited traits — a class survey** (`biology/exploring-inherited-traits-a-class-survey`) — inherited traits can be counted: survey the class trait by trait — earlobe form among them — and count how many show each trait and how many do not; the class itself becomes the data (Kuwait — built for the Kuwait placements, expansion-biology-2026-11-13/kuwait-placements.md)
494. **Linked inheritance and crossing over** (`biology/linked-inheritance-and-crossing-over`) — genes on the same chromosome are linked and are inherited together more often than Mendel's independent assortment predicts — a Punnett square for BbMm × bbmm shows the expected ratios, and linkage is what the real offspring do instead (Kuwait — built for the Kuwait placements, expansion-biology-2026-11-13/kuwait-placements.md)
495. **Modelling bile function — fat emulsification** (`biology/modelling-bile-function-fat-emulsification`) — bile's job on fats is emulsification: it breaks large fat globules into tiny droplets — and the action can be modelled with washing-up liquid and oil (Kuwait — built for the Kuwait placements, expansion-biology-2026-11-13/kuwait-placements.md)
496. **Peristalsis — how food moves through the gut** (`biology/peristalsis-how-food-moves-through-the-gut`) — food moves through the digestive canal by peristalsis: a wave-like muscular movement that pushes the food along, section by section (Kuwait — built for the Kuwait placements, expansion-biology-2026-11-13/kuwait-placements.md)
497. **The kidney — external and internal structure** (`biology/the-kidney-external-and-internal-structure`) — a sheep kidney cut open shows the organ's plan from outside and inside: the external form first, then the internal structures the cut reveals, each identified in place (Kuwait — built for the Kuwait placements, expansion-biology-2026-11-13/kuwait-placements.md)
498. **Effect of exercise on heart rate** (`biology/effect-of-exercise-on-heart-rate`) — exercise raises the heart rate: count the pulse at rest, exercise, count again — and analyse the change (Kuwait — built for the Kuwait placements, expansion-biology-2026-11-13/kuwait-placements.md)
499. **Heart dissection — structure of the mammalian heart** (`biology/heart-dissection-structure-of-the-mammalian-heart`) — a mammalian heart dissected following pictured steps shows its plan directly: chambers and valves identified on a numbered diagram, and the wall thicknesses of the chambers compared (Kuwait — built for the Kuwait placements, expansion-biology-2026-11-13/kuwait-placements.md)
500. **Somatic and autonomic (vegetative) nervous system** (`biology/somatic-and-autonomic-vegetative-nervous-system`) — the peripheral nervous system divides by control: the somatic system serves voluntary movement, while the autonomic (vegetative) system — sympathetic and parasympathetic divisions — runs its effectors through two-neuron chains (Kuwait — built for the Kuwait placements, expansion-biology-2026-11-13/kuwait-placements.md)
501. **Hygiene, dysfunctions and diseases of the nervous system** (`biology/hygiene-dysfunctions-and-diseases-of-the-nervous-system`) — the nervous system has its common disorders — multiple sclerosis among them — and its health is kept by named habits: the lesson covers the system's dysfunctions and how the system is kept healthy (Kuwait — built for the Kuwait placements, expansion-biology-2026-11-13/kuwait-placements.md)
502. **Endocrine dysfunctions — hypo- and hypersecretion and diagnosis** (`biology/endocrine-dysfunctions-hypo-and-hypersecretion-and-diagnosis`) — endocrine disorders are disorders of amount: under-secretion (hypo-) and over-secretion (hyper-) of a gland's hormone produce opposite diseases — and the applied case is the side effects of steroids (Kuwait — built for the Kuwait placements, expansion-biology-2026-11-13/kuwait-placements.md)
503. **Immune system health — allergy and autoimmune diseases** (`biology/immune-system-health-allergy-and-autoimmune-diseases`) — the immune system itself can be the problem: hypersensitivity and allergy are its over-reactions, autoimmune diseases its attacks on the body's own tissues — and transplant rejection is the same system defending against a graft (Kuwait — built for the Kuwait placements, expansion-biology-2026-11-13/kuwait-placements.md)
504. **Gene and chromosomal mutations** (`biology/gene-and-chromosomal-mutations`) — mutations come at two scales: chromosomal mutations — Robertsonian translocation between chromosomes 14 and 21, inversion, and numerical mutations — and gene mutations: deletion and insertion shifting the reading frame, and the single base substitution in the haemoglobin gene (Kuwait — built for the Kuwait placements, expansion-biology-2026-11-13/kuwait-placements.md)
505. **Risks of endogamy — consanguineous marriage** (`biology/risks-of-endogamy-consanguineous-marriage`) — endogamy — marriage between relatives — raises the chance that harmful recessive alleles meet in the children: what can follow when two related individuals marry is set out as its own section of human heredity (Kuwait — built for the Kuwait placements, expansion-biology-2026-11-13/kuwait-placements.md)
506. **Molecular and prenatal methods in human genetics — PCR and amniocentesis** (`biology/molecular-and-prenatal-methods-in-human-genetics-pcr-and-amniocentesis`) — human genetics works at the molecular scale and before birth: the Human Genome Project mapped the whole set, sequencing reads it, and prenatal screening means genetic testing is not limited to adults (Kuwait — built for the Kuwait placements, expansion-biology-2026-11-13/kuwait-placements.md)
507. **Prions, viroids and other acellular agents** (`biology/prions-viroids-and-other-acellular-agents`) — infectious agents smaller and simpler than true viruses: the viroid a small circular RNA molecule with no protein coat, the prion an infectious protein — a misfolded protein converting normal protein, taught through mad-cow disease (Jordan — *العلوم الحياتية* (Life Science), Grade 10 student textbook, Term 1, printed p. 30, and its Activities and Practical Experiments workbook, printed p. 14; the Grade 10 teacher guide's mad-cow script, guide pp. 30–33, cited as evidence only)
508. **Tree rings — reading past climates** (`biology/tree-rings-reading-past-climates`) — trees record local climate in their rings: thick in warm, wet years and thin in cold, dry or harsh years, so scientists read past climates from ring widths (Jordan — *العلوم الحياتية* (Life Science), Grade 11, Academic Track, Term 1 student textbook, enrichment, printed p. 32)
509. **Telomeres and cellular ageing** (`biology/telomeres-and-cellular-ageing`) — the caps at the ends of chromosomes, and their shortening as the cell ages (Jordan — *العلوم الحياتية* (Life Science), Grade 12, Academic Track, Term 1 student textbook, enrichment, printed p. 102)
510. **Protist algae — dinoflagellates, euglenoids and diatoms** (`biology/protist-algae-dinoflagellates-euglenoids-diatoms`) — The protist algae are sorted into phyla by their pigments, walls and tails: the Euglenophyta; the Chrysophyta, silica-walled algae of the marine plankton (the diatoms); and the Pyrrophyta — the dinoflagellates, with two flagella and a bioluminescence that sparkles on night wave-crests — and together the algae are the food base on which aquatic heterotrophs live. (Mozambique — *Módulo 1 de Biologia — Sistemática, Monera e fungos*, student module, Lesson 9, protist algae and their importance, printed p. 49; built for the Mozambique placements, expansion-biology-2026-11-13/mozambique-placements.md)
511. **Bryophytes — mosses and liverworts** (`biology/bryophytes-mosses-and-liverworts`) — Bryophytes are the amphibians of the plant kingdom: small avascular plants — under about 30 cm — protected by a cuticle but still needing liquid water for fertilisation; the liverworts have a flat thalloid body, while the mosses have rhizoids, cauloids and phylloids, as in the module's example, Funaria. (Mozambique — *Módulo 2 de Biologia — Reino Plantae*, student module, Lesson 3, phylum Bryophyta, printed p. 14; built for the Mozambique placements, expansion-biology-2026-11-13/mozambique-placements.md)
512. **Alternation of generations — the Funaria life cycle** (`biology/alternation-of-generations-funaria-life-cycle`) — In Funaria the generations alternate with the haploid gametophyte on top: the familiar moss plant is the gametophyte; its antherozoids swim through a drop of water to the archegonium, and the zygote grows into the diploid sporophyte, which stays dependent on the gametophyte and makes the spores in its capsule that start the cycle again. (Mozambique — *Módulo 2 de Biologia — Reino Plantae*, student module, Lesson 4, life cycle of Funaria, printed p. 19; built for the Mozambique placements, expansion-biology-2026-11-13/mozambique-placements.md)
513. **Porifera — sponges: body plan and feeding** (`biology/porifera-sponges-body-plan-and-feeding`) — Sponges are the simplest animals: they have no true tissues, stay fixed in one place, and feed by driving water through their bodies — in through many small pores and out through one big opening, the osculum. (Mozambique — *Módulo 3 de Biologia — Reino Animal*, student module, Lesson 2, phylum Porifera, printed p. 11; built for the Mozambique placements, expansion-biology-2026-11-13/mozambique-placements.md)
514. **Cnidaria — polyps, medusae and stinging cells** (`biology/cnidaria-polyps-medusae-and-stinging-cells`) — Cnidarians are radially symmetrical animals with two body forms — the fixed polyp and the free-swimming medusa, a medusa being an inverted polyp — one gastrovascular cavity for digestion, and stinging cells, the cnidoblasts with their nematocysts, as the phylum's signature. (Mozambique — *Módulo 3 de Biologia — Reino Animal*, student module, Lesson 3, phylum Cnidaria (Coelenterata), printed p. 18; built for the Mozambique placements, expansion-biology-2026-11-13/mozambique-placements.md)
515. **Flatworms — planaria, tapeworm and liver fluke** (`biology/flatworms-planaria-tapeworm-and-liver-fluke`) — Flatworms are dorsoventrally flattened, bilateral, acoelomate worms with three germ layers, a single gut opening and both sexes in one body — a group that runs from the free-living planarian to two parasites with worked life cycles: the tapeworm (Taenia), ending in the human definitive host, and the liver fluke (Fasciola), passing through sheep and snail hosts. (Mozambique — *Módulo 3 de Biologia — Reino Animal*, student module, Lessons 4–5, phylum Platyhelminthes, printed p. 24; built for the Mozambique placements, expansion-biology-2026-11-13/mozambique-placements.md)
516. **Nematodes — Ascaris, hookworm and filaria** (`biology/nematodes-ascaris-hookworm-and-filaria`) — Nematodes are cylindrical pseudocoelomate worms with a complete gut — mouth and anus — and a tough cuticle; the module works three parasites one by one: Ascaris, the hookworm that sucks blood and enters through the skin, and Wuchereria bancrofti, the filaria carried by mosquitoes whose larvae appear in the peripheral blood at night. (Mozambique — *Módulo 3 de Biologia — Reino Animal*, student module, Lessons 6–8, phylum Nemathelminthes and its parasites, printed p. 37; built for the Mozambique placements, expansion-biology-2026-11-13/mozambique-placements.md)
517. **Mollusca — snails, bivalves and cephalopods** (`biology/mollusca-snails-bivalves-and-cephalopods`) — Molluscs are soft, unsegmented animals whose body has three regions — head, visceral mass and foot — usually with a shell and a scraping tongue, the radula; Mozambique works the plan through two classes: the gastropods, with their spiral shell and eyes on tentacle tips, and the bivalves, with two hinged valves, an axe-shaped foot and no distinct head. (Mozambique — *Módulo 3 de Biologia — Reino Animal*, student module, Lessons 11–12, phylum Mollusca, printed p. 64; built for the Mozambique placements, expansion-biology-2026-11-13/mozambique-placements.md)
518. **Echinodermata and the water-vascular system** (`biology/echinodermata-and-the-water-vascular-system`) — Echinoderms are marine animals with pentaradial (five-sided) symmetry, an internal skeleton of calcareous plates carrying spines, and a water-vascular (ambulacral) system — a network of water canals found in no other phylum — with reduced circulation, no excretory organs, separate sexes and a larval metamorphosis. (Mozambique — *Módulo 3 de Biologia — Reino Animal*, student module, Lesson 13, phylum Echinodermata, printed p. 74; built for the Mozambique placements, expansion-biology-2026-11-13/mozambique-placements.md)
519. **Reptiles — the amniotic egg and embryonic membranes** (`biology/reptiles-amniotic-egg-and-embryonic-membranes`) — Reptiles are the first fully terrestrial vertebrates: a thick waterproof skin of keratin scales keeps water in, fertilisation is internal, development is direct, and the amniotic egg — with its four embryonic annexes, chorion, amnion, allantois and yolk sac — lets the embryo grow up inside its own private pond, away from water. (Mozambique — *Módulo 3 de Biologia — Reino Animal*, student module, Lesson 17, reptiles, printed p. 94; built for the Mozambique placements, expansion-biology-2026-11-13/mozambique-placements.md)
520. **Plant tissues — meristems and definitive tissues** (`biology/plant-tissues-meristems-and-definitive-tissues`) — A plant is built from tissues that all begin in its meristems: primary meristems lengthen the plant, secondary meristems thicken root and stem, and the meristems produce the four definitive tissues — covering, parenchyma (storage), support and vascular (conducting) tissues. (Mozambique — *Módulo 5 de Biologia — Fisiologia Vegetal*, student module, Lesson 1, plant physiology/histology, printed p. 5; built for the Mozambique placements, expansion-biology-2026-11-13/mozambique-placements.md)
521. **Stomata — opening, closing and transpiration** (`biology/stomata-opening-closing-and-transpiration`) — A stoma opens when its two guard cells take up water and swell with turgidity, and closes when they lose it; the water vapour a plant loses through its open stomata is transpiration — and the same lesson introduces the plastids, double-membraned organelles found only in photosynthetic organisms, the chloroplast among them. (Mozambique — *Módulo 5 de Biologia — Fisiologia Vegetal*, student module, Lesson 4, stomata and plastids, printed p. 20; built for the Mozambique placements, expansion-biology-2026-11-13/mozambique-placements.md)
522. **Embryonic development — segmentation, gastrulation and organogenesis** (`biology/embryonic-development-segmentation-gastrulation-organogenesis`) — Development runs continuously from the egg through three phases — segmentation (cleavage divisions), gastrulation (the cell territories move into place and the germ layers form, the mesoderm arising from the primitive gut) and organogenesis (the organs are built) — and it starts from an egg with two poles, animal and vegetative, classified by how much yolk it carries. (Mozambique — *Módulo 7 de Biologia — Nervoso, endócrino, sentidos, reprodução e ontogénese*, student module, Lessons 14–15, ontogenesis, printed p. 99; built for the Mozambique placements, expansion-biology-2026-11-13/mozambique-placements.md)
523. **Transpiration and sap movement in plants** (`biology/transpiration-and-sap-movement-in-plants`) — A plant loses about 99% of the water it absorbs by transpiration — its biggest problem, met by adaptation mechanisms — and that same loss helps pull the raw sap (seiva bruta) up through the stem from roots to leaves, while the elaborated sap (seiva elaborada) flows out of the leaves to feed the rest of the plant. (Mozambique — *Biologia — Módulo 1 (PESD I, 2023)*, student module, Lesson 9, plants — transpiration, printed p. 59; built for the Mozambique placements, expansion-biology-2026-11-13/mozambique-placements.md)
524. **Plant organ adaptations — roots, stems and leaves** (`biology/plant-organ-adaptations-roots-stems-leaves`) — Plant organs are reshaped by the environment: roots may live in water, climb into the air as maize prop roots, or swell with stored food; stems climb by tendrils or hide underground as the banana's rhizome (its apparent trunk being only leaf sheaths); and leaves shrink to spines or flatten into cladodes to save water. (Mozambique — *Biologia — Módulo 1 (PESD I, 2023)*, student module, Lesson 11, adaptation of plant organs, printed p. 73; built for the Mozambique placements, expansion-biology-2026-11-13/mozambique-placements.md)
525. **Intestinal parasites — ascariasis, tapeworm and pinworm** (`biology/intestinal-parasites-ascariasis-tapeworm-pinworm`) — The food-borne intestinal parasitoses are learned worm by worm: ascariasis, from Ascaris — usually asymptomatic, but able to cause abdominal pain, appetite and weight loss, nausea, diarrhoea and even intestinal obstruction; the tapeworms Taenia solium, from raw or undercooked pork, and T. saginata, from beef; and the pinworm, whose transmission includes a direct route — anus to mouth, after scratching. (Mozambique — *Biologia — Módulo 2 (PESD I, 2023)*, student module, Lesson 10, printed p. 74–77; built for the Mozambique placements, expansion-biology-2026-11-13/mozambique-placements.md)
526. **Medicines — safe use, storage and allergy** (`biology/medicines-safe-use-storage-and-allergy`) — Medicines need care like any powerful tool: they are stored away from bathrooms, sinks and cleaning materials, and anyone taking a medicine should know the symptoms of an allergic reaction to it — the closing lesson of Mozambique's PESD I Módulo 4 puts medicine care inside the biology course itself. (Mozambique — *Biologia — Módulo 4 (PESD I, 2023)*, student module, Lesson 17, medicines, printed p. 127; built for the Mozambique placements, expansion-biology-2026-11-13/mozambique-placements.md)
527. **Stigma, prejudice and inclusion in health** (`biology/stigma-prejudice-and-inclusion-in-health`) — Stigma is a mark that pushes a person out — individual stigma, as around alcoholism, mental illness or homosexuality, and tribal stigma, by race or religion; prejudice is judgement made without critical analysis; and against intolerance and exclusion the lesson sets inclusion, taught as human-rights content inside Mozambique's biology course. (Mozambique — *Biologia — Módulo 4 (PESD I, 2023)*, student module, Lesson 15 (unnumbered in the ÍNDICE sequence between 14 and 16), printed p. 112; built for the Mozambique placements, expansion-biology-2026-11-13/mozambique-placements.md)
528. **Pheromones — chemical signals between animals** (`biology/pheromones-chemical-signals-between-animals`) — pheromones are chemical signals an animal releases that carry a message to others of its own species, taught through the silkworm moth (Bombyx mori) and turned to work in pheromone mass-trapping of male pests (Vietnam — *Sinh học 11*, student textbook, Grade 11, Bài 18 IV, printed p. 118, and *Chuyên đề học tập Sinh học 12*, student specialisation book, Grade 12, Topic 2, Bài 5, printed p. 39; built for the Vietnam placements, expansion-biology-2026-11-13/vietnam-placements.md)
529. **Hydroponics — growing plants without soil** (`biology/hydroponics-growing-plants-without-soil`) — plants grown with their roots in a nutrient solution: the full model of tank, channels, pump and aeration, solution and pH control, disinfected channels and a 2–3 day clean-water flush that clears nitrate before harvest, giving 5–6 lettuce crops a year against 2–3 in soil (Vietnam — *Chuyên đề học tập Sinh học 11*, student specialisation book, Grade 11, Topic 1, Bài 2, printed p. 12; built for the Vietnam placements, expansion-biology-2026-11-13/vietnam-placements.md)
530. **Food poisoning and food safety** (`biology/food-poisoning-and-food-safety`) — food poisoning defined by Vietnam's 2010 Food Safety Law, its causes in four groups, and its agents named: bacteria and their toxins (Staphylococcus aureus enterotoxins A–E, Bacillus cereus heat-stable toxins among them), food-borne viruses, and parasites including the small liver fluke that reaches people through raw fish salad (gỏi cá) (Vietnam — *Chuyên đề học tập Sinh học 11*, student specialisation book, Grade 11, Topic 3, Bài 10, printed p. 45; built for the Vietnam placements, expansion-biology-2026-11-13/vietnam-placements.md)
531. **Genetic counselling and prenatal diagnosis** (`biology/genetic-counselling-and-prenatal-diagnosis`) — a family's risk calculated from pedigrees and population genetics, the unborn child tested by amniocentesis (ultrasound-guided, about 20 mL of fluid, culture, analysis) or chorionic villus sampling at 11–13 weeks, advice against consanguineous marriage, and newborn screening after the birth, with the galactosaemia diet as the worked case (Vietnam — *Sinh học 12*, student textbook, Grade 12, Bài 13 III, printed pp. 71–74; built for the Vietnam placements, expansion-biology-2026-11-13/vietnam-placements.md)
532. **Biological control of pests** (`biology/biological-control-of-pests`) — pests fought with natural enemies and biological products instead of poison alone: its limits stated honestly (slow, cannot answer an outbreak, costly) inside integrated pest management, the sterile-insect technique against Aedes aegypti and Vietnam's oriental fruit fly Bactrocera dorsalis, Bt and other resistance genes, pheromone mass-trapping, and the six-step workflow for conserving natural enemies (Vietnam — *Chuyên đề học tập Sinh học 12*, student specialisation book, Grade 12, Topic 2, Bài 5–7, printed pp. 33–54; built for the Vietnam placements, expansion-biology-2026-11-13/vietnam-placements.md)
533. **Human ecology — people inside ecosystems** (`biology/human-ecology-people-inside-ecosystems`) — human ecology as a named field, valued for sustainable development and worked through the city as a human-made ecosystem: about 3% of Earth's land, about 75% of world energy use, about 85% of greenhouse-gas emissions (IPCC 2022), Vietnam's urbanisation at 40.5% in 2021 heading to about 45% by 2026, and the eco-city criteria with Hanoi's 2030/2050 green-city plan (Vietnam — *Chuyên đề học tập Sinh học 12*, student specialisation book, Grade 12, Topic 3, Bài 8, printed p. 58 and Bài 9 II, printed pp. 64–66; built for the Vietnam placements, expansion-biology-2026-11-13/vietnam-placements.md)
534. **Energy requirements and BMR** (`biology/energy-requirements-and-bmr`) — How much energy a person needs is not one number for everybody: Kenya's Form 1 book closes its nutrition chapter by working out what sets the requirement — basal metabolic rate, occupation, age, body size, sex, lactation and pregnancy — with a Kenyan labourer pulling a cart set against an office worker. (Kenya — *Secondary Biology Form One Students' Book (Fifth Edition)*, student textbook, grade-09 (Form 1), Ch. 5 Nutrition in Plants and Animals, printed p. 88; built for the Kenya placements, expansion-biology-2026-11-13/kenya-placements.md)
535. **Gaseous exchange in plants** (`biology/gaseous-exchange-in-plants`) — A plant is exchanging gases all the time, in two directions at once: Kenya's Form 2 book teaches plant gaseous exchange as the day/night balance of photosynthesis and respiration, proved with a bicarbonate-indicator investigation and by collecting the gas a water plant gives off. (Kenya — *Secondary Biology Form Two Students' Book (Fifth Edition)*, student textbook, grade-10 (Form 2), Ch. 2 Gaseous Exchange, printed p. 56; built for the Kenya placements, expansion-biology-2026-11-13/kenya-placements.md)
536. **Gaseous exchange in insects and fish** (`biology/gaseous-exchange-in-insects-and-fish`) — An insect breathes through a pipework of air tubes and a fish through feathery gills worked by pumping and counter-current flow: Kenya's Form 2 book teaches the two systems side by side, with a breathing-movement practical on a grasshopper, locust or cockroach and a gill dissection. (Kenya — *Secondary Biology Form Two Students' Book (Fifth Edition)*, student textbook, grade-10 (Form 2), Ch. 2 Gaseous Exchange, printed p. 63, printed p. 66; built for the Kenya placements, expansion-biology-2026-11-13/kenya-placements.md)
537. **Respiratory quotient and respiratory substrates** (`biology/respiratory-quotient-and-respiratory-substrates`) — What a cell is burning can be read off the gases: Kenya's Form 2 book closes its respiration chapter with the respiratory substrates — carbohydrates, fats at about 38 kJ/g, and proteins — and the respiratory quotient, RQ = CO₂ produced ÷ O₂ used, about 0.7 for fat, 1.0 for carbohydrate and 0.9 for protein. (Kenya — *Secondary Biology Form Two Students' Book (Fifth Edition)*, student textbook, grade-10 (Form 2), Ch. 3 Respiration, printed p. 92; built for the Kenya placements, expansion-biology-2026-11-13/kenya-placements.md)
538. **Diabetes mellitus and its control** (`biology/diabetes-mellitus-and-its-control`) — Diabetes mellitus is what happens when insulin fails: Kenya's Form 2 book teaches it inside homeostasis — insulin deficiency, its symptoms (glucose in the urine, thirst, weight loss), and its control by insulin injections, diet and exercise. (Kenya — *Secondary Biology Form Two Students' Book (Fifth Edition)*, student textbook, grade-10 (Form 2), Ch. 4 Excretion and Homeostasis, printed p. 121; built for the Kenya placements, expansion-biology-2026-11-13/kenya-placements.md)
