660. **Acceleration — earlier growth and maturation** (`biology/acceleration-earlier-growth-and-maturation`) — Children in recent generations grow taller and mature faster than earlier generations — acceleration — read off height-by-age graphs for boys and girls: a change in the pace of development itself, not in its stages. (Kazakhstan — *Биология — 7-сынып (NIS Biology, Grade 7), Kazakh-language edition (record 1039)*, student textbook, §9.4, printed p. 103; built for the Kazakhstan placements, expansion-biology-2026-11-13/kazakhstan-placements.md)
661. **Antibiotics and antibiotic resistance** (`biology/antibiotics-and-antibiotic-resistance`) — Antibiotics kill bacteria — Fleming's discovery of penicillin in 1928 opened the antibiotic age — but bacteria evolve resistance, so an antibiotic that once cured can fail, as with MRSA. (Kazakhstan — *Biology — Grade 8 Student's Book (O'Callaghan et al., Express Publishing) (record 368)*; *Биология — 7-сынып (NIS Biology, Grade 7), Kazakh-language edition (record 1039)*, student textbook, §10.3, printed p. 115; Module 4: Antibiotics and resistance — questions name the common antibiotic-resistant bacteria in Kazakhstan, printed p. 57; built for the Kazakhstan placements, expansion-biology-2026-11-13/kazakhstan-placements.md)
662. **Artificial ecosystems and agrocenoses** (`biology/artificial-ecosystems-and-agrocenoses`) — People build artificial ecosystems — fields and orchards, which are agrocenoses, and cities and oil fields — and unlike natural ecosystems they cannot hold themselves steady without constant human work, energy and materials. (Kazakhstan — *БИОЛОГИЯ 7 (Solovyova et al., Atamura, first edition 2017), Kazakh-language edition (record 245)*; *Биология 7 (Ochkur & Kurmanalieva, Mektep, first edition 2017), Kazakh-language edition (record 246)*; *Биология 7 (Ochkur et al., Mektep 2025), Kazakh-language edition (record 1476)*; *Биология 7 (Solovyova, Atamura 2025), Russian-language edition (record 1519)*, student textbook, §4 Humans as part of the ecosystem, printed p. 18; §4 Human — part of the ecosystem, printed p. 22; §4 Human — a part of the ecosystem, printed p. 15; §5 The relationship between human and ecosystem, printed p. 22; built for the Kazakhstan placements, expansion-biology-2026-11-13/kazakhstan-placements.md)
663. **How roots absorb water and mineral salts** (`biology/how-roots-absorb-water-and-mineral-salts`) — A root does its absorbing through its zones — cap, division, growth, absorption and conduction — and in the absorption zone its root hairs take in water and mineral salts and pass them up into the plant. (Kazakhstan — *Биология 7 (Solovyova, Atamura 2025), Russian-language edition (record 1519)*, student textbook, §18 Root zones, printed p. 80; built for the Kazakhstan placements, expansion-biology-2026-11-13/kazakhstan-placements.md)
664. **Respiration in plants** (`biology/respiration-in-plants`) — Plants respire as animals do — every living cell takes in oxygen and releases energy from food — but with no special breathing organs: air moves in through stomata, lenticels and root surfaces, day and night, even while photosynthesis runs the other way in the light. (Kazakhstan — *BIOLOGY 7 (Sagintayev et al., Astana-kitap), English-language edition (record 1582)*; *БИОЛОГИЯ 7 (Solovyova et al., Atamura, first edition 2017), Kazakh-language edition (record 245)*; *Биология 7 (Ochkur & Kurmanalieva, Mektep, first edition 2017), Kazakh-language edition (record 246)*; *Биология 7 (Ochkur et al., Mektep 2025), Kazakh-language edition (record 1476)*; *Биология 7 (Solovyova, Atamura 2025), Russian-language edition (record 1519)*; *Биология — 8-сынып (NIS Biology, Grade 8), Kazakh-language edition (record 1069)*, student textbook, §25 Respiration of plants, printed p. 108; §27 Respiration of plants, printed p. 135; 7.2 Respiration in plants (Laboratory work-7), printed p. 104; §25 Respiration of plants, printed p. 92; §29 Respiration of plants, printed p. 121; 7.3 Respiration and excretion of plants, printed p. 78; built for the Kazakhstan placements, expansion-biology-2026-11-13/kazakhstan-placements.md)
665. **Evolution of excretory systems in animals** (`biology/evolution-of-excretory-systems-in-animals`) — Excretory systems evolve along the animal line — from contractile vacuoles and worm tubules to the vertebrate kidney — while the waste itself changes with habitat: ammonia where water is plentiful, urea, and uric acid where water must be saved. (Kazakhstan — *BIOLOGY 7 (Sagintayev et al., Astana-kitap), English-language edition (record 1582)*; *BIOLOGY Grade 9 — Bilingual textbook (CLIL), Kazakh-text version (record 422)*; *Biology Grade 9 Student's Book (O'Callaghan et al., Express Publishing) (record 475)*; *БИОЛОГИЯ 7 (Solovyova et al., Atamura, first edition 2017), Kazakh-language edition (record 245)*; *БИОЛОГИЯ 9 (Asanov, Solovyova & Ibraimova, Atamura 2019), Kazakh-language edition (record 402)*; *Биология 7 (Ochkur & Kurmanalieva, Mektep, first edition 2017), Kazakh-language edition (record 246)*; *Биология 7 (Ochkur et al., Mektep 2025), Kazakh-language edition (record 1476)*; *Биология 7 (Solovyova, Atamura 2025), Russian-language edition (record 1519)*; *Биология — 9-сынып (NIS Biology, Grade 9), Kazakh-language TRIAL (draft) edition (record 1085)*, student textbook, §31 Evolution of the excretory system of animals, printed p. 132; §33 Excretory systems of animals, printed p. 162; 8.3 Animals excretion, printed p. 124; §31 Evolution of the urinary system of animals, printed p. 112; §35 Structure of the urinary (excretory) systems of invertebrate and vertebrate animals, printed p. 143; 3.1 Systems of excretion, printed p. 52; §21 Excretory products of living organisms, printed p. 90; 7.4 Nitrogenous wastes, PDF p. 136; Module 5: Excretion in the animal kingdom — ammonia, urea and uric acid matched to habitat (Table 5.1), printed p. 73; built for the Kazakhstan placements, expansion-biology-2026-11-13/kazakhstan-placements.md)
666. **Improving memory and protecting the nervous system** (`biology/improving-memory-and-protecting-the-nervous-system`) — Memory can be improved by named methods — repetition, association and a regular regime — while alcohol, nicotine and drugs harm the work of the nervous system, so training memory and protecting the system belong to one lesson. (Kazakhstan — *BIOLOGY 7 (Sagintayev et al., Astana-kitap), English-language edition (record 1582)*; *БИОЛОГИЯ 7 (Solovyova et al., Atamura, first edition 2017), Kazakh-language edition (record 245)*; *Биология 7 (Ochkur & Kurmanalieva, Mektep, first edition 2017), Kazakh-language edition (record 246)*; *Биология 7 (Ochkur et al., Mektep 2025), Kazakh-language edition (record 1476)*; *Биология 7 (Solovyova, Atamura 2025), Russian-language edition (record 1519)*; *Биология — 9-сынып (NIS Biology, Grade 9), Kazakh-language TRIAL (draft) edition (record 1085)*, student textbook, §47 Ways to improve memory. The effect of harmful factors on the work of the nervous system, printed p. 164; §49 The harmful effect of alcohol, smoking and narcotic substances on the nervous system, printed p. 193; 7.6 The effect of harmful substances on the nervous system, printed p. 125; built for the Kazakhstan placements, expansion-biology-2026-11-13/kazakhstan-placements.md)
667. **Variation — hereditary and non-hereditary** (`biology/variation-hereditary-and-non-hereditary`) — Organisms vary in two ways: hereditary variation, carried in their genes and passed to their offspring, and non-hereditary — modification — variation, shaped by living conditions and not inherited; telling the two apart is the first step of genetics. (Kazakhstan — *Biology Grade 9 Student's Book (O'Callaghan et al., Express Publishing) (record 475)*; *БИОЛОГИЯ 7 (Solovyova et al., Atamura, first edition 2017), Kazakh-language edition (record 245)*; *Биология 7 (Ochkur & Kurmanalieva, Mektep, first edition 2017), Kazakh-language edition (record 246)*; *Биология 7 (Solovyova, Atamura 2025), Russian-language edition (record 1519)*; *Биология — 8-сынып (NIS Biology, Grade 8), Kazakh-language edition (record 1069)*, student textbook, §50 Hereditary and acquired traits, printed p. 208; §50 Hereditary and acquired traits, printed p. 175; §50 Hereditary and non-hereditary changes in the human organism, printed p. 197; 10.2 Variation and its types, printed p. 118; Module 8: Activity 8.1 — demonstrating variation within the human population (a PTC-tasting class survey), printed p. 110; built for the Kazakhstan placements, expansion-biology-2026-11-13/kazakhstan-placements.md)
668. **Measuring growth of plants** (`biology/measuring-growth-of-plants`) — Plant growth is a process you can measure — elongation growth at the shoot and root tips and thickening growth of the stem — followed with marks, rulers and graphs over days and weeks. (Kazakhstan — *BIOLOGY 7 (Sagintayev et al., Astana-kitap), English-language edition (record 1582)*; *БИОЛОГИЯ 7 (Solovyova et al., Atamura, first edition 2017), Kazakh-language edition (record 245)*; *Биология 7 (Ochkur & Kurmanalieva, Mektep, first edition 2017), Kazakh-language edition (record 246)*; *Биология 7 (Ochkur et al., Mektep 2025), Kazakh-language edition (record 1476)*; *Биология 7 (Solovyova, Atamura 2025), Russian-language edition (record 1519)*, student textbook, §59 Growth of plants, printed p. 239; §59 Growth of plants. Elongation and thickening growth of the stem, printed p. 257; 12.6 Growth of plants (Laboratory work-11), printed p. 202; §59 Growth of plants, printed p. 205; §61 Processes of elongation and thickening growth in plants, printed p. 232; built for the Kazakhstan placements, expansion-biology-2026-11-13/kazakhstan-placements.md)
669. **Transport of substances in multicellular organisms** (`biology/transport-of-substances-in-multicellular-organisms`) — A multicellular organism is too big for diffusion alone, so substances must be carried: in plants through their conducting tissues and in animals through blood and lymph, from where a substance is taken in or made to every cell that needs it. (Kazakhstan — *BIOLOGY 7 (Sagintayev et al., Astana-kitap), English-language edition (record 1582)*; *БИОЛОГИЯ 7 (Solovyova et al., Atamura, first edition 2017), Kazakh-language edition (record 245)*; *Биология 7 (Ochkur & Kurmanalieva, Mektep, first edition 2017), Kazakh-language edition (record 246)*; *Биология 7 (Ochkur et al., Mektep 2025), Kazakh-language edition (record 1476)*, student textbook, §16 The importance of transport of substances for the life activity of living organisms, printed p. 86; 5.1 Importance of nutrient transport, printed p. 72; §16 Transport of substances, printed p. 62; §19 The importance of nutrient transport for living organisms, printed p. 79; built for the Kazakhstan placements, expansion-biology-2026-11-13/kazakhstan-placements.md)
670. **Photoperiodism — day length and plant life** (`biology/photoperiodism-day-length-and-plant-life`) — Plants measure the length of the day and the night — photoperiodism — and use it to time flowering and other seasonal steps, so a plant's calendar is set by light. (Kazakhstan — *BIOLOGY 7 (Sagintayev et al., Astana-kitap), English-language edition (record 1582)*; *БИОЛОГИЯ 7 (Solovyova et al., Atamura, first edition 2017), Kazakh-language edition (record 245)*; *Биология 7 (Ochkur & Kurmanalieva, Mektep, first edition 2017), Kazakh-language edition (record 246)*; *Биология 7 (Ochkur et al., Mektep 2025), Kazakh-language edition (record 1476)*, student textbook, §35 Photoperiodism in plants, printed p. 171; 9.2 Photoperiodism, printed p. 136; §33 Photoperiodism in plants, printed p. 120; §37 Photoperiodism in plants, printed p. 152; built for the Kazakhstan placements, expansion-biology-2026-11-13/kazakhstan-placements.md)
671. **Chromosome numbers in different organisms** (`biology/chromosome-numbers-in-different-organisms`) — Each species carries its own characteristic chromosome number, constant in every body cell and every generation — cattle 60, sheep 54, pig 38, chicken 78 — a fixed mark of the species. (Kazakhstan — *BIOLOGY 7 (Sagintayev et al., Astana-kitap), English-language edition (record 1582)*; *Biology Grade 9 Student's Book (O'Callaghan et al., Express Publishing) (record 475)*; *БИОЛОГИЯ 7 (Solovyova et al., Atamura, first edition 2017), Kazakh-language edition (record 245)*; *Биология 7 (Ochkur & Kurmanalieva, Mektep, first edition 2017), Kazakh-language edition (record 246)*; *Биология 7 (Ochkur et al., Mektep 2025), Kazakh-language edition (record 1476)*; *Биология — 8-сынып (NIS Biology, Grade 8), Kazakh-language edition (record 1069)*, student textbook, §52 The number of chromosomes in different organisms, printed p. 230; 11.3 Number and types of chromosomes, printed p. 182; §51 The reproduction period and chromosome number, printed p. 178; §53 The role of chromosome sets in different species, printed p. 204; 9.2 Number and location of chromosomes in different organisms, printed p. 104; Module 7: Chromosome numbers in agricultural animals (Table 7.1: cattle 60, sheep 54, pig 38, chicken 78), printed p. 96; built for the Kazakhstan placements, expansion-biology-2026-11-13/kazakhstan-placements.md)
672. **Elements of life in the body** (`biology/elements-of-life-in-the-body`) — A living body is built from chemical elements — macroelements taken in bulk and microelements in traces — each with its own role in the life of organisms, from the carbon and oxygen of every molecule to the iron in blood. (Kazakhstan — *BIOLOGY 7 (Sagintayev et al., Astana-kitap), English-language edition (record 1582)*; *Биология — 8-сынып (NIS Biology, Grade 8), Kazakh-language edition (record 1069)*, student textbook, 4.2 Chemical elements in human body, printed p. 58; 3.3 The role of macro- and microelements in the life of organisms, printed p. 32; built for the Kazakhstan placements, expansion-biology-2026-11-13/kazakhstan-placements.md)
673. **Centres of origin of cultivated plants** (`biology/centres-of-origin-of-cultivated-plants`) — Vavilov showed that every cultivated plant comes from a centre of origin — a region where its wild relatives grow and its diversity is greatest — and the same mapping of centres was made for domestic animals. (Kazakhstan — *Biology — Grade 8 Student's Book (O'Callaghan et al., Express Publishing) (record 368)*; *БИОЛОГИЯ 8 (Solovyova & Ibraimova, Atamura 2018), Kazakh-language edition (record 311)*; *Биология — 8-сынып (NIS Biology, Grade 8), Kazakh-language edition (record 1069)*, student textbook, 10.4 Centres of origin of cultivated plants, printed p. 122; §52 Centres of origin of cultivated plants and domestic animals, printed p. 209; Module 9: Research tasks on Vavilov's centre of origin of cultivated plants and on Kazakhstan's cultivated plant and livestock varieties, printed p. 114; built for the Kazakhstan placements, expansion-biology-2026-11-13/kazakhstan-placements.md)
674. **Life-history strategies — r and K selection** (`biology/life-history-strategies-r-and-k-selection`) — Organisms hold their place by two broad life strategies: r-strategists make many offspring with little care, and K-strategists make few offspring with much care — the ecological strategies of a species' viability. (Kazakhstan — *Биология — 8-сынып (NIS Biology, Grade 8), Kazakh-language edition (record 1069)*, student textbook, 11.4 Ecological strategies of organisms' viability, printed p. 134; built for the Kazakhstan placements, expansion-biology-2026-11-13/kazakhstan-placements.md)
675. **Ecological problems of Kazakhstan** (`biology/ecological-problems-of-kazakhstan`) — Kazakhstan teaches its own ecological problems as a named topic — oil and mineral extraction and processing, invasive species such as ragweed and nutria, and pressure on its steppes, parks and waters — with their causes and consequences inside the country itself. (Kazakhstan — *Biology Grade 9 Student's Book (O'Callaghan et al., Express Publishing) (record 475)*; *Biology — Grade 8 Student's Book (O'Callaghan et al., Express Publishing) (record 368)*; *БИОЛОГИЯ 8 (Solovyova & Ibraimova, Atamura 2018), Kazakh-language edition (record 311)*; *БИОЛОГИЯ 9 (Asanov, Solovyova & Ibraimova, Atamura 2019), Kazakh-language edition (record 402)*; *Биология — 8-сынып (NIS Biology, Grade 8), Kazakh-language edition (record 1069)*; *Биология — 9-сынып (NIS Biology, Grade 9), Kazakh-language TRIAL (draft) edition (record 1085)*, student textbook, 11.5 Ecological problems of Kazakhstan, printed p. 136; §60 Ecological problems of Kazakhstan, printed p. 242; Module 10: Invasive species in Kazakhstan (ragweed, nutria) and national-park research tasks, printed p. 129; 15.2 The impact of developing oil and other mineral resources on the environment and human health, printed p. 242; 15.4 Ecological problems of the Republic of Kazakhstan. Causes and consequences, printed p. 247; §7 The impact of extracting and processing minerals on the environment and human health, printed p. 32; Module 2: Environmental impact of petroleum — drilling, refining, transportation and petrochemical end-uses, printed p. 38; built for the Kazakhstan placements, expansion-biology-2026-11-13/kazakhstan-placements.md)
676. **Skin diseases and skin hygiene** (`biology/skin-diseases-and-skin-hygiene`) — The skin has its own diseases — infectious, allergic and parasitic — and skin hygiene is their prevention: washing, individual towels and care of small wounds keep the body's cover healthy. (Kazakhstan — *БИОЛОГИЯ 8 (Solovyova & Ibraimova, Atamura 2018), Kazakh-language edition (record 311)*, student textbook, §30 Skin diseases and hygiene, printed p. 126; built for the Kazakhstan placements, expansion-biology-2026-11-13/kazakhstan-placements.md)
677. **Biomechanics of upright walking** (`biology/biomechanics-of-upright-walking`) — Upright walking is a physical balancing act: the spine's curves, the arched foot, the pelvis and the five phases of a step fit the human skeleton and muscles to moving steadily on two legs. (Kazakhstan — *Biology — Grade 8 Student's Book (O'Callaghan et al., Express Publishing) (record 368)*; *БИОЛОГИЯ 8 (Solovyova & Ibraimova, Atamura 2018), Kazakh-language edition (record 311)*, student textbook, §37 Biomechanical features of human movement connected with upright walking, printed p. 152; Module 7: Walking — the five phases of a step modelled, printed p. 82; built for the Kazakhstan placements, expansion-biology-2026-11-13/kazakhstan-placements.md)
678. **Breeding achievements in Kazakhstan** (`biology/breeding-achievements-in-kazakhstan`) — Kazakhstan has its own breeding record — plant varieties and animal breeds selected by its own breeders for its steppe conditions — which its Grade 8 book teaches as a named section of the selection chapter, beside Vavilov's centres of origin. (Kazakhstan — *БИОЛОГИЯ 8 (Solovyova & Ibraimova, Atamura 2018), Kazakh-language edition (record 311)*, student textbook, §53 Selection achievements in Kazakhstan, printed p. 214; built for the Kazakhstan placements, expansion-biology-2026-11-13/kazakhstan-placements.md)
679. **Global food production and the food gap** (`biology/global-food-production-and-the-food-gap`) — The world's food production does not reach everyone evenly — that unevenness is the food gap — and raising yields, as the green revolution did with new varieties and fertilisers, is humanity's main answer, with its costs and limits. (Kazakhstan — *Biology — Grade 8 Student's Book (O'Callaghan et al., Express Publishing) (record 368)*, student textbook, Module 10: How can humans contribute to global food production? The green revolution; increasing yield; the food gap, printed p. 129; built for the Kazakhstan placements, expansion-biology-2026-11-13/kazakhstan-placements.md)
680. **Seed banks and ex-situ conservation** (`biology/seed-banks-and-ex-situ-conservation`) — Seed banks conserve species outside the place they live — ex-situ conservation: seeds are dried and frozen so they keep for decades, as the Svalbard Global Seed Bank stores the world's crop seeds in Arctic cold, as insurance against their loss in the wild. (Kazakhstan — *Biology — Grade 8 Student's Book (O'Callaghan et al., Express Publishing) (record 368)*, student textbook, Module 10: The Svalbard Global Seed Bank, printed p. 131; built for the Kazakhstan placements, expansion-biology-2026-11-13/kazakhstan-placements.md)
681. **Harmful substances and the developing fetus** (`biology/harmful-substances-and-the-developing-fetus`) — The placenta feeds the fetus but does not seal it off: alcohol, nicotine and narcotic drugs pass through it and can harm the developing baby, which is why those substances are forbidden in pregnancy. (Kazakhstan — *BIOLOGY Grade 9 — Bilingual textbook (CLIL), Kazakh-text version (record 422)*; *Biology Grade 9 Student's Book (O'Callaghan et al., Express Publishing) (record 475)*; *БИОЛОГИЯ 9 (Asanov, Solovyova & Ibraimova, Atamura 2019), Kazakh-language edition (record 402)*; *Биология — 9-сынып (NIS Biology, Grade 9), Kazakh-language TRIAL (draft) edition (record 1085)*, student textbook — NIS TRIAL (draft) edition, 10.4 The effect of harmful substances on the fetus, printed p. 169; §51 The effect of smoking, narcotic substances and alcohol on the development of the human fetus, printed p. 221; 15.2 Impacts of cigarettes, alcohol and drugs on fetal development, PDF p. 309; Module 11: The placenta passes harmful substances too — alcohol, smoke and drugs reach the baby (in "The functions of the placenta"), printed p. 149; built for the Kazakhstan placements, expansion-biology-2026-11-13/kazakhstan-placements.md)
682. **Pesticides and the environment** (`biology/pesticides-and-the-environment`) — Pesticides protect crops, but they persist in the environment and concentrate as they pass through food chains — bioaccumulation — harming wildlife and human health far beyond the field that was sprayed. (Kazakhstan — *BIOLOGY Grade 9 — Bilingual textbook (CLIL), Kazakh-text version (record 422)*; *Biology Grade 9 Student's Book (O'Callaghan et al., Express Publishing) (record 475)*; *БИОЛОГИЯ 9 (Asanov, Solovyova & Ibraimova, Atamura 2019), Kazakh-language edition (record 402)*, student textbook, §8 The effect of pesticides on the environment and human health, printed p. 36; 3.2 Pesticides, PDF p. 59; Module 2: The impact of pesticides on the environment — persistence and bioaccumulation through food chains, printed p. 40; built for the Kazakhstan placements, expansion-biology-2026-11-13/kazakhstan-placements.md)
683. **Muscle fatigue and lactic acid** (`biology/muscle-fatigue-and-lactic-acid`) — A hard-working muscle tires: when it works beyond its oxygen supply it builds up lactic acid and its force falls — fatigue — and rest lets it recover, as hand-clenching and endurance tests show. (Kazakhstan — *BIOLOGY Grade 9 — Bilingual textbook (CLIL), Kazakh-text version (record 422)*; *Biology Grade 9 Student's Book (O'Callaghan et al., Express Publishing) (record 475)*; *БИОЛОГИЯ 9 (Asanov, Solovyova & Ibraimova, Atamura 2019), Kazakh-language edition (record 402)*, student textbook, §17 Muscle fatigue processes under anaerobic and aerobic load, printed p. 76; 6.2 Muscle fatigue, PDF p. 114; Module 5: Activity 5.1 — showing that muscles working harder tire sooner (hand-clenching and bicep-curl protocols), printed p. 68; Module 10: Muscle fatigue (with Activity 10.1, hand-endurance testing of dominant vs non-dominant hands), printed p. 136; built for the Kazakhstan placements, expansion-biology-2026-11-13/kazakhstan-placements.md)
684. **Methods of human genetics** (`biology/methods-of-human-genetics`) — Human genetics cannot make breeding experiments on people, so it reads heredity by its own methods — the genealogical method of pedigree charts, the twin method comparing identical and fraternal twins, and population methods — to find how traits and diseases are inherited. (Kazakhstan — *BIOLOGY Grade 9 — Bilingual textbook (CLIL), Kazakh-text version (record 422)*; *Biology Grade 9 Student's Book (O'Callaghan et al., Express Publishing) (record 475)*; *БИОЛОГИЯ 9 (Asanov, Solovyova & Ibraimova, Atamura 2019), Kazakh-language edition (record 402)*, student textbook, §41 Human genetics and its research methods, printed p. 178; 12.6 Human genetics, PDF p. 251; Module 8: Methods of studying human genetics — pedigree charts and twin studies, printed p. 126; built for the Kazakhstan placements, expansion-biology-2026-11-13/kazakhstan-placements.md)
685. **Identifying plant families** (`biology/identifying-plant-families`) — Plant families are recognised by the build of their flowers and inflorescences — the grass family, Gramineae, by its raceme, panicle and spike, and the Compositae by many small florets packed into one head — a working key taught on Kazakhstan's grassland plants. (Kazakhstan — *Biology Grade 9 Student's Book (O'Callaghan et al., Express Publishing) (record 475)*, student textbook, Module 1: Identifying plant families — Family Gramineae by inflorescence (raceme, panicle, spike), then Family Compositae, printed p. 16; built for the Kazakhstan placements, expansion-biology-2026-11-13/kazakhstan-placements.md)

228. **Thermodynamics — the laws and heat engines** (`physics/thermodynamics-laws-and-heat-engines`) — the laws as a whole — First law ΔU = Q − W (energy conserved; heat and work are its two doors), second law (heat flows hot to cold, entropy grows, no engine turns all its heat into work), third law (absolute zero approached but never reached), and heat engines cycling hot source → work → cold sink, their efficiency capped by Carnot's limit (Ecuador, Tema 13 Termodinámica).
229. **Thermometers and temperature scales — including making your own** (`physics/thermometers-and-temperature-scales`) — A thermometer uses a thermometric property that changes with heat.

253. **Uniform circular motion — period, frequency and angular velocity** (`physics/uniform-circular-motion-period-frequency-and-angular-velocity`) — uniform circular motion itself — a body rounds a circle at constant speed: the period T (seconds per turn) and the frequency f = 1/T (turns per second, in hertz), the linear (tangential) velocity v = 2πr/T = 2πrf pointing along the tangent, and the angular velocity ω = 2π/T = 2πf, the two speeds joined by v = ωr — the lesson the centripetal lessons build on (Nicaragua, Grade 10 — Física, Módulo Autoformativo, Décimo grado, Unidad II §§2.1–2.3).
254. **Unit vectors — writing vectors with i and j** (`physics/unit-vectors-writing-vectors-with-i-and-j`) — unit vectors and the i, j notation — A unit vector has magnitude one and only points; the special unit vectors i and j along the x and y axes let any vector be written as a comb…
255. **Universal gravitation** (`physics/universal-gravitation`) — newton's law of universal gravitation — Every mass attracts every other with F = Gm₁m₂/r²; a worked example weighs a 60 kg person by the formula (≈584 N, checked against mg ≈ 58…
256. **Wave words — period, frequency, wavelength, amplitude** (`physics/wave-words`) — Period is the time for one complete oscillation; frequency is oscillations per second, in hertz.
257. **Wave–particle duality of light** (`physics/wave-particle-duality-of-light`) — wave–particle duality — Slits give interference bands (waves); the photoelectric effect gives photons, one packet freeing one electron with E = hf (particles); light shows the face the experiment asks for (Brazil, Eletromagnetismo Ch. 13).
258. **What a force is — pushes, pulls and weight** (`physics/what-a-force-is`) — A force is a push or a pull, measured in newtons.
259. **What heat is** (`physics/what-heat-is`) — the concept of heat — Heat is energy in transit between bodies at different temperatures; heating changes temperature, size and state — and heat is distinguished from work and f…
260. **What physics is** (`physics/what-physics-is`) — Physics comes from Greek phusis, meaning nature.
261. **What temperature change does** (`physics/what-temperature-change-does`) — Heating and cooling change matter, environments and living things.
262. **Wheel and axle** (`physics/wheel-and-axle`) — Wheel and axle: a large wheel fixed to a smaller axle, turning together.
263. **When waves meet — superposition and standing waves** (`physics/when-waves-meet`) — Superposition: displacements of meeting waves add.

265. **Why measurements need standards — the SI base quantities** (`physics/why-measurements-need-standards`) — A standard must be accessible, reliable, and give the same result anywhere.
266. **Why ships float** (`physics/why-ships-float`) — sinking, floating, and big ships — Fully submerged objects rise or sink by density; a floating body displaces its own weight, with the submerged fraction set by the density rati…
267. **Work–energy theorem; power** (`physics/work-energy-theorem-and-power`) — the work–energy theorem and power — Net work equals the change in kinetic energy; power is the rate of doing work (watts), applied to machines and people in worked examples.
268. **Work — force times distance** (`physics/work-force-times-distance`) — work — Work is force times displacement in the force's direction; angled forces contribute only their along-motion component, and work can be zero or negative.
269. **Work in physics — force times distance** (`physics/work-in-physics`) — Work W = F × s, measured in joules.
270. **Young's modulus — how stiff a material is** (`physics/youngs-modulus`) — Y = stress ÷ strain — a property of the material, not the specimen's size.
271. **Zeroth law of thermodynamics and temperature equilibrium** (`physics/zeroth-law-of-thermodynamics-and-temperature-equilibrium`) — the zeroth law and thermal equilibrium — Heat flows from hotter to colder until temperatures match; if A and B each match a third body they match each other — the law that makes temperature measurable and thermometers work (Brazil, Termodinâmica Ch. 4).
272. **Signals — a message carried by sound, light or wire** (`physics/signals-a-message-carried-by-sound-light-or-wire`) — a signal is a message made travel-ready: emitter, carrier, receiver — whistles and drums on sound, flares and lighthouses on light, telephones and doorbells on electricity in wires, and distress patterns (the flare, SOS) built to be unmissable — teacher-guide evidence only, acceptance 0 (Gabon, Grade 5 — Super Efficace, Guide pédagogique, 5e année)
273. **Brownian motion and the kinetic theory of matter** (`physics/brownian-motion-and-the-kinetic-theory-of-matter`) — the visible evidence for the particle picture: specks suspended in a fluid jiggle forever, kicked unevenly by the fluid's own invisible moving particles — the kinetic theory (particles flying in gases, sliding in liquids, vibrating in solids) then explains temperature as average kinetic energy, diffusion as random-motion mixing, and gas pressure as wall collisions (Palestine, Grade 10 — Physics for Grade 10, Unit 3, Ch. 2: Thermodynamics, printed p. 108)
274. **The gas laws — Boyle, Charles and the pressure law** (`physics/the-gas-laws-boyle-charles-and-the-pressure-law`) — the three laws of a fixed mass of gas: Boyle (at constant temperature, P × V constant, proved by the sealed-syringe activity), Charles (at constant pressure, V/T constant) and the pressure law (at constant volume, P/T constant), temperatures always in kelvin, all three gathered in the combined law P₁V₁/T₁ = P₂V₂/T₂ (Palestine, Grade 10 — Physics for Grade 10, Unit 3, Ch. 2: Thermodynamics, printed p. 112)
275. **EMF, internal resistance and terminal voltage of a source** (`physics/emf-internal-resistance-and-terminal-voltage`) — a real source's EMF is the energy it gives each coulomb, but its own internal resistance spends part of it as lost volts Ir, so the terminal voltage V = E − Ir sags as current is drawn — measured together on the V–I graph (intercept E, slope r), with sources in series and parallel — teacher-guide evidence only, acceptance 0 (Papua New Guinea, Grades 11–12 — Physics, Upper Secondary Teacher Guide (OBE), Unit 11.5, printed p. 35)
276. **Photons and the photoelectric effect** (`physics/photons-and-the-photoelectric-effect`) — light arrives in photons of E = hf (Planck's quantum hypothesis); a photon above a metal's threshold frequency frees one electron instantly, and Einstein's equation hf = φ + KE_max keeps the account — brightness counts electrons, frequency sets their speed — counted from Italy, Grade 12 — *L'Amaldi per i licei scientifici.blu, Vol. 3*, Ch. 32 La crisi della fisica classica, the photoelectric effect strand, PDF p. 200; teacher-guide evidence only (Papua New Guinea, Grade 12 — Physics Teacher Guide Grade 12, Standards-Based, Topic Photons, printed p. 96 approx.)
277. **Quantum physics foundations** (`physics/quantum-physics-foundations`) — the foundations of the quantum world as one lesson: black-body radiation and Planck's quantisation (E = hf), the photoelectric effect and Compton scattering (photons carry energy and momentum), wave–particle duality (de Broglie λ = h/p) and the uncertainty principle Δx · Δp ≥ h/4π — counted from Italy, Grade 12 — *L'Amaldi per i licei scientifici.blu, Vol. 3*, Ch. 32 La crisi della fisica classica: black-body radiation, Planck's quanta, photoelectric and Compton strands, PDF p. 200; curriculum-programme evidence only (Andorra — Programa de física del batxillerat general de l'Escola Andorrana, Decret 238/2025, Bloc 6, printed p. 37; Programa de física 2, Decret del 9-12-2020, Bloc 6, printed p. 34)
278. **Special relativity — basic principles** (`physics/special-relativity-basic-principles`) — Einstein's two postulates (physics the same in every inertial frame; c the same for every observer) and what they force: time dilation, length contraction, relative simultaneity, spacetime, general relativity in outline, and E = mc² — counted from Italy, Grade 12 — *L'Amaldi per i licei scientifici.blu, Vol. 3*, Ch.29–31 Relatività ristretta: postulates, simultaneity and time dilation, Lorentz transformations, relativistic dynamics, mass–energy equivalence, PDF p. 130 (Italy also adds Sub lesson 4 — the Lorentz transformations and relativistic dynamics); curriculum-programme evidence only (Andorra — Programa de física del batxillerat general de l'Escola Andorrana, Decret 238/2025, Bloc 6, printed p. 37; Programa de física 2, Decret del 9-12-2020, Bloc 6, printed p. 34)
279. **Bernoulli's principle and flight** (`physics/bernoullis-principle-and-flight`) — beside the fluids lessons: along a streamline pressure + ½ρv² + ρgh stays constant, so faster flow means lower pressure (ΔP = ½ρ(v₂² − v₁²)) — the trade that lifts wings and sails (lift priced in numbers, angle of attack and the stall), pulls shower curtains in and drives atomisers, and lifts roofs off in a hurricane (fast wind above, still air inside pushing up) — curriculum evidence only, acceptance 0 (Bahamas — Curriculum Guidelines — Physics, Grades 10, 11, 12, Ministry of Education, 2010, Bernoulli's principle, printed p. xiv)
280. **AC circuits — RC and RL dipoles** (`physics/ac-circuits-rc-and-rl-dipoles`) — beside the AC and electronics lessons: alternating current through resistor-capacitor and resistor-inductor dipoles — capacitive reactance X_C = 1/(2πfC) falling with frequency and current leading the voltage by 90°, inductive reactance X_L = 2πfL rising with frequency and current lagging by 90°, and the series impedance Z = √(R² + X²) with I = V/Z, checked by a multimeter's part-voltages adding by squares (V² = V_R² + V_X²) — programme evidence only, acceptance 0 (Haiti — Programme détaillé NS, Physique 4e année / NS4, MENFP, Nouveau Secondaire, 2008–2009, Theme: variable currents, RC/RL dipoles, multimeter on domestic supply, PDF p. 6)

281. **How this Physics course works — the Italian (Amaldi) way** (`physics/how-this-physics-course-works-the-amaldi-way`) — Italy's way: the three-volume Amaldi course for the licei scientifici (Vol. 1 mechanics and thermodynamics, Vol. 2 waves and fields, Vol. 3 induction, relativity and quanta) — every chapter splitting theory from exercises, model problems (Problemi modello) solved in full before the student's sets, “stop and think” (Fermati a pensare) prompts inside the theory, maths taught exactly when physics needs it (La matematica per la fisica), chapters closing with a formula map, a test and the “Sei pronto per la verifica?” self-test, and the whole course mapped onto the esame di Stato (Italy — *Il nuovo Amaldi per i licei scientifici.blu, Vol. 1*, Zanichelli, 2020, printed pp. III–X; *L'Amaldi per i licei scientifici.blu, Vol. 2*, 2012, PDF p. 2; *L'Amaldi per i licei scientifici.blu, Vol. 3*, 2012, PDF p. 2)
282. **Non-inertial frames and apparent forces** (`physics/non-inertial-frames-and-apparent-forces`) — frames that accelerate or rotate need apparent forces: the phantom push −m·a_frame (the braking-bus lurch), the outward centrifugal force of a rotating frame, and the Coriolis deflection of Earth's winds — agentless bookkeeping that vanishes in an inertial description (Italy, Grade 9–10 — *Il nuovo Amaldi per i licei scientifici.blu, Vol. 1*, Ch. 2 §4, printed p. 59)
283. **Thermodynamic systems and the heat engine** (`physics/thermodynamic-systems-and-heat-engines`) — the full systems treatment: the system and its internal energy, the first law ΔU = Q − W, the named transformations (isothermal, adiabatic, isobaric, isochoric, cyclic) with work as area on the p–V diagram, heat engines and why efficiency is capped, Carnot's cycle η = 1 − T_cold/T_hot, the second law and entropy with its microscopic reading (Italy, Grade 9–10 — *Il nuovo Amaldi per i licei scientifici.blu, Vol. 1*, Ch. 10, printed p. 410; Ch. 11, printed p. 456)
284. **Electric current in metals — resistance and temperature, superconductivity** (`physics/electric-current-in-metals-resistance-and-superconductivity`) — current as the drift of a free-electron sea; resistivity rising with temperature as the vibrating lattice scatters the drift (ρ ≈ ρ₀(1 + αΔT)); electron extraction from metals; and superconductivity — resistance exactly zero below the critical temperature, Onnes's mercury of 1911 and the Meissner effect (Italy, Grade 11 — *L'Amaldi per i licei scientifici.blu, Vol. 2*, Ch. 23, PDF p. 282)
285. **Alternating-current circuits — reactance, impedance and resonance** (`physics/alternating-current-circuits-reactance-impedance-and-resonance`) — the full AC lesson: the alternator and RMS values; capacitive reactance X_C = 1/(2πfC) falling with frequency and inductive reactance X_L = 2πfL rising; impedance Z = √(R² + (X_L − X_C)²) combining the parts by phase; and resonance at f₀ = 1/(2π√(LC)) — impedance minimum, current maximum — from radio tuning to power-system hazard (Italy, Grade 12 — *L'Amaldi per i licei scientifici.blu, Vol. 3*, Ch. 27, PDF p. 60)
286. **Bohr's model of the hydrogen atom and energy levels** (`physics/bohrs-model-of-the-hydrogen-atom`) — Bohr's two postulates (allowed orbits mvr = nħ that radiate nothing; jumps that emit or absorb one photon hf = ΔE), the level ladder E_n = −13.6/n² eV with ionisation at 13.6 eV, the Lyman, Balmer and Paschen series as named falls, and the model's honest limits (Italy, Grade 12 — *L'Amaldi per i licei scientifici.blu, Vol. 3*, Ch. 33, PDF p. 240)
287. **Matter waves and the uncertainty principle** (`physics/matter-waves-and-the-uncertainty-principle`) — de Broglie's λ = h/p for every particle and its electron-diffraction proof; bound electrons as standing probability waves (the real reason Bohr's rungs exist); and Heisenberg's Δx·Δp ≥ h/4π — a floor set by what a wave is, forcing zero-point motion in anything confined (Italy, Grade 12 — *L'Amaldi per i licei scientifici.blu, Vol. 3*, Ch. 34, PDF p. 280)
288. **Elementary particles and quarks — the fundamental interactions** (`physics/elementary-particles-and-quarks`) — the Standard-Model survey: quarks confined in hadrons (proton uud, neutron udd), the elementary leptons, three generations with ordinary matter using only the first, antimatter and annihilation, the exchange particles of each interaction (photon, gluons, W and Z), and mass from the Higgs field (Italy, Grade 12 — *L'Amaldi per i licei scientifici.blu, Vol. 3*, Ch. 35, PDF p. 330)
289. **Frontiers of physics — Planck units and the scientific worldview** (`physics/frontiers-of-physics-planck-units-and-the-scientific-worldview`) — the course's capstone: the three constants c, G and h combine into nature's own scale — Planck length ≈ 1.6 × 10⁻³⁵ m, time ≈ 5.4 × 10⁻⁴⁴ s, mass ≈ 2.2 × 10⁻⁸ kg, energy ≈ 2 × 10⁹ J — where general relativity and quantum theory must merge into a quantum gravity that does not yet exist; and the closing essay: physics advances by models tested against the world and revised when they fail (Italy, Grade 12 — *L'Amaldi per i licei scientifici.blu, Vol. 3*, Ch. 36, PDF p. 392)

290. **The vernier caliper and travelling microscope** (`physics/the-vernier-caliper-and-travelling-microscope`) — the vernier principle: 10 vernier divisions span 9 main divisions, so the least count is 0.1 mm; a caliper reading is main scale plus coinciding division × least count, corrected for zero error, across outside, inside and depth measures; the travelling microscope puts cross-wires on tiny features and takes lengths as the difference of two vernier readings (Sri Lanka, Grade 12 — *Science for Technology: Physics Part I*, §1.1, printed pp. 7–18)
291. **The micrometer screw gauge and the screw principle** (`physics/the-micrometer-screw-gauge-and-the-screw-principle`) — the screw principle: one turn advances the spindle by the pitch, so least count = pitch ÷ thimble divisions = 0.01 mm; reading sleeve then thimble, finishing on the ratchet, correcting zero error, and averaging measures taken at several places (Sri Lanka, Grade 12 — *Science for Technology: Physics Part I*, §1.2, printed pp. 20–21)
292. **Errors and error estimation in measurement** (`physics/errors-and-error-estimation-in-measurement`) — systematic errors corrected, random errors averaged, gross errors discarded; absolute, relative and percentage error; sums add absolute errors and products add percentage errors, and a result is reported as value ± error (Sri Lanka, Grade 12 — *Science for Technology: Physics Part I*, §1.5, printed p. 30)
293. **Resolving and combining forces — resultant and the parallelogram law** (`physics/resolving-and-combining-forces-resultant-and-the-parallelogram-law`) — a force resolves into F cos θ along and F sin θ across; two forces combine by the parallelogram law, R = √(A² + B² + 2AB cos θ); many forces are handled by summing resolved components ΣFx and ΣFy (Sri Lanka, Grade 12 — *Science for Technology: Physics Part I*, §§3.1.5–3.2, printed pp. 93–103)
294. **Angular momentum** (`physics/angular-momentum`) — L = mvr for a particle and L = Iω for a rigid body; torque is its rate of change, τ = dL/dt; with zero external torque L is conserved, so a spinner that reduces I must raise ω — the skater, the diver and the planets (Sri Lanka, Grade 12 — *Science for Technology: Physics Part II*, §1.10, printed p. 14)
295. **Efficiency of a machine** (`physics/efficiency-of-a-machine`) — efficiency = useful output ÷ total input, for work, energy or power, always below 100%; input = useful output + wasted output, and the waste is traced mainly to friction turned into heat, cut by lubrication and bearings but never removed (Sri Lanka, Grade 12 — *Science for Technology: Physics Part II*, §2.6, printed p. 43)
296. **Energy stored in a stretched wire** (`physics/energy-stored-in-a-stretched-wire`) — the work stored in stretching is ½Fx, the triangular area under the force–extension graph; per unit volume it is ½ × stress × strain, growing with the square of the strain, and it is returned in full only within the elastic limit (Sri Lanka, Grade 12 — *Science for Technology: Physics Part II*, §3.6, printed p. 54)
297. **Magnetic theory — poles, inverse-square law and magnetic measurements** (`physics/magnetic-theory-poles-inverse-square-law-and-magnetic-measurements`) — the classical pole theory: force between poles ∝ m₁m₂/r², field strength as force on a unit north pole, and measurement by the tangent law tan θ = B/H in the deflection magnetometer (Sri Lanka, Grade 12 — *Intermediate Physics, Part V: Magnetism and Electricity* (1968), Ch. 45, printed p. 169)
298. **Thermoelectricity — Seebeck effect and thermocouples** (`physics/thermoelectricity-seebeck-effect-and-thermocouples`) — unlike-metal junctions at different temperatures generate an EMF (Seebeck); the thermocouple turns it into a thermometer of tiny size, fast response and furnace range against a reference junction in ice; the Peltier effect runs the trade backwards (Sri Lanka, Grade 12 — *Intermediate Physics, Part V: Magnetism and Electricity* (1968), Ch. 51, printed p. 350)
299. **Magnetic properties of iron — permeability and hysteresis** (`physics/magnetic-properties-of-iron-permeability-and-hysteresis`) — permeability μ = B/H measures how strongly iron multiplies a field, up to saturation; the hysteresis loop gives remanence, coercive force and the energy lost as heat per cycle — narrow-loop soft iron for cores and electromagnets, wide-loop hard steel for permanent magnets (Sri Lanka, Grade 12 — *Intermediate Physics, Part V: Magnetism and Electricity* (1968), Ch. 54, printed p. 488)
300. **How this Physics course works — the Syrian Grade 7 way** (`physics/how-this-physics-course-works-the-syrian-grade-7-way`) — the Syrian Grade 7 book's way: a research-and-experiment curriculum tied to daily life and the local environment; every lesson opens with printed objectives and key words, is built around an "I try and I conclude" bench experiment with an explicit "I conclude" box, worked applications and enrichment boxes, and units close with unit questions plus a hands-on project (Syria, Grade 7 — *Physics & Chemistry, Grade 7 Basic*, whole book, printed p. 3)
301. **How this Physics course works — the Syrian Grade 8 way** (`physics/how-this-physics-course-works-the-syrian-grade-8-way`) — the same Syrian lesson design in the Grade 8 combined volume, physics and chemistry alternating by unit: U2 Motion & forces, U3 Electricity, U4 Light (Syria, Grade 8 — *Physics & Chemistry, Grade 8 Basic*, whole book, printed p. 3)
302. **How this Physics course works — the Syrian Grade 9 way** (`physics/how-this-physics-course-works-the-syrian-grade-9-way`) — the Syrian lesson design in the Grade 9 combined volume, physics units first (Electricity & magnetism, Mechanics & energy, Waves & oscillations), chemistry after (Syria, Grade 9 — *Physics & Chemistry, Grade 9 Basic*, whole book, printed p. 3)
303. **How this Physics course works — the Syrian Grade 12 way** (`physics/how-this-physics-course-works-the-syrian-grade-12-way`) — the Grade 12 book's derivation-first way: activities feed an "I conclude" box, then the mathematics is carried through in full across five units (Syria, Grade 12 — *Physics, Third Secondary, Scientific Track*, whole book, contents p. 6)
304. **Damped and forced oscillations — the torsion and gravity pendulums** (`physics/damped-and-forced-oscillations-the-torsion-and-gravity-pendulums`) — the torsion pendulum T = 2π√(I/κ) measures moments of inertia, the gravity pendulum T = 2π√(L/g) measures g; damping drains amplitude, and driving at the natural frequency gives resonance (Syria, Grade 12 — *Physics, Third Secondary, Scientific Track*, U1, contents pp. 20, 28)
305. **Standing waves in air columns — longitudinal standing waves** (`physics/standing-waves-in-air-columns-longitudinal-standing-waves`) — nodes and antinodes in pipes: a closed pipe fits λ = 4L and sounds odd harmonics only, an open pipe fits λ = 2L and sounds the full series (Syria, Grade 12 — *Physics, Third Secondary, Scientific Track*, U3, contents p. 182)
306. **Atomic spectra** (`physics/atomic-spectra`) — continuous, emission and absorption spectra; lines arise from electrons falling between fixed energy levels, h f = E_upper − E_lower, and each element's line pattern is a unique fingerprint (Syria, Grade 12 — *Physics, Third Secondary, Scientific Track*, U4, contents p. 198)
307. **X-rays and the laser** (`physics/x-rays-and-the-laser`) — X-rays from fast electrons striking a target: penetrating, ionising, bone-stopping; laser light from stimulated emission under population inversion: coherent, monochromatic, collimated (Syria, Grade 12 — *Physics, Third Secondary, Scientific Track*, U4, contents pp. 240, 246)
308. **The first law of thermodynamics — heat, work and internal energy in one account** (`physics/the-first-law-of-thermodynamics-heat-work-and-internal-energy-in-one-account`) — one account for a system's energy: ΔU = Q − W — heat in raises internal energy, work done by the system spends it; the adiabatic, isothermal and cyclic cases, and why every heat engine is this same bookkeeping run round and round (Slovenia, Grade 9 — *Fizika 9 — i-učbenik*, Heat and internal energy: Energijski zakon, printed p. 141 (unit folder 188))

309. **Damping and resonance** (`physics/damping-and-resonance`) — damping is the steady shrinking of an oscillation's amplitude as friction and air resistance drain its energy; resonance is the maximum-amplitude answer when a driving force arrives in step at the oscillator's natural frequency — the swing, the tuning fork, the bridge and the building damper (Malaysia, Form 4 — *Physics Form 4*, Ch. 5 Waves, §5.2 Damping and Resonance, printed p. 184)
310. **Diffraction of waves** (`physics/diffraction-of-waves`) — waves spread into the shadow region through gaps and past edges; the spread is strongest when the gap is about one wavelength wide — the rule that prices harbours, hearing around corners and why light needs a hair-wide slit to spread (Malaysia, Form 4 — *Physics Form 4*, Ch. 5 Waves, §5.5 Diffraction of Waves, printed p. 202)
311. **The thin lens formula and magnification** (`physics/the-thin-lens-formula-and-magnification`) — 1/f = 1/u + 1/v under the real-is-positive convention, and magnification m = v/u, worked through the projector, the camera and the concave lens (Malaysia, Form 4 — *Physics Form 4*, Ch. 6 Light and Optics, §6.4 Thin Lens Formula, printed p. 259)
312. **How this Physics course works — the Malaysian KSSM way** (`physics/how-this-physics-course-works-the-malaysian-kssm-way`) — Malaysia's way: the Form 4 book organised by four themes (Elementary Physics; Newtonian Mechanics, with gravitation as its own chapter; Heat, with the gas laws inside it; Waves, Light and Optics, with mirrors last), Career Info boxes and 21st-century-skills activities inside the chapters, the same book issued in Malay and English DLP in parallel, and every chapter closing with a Performance Evaluation in SPM examination format (Malaysia, Form 4 — *Physics Form 4*, whole book)
313. **How this Physics course works — the Malaysian KSSM Form 5 way** (`physics/how-this-physics-course-works-the-malaysian-kssm-form-5-way`) — the Form 5 completion of the two-year arc: four themes, all of pressure in one applied sweep (liquids, atmosphere, gas, Pascal, Archimedes, Bernoulli), electronics opening with the electron itself, and the course ending in modern physics — nuclear and a full Quantum Physics chapter as examined SPM core (Malaysia, Form 5 — *Physics Form 5*, whole book)

314. **The sign rule for motion equations** (`physics/the-sign-rule-for-motion-equations`) — before any motion equation is used, one direction is chosen as positive and every displacement, velocity and acceleration is signed by it — along is +, against is − — so with upward positive gravity is −9.8 m/s² for a thrown ball's whole flight, and the signed values are fed into v = u + at and its partners exactly as they stand (Sudan, Grade 10 — *Physics — First Secondary Year*, Ch 3: the sign rule, printed p. 31)
315. **Lami's theorem for three concurrent forces** (`physics/lamis-theorem-for-three-concurrent-forces`) — three forces at a point in equilibrium each stand over the sine of the angle between the other two, F₁ / sin α = F₂ / sin β = F₃ / sin γ, pricing the tensions in two strings that hold a hanging body from the angles alone (Sudan, Grade 11 — *Physics — Second Secondary Year*, Unit 3 worked examples, printed p. 92)
316. **Satellites and escape velocity** (`physics/satellites-and-escape-velocity`) — gravity itself supplies the centripetal force, fixing a circular orbit's speed at v = √(GM/r), about 8 km/s just above the Earth; escape takes √2 times that, about 11.2 km/s from the surface, and at 35,800 km the 24-hour period gives the geostationary perch (Sudan, Grade 12 — *Physics — Third Secondary Year*, Book 1, printed p. 50)
317. **Modulation — AM and FM** (`physics/modulation-am-and-fm`) — sound is written onto a high-frequency carrier: AM varies the carrier's amplitude and keeps its frequency fixed (simple, far-reaching, crackly), FM varies its frequency and holds amplitude constant (clean sound, wider channel, VHF band) (Sudan, Grade 12 — *Physics — Third Secondary Year*, Book 4 (4-17), printed p. 202)
318. **The microphone and radio receiver circuits** (`physics/the-microphone-and-radio-receiver-circuits`) — the microphone's diaphragm-driven coil turns sound into a copying current by induction; the receiver's LC resonance circuit selects one carrier, its diode detector strips the carrier away, and amplification drives the loudspeaker (Sudan, Grade 12 — *Physics — Third Secondary Year*, Book 4, printed p. 203)
319. **Television transmission and reception** (`physics/television-transmission-and-reception`) — the camera scans a scene into 625 lines, brightness becoming a vision signal (with synchronising pulses) that modulates a carrier beside a separate sound carrier; the receiver tunes, separates and detects, and the screen repaints the same lines in step (Sudan, Grade 12 — *Physics — Third Secondary Year*, Book 4 close, printed p. 210)
320. **Wave optics — interference and diffraction of light** (`physics/wave-optics-interference-and-diffraction-of-light`) — light caught behaving as a wave: coherent sources superpose, the path difference deciding bright from dark (whole wavelengths reinforce, half-odd cancel), Young's double-slit fringes of width w = λD/a measuring the wavelength itself, single-slit diffraction spreading the beam (minima at a sin θ = nλ), and the diffraction grating sharpening the maxima to d sin θ = nλ so each wavelength takes its own angle (Fiji, Year 13 — *Physics for Year 13*, Strand 3 §3.3 Wave Optics, printed p. 80)
321. **How this Physics course works — the Indonesian Kurikulum Merdeka way** (`physics/how-this-physics-course-works-the-indonesian-kurikulum-merdeka-way`) — Indonesia's way: physics organised as activity-first chapters (Fase F) — every chapter opens with apperception and a misconception check, builds concepts through numbered Aktivitas investigations, closes each stretch with “Ayo, Cek Pemahaman” checks and the chapter with an Asesmen, and carries a Profil Pelajar Pancasila character dimension and project options (Indonesia, Grade 11 — *Fisika untuk SMA/MA Kelas XI*, whole book)
322. **Teaching physics the Kurikulum Merdeka way — schemes, misconception checks and LKPD** (`physics/teaching-physics-the-kurikulum-merdeka-way-schemes-misconception-checks-and-lkpd`) — the guide's per-chapter apparatus: Skema Pembelajaran (meetings, hours, strategies), miskonsepsi lists taught against, LKPD worksheets, parent interaction, alternative routes and teacher reflection — teacher-guide evidence only, acceptance 0 (Indonesia, Grade 11 — *Buku Panduan Guru Fisika untuk SMA/MA Kelas XI*, whole guide)
323. **Misconception-first teaching — the Indonesian guide way** (`physics/misconception-first-teaching-the-indonesian-guide-way`) — every chapter carries a named Miskonsepsi section listing this topic's wrong ideas before the teaching plan; teaching confronts them (predict, watch it fail, Pembelajaran Alternatif as a second route), and checks, reflection and follow-up track whether the wrong idea moved — teacher-guide evidence only, acceptance 0 (Indonesia, Grade 12 — *Buku Panduan Guru Fisika untuk SMA/MA Kelas XII*, whole guide)
324. **Sinusoidal alternating current — period, frequency and rms values with the oscilloscope** (`physics/sinusoidal-alternating-current-period-frequency-and-rms-with-the-oscilloscope`) — AC reverses in a sine wave: period T and frequency f = 1/T (mains 50 Hz, T = 0.02 s), period and peak read off an oscilloscope trace, and the working value is the rms value, Urms = Umax/√2 — teacher-guide evidence only, acceptance 0 (Morocco, Grade 8 — *Al Moufid en Physique/Chimie, 2AC — Guide de l'enseignant(e)*, Chapitre 16, printed p. 136; *Al Marjie Physique Chimie, 2AC — Guide de l'enseignant(e)*, Partie 2, printed pp. 115–125)
325. **Speed and road safety — braking, reaction and the dangers of speed** (`physics/speed-and-road-safety-braking-reaction-and-the-dangers-of-speed`) — the reaction distance travelled while a driver reacts grows in proportion to speed, the braking distance grows with the square of the speed, and their sum, the stopping distance, sets the safe gap — road safety as a timetabled activity inside the speed chapter — teacher-guide evidence only, acceptance 0 (Morocco, Grade 9 — *Al Moufid en Physique/Chimie, 3AC — Guide de l'enseignant(e)*, Chapitre 9, Activity 3, printed p. 85)
326. **Electrical energy consumed — the meter, E = P·t and the electricity bill** (`physics/electrical-energy-consumed-the-meter-and-the-electricity-bill`) — E = P·t counted in kilowatt-hours by the home's meter, the bill pricing the units (cost = kWh × price per unit), worked from a real electricity bill as the closing activity of collège physics — teacher-guide evidence only, acceptance 0 (Morocco, Grade 9 — *Al Moufid en Physique/Chimie, 3AC — Guide de l'enseignant(e)*, Chapitre 16, printed p. 117; *Al Marjie Physique Chimie, 3AC — Guide de l'enseignant(e)*, Partie 2, printed pp. 93–107)
327. **Renewable energy — evaluating energy resources** (`physics/renewable-energy-evaluating-energy-resources`) — renewable means refilled as fast as it is used (flows: solar, wind, hydro, geothermal, biomass — against stocks that finish), and a resource is evaluated by four tests: energy returned for energy invested, steadiness (capacity factor = average output ÷ rated output), what it takes in land/materials/water, and what it leaves in emissions/waste — the verdict always a local match of resource, place and job, argued with numbers (United States, Grade 12 — *Physics for Utah SEEd Standards (Revised 2025)*, PHYS.2.5, printed p. 80)
328. **Fields — interacting objects and field characteristics** (`physics/fields-interacting-objects-and-field-characteristics`) — objects interact without touching through fields: a source sets up the field, a visitor feels its force; a field is characterised by strength (g = F/m in N/kg, E = F/q in N/C), direction and pattern (field lines never cross and crowd where strong) — gravity only attracts and cannot be screened, electric fields attract and repel and cancel inside a hollow conductor, and overlapping fields superpose as vectors (United States, Grade 12 — *Physics for Utah SEEd Standards (Revised 2025)*, PHYS.3.3–3.4, printed pp. 121–126)
329. **Biological effects of electromagnetic radiation** (`physics/biological-effects-of-electromagnetic-radiation`) — photon energy E = hf splits the spectrum into non-ionising radiation (radio to low ultraviolet — warming only) and ionising radiation (high ultraviolet, X-rays, gamma rays — DNA breakable); harm is counted in dose (deterministic effects above thresholds, stochastic cancer risk rising with dose) and protection is time, distance by the inverse square, and shielding (United States, Grade 12 — *Physics for Utah SEEd Standards (Revised 2025)*, PHYS.4.3, printed p. 165)
330. **Ocean currents and water density** (`physics/ocean-currents-and-water-density`) — water's density falls as it warms and rises as it cools or grows saltier, and those differences drive the ocean: cold salty water sinks and creeps along the deep floor while warm light water spreads on top — the thermohaline circulation — carrying heat, oxygen and nutrients that shape coastal climates and fishing grounds (Uganda, Senior One — *Physics Textbook — Senior One (NCDC Prototype)*, Ch 2, printed p. 39)
331. **Surface tension and capillarity** (`physics/surface-tension-and-capillarity`) — intermolecular forces pull a liquid's surface tight like a skin (surface tension: round drops, a floating clip, a pond skater), and the contest of adhesion against cohesion drives capillarity — water climbing narrow tubes, wicks and soil pores, higher the narrower the space, while mercury is pushed down (Uganda, Senior One — *Physics Textbook — Senior One (NCDC Prototype)*, Ch 4, printed p. 78)
332. **How this Physics course works — the Uganda competency-based way** (`physics/how-this-physics-course-works-the-uganda-competency-based-way`) — Uganda's revised curriculum: competences over coverage, generic skills and values practised inside every topic, lessons planned backwards from learning outcomes with activity-first teaching, and assessment split into formative checks that steer learning and summative judgements certified with sample rubrics — teacher-guide evidence only, acceptance 0 (Uganda, Senior One — *Physics Teacher's Guide — Senior One (NCDC Prototype)*, Note to the Teacher, printed p. v)
333. **Electric power transmission and the grid** (`physics/electric-power-transmission-and-the-grid`) — power P = VI is moved at high voltage and low current because wire heating loss is I²R (halving current quarters the loss), so transformers step voltage up for transmission lines and down in stages through substations to the home — the whole linked network is the grid, balancing supply with demand moment by moment (Uganda, Senior Four — *Physics Home-Study Learning — Senior Four (NCDC, 2020)*, Lesson 4: Power Transmission, PDF p. 30)
334. **The cathode ray tube and oscilloscope** (`physics/the-cathode-ray-tube-and-oscilloscope`) — a heated cathode boils off electrons into a beam (cathode rays) in an evacuated tube; the tube's electron gun, deflection plates and fluorescent screen aim that beam, and the oscilloscope adds a time base so the spot draws voltage against time — height gives voltage, width of a cycle gives period and frequency (Uganda, Senior Four — *Physics Home-Study Learning — Senior Four (NCDC, 2020)*, Electrons and X-Rays chapter, PDF p. 48)
335. **The pinhole camera** (`physics/the-pinhole-camera`) — a tiny hole in a dark box projects an inverted image of a bright scene onto a screen, because light travels in straight lines and the rays cross at the hole; a smaller hole gives a sharper but dimmer image, a larger hole a brighter but blurred one, and the size is set by magnification = image distance ÷ object distance (Kenya, Form One — *Secondary Physics — Student's Book One (KLB, Fourth Edition)*, Ch. 8, printed p. ~215)
336. **The domain theory of magnetism** (`physics/the-domain-theory-of-magnetism`) — a magnetic material is made of domains, tiny regions each magnetised in one direction: pointing every which way they cancel (unmagnetised), lined up by stroking or by a current in a coil they make a magnet (soft iron easily and temporarily, steel hard and permanently), and heating, hammering or a fading alternating-current field shakes them out of line again — demagnetising (Kenya, Form Two — *Secondary Physics — Student's Book Two (KLB, 5th edition 2013)*, Ch. 1, printed p. ~15)
337. **Examination practice — sample papers inside the textbook** (`physics/examination-practice-sample-papers-inside-the-textbook`) — Kenya's closing-chapter way of carrying mock papers inside the textbook itself: the Form Four book ends not with a topic but with four full sample papers, and the course-way lesson teaches sitting each paper honestly (full time, one stretch, no book, working shown), marking it strictly, and logging every lost mark by topic and kind of loss so the log becomes the revision map for the next paper (Kenya, Form Four — *Secondary Physics — Student's Book Four (KLB)*, Sample Papers One–Four, printed p. 203)
338. **How this Physics course works — the Iraqi Grade 7 way** (`physics/how-this-physics-course-works-the-iraqi-grade-7-way`) — Iraq's Grade 7 way: a 5-chapter, 12-lesson book built on the 5-stage learning cycle (engage–explore–explain–evaluate–extend) stated in its preface, every lesson opening with an activity or chapter vocabulary (مفردات الفصل), terms in Arabic and English side by side, and every chapter closing with a vocabulary/concept review plus short-answer and multiple-choice tests (Iraq, Grade 7 — *الفيزياء كتاب الطالب للصف الأول المتوسط (Physics, Student Book, First Intermediate = Grade 7)*, whole book, printed p. 3)
339. **How this Physics course works — the Iraqi Grade 8 way** (`physics/how-this-physics-course-works-the-iraqi-grade-8-way`) — Iraq's Grade 8 way: 3 units, 6 chapters and 13 lessons (Motion & Force; Force, Work & Energy; Sound & Light), each unit opening with a starter activity, lessons opening with activity or chapter vocabulary, bilingual terms and worked examples, and chapters closing with reviews and tests (Iraq, Grade 8 — *الفيزياء كتاب الطالب للصف الثاني المتوسط (Physics, Student Book, Second Intermediate = Grade 8)*, whole book, printed p. 3)
340. **How this Physics course works — the Iraqi Grade 9 way** (`physics/how-this-physics-course-works-the-iraqi-grade-9-way`) — Iraq's Grade 9 way: the fullest statement of the Iraqi design — 9 chapters, each printing its chapter vocabulary section list, behavioural objectives and a bilingual glossary, after a maths refresher on powers of ten (Iraq, Grade 9 — *الفيزياء للصف الثالث المتوسط (Physics, Third Intermediate = Grade 9)*, whole book, printed p. 3)
341. **How this Physics course works — the Iraqi Grade 11 way** (`physics/how-this-physics-course-works-the-iraqi-grade-11-way`) — Iraq's Grade 11 (Fifth Scientific) way: 10 chapters whose bilingual presentation is at its strongest — Arabic text with English chapter and section titles printed alongside throughout (Iraq, Grade 11 — *الفيزياء للصف الخامس العلمي (Physics, Fifth Scientific = Grade 11)*, whole book, printed p. 4)
342. **How this Physics course works — the Iraqi Grade 12 way** (`physics/how-this-physics-course-works-the-iraqi-grade-12-way`) — Iraq's Grade 12 (Sixth Scientific) way: 9 chapters from capacitors to nuclear physics, its contents table printed at the back of the book, each chapter opener listing its vocabulary (مفردات الفصل) with English titles (Iraq, Grade 12 — *الفيزياء للصف السادس العلمي (Physics, Sixth Scientific = Grade 12)*, whole book, printed p. 284)
343. **Batteries — primary, secondary and fuel cells** (`physics/batteries-primary-secondary-and-fuel-cells`) — a battery is one or more cells whose chemical action gives an electromotive force; primary cells run once and are thrown away (the dry cell), secondary cells are recharged by passing current backwards (lead–acid, lithium-ion), and a fuel cell makes electricity continuously from fuel fed to it — hydrogen and oxygen in, water out (Iraq, Grade 9 — *الفيزياء للصف الثالث المتوسط (Physics, Third Intermediate = Grade 9)*, Ch.4 §§4-2–4-3, printed p. 79)
344. **Resonance and pitch — why sound rings in some spaces** (`physics/resonance-and-pitch-why-sound-rings-in-some-spaces`) — pitch is set by frequency, and resonance is the large build-up when sound arrives at the natural frequency of an object or of the air in a space, which is why practice rooms, drums and horns ring at some notes and swallow others — private-booklet evidence only, acceptance 0 (Cambodia, Grade 8 — *PISA Sample Test — Physics Grade 8, Student version* (PRIVATE booklet, Preah Sisowath High School, New Generation School), Ch. 6 Lesson 3, printed p. 88)
345. **Dimensional formulas — checking equations by their dimensions** (`physics/dimensional-formulas-checking-equations-by-their-dimensions`) — every quantity written in the base dimensions M, L, T, and the homogeneity sieve: an equation passes only if every term matches dimensionally — a check that kills wrong formulas for free, rebuilds the pendulum law's shape as √(L/g), and honestly cannot see pure numbers like ½ and 2π (Egypt, Grade 11 — *Physics, Second Secondary — First Term*, Unit 1, Ch. 1, printed p. 15)
346. **Viscosity — how thick a liquid flows** (`physics/viscosity-how-thick-a-liquid-flows`) — the internal friction between a liquid's sliding layers, priced by the coefficient η in pascal-seconds — terminal speed of a falling ball, viscosity falling as a liquid warms, and the grade of oil an engine (or a heart pushing blood) actually needs (Egypt, Grade 11 — *Physics, Second Secondary — First Term*, Unit 3, Ch. 4, printed p. 56)
347. **The triangular prism — deviation, minimum deviation and dispersion** (`physics/the-triangular-prism-deviation-minimum-deviation-and-dispersion`) — a ray bent twice toward the base, deviation δ = (i₁ + i₂) − A bottoming out at the symmetric minimum-deviation path where n = sin((A + δm)/2) ÷ sin(A/2), and dispersion fanning white light into its spectrum — violet most, red least (Egypt, Grade 11 — *Physics, Second Secondary — Term 2*, Unit 2, Ch. 3, printed p. 63)
348. **Force between two parallel current-carrying wires** (`physics/force-between-two-parallel-current-carrying-wires`) — each wire's current pushed by the other's field — F/L = μ₀I₁I₂/(2πd), same-direction currents attracting, opposed repelling, and the force that once defined the ampere itself (2 × 10⁻⁷ N per metre at one metre) (Egypt, Grade 12 — *Physics, Third Secondary*, Unit 1, Ch. 2, printed p. 40)
349. **The ohmmeter and the multimeter** (`physics/the-ohmmeter-and-the-multimeter`) — resistance measured directly by a battery-powered series ohmmeter — reversed, non-linear scale, compulsory re-zeroing on every range — and the multimeter that folds ohmmeter, voltmeter and ammeter into one switched instrument (Egypt, Grade 12 — *Physics, Third Secondary*, Unit 1, Ch. 2, printed p. 55)
350. **Self-induction and mutual induction** (`physics/self-induction-and-mutual-induction`) — a coil inducing ε = −L dI/dt in itself (inductance in henrys, energy ½LI² stored in the field, the switch-off spark) and ε₂ = −M dI₁/dt in a linked neighbour — the induction coil built on top (Egypt, Grade 12 — *Physics, Third Secondary*, Unit 1, Ch. 3, printed p. 66)
351. **The electric motor and back EMF** (`physics/the-electric-motor-and-back-emf`) — the current-carrying coil torqued round in a field, and the generator it becomes while spinning: back EMF E′ growing with speed, current I = (V − E′)/R, self-regulating load response, and the V/R starting surge that starter resistances exist to tame (Egypt, Grade 12 — *Physics, Third Secondary*, Unit 1, Ch. 3, printed p. 76)
352. **Eddy currents and their uses** (`physics/eddy-currents-and-their-uses`) — induced swirls of current inside bulk metal — Lenz-opposing, energy-to-heat: a nuisance in cores, sliced away by lamination and ferrite, and a servant in contact-free brakes, galvanometer damping, induction heating and metal detection (Egypt, Grade 12 — *Physics, Third Secondary*, Unit 1, Ch. 3, printed p. 80)
353. **The oscillating LC circuit and electrical resonance** (`physics/the-oscillating-lc-circuit-and-electrical-resonance`) — energy sloshing between capacitor field (Q²/2C) and coil field (½LI²) at f = 1/(2π√(LC)) — and resonance, the strong answer at that one frequency, doing the selecting in every radio receiver (Egypt, Grade 12 — *Physics, Third Secondary*, Unit 1, Ch. 4, printed p. 108)
354. **The electron microscope — seeing with electron waves** (`physics/the-electron-microscope-seeing-with-electron-waves`) — resolution bought with wavelength: electrons at microscope voltages carry picometre de Broglie wavelengths, focused by magnetic lenses in vacuum — viruses, cell machinery and lattices photographed where light's 400–700 nm gives up (Egypt, Grade 12 — *Physics, Third Secondary*, Unit 2, Ch. 5, printed p. 131)
355. **The spectrometer — reading spectra** (`physics/the-spectrometer-reading-spectra`) — collimator, prism table and telescope turning dispersion into angle measurement — minimum-deviation readings converted to wavelengths, and line spectra read as the fingerprints that identify elements from a discharge tube to the Sun (Egypt, Grade 12 — *Physics, Third Secondary*, Unit 2, Ch. 6, printed p. 142)
356. **Holography — recording the whole wavefront** (`physics/holography-recording-the-whole-wavefront`) — a photograph records intensity and loses phase; a hologram records the laser interference of object and reference beams, storing amplitude and phase in microscopic fringes — and replaying the reference beam rebuilds the wavefront as a true 3-D image (Egypt, Grade 12 — *Physics, Third Secondary*, Unit 2, Ch. 7, printed p. 156)
357. **Electronic tuning — the varactor diode** (`physics/electronic-tuning-the-varactor-diode`) — the reverse-biased junction used as a capacitor: depletion width set by voltage, so C — and with it the LC resonant frequency — is voltage-controlled, giving remote, preset and AFC tuning with no moving parts (Egypt, Grade 12 — *Physics, Third Secondary*, Unit 2, Ch. 8, printed p. 186)
358. **Energy supply — sources, storage and wasted transfers** (`physics/energy-supply-sources-storage-and-wasted-transfers`) — do we need energy, and where does the supply come from: non-renewable fuels that finish and renewable flows that vary; energy transported (wires, fuel transport, heat pipes) and stored (fuels, batteries, pumped water, hot tanks) because supply and demand do not match; and in every transfer some energy wasted as spread thermal energy, priced by efficiency = useful output ÷ total input and drawn in a Sankey diagram (Mongolia, Grade 8 — *Физик VIII*, §§4.1–4.3, printed pp. 110, 114, 119)
359. **Electronic systems and digital electronics** (`physics/electronic-systems-and-digital-electronics`) — every electronic system as input (a sensor) → process (a decision) → output (lamp, buzzer, motor, display); digital signals with only two levels, 1 and 0, numbers in binary, and the logic gates NOT, AND and OR written whole in truth tables (Mongolia, Grade 10 — *Физик X*, Ch. 4 §§1–2, printed pp. 81, 97 — image-only book, read at contents/section level via the visual route)
360. **Processing measurement results — errors, graphs and reporting** (`physics/processing-measurement-results-errors-graphs-and-reporting`) — a measurement is finished only when it is processed: random errors scattered both ways, shrunk by repeats and the mean; systematic errors pushing one way, beaten by zeroing and calibration; tables and graphs with a line of best fit whose gradient can carry the physics; and a report that carries its uncertainty (Mongolia, Grade 10 — *Физик X*, Ch. 5, printed p. 105 — image-only book, read at contents/section level via the visual route)
361. **Electric current in different media — gases, vacuum and semiconductors** (`physics/electric-current-in-different-media-gases-vacuum-and-semiconductors`) — the carriers change with the medium: in gases, ions and electrons made by ionisation by collision (the discharge, and lightning at sky scale); in a vacuum, electrons freed by thermionic emission from a hot cathode crossing empty space; in semiconductors, electrons and holes together, engineered by doping into n-type and p-type, whose junction is the diode (Mongolia, Grade 11 — *Физик XI*, §3.3, printed p. 89)
362. **How this Physics course works — the Kuwaiti way** (`physics/how-this-physics-course-works-the-kuwaiti-way`) — every Kuwaiti unit opens the same way — objectives that promise what you will be able to do, a "discover it yourself" inquiry box to try before you are told, and "Physics in the lab" / "Physics and technology" sidebars tying the lesson to its experiment and to real devices such as medicine, wind turbines, solar cells and satellites (Kuwait, Grade 10 — *Physics, Grade 10, Term 1*, unit-opener apparatus, printed p. 13)
363. **Multiplying vectors — dot and cross products** (`physics/multiplying-vectors-dot-and-cross-products`) — two vectors multiplied two ways — the dot product A·B = AB cos θ, a scalar measuring how much they work together (work W = F·s), and the cross product A×B, size AB sin θ, a vector perpendicular to both by the right-hand rule (torque τ = r×F) (Kuwait, Grade 10 — *Physics, Grade 10, Term 1*, Ch. 3, lesson 3-3, printed p. 93)
364. **Charging by induction and charge polarisation** (`physics/charging-by-induction-and-charge-polarisation`) — a charged rod charges a conductor without touching it — free electrons separate, the far side is earthed while the rod stays near, and the conductor keeps the opposite-sign charge — while in insulators the same rod only polarises each atom's charge cloud, which is why neutral paper is attracted (Kuwait, Grade 10 — *Physics, Grade 10, Term 2*, Unit 4, Ch. 1, lesson 1-3, printed p. 52)
365. **Centrifugal force — the outward effect in rotating frames** (`physics/centrifugal-force-the-outward-effect-in-rotating-frames`) — the outward effect riders feel in a turning frame, priced at mv²/r and put to work in the centrifuge and spin dryer — and the ground-frame truth behind it: no outward force, only inertia, straight-line tendency and real inward forces, with released objects flying off along the tangent (Kuwait, Grade 11 — *Physics, Grade 11, Term 1*, Ch. 2, lesson 2-3, printed p. 61)
366. **Toppling — when a body tips over** (`physics/toppling-when-a-body-tips-over`) — a body stands while the line of gravity from its centre of gravity falls inside its base and topples the instant it falls outside — the pivot edge, weight's moment switching from restoring to overturning, and the design rules of low centre of gravity and wide base (Kuwait, Grade 11 — *Physics, Grade 11, Term 1*, Ch. 3, lesson 3-4, printed p. 85)
367. **Centre of gravity of the human body** (`physics/centre-of-gravity-of-the-human-body`) — the body's balance point in the pelvis at about 55% of height, moving with every posture — even outside the body — and balance, walking, carrying and sport as the art of keeping its line of gravity inside the base of the feet (Kuwait, Grade 11 — *Physics, Grade 11, Term 1*, Ch. 3, lesson 3-6, printed p. 95)
368. **Rotational dynamics — torque, inertia and angular acceleration** (`physics/rotational-dynamics-torque-inertia-and-angular-acceleration`) — Newton's second law for rotation, τ = Iα, taught whole — moment of inertia as mass weighted by r², the hoop and disc, rotational energy ½Iω², work τθ and power τω, and the ordered method for spinning problems (Kuwait, Grade 12 — *Physics, Grade 12, Term 1*, Ch. 2, lesson 2-3, printed p. 66)
369. **Models of the atom — Dalton, Thomson, Rutherford, Bohr** (`physics/models-of-the-atom-dalton-thomson-rutherford-bohr`) — the four classical models in the order evidence forced them — Dalton's indivisible sphere, Thomson's plum pudding after the electron, Rutherford's tiny dense nucleus from gold-foil scattering, and Bohr's fixed orbits pricing hydrogen's spectrum with E = hf (Kuwait, Grade 12 — *Physics, Grade 12, Term 2*, Unit 4, Ch. 1, lesson 1-1, printed p. 93)
