370. **How this Physics course works — the Bulgarian Grade 7 way** (`physics/how-this-physics-course-works-the-bulgarian-grade-7-way`) — the Bulgarian Grade 7 course (Pedagog 6) as a numbered sequence of typed lessons — new knowledge, problem-solving, laboratory work, summary and "I know and I can" self-checks — every lesson carrying the same boxes ("I already know…", a worked example, "What I learned", "Curious", questions and tasks), opening with an entry test and closing with an exit test and a glossary (Bulgaria, Grade 7 — *Физика и астрономия за 7. клас (Педагог 6, 2024)*, orientation pages, printed p. 4)
371. **How this Physics course works — the Bulgarian Grade 9 way** (`physics/how-this-physics-course-works-the-bulgarian-grade-9-way`) — one Grade 9 course in two printed parts (Part 1 mechanics and heat, Part 2 electricity, oscillations, waves and the laboratory block; Part 1 filed in some records as "Grade 8" but printed as Part 1 for Grade 9) in the same typed-lesson design, with laboratory works inside the numbered sequence (Bulgaria, Grade 9 — *Физика и астрономия, Първа част за 9. клас (Педагог 6, 2024)*, printed p. 5; *Физика и астрономия за 9. клас, Втора част (Педагог 6, 2024)*, printed p. 4)
372. **How this Physics course works — the Bulgarian Grade 10 way** (`physics/how-this-physics-course-works-the-bulgarian-grade-10-way`) — the Grade 10 course in four strands — electromagnetic phenomena, light, from the atom to the cosmos, and a closing practical-activities block — in the same typed-lesson design, closing with practical activities, glossary and answers (Bulgaria, Grade 10 — *Физика и астрономия за 10. клас (Педагог 6, 2024)*, orientation page, printed p. 6)
373. **Magnetic properties of substances — dia-, para- and ferromagnetism** (`physics/magnetic-properties-of-substances-dia-para-and-ferromagnetism`) — substances answer a magnetic field three ways: diamagnets weakly against it (every substance), paramagnets weakly along it (alignment that warming destroys), and ferromagnets through aligned domains — huge multiplication, saturation, remanence, and collapse to paramagnetism above the Curie temperature (Bulgaria, Grade 10 — *Физика и астрономия за 10. клас (Педагог 6, 2024)*, L11, printed p. 65)

374. **How this Physics course works — the Yemeni Grade 10 way** (`physics/how-this-physics-course-works-the-yemeni-grade-10-way`) — the Yemeni Grade 10 course as a two-book package — every unit opening with printed objectives and bilingual Arabic–English titles, short activities (نشاط) embedded in the lessons, a unit evaluation closing every unit, and a companion activities-and-experiments notebook of eleven experiments in the five-part layout aim → theory → apparatus → steps → conclusion, using local materials (Yemen, Grade 10 — *الفيزياء للصف الأول الثانوي (Physics, First Secondary = Grade 10)*, whole book, printed p. 4; *الأنشطة والتجارب العملية — الفيزياء — Grade 10*, whole book, printed p. 4)
375. **How this Physics course works — the Yemeni Grade 11 way** (`physics/how-this-physics-course-works-the-yemeni-grade-11-way`) — the Yemeni Grade 11 course in the same objectives-first, bilingual design as Grade 10 — English unit titles printed on the unit openers (e.g. “Equilibrium of Rigid Bodies”) — across ten units from equilibrium of rigid bodies through thermodynamics and heat engines to magnetism and electrical measurements (Yemen, Grade 11 — *الفيزياء للصف الثاني الثانوي (Physics, Second Secondary = Grade 11)*, whole book, printed p. 4)
376. **How this Physics course works — the Yemeni Grade 12 way** (`physics/how-this-physics-course-works-the-yemeni-grade-12-way`) — the Yemeni Grade 12 course opened by a preface that walks unit by unit through all nine units — momentum and projectiles to cosmic physics — with an applications strand in every unit, and paired with a companion activities-and-experiments notebook whose ten experiments track the hardest units (Yemen, Grade 12 — *الفيزياء للصف الثالث الثانوي (Physics, Third Secondary = Grade 12)*, preface, printed pp. 4–5; *الأنشطة والتجارب العملية — الفيزياء — Grade 12*, whole book, printed p. 4)
377. **Vibrating strings and resonance in air columns** (`physics/vibrating-strings-and-resonance-in-air-columns`) — a stretched string vibrates in segments — its note set by length, tension and mass per unit length — and air columns resound at their own frequencies: a closed column fits a quarter-wavelength and sounds odd harmonics only, an open column fits a half-wavelength and sounds the full series, the two facts every string and wind instrument is built from (Yemen, Grade 11 — *الفيزياء للصف الثاني الثانوي (Physics, Second Secondary = Grade 11)*, U4, printed p. 109)
378. **Rocket propulsion and escape velocity** (`physics/rocket-propulsion-and-escape-velocity`) — a rocket throws exhaust backward and momentum conservation pushes it forward — self-propulsion needing nothing to push against — escape velocity (about 11.2 km/s from Earth's surface) is the launch speed that leaves Earth's gravity for good, and a satellite launched sideways below it falls around the Earth in an orbit whose speed its height fixes (Yemen, Grade 12 — *الفيزياء للصف الثالث الثانوي (Physics, Third Secondary = Grade 12)*, U1, printed p. 14)
379. **Using solar energy — thermal and photovoltaic generation** (`physics/using-solar-energy-thermal-and-photovoltaic-generation`) — sunlight used two ways — collected as heat in dark collectors for direct thermal uses and, concentrated, to raise steam for thermal generation (light → heat → motion → electricity), or converted straight into electricity by photovoltaic cells with no heat stage and no moving parts — with the Grade 12 companion experiment on collecting and exploiting solar energy (Yemen, Grade 12 — *الفيزياء للصف الثالث الثانوي (Physics, Third Secondary = Grade 12)*, U8, printed p. 192; *الأنشطة والتجارب العملية — الفيزياء — Grade 12*, Exp. 8, printed p. 23)

380. **Power of a lens and combining lenses** (`physics/power-of-a-lens-and-combining-lenses`) — the power of a lens is P = 1/f with the focal length in metres, measured in dioptres — positive for a converging (convex) lens, negative for a diverging (concave) one — and lenses placed in contact add their powers, P = P1 + P2, so the combined focal length follows from 1/F = 1/f1 + 1/f2 (Afghanistan, Grade 10 — *فیزیک — صنف دهم (Physics — Grade 10)*, Ch. 5 Lenses, printed p. 118)
381. **Optical instruments — the camera and the microscope** (`physics/optical-instruments-the-camera-and-the-microscope`) — the camera throws one real, diminished, upside-down image onto a sensor or film with a single converging lens, while the compound microscope magnifies in two stages — objective then eyepiece — multiplying the two magnifications into one large total (Afghanistan, Grade 10 — *فیزیک — صنف دهم (Physics — Grade 10)*, Ch. 5, printed p. 126)
382. **Electric potential and potential difference** (`physics/electric-potential-and-potential-difference`) — electric potential is the work done per unit charge in bringing a charge to a point, measured in volts; the potential difference between two points is what drives current between them, and in a uniform field the two connect by E = V/d — field strength is potential difference per metre (Afghanistan, Grade 10 — *فیزیک — صنف دهم (Physics — Grade 10)*, Ch. 6 Static Electricity, printed p. 154)
383. **Force on a current-carrying conductor and torque on a coil** (`physics/force-on-a-current-carrying-conductor-and-torque-on-a-coil`) — a conductor carrying a current across a magnetic field feels a force F = BIL sinθ, directed by Fleming's left-hand rule; a coil in a field feels two such forces as a couple, giving a turning moment (torque) greatest when the coil is side-on to the field — the working principle of the electric motor and the moving-coil meter (Afghanistan, Grade 10 — *فیزیک — صنف دهم (Physics — Grade 10)*, Ch. 8 Magnetism, printed p. 204)
384. **Wien's law and the Stefan–Boltzmann law** (`physics/wiens-law-and-the-stefan-boltzmann-law`) — a black body's spectrum peaks at a wavelength set by Wien's law, λmax × T = 2.9 × 10⁻³ m·K — hotter means bluer — while the Stefan–Boltzmann law prices its total radiation, P/A = σT⁴ — hotter means enormously brighter, because the fourth power multiplies fast (Afghanistan, Grade 12 — *فزیک — صنف دوازدهم (Physics — Grade 12)*, Ch. 4 Thermal Properties of Materials, printed p. 100)
385. **Kirchhoff's laws — branching circuits** (`physics/kirchhoffs-laws-branching-circuits`) — Kirchhoff's two laws solve circuits that series–parallel rules cannot: the junction rule — currents entering a junction sum to the currents leaving it, because charge is conserved — and the loop rule — the voltage gains and drops around any closed loop sum to zero, because energy is conserved — applied together, loop by loop, to branching circuits with more than one source (Afghanistan, Grade 10 — *فیزیک — صنف دهم (Physics — Grade 10)*, Ch. 7 Circuits and Current, printed p. 193)
386. **How this Physics course works — the Afghanistan way** (`physics/how-this-physics-course-works-the-afghanistan-way`) — Afghanistan's physics course runs in the reverse order of most countries in this library: Grade 9 opens the subject with motion, waves and current electricity, Grade 10 is a full optics + electricity + magnetism year that opens with an explicit “What is physics?” chapter, and only Grade 11 turns to the mechanics year — equilibrium, motion, Newton's laws, energy, momentum and fluids — before Grade 12 closes with oscillations, waves, thermal properties, atomic physics and nuclear physics (Afghanistan, Grades 9–12 — *فیزیک — صنف دهم (Physics — Grade 10)*, Ch. 1, printed p. 1, and the companion grade books, whole books)
387. **How this Physics course works — the Belarusian Grade 7 way** (`physics/how-this-physics-course-works-the-belarusian-grade-7-way`) — the Belarusian Grade 7 book teaches the study method before the physics: its frontispiece «How to work with this textbook» sets the four-move method (read quickly, then deeply; reproduce every derivation in your notebook; repeat the experiments at home), and every numbered paragraph (§) is built to serve it — closing with «Main conclusions» (Галоўныя вывады) and control questions, exercises graded with the hardest starred, small-print optional extras, a closing laboratory chapter and the answers at the back (Belarus, Grade 7 — *Фізіка. 7 клас (пад рэд. Л. А. Ісачанкавай)*, frontispiece, printed p. 3)
388. **How this Physics course works — the Belarusian Grade 8 way** (`physics/how-this-physics-course-works-the-belarusian-grade-8-way`) — the Grade 8 book carries the exact Grade 7 design — frontispiece method, § paragraphs with «Main conclusions» and control questions, starred hardest exercises, small-print extras, closing laboratory chapter — into thermal phenomena, electromagnetic phenomena and light phenomena (Belarus, Grade 8 — *Фізіка. 8 клас (пад рэд. Л. А. Ісачанкавай)*, frontispiece, printed p. 3)
389. **How this Physics course works — the Belarusian Grade 9 way** (`physics/how-this-physics-course-works-the-belarusian-grade-9-way`) — the series design carried into the formal mechanics course: the same frontispiece method and paragraph shape, now fronted by an Introduction on matter, space and time and on scalars and vectors, before Kinematics, Dynamics and the Conservation laws (Belarus, Grade 9 — *Фізіка. 9 клас (пад рэд. А. А. Сакольскага)*, frontispiece/Introduction, printed p. 5)
390. **How this Physics course works — the Belarusian Grade 10 way** (`physics/how-this-physics-course-works-the-belarusian-grade-10-way`) — the Grade 10 study aid opens with «How to work with the study aid» and runs the same series method across two great halves — molecular physics (molecular-kinetic theory, thermodynamics) then electrodynamics (electrostatics, direct current, current in different media, magnetic field and electromagnetic induction) (Belarus, Grade 10 — *Фізіка. 10 клас*, frontispiece, printed p. 3)
391. **Measuring force — the dynamometer** (`physics/measuring-force-the-dynamometer`) — force is measured in newtons (about the weight of a 100 g mass per newton), and the instrument is the dynamometer: a spring whose stretch is proportional to the force (Hooke's law) moves a pointer along an even scale — used by checking the zero, loading smoothly, reading at eye level, and never stretching past the scale's limit (Belarus, Grade 7 — *Фізіка. 7 клас (пад рэд. Л. А. Ісачанкавай)*, §11, printed p. 45; §12, printed p. 48)
392. **Internal energy and how it changes** (`physics/internal-energy-and-how-it-changes`) — a body's internal energy is the total of its particles' kinetic energy of random motion and potential energy of interaction, and it changes in exactly two ways: by work done on or by the body (rubbing, hammering, compressing — or an expanding gas cooling) and by heat transfer from a hotter body to a colder one (Belarus, Grade 8 — *Фізіка. 8 клас (пад рэд. Л. А. Ісачанкавай)*, §2, printed p. 9; §3, printed p. 12)
393. **The structure of solids and liquids — crystals and humidity** (`physics/the-structure-of-solids-and-liquids-crystals-and-humidity`) — crystalline solids hold a strict lattice and one sharp melting point while amorphous solids soften over a range; a liquid's inwards-pulled surface is surface tension; evaporation and condensation balance into saturated vapour; and air humidity prices the vapour actually present against the vapour that would saturate the air, measured with the psychrometer (Belarus, Grade 10 — *Фізіка. 10 клас*, §6, printed p. 43; §7, printed p. 47; §8, printed p. 56)
394. **Alternating and direct current — two kinds of current** (`physics/alternating-and-direct-current-two-kinds-of-current`) — d.c. flows steadily in one direction from batteries, a.c. reverses direction again and again from the mains; transformers step a.c. so grids carry it, and rectifiers in chargers convert it to the d.c. devices store (Bhutan, Class IX — *Physics, Class Nine*, Ch. 4, printed p. 96)
395. **Terminal velocity — why falling objects stop speeding up** (`physics/terminal-velocity-why-falling-objects-stop-speeding-up`) — a falling object accelerates only while weight beats drag; drag grows with speed until it balances weight and the fall settles at a steady terminal velocity — bigger area or less weight means a lower one, which is how a parachute works (Bhutan, Class X — *Physics Textbook, Class X*, Ch. 1, printed pp. 15–20; and Halliday, Resnick & Walker, *Fundamentals of Physics, 10th Edition (Extended)*, Grade 11 record, Ch. 6, printed p. 130)
396. **Entropy and the second law of thermodynamics** (`physics/entropy-and-the-second-law-of-thermodynamics`) — heat flows by itself only from hotter to colder, no heat engine turns all its heat into work, and the universe's entropy never decreases in any real process; refrigerators run heat backwards only by paying work in (Bhutan, adopted text — Halliday, Resnick & Walker, *Fundamentals of Physics, 10th Edition (Extended)*, Grade 11 record, Ch. 20, printed p. 583)
397. **Gauss's law — electric flux and the charge inside** (`physics/gausss-law-electric-flux-and-the-charge-inside`) — the net electric flux out of any closed surface equals the enclosed charge divided by ε₀; outside charges cancel out, and with symmetry the law hands back fields directly, Coulomb's law included (Bhutan, adopted text — Halliday, Resnick & Walker, *Fundamentals of Physics, 10th Edition (Extended)*, Grade 11 record, Ch. 23, printed p. 659)
398. **Maxwell's equations — the laws that unite electricity, magnetism and light** (`physics/maxwells-equations-the-laws-that-unite-electricity-magnetism-and-light`) — four laws gather the whole of electricity and magnetism: charges source electric fields, there are no magnetic monopoles, changing magnetic fields create electric fields, and currents plus changing electric fields create magnetic fields — together predicting electromagnetic waves at light speed, so light itself is one (Bhutan, adopted text — Halliday, Resnick & Walker, *Fundamentals of Physics, 10th Edition (Extended)*, Grade 11 record, Ch. 32, printed p. 941)
399. **The Doppler effect — pitch shifts when source or listener moves** (`physics/the-doppler-effect-pitch-shifts-when-source-or-listener-moves`) — approach squeezes wavefronts and raises pitch, recession stretches them and lowers it, for a moving listener and a moving source alike; in light the same shift becomes blueshift and redshift, the astronomer's speedometer (Bhutan, adopted text — Halliday, Resnick & Walker, *Fundamentals of Physics, 10th Edition (Extended)*, Grade 11 record, Ch. 17, printed pp. 498–503)
400. **Electrons in wells — quantised levels and tunnelling** (`physics/electrons-in-wells-quantised-levels-and-tunnelling`) — a trapped electron is a standing matter wave, so only quantised energies Eₙ = n²h²/(8mL²) are allowed, never zero at the bottom and further apart in narrower wells; and through a thin barrier the wave leaks — tunnelling (Bhutan, adopted text — Halliday, Resnick & Walker, *Fundamentals of Physics, 10th Edition (Extended)*, Grade 11 record, Chs. 38–39, printed pp. 1176, 1186)
401. **History of physics — the Central Asian scholars** (`physics/history-of-physics-the-central-asian-scholars`) — physics did not begin in one country: beside Aristotle, Archimedes, Galileo and Newton stand al-Khwarizmi, Beruni, Ibn Sina and Omar Khayyam, whose measurements, instruments and arguments carried the science forward for centuries (Uzbekistan, Grade 6 — *ФИЗИКА 6*, Introduction, Tema 2, printed p. 6)
402. **The structure of matter — Democritus, Razi, Beruni and Avicenna** (`physics/the-structure-of-matter-democritus-razi-beruni-and-avicenna`) — the atom as an argument two thousand years long: Democritus proposes indivisible particles in the void, Razi defends them, and Beruni questions Avicenna by letter on whether the atom can be divided — settled only when experiment found atoms real and themselves built of smaller parts (Uzbekistan, Grade 6 — *ФИЗИКА 6*, Ch. I, Tema 5, printed p. 12)
403. **Density — the Beruni and Khazin way** (`physics/density-the-beruni-and-khazin-way`) — density ρ = m/V found for any irregular body by Beruni's overflow vessel, which catches the water the body pushes aside, and refined by Khazin's weighing corrected for the buoyancy of air — Central Asia's own historical method for the fingerprint of a substance (Uzbekistan, Grade 6 — *ФИЗИКА 6*, Ch. II, Tema 16, printed p. 39)
404. **The golden rule of mechanics — no mechanism gives a gain in work** (`physics/the-golden-rule-of-mechanics-no-mechanism-gives-a-gain-in-work`) — lever, block, plane and screw all trade force for distance and none creates work; friction takes its share, so efficiency η = A_useful/A_total is always under 100% — the book's table: block 94–98%, screw jack 30–40% (Uzbekistan, Grade 6 — *ФИЗИКА 6*, Ch. III, Temas 31–32, printed pp. 84–86)
405. **Heat in the eyes of Farabi, Beruni and Avicenna** (`physics/heat-in-the-eyes-of-farabi-beruni-and-avicenna`) — heat before the thermometer: Farabi classifies it among the sciences, Beruni measures it by comparison — hot springs, seasons, places — and questions how it travels, and Avicenna builds it into medicine, dosing heating and cooling like drugs (Uzbekistan, Grade 6 — *ФИЗИКА 6*, Ch. IV, Tema 37, printed p. 100)
406. **Electric phenomena — Beruni and Ibn Sina on lightning** (`physics/electric-phenomena-beruni-and-ibn-sina-on-lightning`) — electricity enters through nature: Beruni collects its phenomena (rubbed amber, stunning fish, storm-fire) with his gather-and-compare method, and Ibn Sina places lightning inside the storm cloud as a natural discharge with natural causes, not an omen (Uzbekistan, Grade 8 — *ФИЗИКА 8*, Ch. I, Temas 1 and 8, printed pp. 8, 24)
407. **The strength of solids — Beruni the mineralogist** (`physics/the-strength-of-solids-beruni-the-mineralogist`) — solids answer force elastically, plastically or brittlely, and every one has an ultimate strength; framed through Beruni, whose overflow weighing and scratch tests made minerals' properties a measured science — stress, strain and Young's modulus are his programme in modern form (Uzbekistan, Grade 9 — *ФИЗИКА 9*, Ch. I, Tema 16, printed p. 41)
408. **Measuring the speed of light** (`physics/measuring-the-speed-of-light`) — light is fast but finite: Rømer (1676) read about 300,000 km/s off the eclipses of Jupiter's moon Io, late as Earth recedes and early as it approaches — the delay is travel time across the changing distance, and c stands as the universal speed limit (Uzbekistan, Grade 6 — *ФИЗИКА 6*, Ch. V, Tema 46, printed p. 122; Grade 9 — *ФИЗИКА 9*, Ch. II, Tema 30, printed p. 79)
409. **Light in the letters of Beruni and Avicenna** (`physics/light-in-the-letters-of-beruni-and-avicenna`) — the famous correspondence argues optics itself: the eye receives light rather than sending rays (Avicenna), the rainbow stands opposite the Sun as geometry plus droplets, and a water-filled sphere bends light to a burning focus — a lens in a bowl (Uzbekistan, Grade 6 — *ФИЗИКА 6*, Ch. V, Tema 47, printed p. 124)
410. **Resistors, rheostats and potentiometers** (`physics/resistors-rheostats-and-potentiometers`) — three governors of a circuit: the fixed resistor (ohms, I = V/R, heat P = I²R), the rheostat — a sliding variable resistor in series governing current — and the potentiometer, the same hardware on three terminals dividing voltage for the next stage (Uzbekistan, Grade 8 — *ФИЗИКА 8*, Ch. II, Temas 19–20, printed pp. 49–51)
411. **Light at work — photosynthesis and photography** (`physics/light-at-work-photosynthesis-and-photography`) — light does chemical work: in the leaf it builds sugar from CO₂ and water, banking sunlight as food and releasing oxygen; on film it writes a latent image in silver halide that developer and fixer make permanent (Uzbekistan, Grade 9 — *ФИЗИКА 9*, Ch. II, Tema 37, printed p. 90)
412. **Radiation and living things — protection** (`physics/radiation-and-living-things-protection`) — ionising radiation breaks molecules in cells, DNA included, and harm follows dose (sieverts; background a few mSv a year); protection is ALARA plus three rules anyone can use — shorten the time, increase the distance (inverse-square), and shield (Uzbekistan, Grade 9 — *ФИЗИКА 9*, Ch. III, Tema 42, printed p. 111)
413. **The physical picture of the world — physics, technology and society** (`physics/the-physical-picture-of-the-world-physics-technology-and-society`) — the series' closing assembly: matter in motion interacting through fields under laws experiment keeps correcting; the picture applied as technology, with society choosing what gets built; and Uzbekistan inside the picture, from Beruni to its solar and nuclear work (Uzbekistan, Grade 9 — *ФИЗИКА 9*, Ch. V, Temas 56–58, printed pp. 149–151)
414. **How this Physics course works — the NCERT way** (`physics/how-this-physics-course-works-the-ncert-way`) — how an NCERT Physics chapter is built and how to use it: numbered sections teach one idea at a time, worked examples sit inside the text at the moment the idea lands, blue-box margin notes and Points to Ponder boxes stop the reader to think, and every chapter closes with exercises plus additional exercises (India, Class XI — *Physics Part–I, Textbook for Class XI, NCERT*, whole book)
415. **Newton's law of cooling** (`physics/newtons-law-of-cooling`) — a hot body loses heat at a rate proportional to the difference between its temperature and the surroundings' temperature, so cooling is fast while the gap is wide and slows as the body nears room temperature (India, Class XI — *Physics Part–II, Textbook for Class XI, NCERT*, Ch. 10, §10.10, printed p. 219)

## Unit 17 — Chemistry

1. **Acids — properties and behaviour** (`chemistry/acids-properties-and-behaviour`) — Arrhenius acids release hydrogen ions (protons) in aqueous solution;
2. **Addition vs condensation polymerisation** (`chemistry/addition-vs-condensation-polymerisation`) — Addition polymerisation: monomers with C=C bonds open their double bonds and add on, one after another — nothing is lost.
3. **Air pollution** (`chemistry/air-pollution`) — Major air pollutants include carbon monoxide, sulfur and nitrogen oxides, particulates and unburned hydrocarbons, from vehicles, industry and burning as presented.
4. **Alcohol properties — hydrogen bonding again** (`chemistry/alcohol-properties-hydrogen-bonding-again`) — Alcohols hydrogen-bond through their –OH groups — to each other and to water.
5. **Aldehydes and ketones — the carbonyl group** (`chemistry/aldehydes-and-ketones-the-carbonyl-group`) — Aldehydes and ketones share the carbonyl group, C=O.
6. **Aldehydes vs ketones — telling them apart** (`chemistry/aldehydes-vs-ketones-telling-them-apart`) — Both families are polar (higher boiling than alkanes) but cannot hydrogen-bond to each other, and the smaller members dissolve in water.
7. **Alkane properties — gases, liquids and the trend** (`chemistry/alkane-properties-gases-liquids-and-the-trend`) — Across the series, melting/boiling points and density rise with chain length (and branching lowers boiling point);
8. **Alkane reactions — burning and substitution** (`chemistry/alkane-reactions-burning-and-substitution`) — Alkanes are relatively unreactive ("paraffin" behaviour): they burn completely in plentiful oxygen to carbon dioxide and water (the fuel reaction — octane's combustion equation is
9. **Alkanes and the homologous series** (`chemistry/alkanes-and-the-homologous-series`) — A homologous series is a family whose members differ by successive –CH₂– units, share a general formula and similar chemistry, and show graded physical properties.
10. **Alkanes at work — fuels** (`chemistry/alkanes-at-work-fuels`) — The alkane family at work: fuels (natural gas, bottled gas, petrol-range liquids, paraffin wax and the rest of the fraction ladder), with methane's role as the main constituent of
11. **Alkenes — addition across the double bond** (`chemistry/alkenes-addition-across-the-double-bond`) — Alkenes (olefins), CₙH₂ₙ, carry one C=C double bond that dominates their chemistry.
12. **Alkynes — ethyne from carbide** (`chemistry/alkynes-ethyne-from-carbide`) — Alkynes, CₙH₂ₙ₋₂, carry a C≡C triple bond.
13. **Alloys — mixing metals on purpose** (`chemistry/alloys-mixing-metals-on-purpose`) — Alloys are mixtures of metals (or a metal with a nonmetal such as carbon) made to gain properties the pure metals lack — hardness, strength, corrosion resistance;
14. **Aluminium — extraction by electrolysis** (`chemistry/aluminium-extraction-by-electrolysis`) — Aluminium, the most abundant metal in the crust, is extracted by electrolysis of molten aluminium oxide mixed with cryolite (which lowers the working temperature) — the Hall (Ha…
15. **Ammonia — the Haber process** (`chemistry/ammonia-the-haber-process`) — The Haber process makes ammonia: N₂ + 3H₂ ⇌ 2NH₃, a reversible, exothermic reaction.
16. **Arrhenius acids and bases — and where the idea stops** (`chemistry/arrhenius-acids-and-bases-and-where-the-idea-stops`) — Arrhenius: an acid is a substance that gives H⁺ ions in aqueous solution; a base gives OH⁻ ions in aqueous solution.
17. **Atomic number and mass number** (`chemistry/atomic-number-and-mass-number`) — Atomic number Z = number of protons (the identity of the element);
18. **Atomic radius — size trends** (`chemistry/atomic-radius-size-trends`) — Atomic radius is half the distance between nuclei of two neighbouring atoms (in a metal network, or in a diatomic molecule such as iodine).
19. **Average atomic mass — the weighted mean** (`chemistry/average-atomic-mass-the-weighted-mean`) — Isotope masses are expressed relative to carbon-12 (the atomic mass unit, also called the dalton).
20. **Balancing equations — inspection, LCM and algebra** (`chemistry/balancing-equations-inspection-lcm-and-algebra`) — Three named balancing methods are taught: the inspection method (trial adjustment of coefficients), the least common multiple (LCM) method, and the algebraic method (assigning u…
21. **Balancing redox equations** (`chemistry/balancing-redox-equations`) — A stepwise method: assign oxidation numbers, draw the change bridges, make total increase equal total decrease (the electrons lost equal those gained), insert those coefficients, t
22. **Bases — properties and behaviour** (`chemistry/bases-properties-and-behaviour`) — Arrhenius bases release hydroxide (OH⁻) ions in aqueous solution;
23. **Batteries — dry cells and the car battery** (`chemistry/batteries-dry-cells-and-the-car-battery`) — Secondary cells are rechargeable (reversible reactions): the automobile lead–acid battery, whose electrolyte IS aqueous sulphuric acid, is the model.
24. **Benzene — Kekulé's ring and the hybrid** (`chemistry/benzene-kekule-s-ring-and-the-hybrid`) — Benzene opens the aromatics: Kekulé (1872) proposed a six-carbon ring with alternating double and single bonds;
25. **Benzene reactions — substitution, not addition** (`chemistry/benzene-reactions-substitution-not-addition`) — Physically a colourless liquid of characteristic smell, insoluble in water.
26. **Bohr's model — electrons in fixed levels** (`chemistry/bohr-s-model-electrons-in-fixed-levels`) — Bohr (1913), Rutherford's student, repaired the collapse problem: electrons orbit only at specific allowed distances — energy levels/shells designated K, L, M, N — without radiatin
27. **Bohr's model and hydrogen energy levels** (`chemistry/bohrs-model-and-hydrogen-energy-levels`) — Bohr's model puts the electron in fixed allowed orbits — energy levels labelled n = 1, 2, 3 … up to ∞.
28. **Boiling, vapour pressure and heating curves** (`chemistry/boiling-vapour-pressure-and-heating-curves`) — Vapour pressure is the pressure of a liquid's vapour above it; it rises with temperature.
29. **Brønsted–Lowry — protons handed over** (`chemistry/bronsted-lowry-protons-handed-over`) — Brønsted–Lowry: an acid is a proton (H⁺) donor; a base is a proton acceptor.
30. **Buffers — holding pH steady** (`chemistry/buffers-holding-ph-steady`) — A buffer is a solution of a weak acid with its salt (its conjugate base), or a weak base with its salt.
31. **Burning wood — a transformation in full** (`chemistry/burning-wood-a-transformation-in-full`) — the change releases heat energy to the surroundings, and defining "structure", "transformation" and "energy" is to be done through this example.
32. **Calculating with significant figures** (`chemistry/calculating-with-significant-figures`) — Addition/subtraction answers keep no more decimal places than the least-precise input;
33. **Carboxylic acids in daily life** (`chemistry/carboxylic-acids-in-daily-life`) — Vinegar is a dilute solution of ethanoic (acetic) acid, used in food and preserving.
34. **Carboxylic acids — structure and names** (`chemistry/carboxylic-acids-structure-and-names`) — The carboxyl group –COOH joins a carbonyl C=O and a hydroxyl –OH on one carbon.
35. **Cement — clinker to concrete** (`chemistry/cement-clinker-to-concrete`) — Cement starts from limestone and clay, crushed and blended as a raw mix.
36. **Ceramics — from clay to fired ware** (`chemistry/ceramics-from-clay-to-fired-ware`) — Ceramics begin with clay preparation — cleaning, kneading and mixing to a workable body.
37. **Chadwick and the neutron** (`chemistry/chadwick-and-the-neutron`) — Chadwick bombarded beryllium and produced a radiation made of neutral particles.
38. **Chemical reactions — reactants become products** (`chemistry/chemical-reactions-reactants-become-products`) — A physical change forms no new substance (evaporating, powdering sugar, melting ice);
39. **Chemistry calculations — the Grade 11 problem strand** (`chemistry/chemistry-calculations-the-grade-11-problem-strand`) — The method, for every calculation in the course: write what is Given, state what is Wanted, choose the Formula, then substitute with Units and check the answer's size.
40. **Chemistry in building — cement, steel, glass and the GERD** (`chemistry/chemistry-in-building-cement-steel-glass-and-the-gerd`) — Chemistry supplies glass, steel and cement for safer houses, multi-storey buildings, durable dams and bridges;
41. **Chemistry in farming and food** (`chemistry/chemistry-in-farming-and-food`) — Chemical fertilisers (calcium superphosphate, urea, ammonium sulphate, sodium nitrate) raise crop yields;
42. **Chemistry in medicine — six kinds of drug** (`chemistry/chemistry-in-medicine-six-kinds-of-drug`) — Sulphur drugs and penicillin opened the antibiotic era;
43. **Chemistry, the central science** (`chemistry/chemistry-the-central-science`) — Science learns about the universe by observing, testing and modelling;
44. **Chlorine — bleach and disinfectant from brine** (`chemistry/chlorine-bleach-and-disinfectant-from-brine`) — Chlorine is manufactured by the electrolysis of brine.
45. **Cleaner production — Unit 5 review** (`chemistry/cleaner-production-unit-5-review`) — Cleaner production prevents waste at the source: redesigning processes, reusing materials and improving efficiency inside the plant.
46. **Coal — coke, coal gas and coal tar** (`chemistry/coal-coke-coal-gas-and-coal-tar`) — Heating coal out of contact with air (destructive distillation/coking) splits it into coke (nearly pure carbon — blast-furnace fuel, and the source of water gas, H₂ + CO, and produ
47. **Collision theory — what speeds a reaction up — Unit 4 review** (`chemistry/collision-theory-what-speeds-a-reaction-up-unit-4-review`) — Collision theory: reactions happen when particles collide with enough energy (at least the activation energy) and in the right orientation — an effective collision.
48. **Converting concentration units** (`chemistry/converting-concentration-units`) — A conversion section links all five units: given density, a solution's percent, molarity, molality, mole fraction and normality can be interconverted, and pupils run the full ch…
49. **Coordinate covalent bonds** (`chemistry/coordinate-covalent-bonds`) — A covalent bond in which both shared electrons come from one atom: the donor supplies a lone pair to an electron-poor partner;
50. **Copper — from chalcopyrite to pure wire** (`chemistry/copper-from-chalcopyrite-to-pure-wire`) — Copper is extracted by roasting its ore chalcopyrite (CuFeS₂);
51. **Corrosion — iron as its own cell** (`chemistry/corrosion-iron-as-its-own-cell`) — Rusting is electrochemical: at anodic regions iron is oxidised, Fe → Fe²⁺ + 2e⁻; at cathodic regions oxygen and water take the electrons, O₂ + 2H₂O + 4e⁻ → 4OH⁻.
52. **Covalent bonding and Lewis structures** (`chemistry/covalent-bonding-and-lewis-structures`) — In a covalent bond, atoms share a pair of electrons; both atoms count the shared pair toward their shells.
53. **Covalent bonding — sharing electron pairs** (`chemistry/covalent-bonding-sharing-electron-pairs`) — formation is walked through atom by atom for the first molecules, and pupils model single/double/triple molecules.
54. **Cracking — and what octane number means** (`chemistry/cracking-and-what-octane-number-means`) — Fuel quality is measured by octane number (antiknock rating): 2,2,4-trimethylpentane ("isooctane") = 100, heptane = 0, and an 87-octane petrol knocks like an 87:13 mixture of th…
55. **Cycloalkanes — alkanes in rings** (`chemistry/cycloalkanes-alkanes-in-rings`) — Ring alkanes (alicyclic hydrocarbons) close the chain into a ring, losing two hydrogens to the general formula CₙH₂ₙ;
56. **Dalton revised — the modern atomic theory** (`chemistry/dalton-revised-the-modern-atomic-theory`) — Modern science keeps Dalton's frame but corrects three postulates.
57. **Dalton's atomic theory** (`chemistry/dalton-s-atomic-theory`) — Dalton (1808), the British schoolteacher who revived atomos on a scientific basis: each element consists of one kind of indivisible atom;
58. **Dalton's atomic theory** (`chemistry/daltons-atomic-theory`) — Dalton's early-1800s theory says: every element is made of tiny particles called atoms.
59. **De Broglie — matter waves** (`chemistry/de-broglie-matter-waves`) — De Broglie proposed that every moving particle has a wavelength, given by λ = h/mv (h is Planck's constant, m mass, v speed).
60. **Democritus — atoms and the void** (`chemistry/democritus-atoms-and-the-void`) — Leucippus and his pupil Democritus (c.
61. **Derived units and density** (`chemistry/derived-units-and-density`) — Derived units are built by substituting base units into defining equations (area m², speed m/s, force = newton, pressure = pascal, energy = joule, in a table).
62. **Describing a gas — P, V, T, n** (`chemistry/describing-a-gas-p-v-t-n`) — A gas is described by pressure P, volume V, temperature T and amount n (moles).
63. **Discovering the electron — cathode rays** (`chemistry/discovering-the-electron-cathode-rays`) — their listed properties include affecting a ZnS screen, ionising gases, penetrating matter and producing X-rays on metals.
64. **Discovering the neutron** (`chemistry/discovering-the-neutron`) — Chadwick (1932) bombarded beryllium with α-particles and found radiation of material particles un-deflected by electric fields — neutral particles, the neutrons Rutherford had p…
65. **Discovering the nucleus — the gold-foil experiment** (`chemistry/discovering-the-nucleus-the-gold-foil-experiment`) — Rutherford, Geiger and Marsden fired α-particles at thin gold foil (1909/1911): most passed straight, a small fraction deflected at large angles, a few rebounded — impossible on…
66. **Discovering the proton** (`chemistry/discovering-the-proton`) — Electrical neutrality predicted positive particles;
67. **Döbereiner's triads** (`chemistry/dobereiner-s-triads`) — The scheme failed to generalise: only a few triads exist and new elements did not fit.
68. **Drawing organic molecules; functional groups** (`chemistry/drawing-organic-molecules-functional-groups`) — Hydrocarbons (carbon + hydrogen only) are the parent family: saturated (single bonds only) vs unsaturated (double/triple bonds present).
69. **Dynamic equilibrium** (`chemistry/dynamic-equilibrium`) — Equilibrium is reached when the forward and reverse rates become equal.
70. **Effective nuclear charge and shielding** (`chemistry/effective-nuclear-charge-and-shielding`) — Before the four trends, the explanatory tool is built: outer electrons feel not the full nuclear charge but an effective one, reduced by shielding — inner electrons partially ca…
71. **Electrochemistry review — including fuel cells** (`chemistry/electrochemistry-review-including-fuel-cells`) — Unit 2 consolidates: electrolytic cells consume electricity to force change; galvanic cells produce it from spontaneous change; potentials rank the push; Faraday's laws do the b…
72. **Electrolysis at work in industry** (`chemistry/electrolysis-at-work-in-industry`) — Electroplating coats an object (made the cathode) with a thin layer of a chosen metal — for protection and appearance.
73. **Electrolysis case studies** (`chemistry/electrolysis-case-studies`) — Electrolysis of brine (concentrated sodium chloride solution): hydrogen at the cathode, chlorine at the anode, and sodium hydroxide left in solution — three products, one cell.
74. **Electrolysis of molten salts — and what electrolysis is for** (`chemistry/electrolysis-of-molten-salts-and-what-electrolysis-is-for`) — In a molten binary electrolyte the only ions present discharge at the electrodes: cations are reduced to metal at the cathode, anions oxidised at the anode (worked on a molten s…
75. **Electrolytes — solutions that conduct** (`chemistry/electrolytes-solutions-that-conduct`) — An ionic solute dissolves into separate ions;
76. **Electrolytic cells — electricity forcing change** (`chemistry/electrolytic-cells-electricity-forcing-change`) — Electrolysis is the decomposition of an electrolyte by an electric current, in an electrolytic cell driven by an external power source.
77. **Electrolytic cells — forcing a reaction with electricity** (`chemistry/electrolytic-cells-forcing-a-reaction-with-electricity`) — An electrolytic cell converts electrical energy into chemical energy: a DC source forces a non-spontaneous redox reaction.
78. **Electromagnetic radiation and the spectrum** (`chemistry/electromagnetic-radiation-and-the-spectrum`) — Electromagnetic radiation travels as waves, described by wavelength λ (the length of one wave), frequency ν (waves per second) and amplitude (wave height).
79. **Electron affinity** (`chemistry/electron-affinity`) — Electron affinity is the energy change when a neutral gaseous atom gains an electron to form a negative ion.
80. **Electron configuration — filling the shells** (`chemistry/electron-configuration-filling-the-shells`) — Electrons occupy main energy levels (shells) K, L, M, N around the nucleus, each shell holding a limited number of electrons, filling from the innermost out;
81. **Electron configuration — sublevel notation** (`chemistry/electron-configuration-sublevel-notation`) — Configuration is written as coefficient (main level) + letter (sublevel s, p…) + superscript (electrons in the sublevel): lithium 1s²2s¹, sodium 1s²2s²2p⁶3s¹ = (2,8,1) with 3 sh…
82. **Electronegativity** (`chemistry/electronegativity`) — Electronegativity — an atom's pull on shared electrons in a bond — is the fourth major trend, rising across a period and falling down a group with fluorine at the top;
83. **Empirical and molecular formulas** (`chemistry/empirical-and-molecular-formulas`) — The empirical formula gives the smallest whole-number ratio of atoms;

99. **From volts to equilibrium — and a pH meter** (`chemistry/from-volts-to-equilibrium-and-a-ph-meter`) — Electrical work links to free energy: ΔG° = −nFE°, where n is moles of electrons and F the Faraday constant.
100. **Galvanic cells — chemistry making electricity** (`chemistry/galvanic-cells-chemistry-making-electricity`) — A galvanic (voltaic) cell turns a spontaneous redox reaction into electric current by separating oxidant and reductant into two half-cells joined by a wire and a salt bridge.

104. **Greenhouse gases — which and how much** (`chemistry/greenhouse-gases-which-and-how-much`) — The main greenhouse gases treated are carbon dioxide (CO₂), water vapour (H₂O), methane (CH₄), nitrous oxide (N₂O) and ozone.
105. **Groups and periods** (`chemistry/groups-and-periods`) — Periods are the 7 horizontal rows (same number of shells within a period;

107. **Heisenberg's uncertainty principle** (`chemistry/heisenbergs-uncertainty-principle`) — Heisenberg's uncertainty principle: the position and the momentum of a particle cannot both be known exactly at the same time.
108. **How a chemical industry works** (`chemistry/how-a-chemical-industry-works`) — A manufacturing industry is characterised by its raw materials, its process (the chemistry run continuously), and its products and by-products.
109. **How glass is made** (`chemistry/how-glass-is-made`) — Glass is made by melting sand (silica), soda ash (sodium carbonate) and limestone (calcium carbonate) together at high temperature.

115. **Hydrogen bonding and intermolecular forces** (`chemistry/hydrogen-bonding-and-intermolecular-forces`) — 138–141 and 148–151 (answers 45, 50–57).

122. **Ionisation energy** (`chemistry/ionisation-energy`) — Ionisation energy is the energy an isolated gaseous atom in its ground state must absorb to discharge an electron, forming a cation (kJ/mol).

125. **Isotopes** (`chemistry/isotopes`) — Isotopes are atoms of the same element (same Z) differing in neutrons and mass number.

132. **Lattice energy — why ionic solids hold together** (`chemistry/lattice-energy-why-ionic-solids-hold-together`) — Making ions costs steps of energy: ionisation energy to remove electrons, repaid in part by electron affinity.
133. **Le Chatelier's principle** (`chemistry/le-chateliers-principle`) — Le Chatelier's principle: when a system at equilibrium is disturbed, it shifts in the direction that partly counteracts the change.

151. **Metallic bonding** (`chemistry/metallic-bonding`) — In metallic bonding, metal atoms pool their valence electrons.

187. **Oxygen — manufacture and abundance** (`chemistry/oxygen-manufacture-and-abundance`) — Oxygen is about 20% of air by volume, is manufactured by fractional distillation of liquid air (the same plant as nitrogen), and is the most abundant element in the earth's crust.
188. **Paper and pulp** (`chemistry/paper-and-pulp`) — Pulping breaks wood (or other fibre) down to a fibre pulp — mechanically or chemically, as presented.

200. **Precision vs accuracy** (`chemistry/precision-vs-accuracy`) — Accuracy is closeness of a measurement to the true value;

204. **Preserving food** (`chemistry/preserving-food`) — Drying removes water microbes need; salting draws water out likewise.
205. **Properties of carboxylic acids** (`chemistry/properties-of-carboxylic-acids`) — Carboxylic acids hydrogen-bond in pairs — dimers — two molecules held by two hydrogen bonds, which raises boiling points high.
206. **Properties of covalent compounds** (`chemistry/properties-of-covalent-compounds`) — few polar covalent compounds dissolve in polar solvents such as methanol/ethanol).

213. **Proving conservation of mass by weighing** (`chemistry/proving-conservation-of-mass-by-weighing`) — A calculation applies the law (potassium carbonate mass from KOH + CO₂ − water).

233. **Solids — crystalline and amorphous — Unit 3 review** (`chemistry/solids-crystalline-and-amorphous-unit-3-review`) — Crystalline solids have particles in a regular, repeating order and melt at a sharp temperature.
234. **Solubility rules — will it dissolve?** (`chemistry/solubility-rules-will-it-dissolve`) — A rule table predicts which ionic compounds dissolve in water and which precipitate, organised by ion (the always-soluble families and the exception families), used to predict p…
235. **Solution stoichiometry — moles from molarity × volume** (`chemistry/solution-stoichiometry-moles-from-molarity-volume`) — Stoichiometry where the amounts arrive as volumes × molarities: moles = M × V feeds the mole-ratio chain, covering titration-style and precipitation-amount problems.
236. **Sorting polymers — Unit 4 review** (`chemistry/sorting-polymers-unit-4-review`) — By origin: natural polymers (rubber, cellulose, proteins) versus synthetic ones (plastics, nylon).
237. **Sorting the elements — blocks and kinds** (`chemistry/sorting-the-elements-blocks-and-kinds`) — Elements sort into metals (left and centre), non-metals (right) and metalloids along the border between them.
238. **Standard potentials — ranking the push** (`chemistry/standard-potentials-ranking-the-push`) — The standard hydrogen electrode (SHE) is the reference electrode, assigned E° = 0.
239. **Sulphur — mined with hot water; the Contact Process** (`chemistry/sulphur-mined-with-hot-water-the-contact-process`) — Sulphur occurs as the element in underground deposits and is extracted by the Frasch process (superheated water melts it in place and air lifts it out).
240. **Sulphuric acid — the Contact process** (`chemistry/sulphuric-acid-the-contact-process`) — The Contact process: sulfur (or sulfide ore) is burned to SO₂; SO₂ is oxidised over a vanadium(V) oxide catalyst to SO₃ — the contact step.
241. **Suspensions, solutions and colloids** (`chemistry/suspensions-solutions-and-colloids`) — Heterogeneous mixtures have physically distinct parts;
242. **Tanning — hide to leather** (`chemistry/tanning-hide-to-leather`) — Tannery work prepares hides: soaking, liming and removing hair and flesh.
243. **Temperature scales — kelvin, Celsius, Fahrenheit** (`chemistry/temperature-scales-kelvin-celsius-fahrenheit`) — Temperature measures hotness/coldness;
244. **Testing for ions** (`chemistry/testing-for-ions`) — and heating with aluminium), and carbon dioxide (limewater in the section work).
245. **Testing oxides with indicators** (`chemistry/testing-oxides-with-indicators`) — and aluminium oxide treated with both hydrochloric acid and sodium hydroxide, its indicator behaviour proving it amphoteric.
246. **The common ion effect** (`chemistry/the-common-ion-effect`) — The common ion effect: adding an ion that a weak electrolyte already produces suppresses its ionisation.
247. **The electron-sea model** (`chemistry/the-electron-sea-model`) — The electron-sea model pictures a metal as positive kernels arranged in a lattice, immersed in a 'sea' of delocalised electrons.
248. **The equilibrium constant Kc** (`chemistry/the-equilibrium-constant-kc`) — The law of mass action gives the equilibrium expression: products' concentrations over reactants', each raised to its equation coefficient.
249. **The factor-label method — converting units by cancelling** (`chemistry/the-factor-label-method-converting-units-by-cancelling`) — The guide's Unit 2 overview and competency list state that the factor-label (unit-conversion) method is introduced in this unit and used throughout it for SI conversions.
250. **The five branches of chemistry** (`chemistry/the-five-branches-of-chemistry`) — The scope of chemistry is then widened: explaining the natural world, careers, informed citizens — and a warning that chemistry also produces dangerous substances that harm peop…
251. **The four quantum numbers** (`chemistry/the-four-quantum-numbers`) — The principal quantum number n gives the main energy level and the size of the orbital.
252. **The four spheres of the environment** (`chemistry/the-four-spheres-of-the-environment`) — The atmosphere is the envelope of gases around the Earth; the hydrosphere is all its water.
253. **The gas laws and the ideal gas equation** (`chemistry/the-gas-laws-and-the-ideal-gas-equation`) — Boyle's law: at constant temperature, pressure × volume is constant — squeeze the gas and its pressure rises in inverse proportion.
254. **The Greek four elements** (`chemistry/the-greek-four-elements`) — Greek thought begins with Empedocles' claim that all materials are made of four elements — earth, air, water and fire;
255. **The greenhouse effect and global warming** (`chemistry/the-greenhouse-effect-and-global-warming`) — The greenhouse effect: greenhouse gases let sunlight in but absorb outgoing infrared radiation, warming the Earth — a natural effect that human-added gases are strengthening.
256. **The kinetic theory of matter** (`chemistry/the-kinetic-theory-of-matter`) — The kinetic theory: all matter is made of particles in motion.
257. **The law of conservation of mass** (`chemistry/the-law-of-conservation-of-mass`) — A scientific law describes or predicts phenomena from repeated observation without explaining why;
258. **The law of definite proportions — and molecular weight** (`chemistry/the-law-of-definite-proportions-and-molecular-weight`) — Proust's law: the masses of elements in a compound always occur in the same proportion, wherever the compound comes from.
259. **The law of multiple proportions** (`chemistry/the-law-of-multiple-proportions`) — Dalton's law: when two elements form more than one compound, the masses of one element combining with a fixed mass of the other stand in ratios of small whole numbers — the third o
260. **The liquid state** (`chemistry/the-liquid-state`) — In a liquid, particles are close and attract each other, yet can move past one another.
261. **The modern periodic law** (`chemistry/the-modern-periodic-law`) — The long form of the table is the modern table.
262. **The modern periodic table** (`chemistry/the-modern-periodic-table`) — The periodic law: properties of the elements repeat periodically when elements are arranged by increasing atomic number.
263. **The mole — counting by weighing** (`chemistry/the-mole-counting-by-weighing`) — A mole is the amount containing as many entities as there are atoms in exactly 12 g of carbon-12: 6.022×10²³, Avogadro's number.
264. **The octet rule — why atoms bond** (`chemistry/the-octet-rule-why-atoms-bond`) — Atoms bond to become more stable — the drive behind all bonding.
265. **The pH scale** (`chemistry/the-ph-scale`) — acidity is measured practically with indicators or a pH meter.
266. **The s, p, d and f blocks** (`chemistry/the-s-p-d-and-f-blocks`) — an exercise has pupils find, for N, Na, Si, Fe, Zn, Kr and Ce, the valence electrons and the block of the last electron.
267. **The scientific method** (`chemistry/the-scientific-method`) — Chemistry is an experimental, evidence-based science (the "central science" again, now as method).
268. **The seven SI base units** (`chemistry/the-seven-si-base-units`) — The International System (1960) rests on seven base units, given in a table with quantity, name and symbol: length–metre–m, mass–kilogram–kg, time–second–s, electric current–ampere
269. **Titrations and their curves — Unit 1 review** (`chemistry/titrations-and-their-curves-unit-1-review`) — A titration adds a solution of known concentration from a burette to a measured volume of the other, to find its concentration by neutralisation calculation.
270. **Triglycerides — the structure of fats** (`chemistry/triglycerides-the-structure-of-fats`) — A triglyceride is glycerol esterified with three fatty acids — three ester groups in one molecule.
271. **Uncertainty — no measurement is exact** (`chemistry/uncertainty-no-measurement-is-exact`) — Every measurement carries uncertainty, in two kinds: systematic (consistently too large or small — reaction time, parallax, miscalibrated balance;
272. **Using K and Q — which way will it go?** (`chemistry/using-k-and-q-which-way-will-it-go`) — The reaction quotient Q is the equilibrium expression filled with the concentrations right now, not at equilibrium.
273. **Valence bond theory and hybridisation** (`chemistry/valence-bond-theory-and-hybridisation`) — Valence bond theory: a covalent bond forms where atomic orbitals overlap, the shared pair sitting in the overlap.
274. **Valence electrons — the outer shell** (`chemistry/valence-electrons-the-outer-shell`) — The electrons in the outermost shell are the valence electrons — the ones that will matter for bonding (Unit 5's bridge);
275. **Volume–volume problems — gases only** (`chemistry/volumevolume-problems-gases-only`) — For gases, coefficients also give volume ratios directly (equal conditions), so gas volumes convert through the mole ratio without passing through moles;
276. **VSEPR — the shapes of molecules** (`chemistry/vsepr-the-shapes-of-molecules`) — VSEPR theory: electron pairs around a central atom repel each other and arrange themselves as far apart as possible — the arrangement fixes the molecule's shape.
277. **Water ionises itself — pH and pOH** (`chemistry/water-ionises-itself-ph-and-poh`) — Water self-ionises: H₂O + H₂O ⇌ H₃O⁺ + OH⁻.
278. **Water pollution** (`chemistry/water-pollution`) — Water pollutants include fertiliser runoff (nitrates and phosphates), phosphate detergents, untreated sewage, pesticides and heavy metals — the guide's answer key lists the same…
279. **What changes solubility — temperature and pressure** (`chemistry/what-changes-solubility-temperature-and-pressure`) — Temperature usually raises the solubility of solids and liquids in water but lowers that of gases;
280. **What chemistry is — substance, matter and transformation** (`chemistry/what-chemistry-is-substance-matter-and-transformation`) — Chemistry is defined as the science of the properties, composition and structure of substances, the transformations they undergo, and the energy released or absorbed.
281. **What environmental chemistry studies** (`chemistry/what-environmental-chemistry-studies`) — Environmental chemistry studies chemical processes and pollutants in air, water and soil.
282. **What equilibrium needs** (`chemistry/what-equilibrium-needs`) — Equilibrium needs a closed system — nothing enters or leaves.
283. **What industrial chemistry is** (`chemistry/what-industrial-chemistry-is`) — Industrial chemistry turns raw materials into valuable products at scale.
284. **When both ions hydrolyse** (`chemistry/when-both-ions-hydrolyse`) — When both ions hydrolyse, both reactions run: the anion pulls toward basic, the cation toward acidic.
285. **When the octet rule breaks — ionic cases** (`chemistry/when-the-octet-rule-breaks-ionic-cases`) — The octet rule is a guide, not a law — real compounds break it.
286. **Where Bohr's model fails** (`chemistry/where-bohrs-model-fails`) — Bohr's model works only for one-electron species — hydrogen and ions with a single electron.
287. **Where fats and oils come from** (`chemistry/where-fats-and-oils-come-from`) — Fats come mainly from animal sources and are solid at room temperature.
288. **Where hydrocarbons come from — oil, gas, coal** (`chemistry/where-hydrocarbons-come-from-oil-gas-coal`) — The three great sources are compared: petroleum (crude oil — the main source, its hydrocarbons chiefly alkanes/cycloalkanes), natural gas (mainly methane, with biogas as its ren…
289. **Where the idea of the atom came from** (`chemistry/where-the-idea-of-the-atom-came-from`) — Long ago, Greek thinkers argued about matter.
290. **Why atoms bond — the octet rule** (`chemistry/why-atoms-bond-the-octet-rule`) — Only valence electrons take part in bonding.
291. **Why chemical change matters — energy for life and industry** (`chemistry/why-chemical-change-matters-energy-for-life-and-industry`) — A short section ties chemical change to daily and national life — energy release from fuels, food, industry and the redox energy conversions the rest of the unit develops — as the
292. **Why metals conduct and bend** (`chemistry/why-metals-conduct-and-bend`) — Metals conduct electricity and heat because the sea electrons are mobile and carry charge and energy through the lattice.
293. **Why metals conduct — properties of metals** (`chemistry/why-metals-conduct-properties-of-metals`) — The electron sea explains the metal properties: electrical and thermal conductivity (push electrons in at one end of a wire and free electrons carry charge and heat through at the
294. **Why oils are liquid and fats are solid** (`chemistry/why-oils-are-liquid-and-fats-are-solid`) — Saturated tails are straight, pack closely, and attract strongly — so fats rich in them are solid, with higher melting points.
295. **Why the periodic table works — Unit 1 review** (`chemistry/why-the-periodic-table-works-unit-1-review`) — Classification makes study systematic: learn one group member well and you know the family's pattern.
296. **Why things dissolve — solvation** (`chemistry/why-things-dissolve-solvation`) — Dissolving is solvation: solvent particles surround and separate the solute's particles (hydration when the solvent is water).
297. **Workout: draw it, name it, fix the wrong name** (`chemistry/workout-draw-it-name-it-fix-the-wrong-name`) — 296–298 (Review Exercises, Part III).
298. **Writing a laboratory report** (`chemistry/writing-a-laboratory-report`) — correct significant figures and units throughout — wrong sig figs "amount to lying about the precision";
299. **Writing chemical equations** (`chemistry/writing-chemical-equations`) — Step 1: write the word equation (hydrogen + nitrogen → ammonia;
300. **Writing electron configurations** (`chemistry/writing-electron-configurations`) — The Aufbau principle: electrons fill the lowest-energy orbitals first, in the filling order the diagram gives.
301. **Chemistry in a team escape game** (`chemistry/chemistry-in-a-team-escape-game`) — Finland's way: chemistry carried inside a cooperative table escape game for class bonding — four colour teams solve three boxed puzzles each in phase 1 and combine their jigsaw pieces for a shared final puzzle in phase 2; the game's one chemistry case holds an article about elements plus a periodic table, and the six elements most common in the human body (O, C, H, N, Ca, P), marked with a UV pen, give their atomic numbers to a formula on a yellow card that forms a lock code — game instructions, not a textbook, stocked as printed (Finland, Grade 7 — *JUMISSA! -Pöytäpakopeli*, whole book, printed pp. 1–9; chemistry case printed p. 5)

302. **Water in our environment** (`chemistry/water-in-our-environment`) — where Earth's water is held, why most of it is not directly usable, water in the body and in foods, and good water management (agriculture/livestock 70% of fresh water) — teacher-guide evidence only, acceptance 0 (Djibouti, Grade 6 — *Guide de l'enseignant Physique-Chimie, 6ème*, Ch. 2, printed p. 23)
303. **Testing for water — anhydrous copper sulfate** (`chemistry/testing-for-water-anhydrous-copper-sulfate`) — the recognition test for water — white anhydrous copper sulfate turns blue where water is present, worked on foods and liquids, with acetone as the negative case — teacher-guide evidence only, acceptance 0 (Djibouti, Grade 6 — *Guide de l'enseignant Physique-Chimie, 6ème*, Ch. 2, Exercices 5/10/13/22, printed p. 27)
304. **Mixtures — homogeneous and heterogeneous** (`chemistry/mixtures-homogeneous-and-heterogeneous`) — mixtures with water sorted by what is seen — one phase homogeneous (water+sugar, water+salt), two phases heterogeneous (water+tea, water+sand), the difference being whether the body dissolves — teacher-guide evidence only, acceptance 0 (Djibouti, Grade 6 — *Guide de l'enseignant Physique-Chimie, 6ème*, Ch. 3, printed p. 32)
305. **Separating mixtures — filtration and settling** (`chemistry/separating-mixtures-filtration-and-settling`) — getting a heterogeneous mixture's parts back — filtration (filtrate and residue) and décantation (rest, settle, pour off), carried up to Djibouti's wastewater-treatment station — teacher-guide evidence only, acceptance 0 (Djibouti, Grade 6 — *Guide de l'enseignant Physique-Chimie, 6ème*, Ch. 3 Activités 3–4, printed p. 35)
306. **Fizzy drinks and the limewater test** (`chemistry/fizzy-drinks-and-the-limewater-test`) — a fizzy drink as a liquid+gas mixture — its gas collected by displacement of water, identified as CO₂ by limewater turning cloudy, and shown to have mass by weighing before/after degassing; cold drinks hold more gas — teacher-guide evidence only, acceptance 0 (Djibouti, Grade 6 — *Guide de l'enseignant Physique-Chimie, 6ème*, Ch. 4, printed p. 39)
307. **Molecules and chemical formulas** (`chemistry/molecules-and-chemical-formulas`) — reading a molecule from its formula — H₂O, CO₂, CH₄ counted atom by atom from molecular models — and sorting simple bodies (one kind of atom: H₂, N₂) from compound bodies — teacher-guide evidence only, acceptance 0 (Djibouti, Grade 8 — *Guide du professeur Physique-Chimie, 8ème année*, Ch. 4, printed p. 41)
308. **Ions — atoms that gain or lose electrons** (`chemistry/ions-atoms-that-gain-or-lose-electrons`) — an ion as an atom that has lost or gained electrons — sodium 11+/11e⁻ loses one to become Na⁺ (10 electrons), chlorine gains one to become Cl⁻; the nucleus, and the element, unchanged — teacher-guide evidence only, acceptance 0 (Djibouti, Grade 8 — *Guide du professeur Physique-Chimie, 8ème année*, Ch. 4, printed p. 42)
309. **Mass concentration of a solution** (`chemistry/mass-concentration-of-a-solution`) — mass concentration Cm = m/V in g/L, computed from real drink labels — Coca-Cola 118 g/L vs orange juice 26 g/L — to name the sweetest drink by number — teacher-guide evidence only, acceptance 0 (Djibouti, Grade 8 — *Guide du professeur Physique-Chimie, 8ème année*, Ch. 6 Activité 1, printed p. 52)
310. **Alkyl halides — substitution and elimination** (`chemistry/alkyl-halides-substitution-and-elimination`) — an alkyl halide R–X (a halogen in place of one hydrogen, its polar C–X bond the reactive place) and its two routes: substitution, where the halogen is swapped for another group (aqueous alkali gives an alcohol), and elimination, where HX is removed and a C=C double bond forms (an alkene) (Sudan, Grade 12 — *الكيمياء – الصف الثالث الثانوي*, Unit 1, Lesson 1: Alkyl Halides, printed p. 6)
311. **Thermodynamics — spontaneity, entropy and free energy** (`chemistry/thermodynamics-spontaneity-and-free-energy`) — what decides whether a process can run by itself: spontaneity (direction, never speed), entropy as the dispersal of matter and energy with the second law pricing the universe, and Gibbs free energy ΔG = ΔH − TΔS, whose sign settles spontaneity and whose sign cases make temperature the referee (United States, Grade 13 — *Chemistry 2e*, Ch. 16 Thermodynamics, printed p. 767)
312. **Transition metals and complex ions** (`chemistry/transition-metals-and-complex-ions`) — the d-block metals, whose ions keep a partly filled d subshell — variable oxidation states, coloured ions, magnetism and catalysis — and their coordination compounds: ligands donating lone pairs to a central metal to form complex ions, whose crystal-field splitting of the d orbitals explains their colour and spin (United States, Grade 13 — *Chemistry 2e*, Ch. 19 Transition Metals and Coordination Chemistry, printed p. 917)
313. **Amines, amides and amino acids** (`chemistry/amines-amides-and-amino-acids`) — the nitrogen derivatives taught as one family: amines (ammonia with its hydrogens replaced by carbon groups — primary, secondary, tertiary — their lone pair making them bases), amides (a carbonyl carbon joined to nitrogen, named from the parent alkanoic acid, made from the acid with ammonia and split back by hydrolysis; urea the diamide), and amino acids (an –NH₂ group and a –COOH group in one molecule, joining by amide — peptide — links into proteins) — naming, sources and preparation of the three families (Nigeria, Grade 12 — *Senior Secondary Chemistry Textbook 3*, Ch. 9 Amines, Amides, Amino Acids, printed p. ~155)
314. **Models of the atom — from Dalton to Bohr** (`chemistry/models-of-the-atom-from-dalton-to-bohr`) — what a scientific model is — a working picture judged by what it explains and predicts — learned through the sequence of atom models from Dalton's solid ball through Thomson and Rutherford to Bohr's fixed levels (Sri Lanka, Grade 10 — *இரசாயனம் 3‑1 (Chemistry 3‑1)*, Ch. 1 Models and Patterns, printed p. 1)
315. **The Earth's crust and common minerals** (`chemistry/the-earths-crust-and-common-minerals`) — what the crust is made of: oxygen and silicon together about three-quarters of it, read from a crustal-abundance table, and its two great mineral families — carbonates (calcite, dolomite CaCO₃·MgCO₃) and silicates (quartz, feldspars, beryl Be₃Al₂(SiO₃)₆) (Sri Lanka, Grade 12 — *இரசாயனம் பாகம் II (Chemistry Part II)*, descriptive chapters, printed p. 17)
316. **Hydrogen halides and hydrohalic acids** (`chemistry/hydrogen-halides-and-hydrohalic-acids`) — HF, HCl, HBr and HI compared as one family: fuming gases that dissolve in water to the hydrohalic acids, growing stronger down the family, with HF singled out for its hydrogen bonding, anomalous boiling and attack on glass (Sri Lanka, Grade 12 — *இரசாயனம் பாகம் II (Chemistry Part II)*, Hydrogen halides, printed p. 47)
317. **Crystal structures and unit cells** (`chemistry/crystal-structures-and-unit-cells`) — the solid state as sphere packing: layers resting in hollows, the unit cell as the smallest repeating box, and counting atoms by the sharing rule (corner 1/8, face 1/2, centre 1) — bcc owns 2, simple cubic 1, fcc 4 (Sri Lanka, Grade 12 — *இரசாயனம் பாகம் II (Chemistry Part II)*, Ch. 9 Solid state, printed p. 197)
318. **The ozone layer** (`chemistry/the-ozone-layer`) — a stratospheric shield of three-atom oxygen, O₃, formed when ultraviolet light splits O₂ — absorbing UV-B before it reaches the ground; thinned catalytically by chlorine freed from CFCs, worst over Antarctica, with its consequences for skin, eyes, crops and sea life, and the remediation (the Montreal Protocol phase-out) and personal radiation protection that answer it — Nicaragua's own way: the layer given its own section inside atomic theory (Nicaragua, Grade 10 — *Módulo Autoformativo de Química — Décimo Grado*, Unidad I: Capa de ozono, printed pp. 29–36)
319. **Carbohydrates — sugars and starches** (`chemistry/carbohydrates-sugars-and-starches`) — the carbohydrates (glúcidos) sorted by unit count: monosaccharides (glucose, fructose, galactose — all C₆H₁₂O₆, sorted D/L, natural sugars nearly all D), disaccharides and oligosaccharides (sugars joined, losing water at each join; hydrolysis splits them back), and polysaccharides (starch, glycogen and cellulose — glucose chains differing in the join), closing on their essential role in living beings, daily use, benefits and harms (Nicaragua, Grade 10 — *Módulo Autoformativo de Química — Décimo Grado*, Unidad VI: Glúcidos o carbohidratos, printed pp. 237–248)
320. **Raoult's law and ideal solutions** (`chemistry/raoults-law-and-ideal-solutions`) — Raoult's law (p = x(solvent) × P°), ideal solutions obeying it at every concentration with no heat or volume change on mixing, positive deviation (weaker unlike attractions, vapour pressure above the line, heat taken in) and negative deviation (stronger unlike attractions, vapour pressure below the line, heat given out), vapour-pressure and boiling composition diagrams, and distillation of deviating mixtures up to the azeotrope it cannot cross (Uganda, Grade 12 — *Chemistry Home-Study Learning — Senior Five (NCDC, 2020)*, Lessons 1.2–1.3 Ideal and Non-Ideal Solutions, PDF p. 9)
321. **The partition law** (`chemistry/partition-law`) — a solute between two immiscible solvents settling to a constant ratio of concentrations (the partition coefficient K = C(1)/C(2), dilute, same molecular state in both), solvent extraction with small repeated portions beating one large one, and the law's applications including finding a complex ion's formula from distribution measurements (Uganda, Grade 12 — *Chemistry Home-Study Learning — Senior Five (NCDC, 2020)*, Lesson 1.5 The Distribution/Partition Law, PDF p. 18)
322. **Rocks and minerals** (`chemistry/rocks-and-minerals`) — minerals as nature's definite solids, identified by their properties together (colour, streak, lustre, hardness, cleavage), rocks as masses of one or more minerals identified by their minerals, grains and texture, and the three ways rocks form — igneous (cooled), sedimentary (deposited and compacted/cemented), metamorphic (remade by heat and pressure without melting) (Uganda, Grade 8 — *Chemistry Textbook — Senior One Prototype (Lower Secondary Curriculum)*, Ch. 9 Rocks and Minerals, printed p. 96)
323. **Separating mixtures — filtration, evaporation and distillation** (`chemistry/separating-mixtures-filtration-evaporation-distillation`) — the whole family of separation methods as one chapter: décantation and filtration for a mixture with a visible solid, evaporation for what dissolved, distillation to recover the liquid and to part liquid from liquid — and where the family leads, the pure body (corps pur) each separation recovers — teacher-guide evidence only, acceptance 0 (Morocco, Grade 7 — *Al Moufid en Physique/Chimie 1ère Année — Guide de l'enseignant(e)*, Chapitre 12: Séparation des constituants d'un mélange – Corps pur, printed p. 100)
324. **Ocean and coral chemistry** (`chemistry/ocean-and-coral-chemistry`) — the carbonate chemistry of coral and seawater: seawater's dissolved carbon in equilibrium across CO₂, carbonic acid, hydrogen carbonate and carbonate, with acidity setting the shares; coral polyps precipitating calcium carbonate (Ca²⁺ + CO₃²⁻ → CaCO₃) generation on generation into the reef; and the balance tipping under extra absorbed CO₂ — ocean acidification starving the building and dissolving the built (Samoa, Grade 12 — *Chemistry — Year 12 Student Learning Guide (Samoa Chemistry Curriculum, MESC)*, Storyline: Coral Chemistry in the Ocean, printed p. 15)
325. **Phase changes — melting, evaporation, boiling and condensation** (`chemistry/phase-changes-melting-evaporation-boiling-condensation`) — the changes between the states of matter as one named unit: melting and freezing at one door between solid and liquid, evaporation and condensation at the door between liquid and gas, and boiling as the whole-liquid change at one fixed temperature — in every change the particles themselves stay the same and only their arrangement, spacing and motion change (Mongolia, Grade 7 — *Хими VII (Chemistry VII)*, §1.1 States of matter, phase transitions, printed p. 6; Mongolia, Grade 9 — *Хими IX (Chemistry IX)*, §1.1 States of matter, phase transitions, printed p. 6)
326. **Diffusion — the spreading of particles** (`chemistry/diffusion-spreading-of-particles`) — particles in gases and liquids spreading themselves from crowded to scarce until evenly spread, needing no stirring; proved in gases (a smell crossing a room) and in liquids (ink colouring still water), with the rate set by molecular mass — lighter particles diffuse faster (qualitative Graham-type relation) (Mongolia, Grade 7 — *Хими VII (Chemistry VII)*, §1.2 Diffusion, printed p. 20; Mongolia, Grade 9 — *Хими IX (Chemistry IX)*, §1.2 Diffusion, rate of diffusion, printed p. 15)
327. **Soil — composition, properties and pH** (`chemistry/soil-composition-properties-and-ph`) — soil as a mixture (mineral particles from sand to clay, humus, water, air and living things, shown by its layers on settling), its properties following from its composition (particle size setting drainage and water-holding, humus feeding and darkening it), and soil pH measured on a soil-and-water mixture, steering which nutrients plants can take up (Mongolia, Grade 7 — *Хими VII (Chemistry VII)*, §4.2 Soil composition and properties, printed p. 83)
328. **Water purification — filtering and chlorination** (`chemistry/water-purification-filtering-and-chlorination`) — purifying drinking and household/industrial water as one chain: settling and filtering take out the suspended matter, chlorinating kills the germs the filter lets through and guards the water after, and water is recognised by chemical test rather than trusted by its look (Mongolia, Grade 9 — *Хими IX (Chemistry IX)*, §6.1 Water, printed p. 158)
329. **Amino acids, proteins and denaturation** (`chemistry/amino-acids-proteins-and-denaturation`) — amino acids built on one plan (an amino group and a carboxyl group on one carbon, differing only in the R group), joined by peptide links into proteins whose sequence sets the folded working shape — haemoglobin set beside chlorophyll as Ecuador sets them, and the protein content of home foods — and denaturation as the shape undone by heat or acid without breaking the chain, closing on natural biopolymers and biomaterials in orthopaedics, cardiovascular and ophthalmology work, with silicone implants weighed (Ecuador, Grade 13 — *Química, Tercero de Bachillerato, Bachillerato General — Texto del estudiante para la transición curricular*, Sección 13, Tema 2/4, printed pp. 183–187, 193–198)
330. **Nuclear accidents and radiation safety** (`chemistry/nuclear-accidents-and-radiation-safety`) — a nuclear accident of the lost-source kind as a failure of control: a radioactive source (caesium-137 in a sealed radiotherapy capsule) is a working tool while it stays sealed, shielded, registered and in trained hands, and the accident begins when it is discarded without control into the hands of people who cannot know the danger — Brazil's own case, Goiânia 1987, where a discarded caesium-137 radiotherapy capsule was handled by people who did not know the danger and 4 people died — answered by the controls: shielding, distance and short time, registration and accounted handovers, and finished sources disposed of as radioactive waste under control, never as ordinary rubbish (Brazil, Grade 10 — *Química, Livro Didático Público, Ensino Médio (SEED-PR, 2ª edição, 2006)*, Ch. 6 Radiação e Vida!, printed p. 91)
331. **Amines, amides and nitriles** (`chemistry/amines-amides-and-nitriles`) — the nitrogen functions as one connected family: amines (the –NH₂ group shared by the neurotransmitters, while the nerve impulse itself runs on Na⁺/K⁺ concentration differences that must be restored), amides (the –CONH₂ group, met on the cosmetics label as ceramides, the skin barrier's own amides) and nitriles (the –C≡N group, nitrogen at a triple bond), joined by reactions that run between them — nitrile hydrolysis through the amide to the acid, nitrile reduction to the amine — closing Brazil's whole book on UV-B and skin cancer (Brazil, Grade 10 — *Química, Livro Didático Público, Ensino Médio (SEED-PR, 2ª edição, 2006)*, Ch. 17 A Vida Sem Elas Não Tem Graça, printed pp. 229–241)
332. **Kevlar and high-performance polymers** (`chemistry/kevlar-and-high-performance-polymers`) — Kevlar as the high-performance polymer fibre: a synthetic aramid whose straight, regular chains are aligned side by side and tied to their neighbours by hydrogen bonds, so a pulling force is shared across the whole packed bundle and the fibre is strong for its weight and stands heat — pictured by Iran (Fig. 18) inside its Grade 11 clothing chapter, with its applications around it: bullet-resistant vests and helmets, protective gloves and clothing, reinforcement in tyres, cables and ropes (Iran, Grade 11 — *شیمی (۲) — Chemistry (2)*, Ch. 3, printed p. 118 (PDF p. 126))
333. **Solar cells and photovoltaics** (`chemistry/solar-cells-and-photovoltaics`) — a solar cell producing electricity directly from sunlight by the photovoltaic effect: light frees electrons inside a silicon wafer of two differently treated layers, and the junction between them drives the freed electrons one way and the gaps the other, separating charge to the two faces that become the cell's terminals — cells joined into panels and arrays, bounded by night and cloud and answered by storage — pictured and questioned by Iran as an advanced technology inside its Grade 12 materials chapter (Iran, Grade 12 — *شیمی (۳) — Chemistry (3)*, Ch. 3, printed p. 78 (PDF p. 86))
334. **Composite materials** (`chemistry/composite-materials`) — a material made by combining two or more different materials so the combination does a job neither part does as well alone, each part keeping its own strength inside it — Malaysia's manufactured-substances chapter closes with its four: reinforced concrete (concrete carrying compression, steel bars carrying tension), fibre optics (hair-thin glass fibres guiding light and signals), photochromic glass (darkening in bright light, clearing in shade) and superconductors (carrying current without resistance) (Malaysia, Grade 10 — *Chemistry — Form 4* (DLP), Ch. 8.4, printed p. 265)
335. **Thermochemistry in daily life** (`chemistry/thermochemistry-in-daily-life`) — the heat chapter's two directions put to daily work: cold packs running an endothermic change to draw heat out of injuries, hot packs running an exothermic change to give heat out, self-heating cans heating food with a reaction carried inside the can, and cooking fuel chosen for its steady exothermic burn — Malaysia's own way of closing its heat chapter (Malaysia, Grade 11 — *Chemistry — Form 5* (DLP), Ch. 3.3, printed p. 136)
336. **Natural rubber and synthetic rubber** (`chemistry/natural-rubber-and-synthetic-rubber`) — latex, the rubber tree's milky liquid of rubber particles in water, coagulated into natural rubber and vulcanised — heated with sulfur so cross-links lock its chains — into the hard, strong, springy material of tyres; and synthetic rubbers built in factories from petroleum-based monomers to copy that spring and add what nature lacks, such as oil resistance — Malaysia's own historic industry taught as chemistry (Malaysia, Grade 11 — *Chemistry — Form 5* (DLP), Ch. 4.2–4.3, printed pp. 151–159)
