337. **Nanotechnology in industry** (`chemistry/nanotechnology-in-industry`) — substances built and divided at the nanometre scale (1 nm = a billionth of a metre), where the surface area compared with the volume becomes enormous and the same substance gains new working powers — applied across industry: electronics, medicine and cosmetics, coatings and materials, catalysis and water treatment (Malaysia, Grade 11 — *Chemistry — Form 5* (DLP), Ch. 5.5, printed p. 187)
338. **Careers in chemistry** (`chemistry/careers-in-chemistry`) — chemistry careers taught as content inside the introduction to chemistry itself: the agricultural scientist (chemistry at work on soil, fertilisers and crops), the biochemist (the chemistry of living things) and others, with the strand's exercise pasting pictures of eight chemistry-related careers (Fiji, Grade 11 — *Chemistry for Year 11 (amended 2016)*, Strand 1.1, printed p. 2)
339. **Household chemistry** (`chemistry/household-chemistry`) — the chemistry of the cleaning cupboard as a named closing strand: soap making water able to carry grease away, detergents as manufactured cleaners that keep working where soap struggles, and bleaches and cleaners as the strong end of the cupboard — one at a time, as the label directs, never mixed (Fiji, Grade 12 — *Chemistry for Year 12*, Strand 5.3 Household Chemistry, printed p. 177)
340. **Hydrides — ionic, covalent and metallic** (`chemistry/hydrides-ionic-covalent-metallic`) — the compounds of hydrogen with other elements as one named family, sorted by bonding: ionic hydrides with hydrogen as H⁻ in salt-like solids, covalent hydrides as molecules (water, ammonia, methane), metallic hydrides with hydrogen held in a metal lattice — the kind following the partner across the table, and the properties following the bonding (Fiji, Grade 13 — *Chemistry for Year 13*, Strand 4.1 Hydrides, printed pp. 202–206)
341. **Carboxylic acid derivatives** (`chemistry/carboxylic-acid-derivatives`) — the family made by replacing the –OH of the carboxyl group: amides (named –amide, made by heating the ammonium salt; nicotinamide as the food example), acid anhydrides (water removed from two acid molecules; ethanoic anhydride used for aspirin) and acyl chlorides (named as –oyl chlorides, prepared from the acid with PCl₅ or PCl₃ and separated by fractional distillation) (Fiji, Grade 13 — *Chemistry for Year 13*, Strand 4.2 Carboxylic Acid Derivatives, printed pp. 293–297)
342. **Priority of organic functional groups** (`chemistry/priority-of-organic-functional-groups`) — the fixed priority order for naming a molecule with more than one functional group: carboxylic acids highest, alkyl halides lowest; the highest group takes the suffix and the rest are named as prefixes, with the book's summary table of every family's general formula and ending and its worked examples (Fiji, Grade 13 — *Chemistry for Year 13*, Strand 4.2 Priority of Organic Functional Groups, printed pp. 303–304)
343. **Biodegradable and non-biodegradable polymers** (`chemistry/biodegradable-and-non-biodegradable-polymers`) — polymers sorted by their ending: biodegradable ones broken down by micro-organisms and enzymes (starch, cellulose, polyesters) against non-biodegradable ones that persist (polythene, nylon, PVC, polypropene, Teflon), and the problems of the persisting kind — pollution and harm to land and marine life, toxic emissions on exposure and burning, and recycling costs (Fiji, Grade 13 — *Chemistry for Year 13*, Strand 5.1, printed pp. 313–314)
344. **How this chemistry course works — the Syrian Grade 7 way** (`chemistry/how-this-chemistry-course-works-the-syrian-grade-7-way`) — the course-shape of Syria's Grade 7 Chemistry taught as content: a research-and-experiment curriculum tied to daily life and the local environment, every lesson opening with printed objectives and key words and built around “I try and I conclude” bench experiments with an explicit “I conclude” box (Syria, Grade 7 — *الفيزياء والكيمياء للصف السابع الأساسي (Physics & Chemistry, Grade 7 Basic)*, preface, printed p. 3)
345. **Element symbols and chemical formulas — first steps** (`chemistry/element-symbols-and-chemical-formulas-first-steps`) — chemistry's written language as a first step in its own right: element molecules written with subscripts (He, O₂, O₃, Cl₂, S₈), a table of element symbols with their Latin roots (Na Natrium, K Kalium, Fe Ferrum, Au Aurum, Hg Hydrargyrum, Pb Plumbum), ozone in the upper atmosphere as enrichment, and compound formulas (ammonia, sulphur dioxide, methane, ethanol) read atom-by-atom (Syria, Grade 7 — *الفيزياء والكيمياء للصف السابع الأساسي (Physics & Chemistry, Grade 7 Basic)*, U4 L2, printed p. 146)
346. **How this chemistry course works — the Syrian Grade 8 way** (`chemistry/how-this-chemistry-course-works-the-syrian-grade-8-way`) — the shared Syrian design as its Grade 8 Chemistry unit runs it, taught as content: printed objectives and key words per lesson, “I try and I conclude” bench experiments with an explicit conclusion box, worked applications, enrichment boxes, a self-test per lesson, unit questions and a closing project (Syria, Grade 8 — *الفيزياء والكيمياء للصف الثامن الأساسي (Physics & Chemistry, Grade 8 Basic)*, printed p. 3)
347. **Valence — the combining power of an element** (`chemistry/valence-the-combining-power-of-an-element`) — chemical valency read off models of water, ammonia and methane: in shared-bond compounds it equals the number of shared pairs an atom forms; in ionic compounds, the number of electrons an atom loses or gains — with the element valency table that follows (Syria, Grade 8 — *الفيزياء والكيمياء للصف الثامن الأساسي (Physics & Chemistry, Grade 8 Basic)*, U1 L3, printed p. 19)
348. **Complete and incomplete combustion** (`chemistry/complete-and-incomplete-combustion`) — combustion as reaction with oxygen at a suitable temperature, tested on a Bunsen flame with the air hole open vs closed: complete combustion (plentiful oxygen; blue flame, carbon dioxide + water) against incomplete (oxygen short; yellow sooty flame blackening a porcelain dish, carbon monoxide), with the enrichment box on carbon monoxide's silent poisoning of the blood (Syria, Grade 8 — *الفيزياء والكيمياء للصف الثامن الأساسي (Physics & Chemistry, Grade 8 Basic)*, U1 L4, printed pp. 26–27)
349. **Molar volume of gases** (`chemistry/molar-volume-of-gases`) — one mole of any gas occupies the same 22.4 L at the two standard conditions (0 °C, 1 atm), read off three one-mole cylinders (He, O₂, N₂); V = n × 22.4 L, with the reverse worked (44.8 L of hydrogen = 2 mol) (Syria, Grade 8 — *الفيزياء والكيمياء للصف الثامن الأساسي (Physics & Chemistry, Grade 8 Basic)*, U1 L7, printed pp. 44–45)
350. **How this chemistry course works — the Syrian Grade 9 way** (`chemistry/how-this-chemistry-course-works-the-syrian-grade-9-way`) — the shared Syrian design across its Grade 9 book's three Chemistry units, taught as content: objectives and key words opening each lesson, “I try and I conclude” experiments with an explicit conclusion box, worked applications, enrichment and safety boxes, a self-test per lesson, unit questions, and a closing chemistry project (Syria, Grade 9 — *الفيزياء والكيمياء للصف التاسع الأساسي (Physics & Chemistry, Grade 9 Basic)*, Chemistry units, printed p. 95)
351. **Diluting a solution — C₁V₁ = C₂V₂** (`chemistry/diluting-a-solution-c1v1-c2v2`) — dilution discovered by tasting: a spoonful of sugar solution trebled in volume tastes weaker while the dissolved sugar is unchanged; solute amount is conserved, C₁V₁ = C₂V₂, worked on a sodium hydroxide solution — with the safety enrichment that acid is always added to water, never the reverse (Syria, Grade 9 — *الفيزياء والكيمياء للصف التاسع الأساسي (Physics & Chemistry, Grade 9 Basic)*, U4 L1, printed pp. 99–100)
352. **Proving carbon is in organic compounds** (`chemistry/proving-carbon-is-in-organic-compounds`) — a piece of bread burned and a spoonful of sugar heated both end as black carbon: the element common to all organic substances is carbon, and organic chemistry is the branch that studies carbon's compounds (Syria, Grade 9 — *الفيزياء والكيمياء للصف التاسع الأساسي (Physics & Chemistry, Grade 9 Basic)*, U5 L1, printed pp. 148–149)
353. **Organic compounds and carbon's four bonds** (`chemistry/organic-compounds-and-carbon-s-four-bonds`) — the carbon atom itself: four outer electrons in a Lewis picture, sitting in the second main level so it shares readily and completes its octet by sharing four bonds; then ball-and-stick models of ethane, ethene and ethyne giving the three carbon–carbon bond kinds — single, double and triple shared bonds (Syria, Grade 9 — *الفيزياء والكيمياء للصف التاسع الأساسي (Physics & Chemistry, Grade 9 Basic)*, U5 L1, printed pp. 149–151)
354. **Alkenes and alkynes — the unsaturated families** (`chemistry/alkenes-and-alkynes-the-unsaturated-families`) — the alkenes (olefins): models of ethene and propene give one double bond per molecule, general formula CₙH₂ₙ and -ene names, ethene going to plastics and polyester fibres and speeding fruit ripening; and the alkynes (acetylenes): models of ethyne and propyne give one triple bond, general formula CₙH₂ₙ₋₂ and -yne names, ethyne's complete combustion releasing 1255 kJ per mole in the oxy-acetylene welding flame (Syria, Grade 9 — *الفيزياء والكيمياء للصف التاسع الأساسي (Physics & Chemistry, Grade 9 Basic)*, U5 L4, printed pp. 163–165)
355. **Nuclear fission and fusion** (`chemistry/nuclear-fission-and-fusion`) — radioactive isotopes and nuclear energy at work: carbon-14 dates once-living remains and uranium-235 the age of the Earth; mass converts to energy (the sun, the nuclear bomb — a small mass yielding an enormous energy); controlled fission in a reactor core generates electricity, and nuclear medicine diagnoses, traces and treats cancerous tumours — against the stated harms of tissue damage, with the Geiger counter and the radiation trefoil sign (Syria, Grade 9 — *الفيزياء والكيمياء للصف التاسع الأساسي (Physics & Chemistry, Grade 9 Basic)*, U6 L1, printed pp. 177–178)
356. **Biochemistry — an introduction** (`chemistry/biochemistry-introduction`) — biochemistry as the chemistry of living things: the substances living bodies are made of and the ordered reaction chains, steered by enzyme catalysts, that keep them alive, built from the four big molecule families — carbohydrates, proteins, lipids and nucleic acids (Cambodia, Grade 10 — *គីមីវិទ្យា ថ្នាក់ទី១០ (Chemistry, Grade 10)*, Ch.4 §5 Biochemistry, printed p. 116)
357. **Calcium compounds — lime and limestone** (`chemistry/calcium-compounds-lime-and-limestone`) — the calcium family as one industrial lesson: limestone (calcium carbonate CaCO₃, fizzing with acid), its thermal decomposition to quicklime (CaO + CO₂), slaking to slaked lime (Ca(OH)₂, and limewater), and the family's work in farming, building and industry, with mortar setting closing the circle back to carbonate (Cambodia, Grade 11 — *គីមីវិទ្យា ថ្នាក់ទី១១ (Chemistry, Grade 11)*, Ch.5 §3 Calcium compounds, printed p. 160)
358. **Separation methods — decantation, filtration, distillation and the rest** (`chemistry/separation-methods-decantation-filtration-distillation`) — the seven procedures for separating the components of a mixture taught as one full practical strand: decantation and filtration for the insoluble solid (settle first, then filter), magnetic separation and sublimation where one special property does the work, and distillation, crystallisation and chromatography for what is dissolved or mixed (North Macedonia, Grade 7 — *Хемија за VII одделение во основно образование (Chemistry, Grade 7, primary)*, 1.4 Mixtures, printed p. 33)
359. **Superacids — stronger than pure sulfuric acid** (`chemistry/superacids-stronger-than-pure-sulfuric`) — acids beyond the ordinary strong acids: the defining line (stronger than pure 100% sulfuric acid), the Hammett acidity function H₀ that measures past the pH scale's end, the famous examples (fluorosulfuric acid, magic acid, fluoroantimonic acid), their work as catalysts and carbocation tools, and why they never enter a school laboratory (North Macedonia, Grade 8 — *Хемија за VIII одделение во основно образование (Chemistry, Grade 8, primary)*, 2.3, Superacids, printed p. 100)
360. **Inorganic nomenclature — hydrides, hydroxides and acids** (`chemistry/inorganic-nomenclature-hydrides-hydroxides-and-acids`) — the systematic Spanish nomenclature strand for the families the binary-compounds lessons stop short of: metallic hydrides (hidruro de + metal, the metal's charge fixing the H⁻ count) and the derivatives of ammonia (named from NH₃ as parent), hydroxides (hidróxido de + metal, brackets for repeated OH groups, Roman numerals where the metal's charge varies), and the polyhydrated oxoacids (one anhydride drinking different amounts of water — P₂O₅ giving the meta, pyro and ortho phosphoric acids up the water ladder) — every name a construction from which the formula rebuilds — teacher-compendium evidence only, acceptance 0 (Bolivia, Grade 9 — *Compendio para Maestras y Maestros, Textos de Aprendizaje 2023–2024, Área Ciencias Naturales: Química*, Tercer año, compendium PDF pp. 12–17)
361. **Colligative properties — Raoult's law and the freezing and boiling shifts** (`chemistry/colligative-properties-raoult-and-freezing-boiling`) — the four shifts a dissolved substance makes to its solvent (vapour pressure falls, boiling point rises, freezing point falls, osmotic pressure appears), all set by the number of dissolved particles and not their kind, with the full maths: Raoult's law p = x(solvent) × P° with the lowering as the solute's share, freezing-point depression ΔTf = Kf × m and boiling-point elevation ΔTb = Kb × m, each carried through a worked problem — teacher-compendium evidence only, acceptance 0 (Bolivia, Grade 11 — *Compendio para Maestras y Maestros, Textos de Aprendizaje 2023–2024, Área Ciencias Naturales: Química*, Quinto año, Soluciones II, compendium PDF pp. 84–87 (source p. 106))
362. **Separating mixtures — filtration, distillation and chromatography** (`chemistry/separating-mixtures-filtration-distillation-and-chromatography`) — the method is chosen from the components' physical properties: filtration (residue and filtrate), evaporation against crystallisation, simple distillation with a Liebig condenser and fractional distillation (the rum and petroleum industries named), the separating funnel for immiscible liquids, and paper chromatography with its forensic uses (Dominica, Grade 11 — *Chemistry for CSEC® — 2nd Edition*, A2.4 Separating mixtures, printed pp. 25–29)
363. **Radioactive isotopes at work — dating, medicine and tracers** (`chemistry/radioactive-isotopes-at-work-dating-medicine-and-tracers`) — carbon-14 dating of remains to about 60,000 years on the 5,700-year half-life; radiotherapy (cobalt-60 beams, iodine-131 for the thyroid, implanted iridium-192, palladium-103 and iodine-125 seeds); medical tracers (technetium-99, iodine-131); plant-uptake tracers read with a Geiger counter; and plutonium-238 pacemaker batteries (Dominica, Grade 11 — *Chemistry for CSEC® — 2nd Edition*, A3.3 Isotopes and radioactivity, printed pp. 42–43)
364. **Calorimetry — heats of reaction, q = mcΔT** (`chemistry/calorimetry-heats-of-reaction-q-equals-mc-delta-t`) — q = m × c × ΔT in a simple calorimeter under the three standard assumptions; the heat of neutralisation (about −56.3 kJ mol⁻¹ for every strong acid with a strong alkali, the ionic equation underneath always being OH⁻ + H⁺ → H₂O) by experiment and by thermometric titration; and the heat of solution of ammonium chloride (+25.6 kJ mol⁻¹, endothermic) explained by bond breaking against solvation (Dominica, Grade 11 — *Chemistry for CSEC® — 2nd Edition*, A12.2 Calculating energy changes, printed pp. 217–222)
365. **Biogas — from anaerobic digestion** (`chemistry/biogas-from-anaerobic-digestion`) — a sealed anaerobic digester where bacteria break down manure, crop residues and food waste to about 60% methane and 40% carbon dioxide; the gas upgraded to biomethane for the gas grid or liquefied vehicle fuel; and the leftover digestate returned to the fields as fertiliser (Dominica, Grade 11 — *Chemistry for CSEC® — 2nd Edition*, B14.2 Alkanes, printed p. 245)
366. **Metals in living systems — and heavy-metal poisoning** (`chemistry/metals-in-living-systems-and-heavy-metal-poisoning`) — magnesium at the centre of chlorophyll, iron in haemoglobin, calcium, zinc, sodium and potassium, sorted macro (over 100 mg a day) against micro; then the heavy metals lead, mercury (Minamata disease, the mad hatter), cadmium and arsenic with their sources and toxic effects, their build-up through food chains into shark, marlin, tuna and swordfish, and the disposal and recycling problem of lead-acid batteries, mercury thermometers and fluorescent bulbs, and nickel-cadmium batteries (Dominica, Grade 11 — *Chemistry for CSEC® — 2nd Edition*, C19.2, printed pp. 311–314)
367. **Laboratory preparation of gases — oxygen, carbon dioxide and ammonia** (`chemistry/laboratory-preparation-of-gases-oxygen-carbon-dioxide-and-ammonia`) — each preparation designed from the gas's properties: oxygen from hydrogen peroxide on a manganese(IV) oxide catalyst; carbon dioxide from calcium carbonate and hydrochloric acid (never sulfuric — insoluble calcium sulfate coats the carbonate and stops it); ammonia from calcium hydroxide and ammonium chloride, dried only with calcium oxide, collected by downward displacement and tested full with moist red litmus; the uses of oxygen and carbon dioxide tied back to properties (Dominica, Grade 11 — *Chemistry for CSEC® — 2nd Edition*, C20.3 Laboratory preparation and uses of gases, printed pp. 321–323)
368. **Water hardness — temporary and permanent** (`chemistry/water-hardness-temporary-and-permanent`) — hard water from dissolved Ca²⁺/Mg²⁺ wasting soap as scum; temporary hardness from calcium and magnesium hydrogencarbonates, formed as rainwater CO₂ dissolves limestone (CaCO₃ + H₂O + CO₂ → Ca(HCO₃)₂ — the reaction that carves limestone caves); and permanent hardness from calcium and magnesium sulfates, with soil leaching as the same solvent power at work (Dominica, Grade 11 — *Chemistry for CSEC® — 2nd Edition*, C21.1 Water, printed p. 341)
369. **Domestic water treatment — and softening** (`chemistry/domestic-water-treatment-and-softening`) — large-scale treatment in order (flocculation with alum and sedimentation, filtration through gravel, sand and charcoal beds, chlorination at 5 ppm or with monochloramine); home treatment by fibre and carbon filters, chlorination and boiling; and the five softenings — boiling (temporary only, leaving limescale), washing soda precipitating CaCO₃ from both kinds, ion exchange on zeolite resin Na₂Z with brine regeneration, distillation and the solar still, and reverse osmosis, the desalination workhorse (Dominica, Grade 11 — *Chemistry for CSEC® — 2nd Edition*, C21.2 The treatment of water for domestic purposes, printed pp. 342–345)
370. **The genetic connection between the classes of inorganic substances** (`chemistry/genetic-connection-between-inorganic-classes`) — the transformation chains linking oxides, acids, bases and salts into one connected system: a typical metal walks metal → basic oxide → base → salt (calcium: Ca → CaO → Ca(OH)₂ → CaCl₂), a typical nonmetal walks nonmetal → acidic oxide → acid → salt (sulfur: S → SO₂ → H₂SO₃ → Na₂SO₃), the chains meet at the salt, and amphoteric oxides cross between them — Belarus' own framing, taught at the head of its classes system (Belarus, Grade 10 — *Хімія. 10 клас (пад рэд. І. Я. Шымановіча)*, Part I §3 Interrelation between the classes of inorganic substances, printed p. 16)
371. **Hydrogen — preparation, properties and uses** (`chemistry/hydrogen-preparation-properties-and-uses`) — the first and lightest element, found on Earth only combined — in water, acids and living matter; prepared in the laboratory from zinc and a dilute acid and proved by the pop test; burning in oxygen to water only, reducing heated metal oxides, and used as balloon lift, fuel and factory feedstock for ammonia and hydrogenation (Belarus, Grade 10 — *Хімія. 10 клас (пад рэд. І. Я. Шымановіча)*, Part VII §36 Hydrogen, printed p. 174)
372. **Carbon and silicon — the group-14 pair** (`chemistry/carbon-and-silicon-group-14`) — the group-14 pair taught together: four outer electrons and tetravalence shared, the pattern showing in CO₂ and SiO₂ alike; carbon's allotropy — diamond's rigid network (hardest, no conduction), graphite's sliding layers (soft, conducting) and the amorphous carbons — properties written by structure; and silicon, the crust's second most abundant element, never found free, a semiconductor (Belarus, Grade 10 — *Хімія. 10 клас (пад рэд. І. Я. Шымановіча)*, Part VII §46 Carbon and silicon, printed p. 223)
373. **Dienes — conjugated systems** (`chemistry/dienes-conjugated-systems`) — hydrocarbons with two double bonds (CₙH₂ₙ₋₂, the alkynes' formula), sorted cumulated / conjugated / isolated by where the bonds stand; the conjugated system — π electrons delocalised over all four carbons of buta-1,3-diene, its steadiness and its 1,2- and 1,4-addition as the evidences; and the polymerisation of conjugated dienes to synthetic rubber, with isoprene's polymer as natural rubber (Belarus, Grade 11 — *Хімія. 11 клас — А. П. Ельніцкі, А. І. Шарапа*, Part 3 §14 Dienes, printed p. 59)
374. **Phenol — properties, preparation and uses** (`chemistry/phenol-properties-preparation-and-uses`) — a hydroxyl group joined directly to a benzene ring, weakly acidic where the alcohols are neutral: reacts with sodium and sodium hydroxide but not with carbonates, and its ring so activated that bromine water is decolourised at once with a white precipitate of 2,4,6-tribromophenol; obtained from coal tar historically and from benzene by the cumene process today (acetone as co-product); used in Bakelite and the phenol–formaldehyde resins, antiseptics and disinfectants, dyes and derivatives (Belarus, Grade 11 — *Хімія. 11 клас — А. П. Ельніцкі, А. І. Шарапа*, Part 5 §27–28 Phenols; Obtaining phenol, applying phenol and its derivatives, printed pp. 112–118)
375. **Types of organic reactions** (`chemistry/types-of-organic-reactions`) — the classification of organic reactions as a system, placed in the closing generalisation part: five families by result — addition, substitution, elimination, rearrangement (isomerisation) and oxidation/reduction — crossed with the sorting by mechanism — homolytic breaking to radicals against heterolytic breaking to ions, with nucleophiles donating and electrophiles accepting electron pairs — so every reaction of the organic year carries two names, as free-radical substitution and electrophilic addition (Belarus, Grade 11 — *Хімія. 11 клас — А. П. Ельніцкі, А. І. Шарапа*, Part 10 §59 Classification of organic reactions and the patterns of their course, printed p. 237)
376. **Purification of organic compounds** (`chemistry/purification-of-organic-compounds`) — the organic chemist's choosing among methods, from the compound's nature and its impurity: filtration for the insoluble impurity, crystallisation from a hot solvent for most solids, sublimation for solids that pass straight to vapour (camphor, naphthalene); the four distillations — simple, fractional, under reduced pressure (glycerol), steam (aniline) — and differential extraction; and chromatography, adsorption (column and thin-layer, with its Rf value) and partition (paper), whose one spot is the verdict of purity (India, Grade 11 — *Chemistry, Part II, Textbook for Class XI*, Unit 12 §12.8 Methods of Purification of Organic Compounds, printed p. 356)
377. **Carcinogenicity of polynuclear hydrocarbons** (`chemistry/carcinogenicity-of-polynuclear-hydrocarbons`) — NCERT's health close to the hydrocarbons unit: the polynuclear hydrocarbons, benzene rings fused into one flat stubborn molecule (benzo(a)pyrene, benzo(b)fluoranthene, dibenzo(a,h)anthracene among those named), formed on the incomplete combustion of tobacco, coal and petroleum, entering the body with smoke and tar, and there undergoing biochemical reactions whose products damage DNA and cause cancer (India, Grade 11 — *Chemistry, Part II, Textbook for Class XI*, Unit 13 §13.6 Carcinogenicity and Toxicity, printed p. 403)
378. **Imperfections and electrical properties of solids** (`chemistry/imperfections-and-electrical-properties-of-solids`) — the real crystal closing the Solid State unit: stoichiometric point defects (Schottky — ions missing, density falls; Frenkel — ions displaced, density unchanged), non-stoichiometric defects (metal excess and its colouring F-centres; metal deficiency in FeO), impurity defects and doping; and the electrical sorting by band gap — conductors, insulators, semiconductors — with n-type (group-15 dopant) and p-type (group-13 dopant), and the magnetic kinds from diamagnetic to ferrimagnetic (India, Grade 12 — *Chemistry, Part I, Textbook for Class XII*, Unit 1 §§1.9–1.11 Imperfections in solids; electrical and magnetic properties, printed pp. 24–34)
379. **Integrated rate equations and half-life** (`chemistry/integrated-rate-equations-and-half-life`) — the rate law integrated into working equations: zero order, [R] = [R]₀ − kt with t½ = [R]₀/2k riding on the starting concentration; first order, k = (2.303/t) log ([R]₀/[R]) with t½ = 0.693/k constant at every starting concentration and (½)ⁿ left after n half-lives; and pseudo first order, where an excess reactant folds into the constant (ethyl acetate hydrolysis, cane-sugar inversion) (India, Grade 12 — *Chemistry, Part I, Textbook for Class XII*, Unit 4 §4.3 Integrated Rate Equations, printed p. 105)
380. **Adsorption and catalysis** (`chemistry/adsorption-and-catalysis`) — Surface Chemistry's opening pair: adsorption at a surface (never the bulk), physisorption (van der Waals, multilayer, reversible) against chemisorption (chemical bonds, one layer, specific), measured by the Freundlich isotherm x/m = k·p^(1/n); and catalysis — homogeneous against heterogeneous (Haber iron, Contact V₂O₅, nickel hydrogenation), promoters (molybdenum) against poisons (carbon monoxide), activity and selectivity, and the shape-selective catalysis of the zeolites (ZSM-5 turning alcohols into petrol) (India, Grade 12 — *Chemistry, Part I, Textbook for Class XII*, Unit 5 §§5.1–5.2 Adsorption; Catalysis, printed pp. 124–135)
381. **Noble gases — group 18** (`chemistry/noble-gases-group-18`) — the family of the completed outer shell: monoatomic, the table's highest ionisation enthalpies and lowest boiling points; argon at 0.93% of dry air from liquid air's fractionation, helium from natural gas (balloons, diving mixtures, cryogenics), neon's discharge-tube glow, argon's inert blankets; and Bartlett's 1962 break of the 'inert' name — XePtF₆ by the 1170-against-1175 ionisation match, then XeF₂, XeF₄, XeF₆ and XeOF₄ (India, Grade 12 — *Chemistry, Part I, Textbook for Class XII*, Unit 7 §7.23 Group 18 Elements, printed p. 208)
382. **Diazonium salts** (`chemistry/diazonium-salts`) — the amines unit's second half: diazotisation of aniline with NaNO₂/HCl at 273–278 K to benzenediazonium chloride, the aryl salt resonance-steadied and used fresh in the cold; the –N₂⁺ group leaving as N₂ and replaced — Cl, Br, CN by Sandmeyer (Gattermann beside it), I by KI, F by the fluoroborate (Balz–Schiemann), H by H₃PO₂, OH by warm water to phenol, NO₂ with copper; and coupling, with phenol in mild alkali to orange p-hydroxyazobenzene and with aniline in mild acid to yellow p-aminoazobenzene — the azo dyes (India, Grade 12 — *Chemistry, Part II, Textbook for Class XII*, Unit 13 §§13.7–13.10 Diazonium salts, printed pp. 404–410)
383. **Enzymes — biological catalysts** (`chemistry/enzymes-biological-catalysts-and-biotechnology`) — the Biomolecules unit's workers: globular proteins, each folded around an active site that holds its one substrate, named by substrate (–ase) and reaction class; the enzyme–substrate complex mechanism, activity peaking at an optimum temperature (~310 K) and pH (~7) and ending in denaturation beyond them; and the named roll at work — invertase, maltase, zymase fermenting glucose, urease specific to urea alone, pepsin and trypsin cutting proteins (India, Grade 12 — *Chemistry, Part II, Textbook for Class XII*, Unit 14 §14.3 Enzymes, printed p. 425)
384. **Vitamins and hormones** (`chemistry/vitamins-and-hormones`) — the Biomolecules unit's two regulator families: the vitamins, sorted by solubility into water-soluble B group and C (scarcely stored, needed regularly) and fat-soluble A, D, E and K (stored in liver and fat), with NCERT's table of sources and deficiency diseases (A — night blindness, B₁ — beri-beri, B₁₂ — pernicious anaemia, C — scurvy, D — rickets, K — clotting failure); and the hormones, the endocrine glands' blood-carried messengers — steroids (sex and adrenocortical), peptides (insulin lowering and glucagon raising blood glucose), thyroxine setting the metabolic rate and adrenaline answering emergencies (India, Grade 12 — *Chemistry, Part II, Textbook for Class XII*, Unit 14 §§14.4/14.6 Vitamins; Hormones, printed pp. 425–432)
385. **Nucleic acids** (`chemistry/nucleic-acids`) — the archive molecules of the Biomolecules unit: nucleotides of base (purines A, G; pyrimidines C, T, U), pentose sugar (ribose in RNA, 2-deoxyribose in DNA; T in DNA only, U in RNA only) and phosphate, joined by phosphodiester links; Watson and Crick's double helix with complementary pairing A = T (two hydrogen bonds) and G ≡ C (three), so each strand specifies the other; and the work — DNA storing heredity and replicating, RNA directing protein synthesis as mRNA, rRNA and tRNA (India, Grade 12 — *Chemistry, Part II, Textbook for Class XII*, Unit 14 §14.5 Nucleic Acids, printed p. 427)
386. **Halogens — group 17** (`chemistry/halogens-group-17`) — the halogen family studied whole, as Vietnam's Grade 10 book teaches it: fluorine, chlorine, bromine and iodine, seven outer electrons each, diatomic, growing less reactive and less gaseous down the group, displacing each other from their salts in the family's own order (Vietnam, Grade 10 — *Hóa học 10*, Ch.7 Bài 21 The halogen group, printed p. 104)
387. **Alkali metals — group 1** (`chemistry/alkali-metals-group-1`) — the group IA family as its own lesson: one outer electron each, soft light metals reacting with water to alkaline hydroxides and hydrogen, the violence growing from lithium's fizz to potassium's flame down the group (Vietnam, Grade 12 — *Hóa học 12*, Ch.7 Bài 24 Group IA elements, printed p. 108)
388. **Alkaline earth metals — group 2** (`chemistry/alkaline-earth-metals-group-2`) — the group IIA family as its own lesson: two outer electrons, +2 ions, harder and steadier than group 1, carrying limewater, limestone, bone and flame colours from brick-red calcium to apple-green barium (Vietnam, Grade 12 — *Hóa học 12*, Ch.7 Bài 25 Group IIA elements, printed p. 116)
389. **Fire chemistry — flash point and prevention** (`chemistry/fire-chemistry-flash-point-and-prevention`) — the three temperatures that describe a fuel — flash point, flame temperature and auto-ignition temperature — defined and compared, and prevention built directly on that science: cooling, smothering and fuel removal as the chain's three breaks (Vietnam, Grade 10 — *Chuyên đề học tập Hóa học 10*, Topic 2 Bài 6, printed p. 34; Bài 8, printed p. 43)
390. **Virtual chemistry laboratory** (`chemistry/virtual-chemistry-laboratory`) — experiments done in a virtual laboratory as a named practical: choosing reagents, setting conditions, running the change on screen — reaching the dangerous, costly, slow and fast experiments the bench cannot, while the real bench keeps its half of the teaching (Vietnam, Grade 10 — *Chuyên đề học tập Hóa học 10*, Topic 3 Bài 10, printed p. 60)
391. **Computational chemistry — an introduction** (`chemistry/computational-chemistry-introduction`) — school-level computational chemistry: computing a molecule's structural parameters (bond lengths, angles, shape — the lowest-energy arrangement) and energy parameters, and reading the model's numbers as claims to test against measurement (Vietnam, Grade 10 — *Chuyên đề học tập Hóa học 10*, Topic 3 Bài 11, printed p. 67)
392. **Chitin and glucosamine — from shrimp shells** (`chemistry/chitin-and-glucosamine-from-shrimp-shells`) — a named preparation from shell waste: the shells' chitin, a natural polymer of glucosamine units, hydrolysed by acid to glucosamine and taken as glucosamine hydrochloride, cartilage's own building unit (Vietnam, Grade 11 — *Chuyên đề học tập Hóa học 11*, Topic 2 Bài 6, printed p. 34)
393. **Metals in nature — and extraction methods** (`chemistry/metals-in-nature-and-extraction-methods`) — the general survey: metals free only when least reactive, locked in ores for the rest, and the extraction ladder the reactivity series assigns — heat alone, carbon reduction, electrolysis — taught whole before the single-metal cases (Vietnam, Grade 12 — *Hóa học 12*, Ch.6 Bài 20, printed p. 94)
394. **Water and life — the properties of water** (`chemistry/water-and-life-properties-of-water`) — water's place in life and nature, its unusual properties (liquid across life's temperatures, slow to boil, a near-universal solvent, and expanding as it freezes so ice floats and lakes freeze from the top down) and the water cycle as the entry point to all later chemistry — Greece's "from water to the atom" route (Greece, Grade 8 — *ΧΗΜΕΙΑ Β΄ Γυμνασίου*, Unit 2.1 Το νερό στη ζωή μας, printed p. 24)
395. **Splitting water — electrolysis, elements and compounds** (`chemistry/splitting-water-electrolysis-elements-and-compounds`) — an electric current splits water into hydrogen and oxygen in the 2 : 1 volume ratio, and the split itself proves the sorting of all substances: what chemical means can split is a compound, what they cannot is an element; pure substances are known by their fixed physical constants (§2.6.2) (Greece, Grade 8 — *ΧΗΜΕΙΑ Β΄ Γυμνασίου*, Unit 2.6 Διάσπαση του νερού – Χημικές ενώσεις και χημικά στοιχεία, printed p. 48)
396. **Carbon dioxide — properties, preparation and tests** (`chemistry/carbon-dioxide-properties-preparation-and-tests`) — the one gas studied whole: colourless, denser than air, slightly soluble, supporting neither combustion nor respiration; prepared in the laboratory from marble chips and dilute hydrochloric acid and collected by downward delivery; proved by limewater turning milky; its uses, and the photosynthesis/respiration balance with the greenhouse link (Greece, Grade 8 — *ΧΗΜΕΙΑ Β΄ Γυμνασίου*, Unit 3.3 Διοξείδιο του άνθρακα, printed p. 83; Grade 8 Laboratory Guide, 9η εργαστηριακή άσκηση, printed p. 49)
397. **Calorimetry and Hess's law — the laws of thermochemistry** (`chemistry/calorimetry-and-hess-law-thermochemistry-laws`) — measuring reaction heat in a calorimeter (q = mcΔT, molar ΔH on stated assumptions) and the bookkeeping laws that follow: Lavoisier–Laplace reversal of sign, ΔH scaling with the equation as written, and Hess's law — the total enthalpy change the same by every route, worked by cycles to reach what cannot be measured (Greece, Grade 12 — *Χημεία Γ΄ Λυκείου (Θετικών Σπουδών & Σπουδών Υγείας)*, Ch.2.2 Θερμιδομετρία – Νόμοι θερμοχημείας, printed p. 53)
398. **The rate law — and reaction mechanism** (`chemistry/rate-law-and-reaction-mechanism`) — rate = k[A]^m[B]^n with the orders found from initial-rates experiments, never read off the coefficients; the overall order as the sum of the powers and k carrying the conditions, temperature above all; and mechanism — elementary steps, intermediates, and the rate-determining step that must predict the experimental law (Greece, Grade 12 — *Χημεία Γ΄ Λυκείου (Θετικών Σπουδών & Σπουδών Υγείας)*, Ch.3.3 Νόμος ταχύτητας – Μηχανισμός, printed p. 82)
399. **The solubility product — Ksp** (`chemistry/solubility-product-ksp`) — the equilibrium constant of a sparingly soluble salt dissolving: Ksp as the product of ion concentrations raised to their coefficients, the ion product Q compared with Ksp to predict precipitation, molar solubility calculated from Ksp (s = √Ksp for a 1 : 1 salt, Ksp = 4s³ for AB₂), and the common ion driving solubility down (Greece, Grade 12 — *Χημεία Γ΄ Λυκείου (Θετικών Σπουδών & Σπουδών Υγείας)*, Ch.5.7 Γινόμενο διαλυτότητας, printed p. 172)
400. **Stereoisomerism — enantiomers and diastereomers** (`chemistry/stereoisomerism-enantiomers-and-diastereomers`) — same formula, same connections, a different arrangement in space: the carbon with four different groups and its two non-superposable mirror images, the enantiomers, told apart by optical activity (with the D/L notation strand, p. 318); and diastereomers, the stereoisomers that are not mirror images, differing in physical properties and separable by ordinary means — beyond the Grade 11 constitutional isomerism (Greece, Grade 12 — *Χημεία Γ΄ Λυκείου (Θετικών Σπουδών & Σπουδών Υγείας)*, Ch.7.2 Στερεοϊσομέρεια, printed p. 265)
401. **Electrodialysis and desalination** (`chemistry/electrodialysis-and-desalination`) — separating salt from seawater through ion-exchange membranes: Na⁺ and Cl⁻, dissolved and unfilterable, migrating in opposite directions under a current, each through the membranes that pass its own kind only, leaving desalinated water behind (Laos, Grade 12 — *ແບບຮຽນ ເຄມີສາດ ຊັ້ນມັດທະຍົມສຶກສາ ປີທີ 7 (Chemistry Textbook, Secondary Year 7 / ມ.7)*, Ch. 24 Advances in electrochemistry §3, printed p. 164; Fig. 24.4 printed p. 167)
402. **Textile dyeing chemistry** (`chemistry/textile-dyeing-chemistry`) — dyeing as a chemical industry in its own right: a dye must bind to the fibre and stay, the result controlled by dye, fibre kind, bath, time, temperature and fixing (Laos, Grade 12 — *ແບບຮຽນ ເຄມີສາດ ຊັ້ນມັດທະຍົມສຶກສາ ປີທີ 7 (Chemistry Textbook, Secondary Year 7 / ມ.7)*, Ch. 32 Dyeing industry, printed p. 206)
403. **Resonance structures** (`chemistry/resonance-structures`) — one molecule, two equivalent formulas: SO₂ drawn in two equivalent dot and graphic forms with the coordinate bond arrowed, the real structure their average, its two S–O bonds equal (Laos, Grade 11 — *ແບບຮຽນ ເຄມີສາດ ຊັ້ນມັດທະຍົມສຶກສາ ປີທີ 6 (Chemistry Textbook, Secondary Year 6 / ມ.6)*, Ch. 7 §7, printed p. 43)
404. **Naming covalent compounds** (`chemistry/naming-covalent-compounds`) — which element pairs form covalent compounds, formulas from the outer-electron ratio (CCl₄, CS₂, PCl₃, N₂O₃, SCl₂), and names built from Greek prefixes mono/di/tri/tetra/penta/hexa with an -ide ending — CO₂ carbon dioxide, CO carbon monoxide, P₄O₁₀ tetraphosphorus decaoxide (Laos, Grade 11 — *ແບບຮຽນ ເຄມີສາດ ຊັ້ນມັດທະຍົມສຶກສາ ປີທີ 6 (Chemistry Textbook, Secondary Year 6 / ມ.6)*, Ch. 7 §8, printed pp. 44–45)
405. **The Born–Haber cycle** (`chemistry/born-haber-cycle`) — formation reached in measured steps whose energies sum to zero around the loop, worked on NaCl: sublimation 109 + half-dissociation 121 + ionisation 494 − electron affinity 347 − lattice energy 787 + formation 410 = 0; the book spells it “Bon Haber Cycle”, taught corrected (Laos, Grade 11 — *ແບບຮຽນ ເຄມີສາດ ຊັ້ນມັດທະຍົມສຶກສາ ປີທີ 6 (Chemistry Textbook, Secondary Year 6 / ມ.6)*, Ch. 10 §2, printed p. 53; diagram printed p. 54)
406. **Ionic lattice structures** (`chemistry/ionic-lattice-structures`) — ionic compounds as a regular three-dimensional lattice of alternating ions with no separate molecules and only an empirical formula: the NaCl type, each ion touching 6 of the other kind (NaF, NaBr, KCl, MgO, CaO), and the CsCl type, each touching 8 (CsBr, CsI, NH₄Cl), the ions' sizes deciding (Laos, Grade 11 — *ແບບຮຽນ ເຄມີສາດ ຊັ້ນມັດທະຍົມສຶກສາ ປີທີ 6 (Chemistry Textbook, Secondary Year 6 / ມ.6)*, Ch. 10 §4, printed p. 55)
407. **Textile fibres** (`chemistry/textile-fibres`) — fibres as their own chapter, between plastics and rubber: natural fibres grown (cotton from plants, wool and silk from animals) against synthetic fibres manufactured as polymers and drawn into threads (nylon, polyester) (Laos, Grade 11 — *ແບບຮຽນ ເຄມີສາດ ຊັ້ນມັດທະຍົມສຶກສາ ປີທີ 6 (Chemistry Textbook, Secondary Year 6 / ມ.6)*, Ch. 31 Fibres, printed p. 188)
408. **Metalloids** (`chemistry/metalloids`) — the border elements with both metal and non-metal character, the side shown depending on the conditions: boron (fibres in plastics stronger than steel and lighter than aluminium, used in aircraft and spacecraft bodies; borazon pictured) and silicon (semiconductor; ordinary window glass from melting silicon with CaO; SiO₂ with a structure like diamond) (Afghanistan, Grade 11 — *کیمیا صنف یازدهم (Chemistry, Grade 11)*, Ch.11 Metalloid elements, printed p. 224)
409. **Recognising substances by their properties** (`chemistry/recognising-substances-by-their-properties`) — group experimental work identifying substances from observed properties, indicator behaviour with litmus among the clues (Armenia, Grade 7 — *Քիմիա 7 (Chemistry, Grade 7, student textbook)*, Theme 1, §1.4, printed p. 22)
410. **Simple and compound substances** (`chemistry/simple-and-compound-substances`) — substances sorted by their building: simple substances from atoms of one element, compound substances from atoms of different elements joined (Armenia, Grade 7 — *Քիմիա 7 (Chemistry, Grade 7, student textbook)*, Theme 3, §3.2, printed p. 81)
411. **Classification of inorganic compounds** (`chemistry/classification-of-inorganic-compounds`) — the four main classes — oxides, bases, acids, salts — set out as one scheme, the same scheme the book prints on its cover (Armenia, Grade 8 — *Քիմիա 8 (Chemistry, Grade 8, student textbook)*, Theme 3, §3/1, printed p. 59)
412. **Allotropy — forms of an element** (`chemistry/allotropy-forms-of-an-element`) — allotropy deepened deliberately: molecular against non-molecular structure substances, ozone among the examples (Armenia, Grade 7 — *«Քիմիա» 7-րդ դասարան, մեթոդական ուղեցույց (Chemistry, Grade 7, teacher guide)*, Theme 2.8 extension, guide PDF p. 53; teacher-guide evidence only, acceptance 0)
413. **The young chemist's home laboratory** (`chemistry/young-chemists-home-laboratory`) — a named home-laboratory programme: safe home experiments — H₂O₂ decomposition with foam, natural-gas burner observation, starch and other kitchen reagents (Armenia, Grade 8 — *Քիմիա 8, Մեթոդական ուղեցույց (Chemistry, Grade 8, teacher guide)*, printed p. 31; teacher-guide evidence only, acceptance 0)
414. **Acid rain — formation and effects** (`chemistry/acid-rain-formation-and-effects`) — SO₂ as the acid-rain precursor, its effect researched on Armenia's own stone monuments and structures, and the environmental problems that follow (Armenia, Grade 9 — *Քիմիա 9 (Chemistry, Grade 9, student textbook)*, Theme 1, §1.9–§1.10, printed pp. 26, 28, 30)
415. **Sea water and its salts** (`chemistry/sea-water-and-its-salts`) — a named lesson on sea water and the salts obtained from it (Armenia, Grade 9 — *Քիմիա 9, Մեթոդական ձեռնարկ (Chemistry, Grade 9, teacher guide)*, printed p. 71 (contents); teacher-guide evidence only, acceptance 0)
416. **The genetic connection between organic classes** (`chemistry/genetic-connection-between-organic-classes`) — the genetic-connection idea carried into organic chemistry: hydrocarbons → alcohols, parallel to the inorganic chains of Grade 8 (Armenia, Grade 9 — *Քիմիա 9, Մեթոդական ձեռնարկ (Chemistry, Grade 9, teacher guide)*, printed p. 124 (contents); teacher-guide evidence only, acceptance 0)
417. **Chromium and copper — configuration exceptions** (`chemistry/chromium-and-copper-configuration-exceptions`) — the anomalous configurations: the filling rules predict chromium as [Ar] 4s² 3d⁴, but one electron moves from 4s to 3d so the d sublevel becomes half-full (4s¹ 3d⁵) — the book's named case, "What happened in chromium" — and copper makes the same move to a completely full d (4s¹ 3d¹⁰) (Kuwait, Grade 10 — *الكيمياء — كتاب الطالب، الصف العاشر، الفصل الدراسي الأول (Chemistry, Student Book, Grade 10, Term 1)*, Unit 1, Lesson 5-1, printed p. 62)
418. **Displacement reactions — the more reactive metal takes over** (`chemistry/displacement-reactions-more-reactive-metal-takes-over`) — displacement read off the electrochemical series: series position is reactivity — zinc dipped in copper(II) sulphate takes copper's place (the blue fades, copper deposits), and among the halogens fluorine displaces all of them from their compounds while iodine displaces none (Kuwait, Grade 11 — *الكيمياء — الصف الحادي عشر، الفصل الدراسي الثاني، القسمان الأول والثاني (Chemistry, Grade 11, Term 2, Parts 1 and 2)*, Unit 4, Ch. 2, Lesson 1-2, printed pp. 48–49)
419. **Position isomers — where the group sits** (`chemistry/position-isomers-where-the-group-sits`) — position isomers of the functional group: isomers arising when the functional group's place on the main carbon chain changes, the compounds keeping the same molecular formula, the place carried in the name by its position number (Kuwait, Grade 11 — *الكيمياء — الصف الحادي عشر، الفصل الدراسي الثاني، القسمان الأول والثاني (Chemistry, Grade 11, Term 2, Parts 1 and 2)*, Unit 5, Ch. 1, Lesson 3-1, §2.4, printed p. 98)
420. **Dalton's law of partial pressures** (`chemistry/daltons-law-of-partial-pressures`) — Dalton's law of partial pressures derived from the ideal gas law: the partial pressure defined as the pressure of one mixture component occupying the mixture's volume at the same temperature, the total as the sum of the partials, and air (oxygen, nitrogen, carbon dioxide and traces) as the worked example (Kuwait, Grade 12 — *الكيمياء — الصف الثاني عشر، الفصل الدراسي الأول (Chemistry, Student Book, Grade 12, Term 1)*, Unit 1, Ch. 2, Lesson 3-2, §2, printed pp. 49–51)
421. **Alkyl halides — naming, properties and uses** (`chemistry/alkyl-halides-naming-properties-and-uses`) — alkyl halides (R–X) and aryl halides taught whole: uses first (vinyl chloride for PVC, chloroform, CCl₄, the CFCs), naming both ways (IUPAC — longest chain, di-/tri-/tetra-, alphabetical halogens — and common alkyl-halide names), classification as primary, secondary or tertiary by the carbon holding the halogen, preparation by direct halogenation (alkanes under UV, benzene with a catalyst), physical properties (nearly insoluble in water, boiling points above the alkanes', Br/I compounds denser than water), and substitution reactions as preparations — aqueous bases to alcohols, alkoxides to ethers, sodium amide to amines (Kuwait, Grade 12 — *الكيمياء — كتاب الطالب، الصف الثاني عشر، الفصل الدراسي الثاني، القسم الأول والقسم الثاني (Chemistry, Student Book, Grade 12, Term 2, Sections 1 and 2)*, Unit 5, Ch. 1, Lesson 2-1, §1 printed pp. 65–72)
422. **Waxes and phospholipids** (`chemistry/waxes-and-phospholipids`) — waxes and phospholipids: waxes as esters of long-chain fatty acids with long-chain monohydric alcohols (beeswax from myricyl alcohol and palmitic acid, coating plant leaves and animal fur against water), and phospholipids as glycerol esters carrying two fatty acids and a phosphoric acid, forming the membranes of living cells, with a polar water-loving head and a non-polar water-fearing tail (Kuwait, Grade 12 — *الكيمياء — كتاب الطالب، الصف الثاني عشر، الفصل الدراسي الثاني، القسم الأول والقسم الثاني (Chemistry, Student Book, Grade 12, Term 2, Sections 1 and 2)*, Unit 6, Ch. 2, Lesson 2-2, §5–6, §2 printed p. 85)
423. **Ions and the ECG heart signal** (`chemistry/ions-and-the-ecg-heart-signal`) — Egypt's own application: Na⁺/K⁺/Ca²⁺ ion movements across heart-muscle membranes make the electrical signals an ECG records, so ion imbalance shows up as heart-rhythm disturbance. (Egypt, Grade 11 — *الكيمياء للصف الثانى الثانوى – الفصل الدراسى الثانى (Chemistry — For 2nd Secondary, Second Term (2025–2026), Arabic edition)*, Chapter One, STS box, printed p. 8)
424. **Sigma and pi bonds** (`chemistry/sigma-and-pi-bonds`) — Sigma bonds from head-on overlap and pi bonds from sideways overlap of pure p orbitals; single bonds are sigma, double/triple add pi bonds, and pi is the weaker, easier-broken partner. (Egypt, Grade 11 — *الكيمياء للصف الثانى الثانوى – الفصل الدراسى الثانى (Chemistry — For 2nd Secondary, Second Term (2025–2026), Arabic edition)*, Chapter One, printed p. 19)
425. **Alkali metals and their reactions** (`chemistry/alkali-metals-and-their-reactions`) — The full alkali reaction set in Egypt's order: with oxygen (normal oxides, peroxides, superoxides, and their reactions with water, acids and CO₂), with hydrogen (ionic hydrides), with halogens, with other nonmetals (Li₃N from lithium and nitrogen), with water (why sodium fires are not put out with water), with acids, and the thermal decomposition of their carbonates and nitrates (gunpowder vs Chile saltpetre note). (Egypt, Grade 11 — *الكيمياء للصف الثانى الثانوى – الفصل الدراسى الثانى (Chemistry — For 2nd Secondary, Second Term (2025–2026), Arabic edition)*, Chapter Two, printed pp. 35–37)
426. **Alkali metals in technology** (`chemistry/alkali-metals-in-technology`) — One use per element as Egypt teaches it: lithium in phone/EV batteries and light alloys (Falcon 9 tanks), sodium in vapour street lamps and as liquid coolant in the sodium nuclear reactor, potassium into fertilisers and potassium superoxide in closed breathing apparatus, rubidium bromide in scratch-resistant glass and night-vision kit, caesium in atomic clocks that keep GPS satellite timing. (Egypt, Grade 11 — *الكيمياء للصف الثانى الثانوى – الفصل الدراسى الثانى (Chemistry — For 2nd Secondary, Second Term (2025–2026), Arabic edition)*, Chapter Two, STS section, printed pp. 38–40)
427. **Group 15 elements in technology** (`chemistry/group-15-elements-in-technology`) — Group 5A at work, one spread per element: liquid nitrogen in cryotherapy and storing biological samples; phosphorus into blue LEDs and high-refractive-index optical glass with anti-reflection lens coatings; gallium arsenide (arsenic) in phone solar panels and lasers; antimony(III) oxide as a flame retardant; bismuth in low-melting alloys for fire sprinklers and in lead-free solder. (Egypt, Grade 11 — *الكيمياء للصف الثانى الثانوى – الفصل الدراسى الثانى (Chemistry — For 2nd Secondary, Second Term (2025–2026), Arabic edition)*, Chapter Two, STS section, printed pp. 52–55)
428. **Alpha, beta and gamma radiation compared** (`chemistry/alpha-beta-and-gamma-radiation-compared`) — The comparison table Egypt drills: alpha = helium nucleus, +2, bent slightly in fields; beta = fast electron, −1, bent strongly; gamma = photon, uncharged, unaffected — plus stable vs radioactive isotopes distinguished. (Egypt, Grade 11 — *الكيمياء للصف الثانى الثانوى – الفصل الدراسى الثانى (Chemistry — For 2nd Secondary, Second Term (2025–2026), Arabic edition)*, Chapter Three, printed p. 63)
429. **Nuclear binding energy and mass defect** (`chemistry/nuclear-binding-energy-and-mass-defect`) — Mass–energy calculations: E = mc² in joules and the 931 MeV-per-u shortcut; the mass defect of the helium nucleus (0.0305 u → 28.3955 MeV) and binding energy per nucleon as the measure of nuclear stability, worked again for oxygen-16. (Egypt, Grade 11 — *الكيمياء للصف الثانى الثانوى – الفصل الدراسى الثانى (Chemistry — For 2nd Secondary, Second Term (2025–2026), Arabic edition)*, Chapter Three, printed pp. 64–65)
430. **Radioactive decay — emissions and nuclear equations** (`chemistry/radioactive-decay-emissions-and-nuclear-equations`) — Nuclear transformations with balanced A/Z bookkeeping: alpha emission (²³⁸U → ²³⁴Th), negatron (beta-minus) emission from neutron decay (¹⁴C → ¹⁴N), positron emission (²²Na → ²²Ne) and gamma emission accompanying the others (⁶⁰Co decay). (Egypt, Grade 11 — *الكيمياء للصف الثانى الثانوى – الفصل الدراسى الثانى (Chemistry — For 2nd Secondary, Second Term (2025–2026), Arabic edition)*, Chapter Three, printed pp. 66–68)
431. **Half-life and decay calculations** (`chemistry/half-life-and-decay-calculations`) — Half-life defined off the decay curve, the count of decay steps D = total time ÷ half-life, a table of half-lives from uranium-238 (4.51×10⁹ years) to polonium-214 (fractions of a millisecond), and a worked manganese-56 example. (Egypt, Grade 11 — *الكيمياء للصف الثانى الثانوى – الفصل الدراسى الثانى (Chemistry — For 2nd Secondary, Second Term (2025–2026), Arabic edition)*, Chapter Three, printed p. 68)
432. **Artificial transmutation and projectiles** (`chemistry/artificial-transmutation-and-projectiles`) — Artificial (induced) radioactivity: bombarding a target nucleus with alpha, proton, neutron or deuteron projectiles (the neutron preferred, being uncharged), worked as aluminium-27 + neutron → sodium-24 + alpha. (Egypt, Grade 11 — *الكيمياء للصف الثانى الثانوى – الفصل الدراسى الثانى (Chemistry — For 2nd Secondary, Second Term (2025–2026), Arabic edition)*, Chapter Three, printed pp. 69–70)
433. **Nuclear fission — chain reaction and uranium enrichment** (`chemistry/nuclear-fission-chain-reaction-and-uranium-enrichment`) — Fission of uranium-235 by slow neutrons into two lighter nuclei plus neutrons and huge energy, the self-sustaining chain reaction, and enrichment: natural uranium is 99.3% U-238 / 0.7% U-235, power reactors run at 3–5% enrichment while weapons exceed 90%; Egypt's El Dabaa plant named as the national example, with 1 g of U-235 equated to burning 3 tons of coal. (Egypt, Grade 11 — *الكيمياء للصف الثانى الثانوى – الفصل الدراسى الثانى (Chemistry — For 2nd Secondary, Second Term (2025–2026), Arabic edition)*, Chapter Three, printed pp. 70–71)
434. **Nuclear fusion in the sun and on Earth** (`chemistry/nuclear-fusion-in-the-sun-and-on-earth`) — Fusion as the reverse of fission: deuterium + tritium → helium-4 + neutron in reactors, and the sun's own fusion steps (helium-3 pairs, proton + deuterium) written as equations; closing table contrasting nuclear with chemical reactions (nucleons vs outer electrons, energy conservation vs mass conservation, extreme conditions, vastly larger energies). (Egypt, Grade 11 — *الكيمياء للصف الثانى الثانوى – الفصل الدراسى الثانى (Chemistry — For 2nd Secondary, Second Term (2025–2026), Arabic edition)*, Chapter Three, printed pp. 71–72)
435. **Radioisotopes at work — and the americium smoke detector** (`chemistry/radioisotopes-at-work-and-the-americium-smoke-detector`) — How a smoke detector works (americium-241 alpha ionises the air; smoke stops the current and triggers the alarm) and the isotope uses list: technetium-99 and thallium-201 in medical imaging, cobalt-60 gamma in food irradiation and wheat breeding, phosphorus tracers followed with a Geiger counter, oxygen-18 proving photosynthesis oxygen comes from water, and carbon-14 dating applied to a pharaonic mummy. (Egypt, Grade 11 — *الكيمياء للصف الثانى الثانوى – الفصل الدراسى الثانى (Chemistry — For 2nd Secondary, Second Term (2025–2026), Arabic edition)*, Chapter Three, STS section, printed pp. 69, 73–74)
436. **First transition series — elements and their uses** (`chemistry/first-transition-series-elements-and-their-uses`) — Egypt's element-by-element tour of Sc→Zn: crust percentages, then one use-and-compound set each (scandium in MiG jets and stadium lamps, titanium in aircraft and dental implants with TiO₂ sunscreen, vanadium in car-spring steel and V₂O₅ catalyst, chromium plating and dichromate oxidant, ferromanganese rail steel and KMnO₄ antiseptic, cobalt-60 in food preservation and tumour treatment, nickel in NiCd batteries and hydrogenation, copper sulphate and Fehling's solution, zinc galvanising and ZnS X-ray screens). (Egypt, Grade 12 — *Chemistry — For the General Secondary Certificate (3rd Secondary / Grade 12), Term 1 (2025–2026), English edition)*, Chapter One, printed pp. 5–7)
437. **What makes an element a transition metal** (`chemistry/what-makes-an-element-a-transition-metal`) — The definition Egypt examines on: incomplete d (or f) sublevel in the atom or in one of its ions — so the coinage metals Cu/Ag/Au count (d⁹/d⁸ ions) but Zn/Cd/Hg do not (d¹⁰ in atom and +2 ion alike), and Cr/Cu's anomalous 4s¹ configurations are explained by half-filled/filled d stability. (Egypt, Grade 12 — *Chemistry — For the General Secondary Certificate (3rd Secondary / Grade 12), Term 1 (2025–2026), English edition)*, Chapter One, printed pp. 9–11)
438. **Paramagnetism and diamagnetism — from unpaired electrons** (`chemistry/paramagnetism-and-diamagnetism-from-unpaired-electrons`) — Magnetic behaviour read straight off the d-orbital box diagram: unpaired electrons make a substance paramagnetic (moment grows with unpaired count), all-paired makes it diamagnetic; worked ordering for Zn, Ni²⁺ and Fe²⁺ compounds. (Egypt, Grade 12 — *Chemistry — For the General Secondary Certificate (3rd Secondary / Grade 12), Term 1 (2025–2026), English edition)*, Chapter One, printed pp. 13–14)
439. **Transition metals as catalysts** (`chemistry/transition-metals-as-catalysts`) — Catalytic activity explained by surface adsorption: 4s/3d electrons bond reactant molecules onto the metal surface, concentrating them and weakening their bonds so activation energy falls (energy-profile diagram shown); Ni in oil hydrogenation, Fe in Haber–Bosch, V₂O₅ in the contact process, MnO₂ decomposing hydrogen peroxide. (Egypt, Grade 12 — *Chemistry — For the General Secondary Certificate (3rd Secondary / Grade 12), Term 1 (2025–2026), English edition)*, Chapter One, printed pp. 14–15)
440. **Iron and its three oxides** (`chemistry/iron-and-its-three-oxides`) — Iron's own chemistry: +2/+3 states only of importance, reactions with air, steam, chlorine, sulphur and acids (dilute acids give Fe(II) salts; concentrated nitric acid passivates), then FeO, Fe₂O₃ and Fe₃O₄ one by one — preparation (oxalate decomposition, hydroxide ignition, partial reduction), and Fe₃O₄ proven a mixed oxide because hot concentrated acid gives both Fe(II) and Fe(III) salts. (Egypt, Grade 12 — *Chemistry — For the General Secondary Certificate (3rd Secondary / Grade 12), Term 1 (2025–2026), English edition)*, Chapter One, printed pp. 20–24)
441. **Chemical analysis — qualitative and quantitative** (`chemistry/chemical-analysis-qualitative-and-quantitative`) — What analysis is for (medicine, agriculture, industry, environment, with Egyptian-style examples from blood sugar to soil and air pollutants) and its two kinds: qualitative (identify the constituents) always before quantitative (measure how much), qualitative analysis of inorganic salts meaning the acidic radical (anion) and basic radical (cation). (Egypt, Grade 12 — *Chemistry — For the General Secondary Certificate (3rd Secondary / Grade 12), Term 1 (2025–2026), English edition)*, Chapter Two, printed pp. 31–32)
442. **Gravimetric analysis — by volatilisation and precipitation** (`chemistry/gravimetric-analysis-by-volatilisation-and-precipitation`) — Gravimetric analysis in its two forms: volatilisation (heating hydrated BaCl₂ to constant mass to find its water of crystallisation, BaCl₂·2H₂O) and precipitation (weighing BaSO₄ on ashless filter paper, ignited in a crucible, to find the barium chloride in a solution). (Egypt, Grade 12 — *Chemistry — For the General Secondary Certificate (3rd Secondary / Grade 12), Term 1 (2025–2026), English edition)*, Chapter Two, printed pp. 46–48)
443. **Catalysts and light — in rate and equilibrium** (`chemistry/catalysts-and-light-in-rate-and-equilibrium`) — Catalysts speed both directions equally so the equilibrium position does not move; enzymes named as biological catalysts and catalytic converters as the car application; light as a rate factor through photosynthesis and the silver bromide photographic film (Ag⁺ + e⁻ → Ag). (Egypt, Grade 12 — *Chemistry — For the General Secondary Certificate (3rd Secondary / Grade 12), Term 1 (2025–2026), English edition)*, Chapter Three, printed pp. 64–65)
444. **Ostwald's dilution law and weak-electrolyte calculations** (`chemistry/ostwalds-dilution-law-and-weak-electrolyte-calculations`) — Ostwald's dilution law derived (Ka = α²C, α = √(Ka/C)), degree of ionisation computed for hydrocyanic acid, then [H₃O⁺] = √(Ca·Ka) for weak acids and [OH⁻] = √(Cb·Kb) for weak bases such as ammonia, each with a worked number. (Egypt, Grade 12 — *Chemistry — For the General Secondary Certificate (3rd Secondary / Grade 12), Term 1 (2025–2026), English edition)*, Chapter Three, printed pp. 69–72)
445. **Electroplating and electrolytic metal purification** (`chemistry/electroplating-and-electrolytic-metal-purification`) — Electrolysis at work, first electroplating (a silver-plated jug: object as cathode, silver anode, AgNO₃ electrolyte; chromium on car parts and taps), then the purification of copper — impure anode dissolves, pure copper plates the cathode at 99.95%, iron/zinc impurities stay in solution, silver and gold fall as anode mud and are recovered. (Egypt, Grade 12 — *Chemistry — For the General Secondary Certificate (3rd Secondary / Grade 12), Term 1 (2025–2026), English edition)*, Chapter Four, printed pp. 107–110)
446. **Organic vs inorganic compounds — compared** (`chemistry/organic-vs-inorganic-compounds-compared`) — The eleven-row comparison table Egypt drills (structure, solubility, melting/boiling points, odour, flammability, bond type, conductivity, reaction rate, polymerisation, isomerism), set up from a bench comparison of paraffin, naphthalene, ethanol, acetone and glycerol against water, salt and copper sulphate. (Egypt, Grade 12 — *Chemistry — For the General Secondary Certificate (3rd Secondary / Grade 12), Term 1 (2025–2026), English edition)*, Chapter Five, printed pp. 119–120 (chapter pagination))
447. **Molecular and structural formulas** (`chemistry/molecular-and-structural-formulas`) — Molecular formula vs structural formula, valency as the count of covalent bonds (C tetravalent, O divalent, N trivalent, H monovalent), with structural formulas drawn for C₂H₄Cl₂, C₂H₄, C₂H₂ and CH₃OH. (Egypt, Grade 12 — *Chemistry — For the General Secondary Certificate (3rd Secondary / Grade 12), Term 1 (2025–2026), English edition)*, Chapter Five, printed p. 120 (chapter pagination))
448. **Detecting carbon and hydrogen in an organic compound** (`chemistry/detecting-carbon-and-hydrogen-in-an-organic-compound`) — The standard practical: heat the substance with copper(II) oxide; water turns white anhydrous copper sulphate blue, and CO₂ turns lime water milky — the two observations that prove C and H. (Egypt, Grade 12 — *Chemistry — For the General Secondary Certificate (3rd Secondary / Grade 12), Term 1 (2025–2026), English edition)*, Chapter Five, printed pp. 122–123 (chapter pagination))
449. **Food additives — what each kind does and its limit** (`chemistry/food-additives-what-each-kind-does-and-its-limit`) — Substances added to food on purpose, each named on the label by its E-number, sorted by the job each kind does (colourings first) and sorted again by the harm the book presents — sulfur dioxide and sulfites and phosphates classed toxic, citric acid E330 and sodium benzoate E211 among those classed carcinogenic — closing with the book's advice to avoid long-term consumption of synthetically additivated products. (Moldova, Grade 12 — *Chimie: Manual pentru clasa a XII-a*, Unit 9 §9.1 Aditivii alimentari, printed p. 190)
450. **Radicals and valency** (`chemistry/radicals-and-valency`) — Radicals as charged groups of atoms (ammonium, hydroxide, nitrate, carbonate, sulfate, phosphate), then the valency of elements and radicals as combining power — the balance rule, brackets and all — taught as one step immediately before formulae and nomenclature. (South Sudan, Secondary 1 — *South Sudan Secondary 1 Chemistry — Student's Book 1*, Unit 2 §§2.13–2.14, printed pp. 73–74)
451. **Action of heat on salts** (`chemistry/action-of-heat-on-salts`) — How salts behave on heating, taught as decomposition patterns: most carbonates to the oxide and CO₂, nitrates splitting three ways by the metal's reactivity, the whole read from the gases (limewater, glowing splint, brown fumes) and the residue's colours hot and cold. (South Sudan, Secondary 2 — *South Sudan Secondary 2 Chemistry — Student's Book*, Unit 3 §3.3, printed p. 88)
452. **Carbonic acid and carbonates** (`chemistry/carbonic-acid-and-carbonates`) — Carbon dioxide in water forming weak, dibasic carbonic acid; its two salt families, hydrogencarbonates and carbonates; and the carbonates' reactions — the acid fizz test, heat, and the cave-making dissolution as hydrogencarbonate that heating reverses. (South Sudan, Secondary 2 — *South Sudan Secondary 2 Chemistry — Student's Book*, Unit 4 §4.5, printed p. 153)
453. **Back titration** (`chemistry/back-titration`) — For substances that cannot be titrated directly — insoluble, slow, or without a sharp end point: add a known, measured excess of a standard reagent, titrate the leftover, and added − leftover is the measurement. (South Sudan, Secondary 3 — *South Sudan Secondary 3 Chemistry — Student's Book 3*, Unit 1 §1.7, printed p. 22)
454. **Water of crystallisation** (`chemistry/water-of-crystallisation`) — Water built into a salt's crystal in fixed proportion (CuSO₄·5H₂O), driven off by heating to constant mass — the mass lost is the water, the mole ratio fixes the dot-number — and percentage water of crystallisation, the percentage investigation framed by the Secondary 3 teacher guide. (South Sudan, Secondary 3 — *South Sudan Secondary 3 Chemistry — Student's Book 3*, Unit 1 §1.8, printed p. 30)
455. **Zinc — extraction and uses** (`chemistry/zinc-extraction-and-uses`) — Zinc blende (ZnS) roasted to the oxide, the oxide reduced with carbon, the metal distilling off as vapour to condense; then its work: galvanising steel sacrificially, brass, and dry-cell cases. (South Sudan, Secondary 4 — *South Sudan Secondary 4 Chemistry — Student's Book*, Unit 4 §4.5, printed p. 92)
456. **Chromium — extraction and uses** (`chemistry/chromium-extraction-and-uses`) — Chromite (FeCr₂O₄) by way of the chromates to chromium(III) oxide, reduced by aluminium; most chromium travelling as ferrochrome into stainless steel, its self-sealing oxide layer the root of chrome plating and rust-proof shine. (South Sudan, Secondary 4 — *South Sudan Secondary 4 Chemistry — Student's Book*, Unit 4 §4.6, printed p. 96)
457. **Chromatography — paper chromatography and Rf values** (`chemistry/chromatography-paper-and-rf-values`) — Separation by travel between a stationary paper and a climbing solvent, building on the paper chromatography of Secondary 1: the chromatogram, and Rf = spot distance ÷ front distance as the identifying constant, spent on identification and purity from a single drop. (South Sudan, Secondary 4 — *South Sudan Secondary 4 Chemistry — Student's Book*, Unit 5 §5.1, printed p. 103)
458. **Analytical spectroscopy — IR, NMR, UV and mass** (`chemistry/analytical-spectroscopy-ir-nmr-uv-mass`) — The four spectroscopies of the analytical techniques unit: infrared (each bond vibration absorbing at its characteristic wavelength), NMR (the structure-determining count of hydrogen environments), ultraviolet (the conjugated clue), and mass spectroscopy — the book's own spelling — weighing the molecule and reading its fragments. (South Sudan, Secondary 4 — *South Sudan Secondary 4 Chemistry — Student's Book*, Unit 5 §§5.2–5.5, printed pp. 109–116)
459. **Testing purity by melting and boiling point** (`chemistry/testing-purity-by-melting-and-boiling-point`) — A pure solid melts at one sharp, fixed temperature and a pure liquid boils at one fixed temperature; impurities lower and widen the melting point and raise the boiling point — the moved, smeared point is the verdict, backed by the single-spot chromatogram, and run in the practical workbook on naphthalene and on water or ethanol. (Libya, Grade 10 — *الكيمياء — كتاب الطالب (Chemistry — Student Book)*, Unit 1 §1-3 Testing the Purity of Substances, printed p. 21)
460. **Giant covalent structures** (`chemistry/giant-covalent-structures`) — The third structural class: every atom covalently bonded into one continuous network — diamond, silica (sand) and graphite-style networks — hence very hard with very high melting points, since melting must break the bonds themselves. (Libya, Grade 10 — *الكيمياء — كتاب الطالب (Chemistry — Student Book)*, Unit 3 §3-5, printed p. 46)
461. **Variables in a fair test** (`chemistry/variables-in-a-fair-test`) — The independent variable you change, the dependent variable you measure, and the controlled variables you hold fixed — taught through the practical course's opening investigation, timing how fast an unknown solid dissolves, and answered in the teacher guide in the full variables framework (particle size, solubility, and the controlled set). (Libya, Grade 10 — *الكيمياء — كراسة النشاط العملي (Chemistry — Practical Activity Workbook)*, Experiment 1, printed p. 7)
462. **Flame tests — colours in the flame** (`chemistry/flame-tests-colours-in-the-flame`) — A clean wire loop, a hot flame, and the colour the metal gives it: observation first, the metal's name as the inference — Experiment 10 inside the Grade 12 practical workbook's qualitative-analysis run of ion-identification experiments. (Libya, Grade 12 — *كراسة النشاط العملي (Practical Activity Workbook)*, Experiment 10, printed p. 36)

463. **Hazards of polymers and plastics** (`chemistry/hazards-of-polymers-and-plastics`) — the polymer chain itself is large and unreactive; the hazards ride with what the plastic carries and sheds: unreacted residual monomer trapped in the material, small molecules released on heating and burning, and plasticizers that were mixed in but never bonded in and slowly leach out — closing with the harm discarded plastic does to animals and the prevention measures paired to each hazard (Bhutan, Class IX — *A Textbook of Chemistry, Class IX (Bhutan Edition)*, Ch.6 §6.8 Hazards of polymers, printed pp. 109–111)
464. **Transition elements — characteristic properties** (`chemistry/transition-elements-characteristic-properties`) — the family's six signatures as one lesson: variable oxidation states, coloured ions and compounds, catalytic activity, complex formation, paramagnetism, and hard alloy-forming metals — every one traced to the partly filled (n−1)d subshell — closing with group 11, copper, silver and gold, their similarities, falling reactivity and uses (Bhutan, Class X — *Chemistry, Class X*, Ch.5 §5.3–5.4, printed pp. 138–148)
465. **Denatured alcohol and illicit liquor** (`chemistry/denatured-alcohol-and-illicit-liquor`) — ethanol made undrinkable on purpose (methylated spirit: methanol, foul taste, violet dye) so industrial spirit can be sold duty-free, against spurious liquor faked with methanol that blinds and kills and how suspect liquor is identified — closing with the chapter's reckoning of ethanol's impacts on environment, economy, society and health and its case study "The Myth behind Alcohol Happiness" by Dr. Chencho Dorji (Bhutan, Class X — *Chemistry, Class X*, Ch.9 §9.4/§9.6, printed pp. 223–233, case study pp. 234–236)
466. **Phase rule and phase diagrams** (`chemistry/phase-rule-and-phase-diagrams`) — the formalism under every phase diagram: phase, component and degrees of freedom counted by their own rules, Gibbs' phase rule F = C − P + 2 with the criteria for phase equilibrium, read on water three ways — area F = 2, line F = 1, triple point F = 0 (Bhutan, Class XI — *A Textbook of Chemistry, Class XI (Bhutan Edition)*, Ch.9 §9.2–9.4, printed pp. 286–288)
467. **Haloalkanes — nucleophilic substitution** (`chemistry/haloalkanes-nucleophilic-substitution`) — the family built on the one polar bond C–X: nomenclature, preparation and properties, then the swap itself in its two mechanisms — S_N2's backside attack with inversion, S_N1's flat carbocation — closing with chirality, enantiomers and optical activity, the product's handedness as the mechanism's fingerprint (Bhutan, Class XI — *A Textbook of Chemistry, Class XI (Bhutan Edition)*, Ch.12 Haloalkanes, printed pp. 451–479)
468. **Biological role of sodium, potassium, magnesium and calcium** (`chemistry/biological-role-of-sodium-potassium-magnesium-and-calcium`) — four metal ions on staff in living bodies: Mg²⁺ at the centre of chlorophyll and, with Ca²⁺, catalysing enzymatic reactions; Ca²⁺ triggering muscle contraction and stored in bones as phosphate, its deficiency in children causing malnutrition; Na⁺ outside cells and K⁺ inside them holding the fluids and carrying the nerve impulse (Bhutan, Class XI — *A Textbook of Chemistry, Class XI (Bhutan Edition)*, Ch.5 §5.6, printed p. 178)

469. **The Arab and Muslim founders of chemistry** (`chemistry/the-arab-and-muslim-founders-of-chemistry`) — Yemen's Grade 10 history honour roll: Jabir ibn Hayyan, called the true founder of chemistry for his experimental method and his classification of substances, his first statement of fixed proportions, and the scholars who followed him — al-Razi, Ibn Sina, al-Jildaki, al-Hamdani and al-Zahrawi — with the word chemistry itself traced to an Arabic verb meaning to hide/conceal (Yemen, Grade 10 — *الكيمياء للصف الأول الثانوي (Chemistry for the First Secondary Year)*, Unit 1, printed pp. 11–16)
470. **Phlogiston — the theory that burning died of** (`chemistry/phlogiston-the-theory-that-burning-died-of`) — Stahl's phlogiston theory (1701 onwards) as Yemen's Grade 10 book teaches it: burning releases phlogiston, a metal's calx is the metal minus phlogiston, charcoal restores the metal by giving phlogiston back — and the theory fails on Lavoisier's weighing, since sulfur and phosphorus gain weight when they burn, so phlogiston would need negative weight (Yemen, Grade 10 — *الكيمياء للصف الأول الثانوي (Chemistry for the First Secondary Year)*, Unit 1, printed pp. 16–17)
471. **Basic, applied and technological research — the penicillin chain** (`chemistry/basic-applied-and-technological-research-the-penicillin-chain`) — Yemen's Grade 10 link between chemistry, technology and society: three kinds of research — basic, applied and technological — traced through one worked chain (basic research discovers penicillin and its structure, applied research turns it into an antibiotic industry, technology mass-produces it), with the positive applications, the negative side and society's feedback on science (Yemen, Grade 10 — *الكيمياء للصف الأول الثانوي (Chemistry for the First Secondary Year)*, Unit 1, printed pp. 21–24)
472. **The Downs cell — making sodium from its salt** (`chemistry/the-downs-cell-making-sodium-from-its-salt`) — Sodium prepared by electrolysing molten sodium chloride in the Downs cell, as Yemen's Grade 10 book draws it: graphite anode and iron cathode ring separated by an iron gauze diaphragm, chloride oxidised to chlorine at the anode, sodium ions reduced at the cathode, and the liquid metal floating up into its own collecting chamber so the products never meet (Yemen, Grade 10 — *الكيمياء للصف الأول الثانوي (Chemistry for the First Secondary Year)*, Unit 5, printed pp. 109–110)
473. **Common salt — extraction and uses** (`chemistry/common-salt-extraction-and-uses`) — Sodium chloride placed in Yemen first, as the Grade 10 book places it: high-purity rock salt from al-Salif in al-Hudaydah and the deposits of Shabwa and Marib needing only crushing and packing, solar evaporation of sea water at places such as Aden yielding salt carrying CaCl2 and MgCl2 impurities, and the salt itself — an ionic crystal melting at 804 °C — at work in food, preservation, freezing mixtures and as feedstock for NaOH, soda and hydrochloric acid (Yemen, Grade 10 — *الكيمياء للصف الأول الثانوي (Chemistry for the First Secondary Year)*, Unit 5, printed p. 112)
474. **Sodium bicarbonate — baking soda** (`chemistry/sodium-bicarbonate-baking-soda`) — Sodium hydrogencarbonate as Yemen's Grade 10 book teaches it: prepared in the laboratory by passing CO2 into cold caustic soda solution and met industrially as the Solvay intermediate, breaking down above 100 °C to sodium carbonate, CO2 and water, fizzing CO2 with acids — and at work in baking powder, leather tanning, fire extinguishers and medicine against stomach acidity (Yemen, Grade 10 — *الكيمياء للصف الأول الثانوي (Chemistry for the First Secondary Year)*, Unit 5, printed p. 116)
475. **Gypsum and plaster — setting with water** (`chemistry/gypsum-and-plaster-setting-with-water`) — Calcium sulfate as gypsum, CaSO4·2H2O, met through Yemen's Grade 10 book's own bench moment: powder the plaster of Yemen's traditional qamariya window-screen craftsmen, add water, and it sets quickly into a hard mass giving out heat — the paste that fills ceiling voids and casts statues, setting before it is even placed if you dawdle (Yemen, Grade 10 — *الكيمياء للصف الأول الثانوي (Chemistry for the First Secondary Year)*, Unit 6, printed p. 128)
476. **Group 13 — the boron family survey** (`chemistry/group-13-the-boron-family-survey`) — The third main group (IIIA) read off its data table, as Yemen's Grade 11 book reads it: B, Al, Ga, In, Tl with configurations ns2np1 — all metals except boron the metalloid, hydroxides that will not dissolve in water unlike groups IA/IIA, boron's acidic oxide against alumina the amphoteric, and basic character growing steadily down the group, opened by gallium photographed melting in a hand (Yemen, Grade 11 — *الكيمياء للصف الثاني الثانوي (Chemistry for the Second Secondary Year)*, Unit 1: Elements of the third main group (IIIA), printed pp. 9–10)
477. **The thermite reaction — welding with aluminium** (`chemistry/the-thermite-reaction-welding-with-aluminium`) — Aluminium's hunger for oxygen put to work, as Yemen's Grade 11 book teaches it: a reducing agent for winning metals (3MnO2 + 4Al -> 3Mn + 2Al2O3) and the heart of thermite — aluminium powder with iron(III) oxide in a crucible, lit by a magnesium ribbon, welding rail joints at about 3500 °C (Fe2O3 + 2Al -> 2Fe + Al2O3) (Yemen, Grade 11 — *الكيمياء للصف الثاني الثانوي (Chemistry for the Second Secondary Year)*, Unit 1, printed p. 13)
478. **Aluminium — a metal that fights acids and alkalis** (`chemistry/aluminium-a-metal-that-fights-acids-and-alkalis`) — Aluminium's reactions run systematically, as Yemen's Grade 11 book runs them: with oxygen to Al2O3; fast with dilute HCl but slowly with concentrated acid; SO2 from hot concentrated H2SO4; nothing with nitric acid, because the protective oxide coat forms — which is why aluminium tankers carry nitric acid; soluble aluminates plus hydrogen with NaOH or KOH; and compounds with sulfur, nitrogen, carbon and the halogens — the amphoteric metal behaviour done by hand in the practical workbook's first two activities, aluminium powder on the Bunsen flame and heated with sodium hydroxide solution (Yemen, Grade 11 — *الكيمياء للصف الثاني الثانوي (Chemistry for the Second Secondary Year)*, Unit 1, printed pp. 13–15; Yemen, Grade 11 — *الأنشطة والتجارب العملية — الكيمياء للصف الثاني الثانوي (Activities and Practical Experiments: Chemistry for the Second Secondary Year)*, Activities 1–2, printed pp. 6–8)
479. **Aluminium sulfate — from paper to fire foam** (`chemistry/aluminium-sulfate-from-paper-to-fire-foam`) — Aluminium sulfate as Yemen's Grade 11 book teaches it: made from Al2O3 + 3H2SO4, crystallising with 18 waters, losing them on heating and at red heat breaking down to alumina, SO2 and O2, its solution acidic by hydrolysis — and at work in foam fire extinguishers, where with sodium bicarbonate it makes a stable CO2 foam for oil fires, and in paper making, leather tanning, dye fixing and water purification (Yemen, Grade 11 — *الكيمياء للصف الثاني الثانوي (Chemistry for the Second Secondary Year)*, Unit 1, printed pp. 16–17)
