53. **The geological eras** (`earth-and-space/the-geological-eras`) — the Phanerozoic eon through its Palaeozoic, Mesozoic and Cenozoic eras, closing on the Quaternary — the last 2,600,000 years, read by isotope stratigraphy — in a synoptic history of the Earth that ends with the geological history of Italy (Italy, Grade 9 — *Il globo terrestre e la sua evoluzione, Edizione blu*, printed pp. 264–290)
54. **Karst — limestone caves and sinkholes** (`earth-and-space/karst-limestone-caves-and-sinkholes`) — the solvent action of water on limestone, and the karst it shapes: caves underground and sinkholes at the surface (Italy, Grade 9 — *Il globo terrestre e la sua evoluzione, Edizione blu*, printed p. 430)
55. **Constellations and the stars we navigate by** (`earth-and-space/constellations-and-the-stars-we-navigate-by`) — the sky's named patterns, and the first stars island children learn: the Southern Cross (Saoten kros), the Morning Star and Evening Star (Moning sta mo Ivening sta), and Sirius — the stars people know the sky by and navigate by — teacher-guide evidence only, acceptance 0 (Vanuatu, Grade 3 — *SAENS — Tija Gaed, Yia 3*, Term 3 sky lessons, printed p. 125)
56. **Landforms — hills, plains and valleys** (`earth-and-space/landforms-hills-plains-and-valleys`) — the relief of a region: what relief is and height above sea level, the high forms — hillocks, hills and mountains — and the hollows and flat lands — river beds, gorges, canyons, plains, basins, valleys and plateaus (Serbia, Grade 3 — *Природа и друштво 3*, printed p. 4)
57. **Earth science — the Earth's materials** (`earth-and-space/earth-science-the-earths-materials`) — Earth science as the study of the Earth itself — its land, waters and air — and its four great materials, rock, soil, water and air, endlessly cycling — curriculum evidence only, acceptance 0 (Ghana, Basic 7 — *Science Curriculum for Junior High Schools*, Strand 2 Cycles: Earth science, printed p. 7)
58. **Space junk and satellites** (`earth-and-space/space-junk-and-satellites`) — learning about space debris — Iceland's own way of opening space study with the debris problem — teacher-guide evidence only, acceptance 0 (Iceland, Grade 3 — *Halló heimur 3*, Ch.1 aims, printed p. 1)
59. **Iceland — the volcanic island and its landscapes** (`earth-and-space/iceland-volcanic-island-and-its-landscapes`) — learning about Iceland the volcanic island — the country's own home-island opening — teacher-guide evidence only, acceptance 0 (Iceland, Grade 3 — *Halló heimur 3*, Ch.6 aims, printed p. 1)
60. **Highlands and lowlands** (`earth-and-space/highlands-and-lowlands`) — getting to know the difference between highland and lowland — teacher-guide evidence only, acceptance 0 (Iceland, Grade 3 — *Halló heimur 3*, Ch.6 aims, printed p. 1)
61. **How an atoll and low island form** (`earth-and-space/how-an-atoll-and-low-island-form`) — a ring reef (atoll) in the sea, and piles of coral and shell sand growing on it into narrow, flat islands (Micronesia, Grades 6–8 — *Pacific Low Island Environments*, printed p. 2)
62. **Rock types — igneous, sedimentary, metamorphic** (`earth-and-space/rock-types-igneous-sedimentary-metamorphic`) — all rocks sorted into three types by how they form: igneous from cooled melted rock, sedimentary from settled layers pressed into rock, metamorphic from existing rock changed by heat and pressure (Costa Rica, Grade 8 — *Ciencias, Octavo, Código 80008, Antologías CONED*, printed p. 118)
63. **Earthquakes and the Richter scale** (`earth-and-space/earthquakes-and-the-richter-scale`) — earthquakes (sismos) as phenomena that alter the geosphere, their classification, and the Richter scale that measures an earthquake's magnitude (Costa Rica, Grade 8 — *Ciencias, Octavo, Código 80008, Antologías CONED*, printed p. 120)
64. **Rocks and minerals** (`earth-and-space/rocks-and-minerals`) — rocks and minerals compared and told apart (a mineral is one stuff throughout; a rock is one or more minerals joined), rocks and minerals classified by colour, size and shape, and the uses of rocks and minerals — curriculum evidence only, acceptance 0 (Bahamas, Grade 2 — *Primary Science Curriculum Guidelines — Grades 1-2*, printed p. 99)

65. **Landforms and water on Earth** (`earth-and-space/landforms-and-water-on-earth`) — developing and using models showing patterns of landforms and water on Earth — valleys, canyons, floodplains; water solid and liquid (United States, Grade 2 — *2nd Grade — for Utah SEEd Standards, Revised 2025*, 2.1.1, printed p. 13)
66. **Earth's surface changes — fast and slow** (`earth-and-space/earths-surface-changes-fast-and-slow`) — changes to Earth's surface that happen quickly (volcanic eruptions, earthquakes, landslides) or slowly (weathering, erosion — its rate by water, ice, wind, gravity and vegetation investigated), explained from evidence at varying time and spatial scales (United States, Grades 2, 5 and 7 — *Utah SEEd Standards* books, 2.1.2 printed p. 26; 5.1.3 printed p. 27; 7.2.2 printed p. 56)
67. **Fossils — evidence of life and environments long ago** (`earth-and-space/fossils-evidence-of-life-and-environments-long-ago`) — analysing fossil data as evidence of organisms and environments from long ago (trilobites and their modern relatives), and arguing from patterns in rock layers and their fossils that environments change over time (tropical plant fossils in the Arctic) (United States, Grade 4 — *4th Grade Science for Utah SEEd Standards, Revised 2025*, 4.1.3 printed p. 41; 4.1.4 printed p. 56)
68. **Patterns of Earth's features** (`earth-and-space/patterns-of-earths-features`) — analysing data to describe patterns of Earth's features — most earthquakes and volcanoes in bands along continent–ocean boundaries; mountain chains inside continents or near their edges (United States, Grade 5 — *5th Grade — for Utah SEEd Standards, Revised 2025*, 5.1.1, printed p. 13)
69. **Gravity and inertia keep objects in orbit** (`earth-and-space/gravity-and-inertia-keep-objects-in-orbit`) — modelling the role of gravity and inertia in the orbital motions of objects in the solar system (United States, Grade 6 — *6th Grade — for Utah SEEd Standards, Revised 2025*, 6.1.2, printed p. 36)
70. **The rock cycle** (`earth-and-space/the-rock-cycle`) — modelling how energy flow and matter cycling create igneous, sedimentary and metamorphic rocks — the rock cycle (United States, Grade 7 — *7th Grade — for Utah SEEd Standards, Revised 2025*, 7.2.1, printed p. 49)
71. **Plate tectonics and its patterns** (`earth-and-space/plate-tectonics-and-its-patterns`) — analysing data on the patterns linking plate tectonics to volcanoes, earthquakes, mountains and seafloor features (United States, Grade 7 — *7th Grade — for Utah SEEd Standards, Revised 2025*, 7.2.5, printed p. 87)
72. **The geologic time scale** (`earth-and-space/the-geologic-time-scale`) — arguing from rock strata and fossil evidence how the geologic time scale shows Earth's age and history (United States, Grade 7 — *7th Grade — for Utah SEEd Standards, Revised 2025*, 7.2.6, printed p. 98)
73. **Weightlessness and floating in orbit** (`earth-and-space/weightlessness-and-floating-in-orbit`) — weightlessness, with the falling-bottle activities: when bottle and water fall together nothing pushes back, so weight is not felt — and orbit is that fall kept going around the Earth (Pakistan (Punjab), Grade 7 — *General Science 7*, §12.3, printed p. 142)
74. **Rhythms in nature** (`earth-and-space/rhythms-in-nature`) — nature keeps time in rhythms — daily, yearly, century and multi-century changes that come back in order, together nature's calendar (Armenia, Grade 6 — *Բնություն 6 (Nature 6)*, §9, printed p. 27)
75. **The Earth's own history — how our planet changed** (`earth-and-space/the-earths-own-history-how-our-planet-changed`) — the Earth itself has a history — formed with the Solar System, it then evolved, and its evolution closes the origin story (Armenia, Grade 10 — *Բնագիտություն 10 (Natural Science 10)*, §9.4, printed p. 167)
76. **Movements of sea water — waves, currents and tides** (`earth-and-space/movements-of-sea-water-waves-currents-and-tides`) — sea water is never still: it moves in waves on its surface, in currents through its body, and in tides rising and falling at the shore (Cuba, Grade 5 — *Ciencias Naturales, Quinto grado*, printed p. 106)
77. **Continents and oceans of the Earth** (`earth-and-space/continents-and-oceans-of-the-earth`) — the Earth's surface sorted into its great lands — the continents — and its great waters — the oceans — with islands and seas between them (Cuba, Grade 6 — *Ciencias Naturales, Sexto grado*, printed pp. 27, 28, 34)
78. **Landscape features — and how they formed** (`earth-and-space/landscape-features-and-how-they-formed`) — mapping Samoa's special landscape features (waterfalls, cliffs, caves, lava flows, the chain of volcanoes, blowholes, Sliding Rock, bays, lakes, pools, rivers), its special environments and its people-made features, then a field trip hunting evidence of how the landscape formed and is changing — volcanic ash and lava, water erosion, plants splitting rock, wind-bent trees, and people's changes for better and worse (Samoa, Year 11 — *Science — Year 11, Book 1 (MESC Samoa)*, printed pp. 89–92)

79. **Faults and folds in the Earth's crust** (`earth-and-space/faults-and-folds-in-the-earths-crust`) — rock layers bent into folds or cracked into faults by the plates' pressure — taught between plate tectonics and the chapter's volcanoes and earthquakes (Cambodia, Grade 9 — *វិទ្យាសាស្ត្រ ថ្នាក់ទី៩ (Science, Grade 9)*, Earth Ch. 2, Lesson 2, printed p. 282; built for the Cambodia placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/cambodia-placements.md)
80. **Identifying minerals by their properties** (`earth-and-space/identifying-minerals-by-their-properties`) — telling minerals apart by lustre, streak, hardness, fracture and magnetism, with a table of common minerals' properties (Sudan, Grade 9 — *العلوم الطبيعية (Natural Sciences), Grade 9*, Unit 7, printed p. 163)

81. **Magmatic rocks — plutonic and volcanic** (`earth-and-space/magmatic-rocks-plutonic-and-volcanic`) — magmatic rocks — plutonic and volcanic — rocks born from molten rock, split by their cooling place: plutonic cooled slowly below the surface, volcanic cooled quickly at it (Mauritania, Grade 9 — *Sciences Naturelles, 3ème AS*, printed pp. 101, 110, and Grade 10 — *Sciences Naturelles, 4ème AS*, printed pp. 89, 93; built for the Mauritania placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/mauritania-placements.md)
82. **Metamorphic rocks and the rock cycle** (`earth-and-space/metamorphic-rocks-and-the-rock-cycle`) — metamorphic rocks and the rock cycle — rocks changed by heat and pressure without melting, gneiss as the worked example, and the cycle linking all three rock families (Mauritania, Grade 9 — *Sciences Naturelles, 3ème AS*, printed pp. 129–137, and Grade 10 — *Sciences Naturelles, 4ème AS*, printed pp. 97, 101; built for the Mauritania placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/mauritania-placements.md)
83. **Mauritania's geological ensembles** (`earth-and-space/mauritanias-geological-ensembles`) — Mauritania's geological ensembles — the country's great rock regions, taught between the geological eras and its mining resources (Mauritania, Grade 11 — *Sciences Naturelles, 5ème AS, série D*, printed p. 129; built for the Mauritania placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/mauritania-placements.md)

84. **Polar day and polar night** (`earth-and-space/polar-day-and-polar-night`) — the days near the poles when the Sun never sets and the nights when it never rises, and the Earth's tilted axis that causes both (Bhutan, Class VI — *Science, Class VI*, §12.2, printed p. 202; built for the Bhutan placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/bhutan-placements.md)

85. **Earthquakes and tsunamis** (`earth-and-space/earthquakes-and-tsunamis`) — earthquakes: rock slipping along a fault, seismic waves, and the seismograph that records them; and tsunamis (maremotos), the great sea waves an undersea earthquake sets off — taught together as an island's joined hazard (Dominican Republic, Grade 3 — *Ciencias de la Naturaleza 3*, printed p. 128, and Grade 5 — *Ciencias de la Naturaleza 5*, printed p. 144; built for the Dominican Republic placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/dominican-republic-placements.md)

86. **Landforms and geological features of our island** (`earth-and-space/landforms-and-geological-features-of-our-island`) — the geological characteristics of the island of Hispaniola, above all its mountain systems, read as the landforms of the learner's own island (Dominican Republic, Grade 5 — *Ciencias de la Naturaleza 5*, printed p. 36; built for the Dominican Republic placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/dominican-republic-placements.md)

87. **Identifying rocks by colour, grain and hardness** (`earth-and-space/identifying-rocks-by-colour-grain-and-hardness`) — collected rocks identified by colour, grain, texture and hardness (the scratch test) against the book's set of five: granite, sandstone, shale, limestone and marble (China, Grade 3 — *科学三年级下册 (Science, Grade 3, second volume)*, printed p. 10; built for the China placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/china-placements.md)

88. **Make and launch a model rocket** (`earth-and-space/make-and-launch-a-model-rocket`) — making a model rocket part by part (nose, body, fins) and launching it: the flight as the test of the build, closing the space unit (China, Grade 6 — *科学六年级下册 (Science, Grade 6, second volume)*, printed p. 53; built for the China placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/china-placements.md)

89. **Rockets — and how they fly** (`earth-and-space/rockets-and-how-they-fly`) — the machine that carries its push with it: burning gases thrown down at great speed thrust the rocket up, by Newton's third law, even in empty space (Eritrea, Grade 8 — *Science for Grade 8 Textbook*, printed p. 151; built for the Eritrea placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/eritrea-placements.md)

90. **Artificial satellites** (`earth-and-space/artificial-satellites`) — the machines people put into orbit and what they are used for — weather watching, communications, navigation and observing the Earth (Sri Lanka, Grade 8 — *Science Part – II, Grade 8*, printed p. 114, and Grade 6 New Science (Tamil, 1986), printed p. 115; built for the Sri Lanka placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/sri-lanka-placements.md)
91. **Astrology — a superstition about space** (`earth-and-space/astrology-a-superstition-about-space`) — the prevailing superstition about space — astrology — tested against astronomy: Bangladesh's Class 6 book's own way of teaching critical thinking about the sky (Bangladesh, Grade 6 — *Science Investigative Study Class 6*, printed p. 25; built for the Bangladesh placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/bangladesh-placements.md)

92. **Global wind belts and the Coriolis effect** (`earth-and-space/global-wind-belts-and-the-coriolis-effect`) — global air currents: the sun's rays fall vertically on the equator and slanting on the poles, so equatorial air heats more, rises and moves poleward while cold polar air sinks equatorward; the earth's rotation bends the moving… (Saudi Arabia, First Intermediate, Part 2 — *Science, First Intermediate, Part 2*, printed p. 26; built for the Saudi Arabia placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/saudi-arabia-placements.md)
93. **The Moon's surface — highlands, maria and craters** (`earth-and-space/the-moons-surface-highlands-maria-and-craters`) — Earth's Moon: until Galileo's telescope in 1609 the moon was thought smooth; its highlands are pocked with craters from crashing meteorites, and its dark flat areas, the maria, formed when lava flowed into low ground and… (Saudi Arabia, First Intermediate, Part 2 — *Science, First Intermediate, Part 2*, printed p. 46; built for the Saudi Arabia placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/saudi-arabia-placements.md)
94. **The astronomical unit — measuring distances in the solar system** (`earth-and-space/the-astronomical-unit-measuring-distances-in-the-solar-system`) — distances in space: the solar system holds together (eight planets and other bodies in elliptical orbits) because of the sun's huge gravity; kilometres are too small inside it, so astronomers use the astronomical unit — the… (Saudi Arabia, First Intermediate, Part 2 — *Science, First Intermediate, Part 2*, printed p. 50; built for the Saudi Arabia placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/saudi-arabia-placements.md)
95. **Seeing the past — light travel time from the stars** (`earth-and-space/seeing-the-past-light-travel-time-from-the-stars`) — light coming from the past: even travelling at light's speed, starlight takes years to reach us, so when you look at a star you see it as it was when its light set out — the distances between bodies in space are so huge that… (Saudi Arabia, First Intermediate, Part 2 — *Science, First Intermediate, Part 2*, printed p. 56; built for the Saudi Arabia placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/saudi-arabia-placements.md)
96. **Telescopes — refracting, reflecting, radio and space** (`earth-and-space/telescopes-refracting-reflecting-radio-and-space`) — the means of watching the universe: the refracting telescope gathers light with a convex lens; the reflecting telescope with a concave mirror (the great European reflector's mirror is 8 m across); optical telescopes are housed… (Saudi Arabia, First Intermediate, Part 2 — *Science, First Intermediate, Part 2*, printed pp. 57–58; built for the Saudi Arabia placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/saudi-arabia-placements.md)
97. **The light year — measuring the universe** (`earth-and-space/the-light-year-measuring-the-universe`) — the light year: light travels at 300,000 km/s, circling the earth about seven times a second; the light year is the distance it covers in one year, about 9.5 trillion km — the unit fit for distances between galaxies, whose… (Saudi Arabia, First Intermediate, Part 2 — *Science, First Intermediate, Part 2*, printed p. 64; built for the Saudi Arabia placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/saudi-arabia-placements.md)
98. **Designing a space colony — choosing a planet** (`earth-and-space/designing-a-space-colony-choosing-a-planet`) — real-life inquiry closing the chapter: the space colony — choose a planet, study its surface conditions (solid or gas; hot or cold; a thick, thin or absent atmosphere), and design on paper a colony in which people could live… (Saudi Arabia, First Intermediate, Part 2 — *Science, First Intermediate, Part 2*, printed pp. 66–67; built for the Saudi Arabia placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/saudi-arabia-placements.md)
99. **Pluto and the dwarf planets — why Pluto was reclassified** (`earth-and-space/pluto-and-the-dwarf-planets-why-pluto-was-reclassified`) — science, technology and society: Pluto, from planet to dwarf planet — found in 1930 and counted the ninth planet, it was reclassified when the International Astronomical Union set the planet test (circles the sun; round by its… (Saudi Arabia, First Intermediate, Part 2 — *Science, First Intermediate, Part 2*, printed p. 68; built for the Saudi Arabia placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/saudi-arabia-placements.md)

100. **Volcanoes — benefits and harms** (`earth-and-space/volcanoes-benefits-and-harms`) — Nicaragua's volcanoes from both sides: the benefits of volcanic ash — ash can fertilise the soil — set against the harm of fresh ash for health (Nicaragua, Grade 8 — *Ciencias Naturales, Octavo grado*, printed pp. 92–94; built for the Nicaragua placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/nicaragua-placements.md)

101. **Geohazards — landslides, sinkholes, tsunamis and acid rain** (`earth-and-space/geohazards-landslides-sinkholes-tsunamis-and-acid-rain`) — landslides, sinkholes (tanah jerlus), tsunamis and acid rain, and early-warning instruments for landslides and tsunamis (Malaysia, Grade 7 (Form 1) — *Sains Tingkatan 1*, printed p. 265; built for the Malaysia placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/malaysia-placements.md)

102. **Space weather and its effects on Earth** (`earth-and-space/space-weather-and-its-effects-on-earth`) — space weather defined by its effects — auroras, and disruption to satellites and power grids (Malaysia, Grade 9 (Form 3) — *Sains Tingkatan 3*, printed p. 263; built for the Malaysia placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/malaysia-placements.md)

103. **Oceanography — the world ocean** (`earth-and-space/oceanography-the-world-ocean`) — the earliest explorers, the main oceans and Southeast Asia's seas, the ocean floor mapped zone by zone, seawater's temperature, pressure and density, and ocean life classified by size, mobility and location (Malaysia, Grade 11 (Form 5) — *Sains Tambahan Tingkatan 5*, printed p. 164; built for the Malaysia placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/malaysia-placements.md)

## Unit 20 — Motion, Force and Energy

1. **Motion — motion needs a reference point** (`motion-force-and-energy/motion-needs-a-reference-point`) — motion is a change of position against a chosen reference point or frame — the same body can be moving and at rest at once, depending on the frame chosen (Grade 7 General Science)
2. **Motion — four kinds of motion** (`motion-force-and-energy/motion-four-kinds-of-motion`) — motions sort by their path into four kinds — linear, oscillatory, curvilinear and rotational (Grade 7 General Science)
3. **Motion — distance and speed** (`motion-force-and-energy/motion-distance-and-speed`) — distance is the total length of the path travelled, and average speed = total distance ÷ total time — measured for real in the ball-rolling practical (Grade 7 General Science)
4. **Motion — displacement and velocity** (`motion-force-and-energy/motion-displacement-and-velocity`) — displacement is distance travelled in a specified direction — the straight change from start to finish — and velocity = change of displacement ÷ time (Grade 7 General Science)
5. **Motion — acceleration** (`motion-force-and-energy/motion-acceleration`) — acceleration is the rate of change of velocity — a = Δv/Δt — positive when speeding up, negative when slowing down, zero at steady velocity (Grade 7 General Science)
6. **Force — what force is and what it does** (`motion-force-and-energy/force-what-force-is-and-what-it-does`) — a force is an interaction between bodies that acts by contact (push, pull) or across a gap without contact (gravity, magnetism, electric force) (Grade 7 General Science)
7. **Force — gravity, weight and the newton meter** (`motion-force-and-energy/force-gravity-weight-and-the-newton-meter`) — weight is gravity's force on a mass — W = mg with g = 9.8 — and force is measured with the newton meter, whose spring stretches in proportion to the force (Grade 7 General Science)
8. **Force and motion — Newton's first law** (`motion-force-and-energy/force-and-motion-newtons-first-law`) — a body at rest stays at rest, and a body in motion keeps constant velocity, unless a net external force acts on it; inertia is the name of that resistance (Grade 7 General Science)
9. **Force and motion — Newton's second law** (`motion-force-and-energy/force-and-motion-newtons-second-law`) — read off the cart experiment: acceleration is proportional to the net force, inversely proportional to the mass, and in the force's direction (Grade 7 General Science)
10. **Force and motion — Newton's third law** (`motion-force-and-energy/force-and-motion-newtons-third-law`) — every action has an equal and opposite reaction, and the pair never cancels because the two forces act on two different bodies (Grade 7 General Science)
11. **Energy — kinetic and potential energy** (`motion-force-and-energy/energy-kinetic-and-potential-energy`) — energy is the ability to cause change — measured in joules — kinetic energy KE = ½mv², the energy of moving, and potential energy, the energy of position (Grade 7 General Science)
12. **Energy — forms and conversions** (`motion-force-and-energy/energy-forms-and-conversions`) — energy wears eight named forms — electrical, thermal, sound, radiant, chemical, nuclear, gravitational and mechanical — and its story is conversion from form to form (Grade 7 General Science)
13. **Energy — sources, and using energy wisely** (`motion-force-and-energy/energy-sources-and-using-energy-wisely`) — energy sources split into non-renewable (finite) and renewable (endless) — and because the finite ones run out, energy must be used wisely (Grade 7 General Science)
14. **Energy — degradation: why useful energy is lost as heat** (`motion-force-and-energy/energy-degradation-why-useful-energy-turns-to-heat`) — energy is never destroyed, but in every conversion some of it degrades — spreading out as heat that cannot be gathered back — and efficiency measures the leak (Grade 7, Argentina — Cuadernos para el aula, NAP)
15. **How electricity is generated — power stations from source to grid** (`motion-force-and-energy/how-electricity-is-generated-power-stations-to-the-grid`) — an energy source turns a turbine, the turbine spins a generator, and the grid carries the electricity home — the whole generation chain, source to socket (Grade 7, Argentina — Cuadernos para el aula, NAP)
16. **Force, motion and energy — problem set** (`motion-force-and-energy/force-motion-and-energy-problem-set`) — the unit's review problems worked as one set — reading a speed–time graph, gravity on a falling ball, kinetic energy calculated backwards (Grade 7 General Science)
17. **Air resistance and the parachute** (`motion-force-and-energy/air-resistance-and-the-parachute`) — drag, the air's push-back, grows with speed and with area facing the air; a tissue-paper parachute makes so much of it that drag balances weight and the fall settles slow and steady — bigger canopy, gentler descent — instructor-pack evidence only, acceptance 0 (Finland, Grade 1 recorded — Fysiikkaa: Tutkitaan ilmaa ja lämpöä)
18. **Lift — how a wing's pressure difference holds a glider up** (`motion-force-and-energy/lift-how-a-wing-holds-a-glider-up`) — a wing's curved top leaves lower pressure above and higher below, and the difference is lift: paper-helicopter rotors and engineless gliders both fly on it, a glider paying with height at the rate of its glide ratio G = D/H — instructor-pack evidence only, acceptance 0 (Finland, Grade 1 recorded — Fysiikkaa: Tutkitaan ilmaa ja lämpöä)
19. **Energy — first noticing** (`motion-force-and-energy/energy-first-noticing`) — energy met for the first time, by noticing it at work in the things around us — shining, warming, sounding, moving — inside a materials unit, before forms, sources or measuring — teacher-module evidence only, acceptance 0 (Zambia, Grade 1 — *Mathematics and Science Teaching Module, Grade 1, Module 2*, Topic 4, Sub-Topic 2, printed p. 28)
20. **Air and water resistance — drag** (`motion-force-and-energy/air-and-water-resistance-drag`) — learning about water resistance and air resistance, taught as one pair — teacher-guide evidence only, acceptance 0 (Iceland, Grade 3 — *Halló heimur 3*, Ch.2 aims, printed p. 1)
21. **Conservation of energy and matter** (`motion-force-and-energy/conservation-of-energy-and-matter`) — the law of conservation of matter and energy stated as one law: matter is rearranged and energy is converted — neither is created or destroyed (Costa Rica, Grade 8 — *Ciencias, Octavo, Código 80008, Antologías CONED*, printed p. 47)
22. **Energy in food — calories and joules** (`motion-force-and-energy/energy-in-food-calories-and-joules`) — the joule, the unit of energy, met through the calories that measure the energy held in food (Costa Rica, Grade 8 — *Ciencias, Octavo, Código 80008, Antologías CONED*, printed p. 54)


23. **Energy stores — where energy is kept** (`motion-force-and-energy/energy-stores-where-energy-is-kept`) — energy kept until it is used: fuels, foods, batteries and other stores, and stored energy waiting to be released in the workbook's experiments (Greece, Grade 5 — *«Φυσικά» Ε΄ Δημοτικού — Ερευνώ και Ανακαλύπτω*, Energy ch. 2, printed p. 28)
24. **The pendulum and its swing** (`motion-force-and-energy/the-pendulum-and-its-swing`) — a weight hanging from a string swings back and forth through its lowest point in a steady beat; the experiment: build it, let go without pushing, count and time the swings as they slowly shrink (Cabo Verde, Grade 3 — *Manual de Ciências Integradas, 3.º ano*, Tema 8, printed p. 74)
25. **Means of transport — land, water and air** (`motion-force-and-energy/means-of-transport-land-water-air`) — classifying land, water and air transport — curriculum-proposal evidence only, acceptance 0 (Venezuela, Primary Grade 1 — *Propuestas de contenidos, Septiembre 2025*, printed p. 15; built for the Venezuela placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/venezuela-placements.md)
26. **Building a gurrufío** (`motion-force-and-energy/building-a-gurrufio`) — building a gurrufío, the traditional Venezuelan spinning toy, and asking who supplies its energy (Venezuela, Grade 2 — *Comunidad con Ciencia (Colección Bicentenario)*, printed pp. 118–119; built for the Venezuela placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/venezuela-placements.md)

27. **Pressure in fluids — Pascal and Archimedes** (`motion-force-and-energy/pressure-in-fluids-pascal-and-archimedes`) — hydrostatics: pressure inside a fluid at rest, growing with depth; Pascal's principle — pressure passed through a closed fluid undiminished — and Archimedes' principle — upthrust equal to the weight of fluid displaced (Dominican Republic, Grade 6 — *Ciencias de la Naturaleza 6*, printed pp. 130, 132; built for the Dominican Republic placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/dominican-republic-placements.md)

28. **Work in science — when a force moves a thing** (`motion-force-and-energy/work-in-science-when-a-force-moves-a-thing`) — work as a science idea, opened with Syria's own question: who does the moving of the farmer's cart — the farmer or the horse? A force does work when it moves a thing; a push that moves nothing does no work in the science sense (Syria, Grade 6, Semester 1 — *Science Book (كتاب العلوم), Sixth Basic Grade, First Semester*, printed pp. 49–52; built for the Syria placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/syria-placements.md)

29. **Power — work done per unit time** (`motion-force-and-energy/power-work-done-per-unit-time`) — not how much work, but how fast: power = work ÷ time, counted in watts — closing Eritrea's work, energy, power run (Eritrea, Grade 7 — *Science for Grade 7 Textbook*, printed p. 71; built for the Eritrea placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/eritrea-placements.md)
30. **Centre of gravity and stability** (`motion-force-and-energy/centre-of-gravity-and-stability`) — the centre of gravity, and how lowering it makes objects stable, through the balancing parrot and the balancing ruler — teacher-guide evidence only, acceptance 0 (Fiji, Year 4 — *Elementary Science Year 4 — Teacher Resource Material*, printed pp. 59–60; built for the Fiji placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/fiji-placements.md)
31. **Pressure in solids, liquids and gases** (`motion-force-and-energy/pressure-in-solids-liquids-and-gases`) — pressure and force across solids, liquids and gases, opened Fiji's way through the primus stove and kerosene accidents (Fiji, Year 7 — *Year 7 Basic Science*, printed pp. 106–125; built for the Fiji placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/fiji-placements.md)
32. **Buoyancy — weight in different mediums** (`motion-force-and-energy/buoyancy-weight-in-different-mediums`) — how oil, water and air affect the weight and mass of objects: apparent weight in fluids (Fiji, Year 8 — *Year 8 Basic Science*, printed pp. 105–125; built for the Fiji placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/fiji-placements.md)

33. **Work is force times distance** (`motion-force-and-energy/work-is-force-times-distance`) — Ch. (Saudi Arabia, Grade 3, Part 2 — *Science, Grade 3, Part 2*, printed pp. 122–124; built for the Saudi Arabia placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/saudi-arabia-placements.md)

34. **Radioactivity — radiation and its uses** (`motion-force-and-energy/radioactivity-radiation-and-its-uses`) — nuclear fission and fusion, more than 30 countries generating electricity, Malaysia's 1 MW TRIGA PUSPATI research reactor (1982), Hiroshima and Nagasaki, and the for-and-against debate (Malaysia, Grade 10 (Form 4) — *Sains Tingkatan 4*, printed p. 256; built for the Malaysia placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/malaysia-placements.md)

## Unit 21 — Sound

1. **Sound — vibration, volume and pitch** (`sound/sound-vibration-volume-and-pitch`) — every sound is a vibrating object setting the surrounding medium vibrating in turn; volume and pitch are two independent qualities — loudness from amplitude, pitch from frequency (Grade 8 General Science)
2. **Sound — media and speed: solid beats liquid beats gas** (`sound/sound-media-and-speed-solid-beats-liquid-beats-gas`) — sound travels out in all directions through matter — air, liquids and solids alike — and never through a vacuum; solids carry it fastest (Grade 8 General Science)
3. **Hearing — the path from vibration to brain** (`sound/hearing-the-path-from-vibration-to-brain`) — hearing is a relay in order — object vibrates, air vibrated, vibrations reach the ear, inner ear vibrates, electrical signals carry to the brain (Grade 8 General Science)
4. **Echoes at work — echolocation and ultrasound** (`sound/echoes-at-work-echolocation-and-ultrasound`) — reflected sound at a boundary is an echo, heard separately only when it returns at least 0.1 s after the original — bats, dolphins, sonar and ultrasound above 20 kHz put it to work (Grade 8 General Science)
5. **Noise pollution — harm, rights and compromise** (`sound/noise-pollution-harm-rights-and-compromise`) — noise pollution is sound that is loud, long-lasting, unpleasant or harmful — its hearing harm named, and the rights on both sides argued to a compromise (Grade 8 General Science)
6. **Making sound with code — a programmable tone generator** (`sound/making-sound-with-code-a-programmable-tone-generator`) — Finland's way of teaching sound: a micro:bit (or its MakeCode simulator) plays the frequency you set, so pitch and loudness become numbers you choose, match by ear against a played tone, and stack into a looped composition (Finland, Grade 7 — Yläkoulun äänioppi, micro:bit lesson plan)
7. **Musical instruments as physics** (`sound/musical-instruments-as-physics`) — every instrument is a governed vibration, sorted by what vibrates: strings (f = (1/2L)√(T/μ) — shorter, tighter, lighter means higher), air columns (length sets the pitch; an open pipe's fundamental has λ = 2L, a closed pipe's λ = 4L, so the closed pipe sounds an octave lower at the same length) and membranes (tighter and smaller mean higher), with loudness from amplitude — playing harder is louder, never higher — and the instrument's body resonating to carry the sound — teacher-guide evidence only, acceptance 0 (Nigeria — *Excellence in Physics for Senior Secondary Schools — Teacher's Guide Book 2*, Cambridge University Press, written to the NERDC curriculum, Theme 6 Topic 2: musical instruments, printed p. 60)
8. **Musical sounds, instruments and room acoustics** (`sound/musical-sounds-instruments-and-room-acoustics`) — a musical tone is a fundamental (pitch) dressed in overtones (timbre); instruments family by what vibrates — strings (dutar, rubab, tanbur), air columns, membranes (doira) — and a room answers with reflections: absorption and a reverberation of about two seconds decide whether music sounds rich or muddy (Uzbekistan, Grade 6 — *ФИЗИКА 6*, Ch. VI, Tema 57, printed p. 151)

9. **Materials that block sound — and why** (`sound/materials-that-block-sound-and-why`) — materials that stop sound travelling, tested against our own ears (North Macedonia, Grade 4 — *Природне науке за четврти разред (Natural Sciences for Grade 4)* (Serbian edition), printed pp. 110–112; built for the North Macedonia placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/north-macedonia-placements.md)

## Unit 22 — Heat

1. **Heat — conduction, conductors and insulators** (`heat/heat-conduction-conductors-and-insulators`) — heat is transfer driven by temperature difference — from the hotter to the cooler until temperatures equalise — and conduction through contact sorts materials into conductors (copper and aluminium best) and insulators (Grade 8 General Science)
2. **Heat — convection currents in liquids and gases** (`heat/heat-convection-currents-in-liquids-and-gases`) — in fluids the particles move freely, so warmed fluid expands, grows lighter and rises while cooler heavier fluid sinks — a convection current, traced by sawdust (Grade 8 General Science)
3. **Heat — radiation and the black/white test** (`heat/heat-radiation-and-the-black-white-test`) — radiation transfers heat by electromagnetic waves with no contact and no particles, working even through a vacuum — and black surfaces absorb it where white reflect it, proved by a two-hour fair test (Grade 8 General Science)
4. **Insulation — slowing heat down** (`heat/insulation-slowing-heat-down`) — an insulator slows heat transfer in BOTH directions — the defended claim of the two ice cubes, one under a wool blanket — so food, houses and the gabi all use it (Grade 8 General Science)

5. **Designing shade to reduce warming** (`heat/designing-shade-to-reduce-warming`) — designing, sketching/modelling and testing a shade solution that reduces sunlight warming of an area — eating tables, a dog, a toy (United States, Kindergarten — *Kindergarten — for Utah SEEd Standards, Revised 2025*, K.1.4, printed p. 39)

6. **Expansion and contraction — when things heat and cool** (`heat/expansion-and-contraction-when-things-heat-and-cool`) — metals and liquids expand when heated and contract when cooled (the bar-breaking apparatus; how a thermometer works) (Seychelles, Grade 8 (P8) — *Seychelles Integrated Science — Heat and Molecules*, Unit 10, Topic 2, printed p. 4; built for the Seychelles placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/seychelles-placements.md)

7. **Specific heat capacity** (`heat/specific-heat-capacity`) — equal heat warms equal masses unequally: the heat one kilogram of a material needs to rise one degree — water's high value against a metal pan's low one (Eritrea, Grade 8 — *Science for Grade 8 Textbook*, printed p. 125; built for the Eritrea placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/eritrea-placements.md)
8. **Sources of heat around us** (`heat/sources-of-heat-around-us`) — Where the heat we feel comes from — the sources of heat — and why heat matters, as Rwanda's Primary 2 SET book teaches inside its light and heat unit (Rwanda, Primary 2 — *Science and Elementary Technology, Primary Two, Pupil's Book*, printed pp. 84–85; built for the Rwanda placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/rwanda-placements.md)
9. **Dangers of heat and staying safe** (`heat/dangers-of-heat-and-staying-safe`) — The harmful effects of heat — and staying safe around it — as Rwanda's Primary 2 SET book teaches straight after the sources and importance of heat (Rwanda, Primary 2 — *Science and Elementary Technology, Primary Two, Pupil's Book*, printed p. 87; built for the Rwanda placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/rwanda-placements.md)

10. **Uses of heat in daily life** (`heat/uses-of-heat-in-daily-life`) — the uses of heat: drying, warming, cooking and ironing — grains dried to remove moisture and stop them going bad — with the guide's warning that charcoal stoves in closed rooms give off harmful carbon monoxide (South Sudan, Primary 4 — *Primary Science, Pupil's Book 4*, 6.7 Uses of heat: drying, warming, cooking and ironing, SS P4 SB, printed p. 104; built for the South Sudan placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/south-sudan-placements.md)

## Unit 23 — Electricity and Magnetism

1. **Circuits — source, load, wires, and open vs closed** (`electricity-and-magnetism/circuits-source-load-wires-open-and-closed`) — open tinkering with battery, wire and bulb earns the concepts: current electricity is charge flowing continuously, and a circuit runs only when its loop is closed (Grade 8 General Science)
2. **Series and parallel circuits — one path, many paths** (`electricity-and-magnetism/series-and-parallel-circuits-one-path-many-paths`) — a series circuit is a one-way circular road — break any part and everything stops — while a parallel circuit gives each load its own branch, which is how home lighting is deduced (Grade 8 General Science)
3. **Voltage, resistance and Ohm's law — V = IR** (`electricity-and-magnetism/voltage-resistance-and-ohms-law`) — what sets the current is two governors — voltage, the strength of the power source, and resistance — tied by Ohm's law V = IR and its three rearrangements (Grade 8 General Science)
4. **Magnets — poles attract and repel; what is magnetic** (`electricity-and-magnetism/magnets-poles-and-magnetic-materials`) — a magnet acts without touching, its force strongest at its two poles; unlike poles attract, like poles repel, and a cut magnet is never a single pole — each piece is a whole magnet (Grade 8 General Science)
5. **Magnetic fields — field lines, Earth's field and the compass** (`electricity-and-magnetism/magnetic-fields-earths-field-and-the-compass`) — the field around a magnet is made visible by iron filings; Earth itself is a magnet, and a compass is made from a floated needle (Grade 8 General Science)
6. **Electromagnets — a magnet you switch on** (`electricity-and-magnetism/electromagnets-a-magnet-you-switch-on`) — a current creates a magnetic field around a wire; wound on an iron nail and switched, it lifts clips — and drops them all when the switch opens (Grade 8 General Science)
7. **Motors and generators — electricity to motion, motion to electricity** (`electricity-and-magnetism/motors-and-generators-electricity-to-motion-motion-to-electricity`) — one machine runs both directions: the motor turns current into spin, the generator turns spin into current — closing on AC against DC (Grade 8 General Science)
8. **Electricity around us** (`electricity-and-magnetism/electricity-around-us`) — electricity met first as the power already at work in the lamps, screens, machines and devices of everyday life, and its jobs named — light, movement, heat, sound, messages — before any circuit is built — teacher-guide evidence only, acceptance 0 (Albania, Grade 2 — *Libër mësuesi — Dituri natyre 2*, printed p. 84)
9. **When there is no electricity** (`electricity-and-magnetism/when-there-is-no-electricity`) — what stops when the electricity goes, and how life goes on the older way: candles for light, fire for cooking and warmth, cool places for food, work by daylight — teacher-guide evidence only, acceptance 0 (Albania, Grade 2 — *Libër mësuesi — Dituri natyre 2*, printed p. 89)
10. **Drawing circuits — with symbols** (`electricity-and-magnetism/drawing-circuits-with-symbols`) — every real circuit part replaced by its agreed symbol — battery, bulb, switch, wires — and a real circuit drawn in symbols in its own order, in a closed loop, and read back part by part — teacher-guide evidence only, acceptance 0 (Albania, Grade 2 — *Libër mësuesi — Dituri natyre 2*, printed p. 94)
11. **Using electricity safely at home** (`electricity-and-magnetism/using-electricity-safely-at-home`) — the dangers of electricity and how to protect yourself — teacher-guide evidence only, acceptance 0 (Djibouti, Grade 4 — *Guide de l'enseignant — Sciences Expérimentales, 4ème année*, Leçon 21, printed p. 76)
12. **Fuses and circuit breakers — keeping circuits safe** (`electricity-and-magnetism/fuses-and-circuit-breakers-keeping-circuits-safe`) — the fuse and its uses: fuse ratings, and the circuit breaker doing the same job as a resettable switch (Pakistan (Punjab), Grade 5 — *General Science 5*, §7.3, printed p. 82)
13. **Static electricity and lightning** (`electricity-and-magnetism/static-electricity-and-lightning`) — how static charges build up by rubbing, like charges repel and unlike attract, and lightning as a giant discharge (Pakistan (Punjab), Grade 5 — *General Science 5*, §7.4, printed p. 83)

14. **Reading the electricity meter and the cost of units** (`electricity-and-magnetism/reading-the-electricity-meter-and-the-cost-of-units`) — reading an electricity meter, calculating the units used in a month and their cost, and measuring an appliance's power in watts (Seychelles, Grade 9 (P9) — *Seychelles Integrated Science — Conserving Our Energy*, Unit 11, Topic 5, printed booklet pp. 12–14; built for the Seychelles placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/seychelles-placements.md)

15. **Using household electrical appliances safely** (`electricity-and-magnetism/using-household-electrical-appliances-safely`) — using household electrical appliances safely — the home's electrical machines used in their right order, and the electric iron studied part by part with its safe handling (Mauritania, Grade 4 — *Sciences Naturelles, 4e année fondamentale*, printed pp. 59, 61; built for the Mauritania placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/mauritania-placements.md)

16. **Sources of electricity** (`electricity-and-magnetism/sources-of-electricity`) — where electricity comes from, named before any circuit — cells and batteries that store it, and the mains supply, generators and solar cells that make it (Bhutan, Class IV — *Science, Class IV*, §7.1, printed p. 83; built for the Bhutan placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/bhutan-placements.md)

17. **Materials that conduct electricity** (`electricity-and-magnetism/materials-that-conduct-electricity`) — metals conduct and rubber, plastic, wood and glass insulate — tested with a gap circuit and a lamp, and why every wire needs both kinds (Kuwait, Grade 3, Term 2 — *Science, Grade 3, Term 2 (Student Book)*, printed p. 25; built for the Kuwait placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/kuwait-placements.md)

18. **Finding circuit faults with a tester** (`electricity-and-magnetism/finding-circuit-faults-with-a-tester`) — why the torch will not light: guessing the fault part by part, building a simple circuit tester, and cornering the break in the loop (China, Grade 3 — *科学三年级上册 (Science, Grade 3, first volume)*, printed p. 33; built for the China placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/china-placements.md)

19. **Electronic appliances and LEDs** (`electricity-and-magnetism/electronic-appliances-and-leds`) — the electronic appliances of daily life and the components inside them, such as LEDs — met at the close of the electricity chapter (Sri Lanka, Grade 6 — *Science, Grade 6*, printed p. 122; built for the Sri Lanka placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/sri-lanka-placements.md)
20. **Tools and materials for electricity** (`electricity-and-magnetism/tools-and-materials-for-electricity`) — The common tools and the common materials used in electricity, as Rwanda's Primary 5 SET book teaches them inside its electricity unit, before the simple circuit (Rwanda, Primary 5 — *Science and Elementary Technology for Rwandan Schools, Pupil's Book 5*, printed p. 197; built for the Rwanda placements, expansion-science-2026-11-13/rwanda-placements.md)
